# M.O.T.H. — design

**Status (2026-11-02, MO1, Opus 5.5 `claude-opus-5-5`): the RECORD of Session 1's design round, not yet the final design.** Six areas were designed from the session's measurements and each design was attacked by a security reviewer and a correctness reviewer, all on Opus 5.5 (her answer to the plan's Q1, *"Opus for all sessions"*). Every design came back *buildable with the fixes, not as written*: the reviewers' blockers and majors below are **unresolved**. The next step rewrites this file in place into the final design, each finding taken or refuted against the code with its reason, before any migration is written (`docs/sessions/handoffs/po-moth1-2026-11-02.md`, In flight). Nothing here has been built.

How it was made: the measuring round (nine read-only readers over the handbook, `OUTSTANDING.md` and the code at `9a2d7023`, whose code equals `91d696b0`, the commit `PLAN_MOTH.md` measured) found no material contradiction with the plan and refined four of its claims (E3, E4, E14, E17); the design round then ran one designer per area, two rival designers and a judge for stable file bodies, and two reviewers per design. The rival drafts are not reproduced; the judge's merged design is. Text is the agents' own, rendered mechanically; MEASURED and INFERRED are theirs.

## 0. Her settled answers (PLAN_MOTH §9, verbatim, 2026-11-01)

- **Q1 Models:** *"Opus for all sessions"*
- **Q2 History:** *"Show graph structure and metadata from Moth’s first recorded baseline onward, retain 90 days, and label periods with no recorded history; file bodies are not historical copies."*
- **Q3 NAS:** *"Refresh metadata when a project is opened on desktop and through a clear Refresh action; show when it was last checked. (Recommended)"*
- **Q4 Long notes:** *"Use the existing save-and-merge behaviour for long notes in this release, state that limit plainly, and add live co-typing after release."*
- **Q5 Plan/cap:** *"Keep the current plan with the spend cap enabled; record its name and remaining usage before live-board acceptance. - note: claude will know the type of plan"*
- **Q6 Performance projects:** *"use the sample project the debug mode has. also the local saved version of the wilson tool locally and use the saved test project. claude will know this project"*

Her brief's rulings (`briefs/MOTH_PLANNING_BRIEF.md` §8, §12 and §12a) bind every section below and are not restated.

## 1. Stable file bodies

**Judged** from two rival drafts (A: a new table `file_body_locations`; B: no table, a resolver over `files`). Winner: **hybrid**.

| Criterion | A | B | Note |
|---|---|---|---|
| security | 4 | 3 | A's INVOKER current-file resolution through files_select denies trashed bodies and is_financial receipts under ordinary keys; its orphaned body rows deny purged keys robustly; delete_own NOT claimed. B's DEFINER uuid resolver ignores trash and money and leaks the destination project; its purge deny rests on one exception-swallowing enqueue (MEASURED 0054); file_events details name the private source; is_financial and folder_id are not guarded. B is right that pre-existing prefix ≠ project rows must be refused (A treated them as already moved, which would turn crafted OSP-07 rows into live leaks). |
| completeness | 3 | 4 | A misses rowNamesTwin's project filter (MEASURED data-loss case after an interrupted re-file), the realtime old-topic removal, projectFilePatch.cjs, FileAuditDrawer labels and the refusal-code client change. B misses the existing authenticated UPDATE probes, and its row-only s3 reads break the connection-check HEAD (MEASURED connectionStatus l.191). |
| migration risk | 3 | 4 | A backfills a body row per file under a lock and refuses on thumbnail mismatches that S4c-09 half-refiles plausibly produce. B is one index plus the policy restatement, with fail-closed preflight counts. |
| operational safety | 3 | 4 | A: the twin bug, a GC trigger deleting body rows (thumbnail window), a pending-claim cron. B: twin fixed, teardown by file_events tie, but claims its partial index serves referencedPaths when it cannot without a provider predicate, and its meter subquery runs per orphan object across tenants. |
| fit with Sessions 2 to 4 | 4 | 3.5 | A gives the NAS locator and lifecycle a home and matches the plan's table. Both leave the fileUrl preview seam unchanged and put sticky fencing on sticky_locks in Session 4. B leaves the NAS columns to the NAS area. |
| simplicity | 2 | 4 | A: a table, ten functions, five triggers, a cron, a mirror sync and nested two-table RLS per object. B: one index, three helpers, one guard, one RPC; no new RLS surface. |

Grafted from the runner-up:
- From A: authority for a claimed key comes from an INVOKER function evaluated under the caller's files_select. A trashed file's body is unreadable, and so is an is_financial receipt under an ordinary key. This replaces B's DEFINER uuid resolver.
- From A: the claimed check is a yes-or-no answer limited to the caller's own workspace (no project id is handed out).
- From A: rabbit_files_delete_own refuses claimed objects; thumbnails keep delete_own for moveReceipt's remove fallback.
- From A: the guard also covers is_financial (money-ness only grows) and folder_id, and INSERT requires a same-project, non-locked folder.
- From A: clients may insert only supabase, s3 or local_server rows (no google_drive or network); the NAS area brings its own definer path.
- From A: rekey_file_body is one call that compare-and-sets path and thumbnail together, with p_financial, so moveReceipt stays one atomic write.
- From A: a move writes moved_out under the old project and moved_in under the new one, neither naming the other project (widening 0093's CHECK, the writer in the same migration).
- From A: the signer keeps the legacy project rule for unclaimed keys (saves the connection-check probe) and always refuses PUT and DEL on claimed keys.
- From A: LOCK TABLE files during the preflight, so nothing slips in between the checks and the guard.
- From A: the list of existing probes in 05/33/78/83/90/95 that must be re-pointed.
- From A: the personal-space routing prerequisite (personal uploads never land as local_server).
- From A: an origin-gone meter term limited to rare rows, here a partial index on moved rows.
- From A: Session 4 deferral of the publication generation; no broadcast of body state.
- Mine (beyond both drafts): a third claimed arm on file_events 'purged' certificates, so a purged moved body falls back to its origin only if both independent swallowing writers fail.
- Mine (beyond both drafts): a CI pgTAP allowlist probe of definers that write the locator columns, in place of B's GUC.

**Area as the designer framed it:** STABLE FILE BODIES: a file moves between projects, or into or out of a personal space, and its body stays where it is. Authority over the body follows the file's current project. MO1 design round, judged from rival designs A (new table) and B (no table).

### Summary

The winner is a hybrid on B's skeleton. There is no new table. files.storage_path stays the physical locator, minted once under the creating project's prefix. After insert, no client role can change it. A partial UNIQUE text_pattern_ops index on files(storage_path) gives every key at most one row, and it also serves storage-gc's LIKE twin lookups. Authority over an object comes from A's split rather than B's DEFINER uuid resolver. A DEFINER boolean, storage_object_claimed(bucket, name), is true when, inside the caller's own workspace, a files row in any trash state names the key, a storage_gc_queue row names it, or a file_events 'purged' certificate names it. Using two independent records means a single swallowed enqueue can no longer give a purged moved body back to its origin project. An INVOKER SQL function, storage_object_current_project, then returns the referencing row's project_id under the caller's own files_select, so the privacy, trash and money/Legal arms decide without being copied. A trashed file's body becomes unreadable, and so does an is_financial receipt filed under an ordinary key. Keys nobody has ever claimed (manifest, rates, bin posters, connection checks, uploads in flight) keep exactly today's prefix rule. The eight base storage policies (0042 and 0053) are restated with that CASE. rabbit_files_delete_own refuses claimed objects. The eight money policies stay unchanged, because a money or Legal row can never move and money-ness can only grow. fn_files_body_guard is an INVOKER trigger in 0093's current_user idiom. For client roles it refuses any change to project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial or folder_id. On INSERT it requires a key that is admissible (own project, the uploader's segment allowlist, unclaimed, never queued, object present for Petal), a derived thumbnail and a same-project folder. Only move_files (the shared-moves area) and rekey_file_body can then change those columns. rekey_file_body uses A's one-call path plus thumbnail compare-and-set with p_financial, and B's rule that moved rows are never re-keyed. s3 signing goes by row for claimed keys and by the legacy project rule for unclaimed ones; PUT and DEL on a claimed key are always refused. storage-read-urls drops its key-project equality. storage-gc's rowNamesTwin drops its project filter. Teardown and the Petal meter count a moved body after its origin project is purged, the meter through a tiny partial index on moved rows. A move writes moved_out under the old project and moved_in under the new one, and neither event names the other project. Sticky publication fencing waits for Session 4.

### Decisions

- **SB-1** No file_body_locations table. files.storage_path (with its derived thumbnail key storage_path||'.jpg') is the immutable physical locator, minted once under projects/{creating project}/. files.project_id is the current association, and authority follows it.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: every body consumer already resolves from the row (resolveFileProvider(row).getUrl(storage_path), fn_files_gc_enqueue OLD.storage_path, storage-gc's referencedPaths and drain, the teardown files scan, runMigration). A second table needs its own lifecycle (pending/live/orphaned), a mirror-sync trigger, a full backfill, a suite and RLS_TABLES line, and a nested two-table RLS evaluation on every storage object. What it uniquely gave can be had more cheaply: (1) denying purged keys, through the queue plus the purged certificate (SB-3); (2) tenancy after the origin project is purged, through files.workspace_id, now immutable and server-set (SB-12, SB-13); (3) s3 anti-adoption, judged a low residual (risks); (4) a NAS locator, which is the NAS area's own columns.
  - *Rejected:* A's file_body_locations: the most robust purge record, but about ten objects, 4–5 indexes, about 0.5 GB per million files, and a backfill that refuses on thumbnail mismatches which S4c-09 half-refiles plausibly create. Body-id keys (bodies/{id}): rewrite every seg3 money consumer. Relocating objects on move: against her ruling, and impossible for s3 (S4c-01).
- **SB-2** CREATE UNIQUE INDEX files_storage_path_key_uidx ON public.files (storage_path text_pattern_ops) WHERE storage_provider IN ('supabase','s3','local_server') (enum literals, not ::text, so the planner can prove the predicate). Every lookup that must use it carries a matching provider predicate. Also: storage_gc_queue_object_idx (bucket_id, object_path); file_events_purged_path_idx ON file_events (old_path) WHERE event = 'purged'; files_moved_body_ws_idx ON files (workspace_id) WHERE storage_provider = 'supabase' AND lower(split_part(storage_path,'/',2)) <> project_id::text.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: files.storage_path has no index today (the storage-gc comment 'storage_path has none'; grep finds only workspace, live-workspace and entity-link indexes). text_pattern_ops serves =, IN and LIKE 'prefix%', which covers the resolver, admissibility, rowNamesTwin without its project filter and readUrls' folder LIKE. B's claim that referencedPaths becomes 'index-served' is wrong unless the query carries the provider predicate, because a partial index serves only queries whose WHERE implies it. So that predicate is added (SB-13). The index is partial so that a later NAS area may catalogue one share file twice. Thumbnails are derived, so no thumbnail_url index is needed for authority.
  - *Rejected:* F7's two plain btrees (no LIKE support, one wasted). A global unique index across all providers (would refuse duplicate NAS or Drive locators). A non-partial plus a partial index (doubles the cost).
- **SB-3** public.storage_object_claimed(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS boolean: SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, plpgsql, search_path pinned. Scoped to current_workspace_id(). Sequential IFs: (1) a files row in any trash state matches by bucket (rabbit-files: provider supabase AND storage_path = name; rabbit-thumbnails: right(name,4)='.jpg' AND provider IN (supabase, local_server) AND storage_path = left(name,-4); byo-s3: provider s3 AND (storage_path = name OR (.jpg AND storage_path = left(name,-4)))); (2) a storage_gc_queue row (workspace, bucket_id, object_path = name); (3) a file_events row with event 'purged', the same workspace and old_path = name (or left(name,-4) for a .jpg), whose storage_provider fits the bucket. Boolean only.
  - *Why:* Graft from A: an answer of yes or no, limited to the caller's own workspace, so a key claimed in another tenant reads as unclaimed and the prefix fallback then denies it through projects_select. Arm (3) fixes B's admitted weakness. fn_files_gc_enqueue swallows every error (MEASURED, 0054 EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS → WARNING), and so does fn_file_events_capture (MEASURED, 0074). They are separate triggers, so a purged moved body falls back to its origin project only if both fail on the same purge.
  - *Rejected:* B's DEFINER resolver returning the current project uuid, masked by workspace and privacy. It returns a trashed row's project and ignores the money arm, and it is a destination oracle wider than a boolean. Queue-only (B, one swallowing writer). Making the enqueue fail loudly instead (one bad row would abort the nightly purge_soft_deleted statement for every row).
- **SB-4** public.storage_object_current_project(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS uuid: SECURITY INVOKER, LANGUAGE sql STABLE. SELECT f.project_id FROM public.files f WHERE <the bucket match of SB-3 arm 1> LIMIT 1, evaluated under the caller's RLS.
  - *Why:* Graft from A, the S33 rule: files_select decides as the caller. It covers live only (0014 deleted_at IS NULL, MEASURED through the refile comment at supabaseAdapter l.1459-1462), workspace, the projects hop with privacy (0072/0082) and the money/Legal arm (0092). It returns NULL for a trashed file, a file in a project the caller cannot see, and an is_financial row the caller lacks money access for, whatever its key's segment 3. That last case closes a pre-existing leak: today the base policy admits any project reader to an is_financial receipt filed under an ordinary key.
  - *Rejected:* Copying files_select's arms into a definer (S33 forbids it). Falling back to segment 2 for claimed keys (the old project keeps reading a moved body).
- **SB-5** Restate the 8 BASE storage policies (rabbit_files_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own from 0042; rabbit_thumbnails_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own from 0053) by DROP + CREATE, names unchanged. Every predicate is kept word for word. Each project argument becomes CASE WHEN public.storage_object_claimed(bucket_id, name) THEN public.storage_object_current_project(bucket_id, name) ELSE public.fn_try_uuid((storage.foldername(name))[2]) END, used both in EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = …) and in can_write_project(…). UPDATE keeps USING and WITH CHECK. rabbit_files_delete_own adds AND NOT public.storage_object_claimed(bucket_id, name). rabbit_thumbnails_delete_own does not.
  - *Why:* Authority follows the file. A claimed key that was purged has no current project, so nobody can read it. Graft from A: no client can delete a live body, even its own uploader within the hour. uploadFile's compensating deletes always target objects no row names yet (MEASURED, supabaseAdapter l.3648 and the thumbnail remove). Thumbnails keep delete_own without the claimed arm because runMigration.moveReceipt removes a thumbnail it could not move (MEASURED l.2598), and that picture is claimed by derivation. An INSERT at a claimed key whose object was purged is refused (no current project), so a key cannot be resurrected under the prefix.
  - *Rejected:* Adding narrow policies beside the old ones (permissive policies OR together, which changes nothing, the 0038 inversion). Switching SELECT only (former writers could still rename or overwrite the body).
- **SB-6** The 8 MONEY storage policies (0092 §4), petal_bin_posters_* (0091) and petal_storage_quota_insert (0078) are NOT touched. A post-condition pins their text.
  - *Why:* A money or Legal row can never change project (SB-8), and money-ness can only be added, through rekey_file_body (SB-9). So for a money key, segment 2 is the current project forever. Leaving them alone keeps 0092 post-condition 12, suite 95 probe 167, 0088 pc8 and 0092 pc5 undisturbed. Both drafts agree.
  - *Rejected:* Re-homing all 16 storage policies on the resolver (it touches the most sensitive pins for no change in behaviour).
- **SB-7** Guard: public.fn_files_body_guard(), plpgsql, NOT SECURITY DEFINER, attached as trg_files_body_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.files. It acts only when current_user IN ('authenticated','anon'). On UPDATE it raises 42501 if any of project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial or folder_id IS DISTINCT FROM OLD, with the message 'a file's project, folder and storage change only through Move to'. On INSERT it requires storage_provider::text IN ('supabase','s3','local_server'), so a client cannot insert google_drive or network rows, plus COALESCE(public.rabbit_file_key_admissible(…), false), plus thumbnail_url NULL or = storage_path||'.jpg'. Server roles pass. That covers the definers (owner postgres), service_role, migrations, and RI actions, which run as the table owner, so a folder delete's ON DELETE SET NULL still works.
  - *Why:* Precedent MEASURED: 0093 fn_gateways_client_guard with its not-prosecdef post-condition. Taken from A: is_financial is guarded, because otherwise someone with money access could flip a receipt to ordinary and then move it, and money-ness becomes monotone. folder_id is guarded too, closing digest fact 12's cross-project and locked-folder route (MEASURED: nothing patches folder_id today, FileEditor l.109-197). The guard fires before trg_files_populate_workspace (alphabetical BEFORE order), so it never reads NEW.workspace_id; it uses current_workspace_id().
  - *Rejected:* B's transaction-local GUC 'wilson.file_body_write' keyed to row id, on top of current_user. It would force every postgres-role pgTAP seed and future backfill to set the GUC, against no client threat (PostgREST cannot set it and cannot be postgres). Future definers are caught instead by a CI pgTAP allowlist probe (tests). A CHECK tying the key to projects/<project_id>/, OSP-07's recorded fix, which every moved row would violate.
- **SB-8** public.rabbit_file_key_admissible(p_provider text, p_path text, p_project uuid, p_file uuid, p_folder uuid, p_financial boolean) RETURNS boolean, SECURITY DEFINER. It first returns false unless current_workspace_id() owns p_project, has_active_membership, passes_project_privacy and can_write_project(p_project) all hold (COALESCEd), so it is no oracle for outsiders. Then all of: char_length ≤ 1024; seg1 'projects'; lower(seg2) = p_project::text; at least 5 segments, each matching '^[A-Za-z0-9._-]+$' and not '.' or '..'; lower(seg3) IN ('scenes','shots','levels','experiences','assets','tasks','phases','project','files','invoices','legal'); no OTHER files row (id <> p_file, any trash state) names p_path; no storage_gc_queue row and no 'purged' file_events row names it; for supabase, a storage.objects row exists in rabbit-files at p_path; folder NULL, or a folder of p_project whose top path segment is not money-locked unless file_row_is_money(p_financial, p_path).
  - *Why:* This closes OSP-07. A row can vouch only for an object that has never been claimed and sits under the row's own creation project, which that project's readers could already read through the prefix rule. Excluding the same id keeps runMigration's insertOrSkip reruns working, because the BEFORE trigger fires before ON CONFLICT arbitration. The segment allowlist (B) fails closed and excludes bin_files, connection-check, FINANCE and PROJECT.json. It is MEASURED against uploadContainerFor (l.920-931), runMigration cloudObjectPathFor ('files') and the INVOICES/LEGAL builders, and pinned by a lockstep vitest. The folder rule is from A.
  - *Rejected:* A's owner_id = auth.uid() check. It breaks runMigration.objectAlreadyThere resumes signed in as another account (MEASURED l.2411-2418, 2522), and runMigration is not admin-only (MigrationPanel). A's s3 pending claims minted by storage-presign (a cron, a lifecycle and a presign write path for a residual judged low). A's segment-3 denylist (opens on any new row-less shape).
- **SB-9** public.rekey_file_body(p_file uuid, p_from_path text, p_to_path text, p_from_thumb text, p_to_thumb text, p_financial boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS boolean, SECURITY DEFINER: A's signature with B's moved-row refusal. SELECT … FOR UPDATE. Every authority failure gives the same refusal (42501, the same words): workspace, membership, privacy, can_write_project, and the 0092 files_update money/Legal arm COALESCEd. Trashed rows are refused. Rows whose lower(split_part(storage_path,'/',2)) ≠ project_id::text (moved rows) are refused. Compare-and-set: it returns false when storage_path IS DISTINCT FROM p_from_path or thumbnail_url IS DISTINCT FROM p_from_thumb. A path change must keep the same seg1/seg2, be admissible by SB-8 for that project (excluding p_file), not be s3 (S4c-01), keep its Legal status, and require a supabase object at p_to_path. Money-ness may change only false→true, with p_financial AND lower(seg3)='invoices' AND can_access_project_money. A thumbnail change must be to NULL or to the new path||'.jpg'. One UPDATE.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the only client writers of storage_path, thumbnail_url and is_financial after insert are S4c rewriteFileRow (two phases: body then picture, l.1616-1641) and runMigration.moveReceipt (one UPDATE of all three, l.2618). A's one-call compare-and-set keeps moveReceipt atomic, as its comment requires ('0088's key check and the money flag agree'), and lets refile call it twice. Refusing moved rows keeps every moved key equal to its origin forever, which the meter, teardown and twin rules rely on. refile's filesUnderPrefix (.eq project_id, l.1468) and moveReceipt (.eq project_id) never meet moved rows anyway.
  - *Rejected:* B's per-column signature (moveReceipt would need two calls with an inconsistent row between them). Leaving storage_path client-writable within the same prefix (OSP-07 returns as 'rename my row onto another orphan').
- **SB-10** Money, Legal and body homes under moves. These are contract rules for move_files (shared-moves area), each enforced twice: move_files refuses the source, and the guard refuses any project_id change by a client. Refused sources: file_row_is_money OR file_row_is_legal OR 'legal' = ANY(tags) OR EXISTS (expenses e WHERE f.id = ANY(e.file_ids)) (the receipt residuals); a destination folder that is money-locked or under one; storage_provider local_server or google_drive changing project. Required: the same workspace; a live, visible, writable source and destination. move_files writes only project_id, folder_id and the entity links it clears, never storage_path, storage_provider or is_financial. Where a legacy thumbnail_url is non-NULL and ≠ storage_path||'.jpg', it sets thumbnail_url NULL and inserts a storage_gc_queue row (kind 'thumbnail', bucket as fn_files_gc_enqueue maps it). That makes the stale picture claimed, so nobody can read it.
  - *Why:* Refusal needs no change to any classifier (file_row_is_money, file_row_is_legal, files_money_key_chk, files_money_provider_chk, files_legal_folder_chk, trg_files_legal_fixed), all of which read split_part(storage_path,'/',3). A local_server body exists on one desktop (MEASURED uploadFile l.3481, routed to the uploading desktop), so moving it would leave everyone else with NOT_HERE. The legacy-thumbnail detach (B) replaces A's fail-closed backfill refusal, which would have stopped the migration on S4c-09 half-refiled rows.
  - *Rejected:* Allowing money moves and re-gating the money policies on the resolver (re-opens the S4b-10 class; receipt residuals cannot be classified).
- **SB-11** s3 signing by row for claimed keys. A new INVOKER public.can_presign_object(p_path text, p_op text). If storage_object_claimed('byo-s3', p_path): get or head is EXISTS a files row the caller can SELECT with provider s3 and (storage_path = p_path OR (storage_path = left(p_path,-4) AND thumbnail_url = p_path)), AND has_active_membership; put and del are false. Otherwise the legacy can_presign_project_read/_write(fn_try_uuid(seg2)) from 0051, unchanged. storage-presign calls it as the caller in place of choosing between the two 0051 predicates. checkRowShapedPath stays first (shape and money).
  - *Why:* This closes OSP-01 for every key that was ever claimed. Trashed and purged keys are no longer signed for GET, and nobody can PUT over or DEL a referenced body; today any writer of the prefix can, including over a moved body. Graft from A: unclaimed keys keep the legacy rule. MEASURED: connectionStatus.bucketProbeKey HEADs a row-less projects/<pid>/connection-check/wilson-connection-check key (l.191), which B's row-only reads would have refused, breaking the bucket probe. s3 thumbnails are put before the row insert (uploadFile l.3606 inserts thumbnail_url), so a claimed put is never needed.
  - *Rejected:* B's row-only reads for every key (breaks the connection check). A's thumbnail put for pending claimers (no pending claims here; no regeneration path exists).
- **SB-12** Petal meter: restate workspace_petal_committed_bytes (0073 body whole) as its existing arm UNION ALL a moved-bodies arm. The new arm reads public.files f JOIN storage.objects o ON ((o.bucket_id='rabbit-files' AND o.name = f.storage_path) OR (o.bucket_id='rabbit-thumbnails' AND o.name = f.thumbnail_url)), WHERE f.workspace_id = ws AND f.storage_provider = 'supabase' AND lower(split_part(f.storage_path,'/',2)) <> f.project_id::text AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = fn_try_uuid(split_part(f.storage_path,'/',2))). It stays service_role only.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the meter joins projects on key segment 2, so moving files out and then hard-deleting the origin would free quota. Taken from A: a term over only the rare moved rows, through files_moved_body_ws_idx. No double count: the new arm requires the origin project row to be gone, and arm 1 requires it present. INSERT-side gates (rabbit_petal_storage_ok, petal_storage_quota_insert, reserve_upload_bytes, rabbit_quota_exempt_*) stay right, because a new object's key is always under the uploading project.
  - *Rejected:* B's LEFT JOIN with a per-object COALESCE subquery (a probe for every object of every purged project across tenants, on every upload). A's origin_gone flag (needs the table).
- **SB-13** storage-gc: rowNamesTwin drops .eq('project_id', projectId) and adds .in('storage_provider', ['supabase','s3','local_server']), so the shot twin pattern (which carries its own projects/<pid>/ prefix) is served by the unique index. referencedPaths gains the same provider filter. The drain is unchanged.
  - *Why:* MEASURED (storage-gc l.197-201, l.531-541): an interrupted S4c re-file (object at the nested key, row still on the old key, l.1555-1569 'run it again') whose row is then moved to another project is no longer found by rowNamesTwin within the prefix project. The scan would delete the only copy of the body after 24h. A missed this. The drain's thumbnail check (eq thumbnail_url) is already correct, because a moved row keeps its thumbnail_url and a detached legacy picture is meant to go.
  - *Rejected:* A's 'storage-gc unchanged' (the data-loss case above). Changing the drain to the derived key (B; unnecessary churn under F1-01's deno KNOWN baseline).
- **SB-14** Teardown (_shared/teardownCollect.ts collectBlobPaths). For paths taken from this workspace's files rows and storage_gc_queue rows, owned(p) = projectIds.has(seg1) OR (no projects row with id seg1 exists in ANY workspace AND a file_events row ties project_id seg1 to this workspace). A path whose segment 1 is another workspace's project is still rejected. reservedProjectObjectPaths and the bin-poster walk stay as they are.
  - *Why:* MEASURED (l.188-196): owned() keys on the workspace's current projects, so a moved body from a purged origin would be rejected and stranded after a certified teardown. files.workspace_id is server-set and now immutable, and insert admissibility ties every new key to a project of that workspace. The file_events tie is the orphan scan's own rule 4 (storage-gc l.485-494), kept as a second proof (B).
  - *Rejected:* Trusting the row alone (fine after this migration, but legacy rows rest on a one-time pre-check; the tie costs one indexed read per gone project). A's body-row tenancy (needs the table).
- **SB-15** file_events: widen file_events_event_check (loop-drop, then ADD restating 0093's full list plus 'moved_out' and 'moved_in'). Restate fn_file_events_capture from 0074 word for word, plus an UPDATE arm: IF OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id THEN insert 'moved_out' (project_id = OLD.project_id) and 'moved_in' (project_id = NEW.project_id), each with old_path = new_path = NEW.storage_path and is_financial from file_event_is_financial; details never names the other project. The vocabulary and the writer ship in ONE migration.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: 0074 emits 'moved' only on a storage_path change, so a project move emits nothing today. 0093 holds the latest CHECK (viewed_remote, MEASURED l.1110-1115). Graft from A: file_events_select reads through can_read_project_topic(project_id), so each audience sees only its own side. B's single 'moved' with details {from_project_id, to_project_id} would show a private or personal source's id to the destination, and an A→B→C chain would show B's id to C. FileAuditDrawer renders unknown terms raw (MEASURED l.200), so FILE_EVENT_META gains labels.
  - *Rejected:* B's reuse of 'moved' with details (leaks ids; FileAuditDrawer reads 'moved' as a path change).
- **SB-16** Trashed bodies are unreadable through the storage API and the signer until restored. Recorded as a behaviour change.
  - *Why:* This follows from SB-4 (files_select hides trashed rows) and closes OSP-01's 'trashed keys still signed' for Petal as well as s3. No client reads a trashed file's body today (MEASURED: restoreFile has no UI caller; FileManager deletes, no trash view). Restore only flips deleted_at, and the body is never touched. Session 1's file trash view therefore shows names and icons, not previews.
  - *Rejected:* B's 'a trashed body stays its project's' (keeps OSP-01 open for Petal for no current reader).
- **SB-17** Sticky-note publication fencing belongs to Session 4. No generation column now. Session 4 puts the lease and generation on sticky_locks and adds one lease-checked arm to rekey_file_body (or a sibling definer), which by then is the only post-insert writer of a body locator.
  - *Why:* Both drafts agree. A column with no writer cannot be pinned by a test that bites (thumbnail_url was dead from 0000 to S39, MEASURED by the uploadFile comment). The seam exists from Session 1.
  - *Rejected:* The plan's 'publication generation' column now.

### Schema changes

- **public.files — UNIQUE INDEX files_storage_path_key_uidx** (restates from: new): Preflight count of duplicate storage_path among supabase/s3/local_server rows, RAISE with the count when > 0. Then CREATE UNIQUE INDEX files_storage_path_key_uidx ON public.files (storage_path text_pattern_ops) WHERE storage_provider IN ('supabase','s3','local_server'). Plain CREATE inside the migration transaction (not CONCURRENTLY). The rehearsal EXPLAINs each lookup listed in SB-2 to prove the index is used.
- **public.files — INDEX files_moved_body_ws_idx** (restates from: new): CREATE INDEX files_moved_body_ws_idx ON public.files (workspace_id) WHERE storage_provider = 'supabase' AND lower(split_part(storage_path,'/',2)) <> project_id::text; (all functions IMMUTABLE; near empty until moves ship).
- **public.storage_gc_queue — INDEX storage_gc_queue_object_idx; public.file_events — INDEX file_events_purged_path_idx** (restates from: new): CREATE INDEX storage_gc_queue_object_idx ON public.storage_gc_queue (bucket_id, object_path); CREATE INDEX file_events_purged_path_idx ON public.file_events (old_path) WHERE event = 'purged'.
- **public.rabbit_file_key_admissible(p_provider text, p_path text, p_project uuid, p_file uuid, p_folder uuid, p_financial boolean) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, plpgsql, SET search_path = public. The rules of SB-8, in order: caller rights first (workspace = current_workspace_id() via projects.workspace_id, has_active_membership, COALESCE(passes_project_privacy), COALESCE(can_write_project)) else false; then shape, prefix, the segment allowlist, uniqueness excluding p_file, the queue and purged-certificate absence, supabase object existence (storage.objects bucket_id='rabbit-files' AND name = p_path), and the folder rule (folders.project_id = p_project AND (NOT rabbit_money_segment(split_part(folders.path,'/',1)) OR file_row_is_money(p_financial, p_path))). Never NULL (COALESCE false). REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role. COMMENT names its callers (fn_files_body_guard, rekey_file_body).
- **public.storage_object_claimed(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, STABLE, plpgsql, SET search_path = public. v_ws := current_workspace_id(); IF v_ws IS NULL OR p_name IS NULL THEN RETURN false. IF EXISTS (files arm, any trash state, f.workspace_id = v_ws, the bucket match of SB-3 with provider equality so the partial unique index serves it) THEN RETURN true. IF EXISTS (storage_gc_queue q WHERE q.workspace_id = v_ws AND q.bucket_id = p_bucket AND q.object_path = p_name) THEN RETURN true. IF EXISTS (file_events e WHERE e.event = 'purged' AND e.workspace_id = v_ws AND e.old_path = CASE WHEN right(p_name,4)='.jpg' AND p_bucket <> 'rabbit-files' THEN left(p_name, length(p_name)-4) ELSE p_name END AND e.storage_provider = CASE p_bucket WHEN 'byo-s3' THEN 's3' WHEN 'rabbit-files' THEN 'supabase' ELSE e.storage_provider END) THEN RETURN true. RETURN false. (For byo-s3, check both the name and its .jpg-stripped form in the purged arm.) REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role.
- **public.storage_object_current_project(p_bucket text, p_name text) RETURNS uuid** (restates from: new): SECURITY INVOKER, LANGUAGE sql STABLE, SET search_path = public. SELECT f.project_id FROM public.files f WHERE <the SB-3 files-arm bucket match> LIMIT 1. Runs under the caller's files_select (live, workspace, projects hop with privacy, money/Legal arm). A post-condition asserts NOT prosecdef. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role.
- **public.fn_files_body_guard() + TRIGGER trg_files_body_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.files** (restates from: new (idiom of 0093 fn_gateways_client_guard)): plpgsql, NOT SECURITY DEFINER. IF current_user IN ('authenticated','anon') THEN: on UPDATE, RAISE 42501 if (NEW.project_id, NEW.workspace_id, NEW.storage_provider, NEW.storage_path, NEW.thumbnail_url, NEW.is_financial, NEW.folder_id) IS DISTINCT FROM (OLD.…); on INSERT, RAISE 42501 'this file''s storage place was not made for this project' unless NEW.storage_provider::text IN ('supabase','s3','local_server') AND COALESCE(rabbit_file_key_admissible(NEW.storage_provider::text, NEW.storage_path, NEW.project_id, NEW.id, NEW.folder_id, NEW.is_financial), false) AND (NEW.thumbnail_url IS NULL OR NEW.thumbnail_url = NEW.storage_path || '.jpg'). RETURN NEW. A COMMENT states that it fires before trg_files_legal_fixed and trg_files_populate_workspace and depends on neither. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Post-conditions: the trigger is attached; the function is not prosecdef; its comment-stripped body names all seven columns.
- **public.rekey_file_body(p_file uuid, p_from_path text, p_to_path text, p_from_thumb text, p_to_thumb text, p_financial boolean DEFAULT false) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, plpgsql, SET search_path = public; the SB-9 rules. Sketch: SELECT * INTO v FROM files WHERE id = p_file FOR UPDATE. A single refusal (42501 'file not found or not writable') for not found, workspace ≠ current_workspace_id(), NOT has_active_membership, NOT COALESCE(passes_project_privacy(v.project_id)), NOT COALESCE(can_write_project(v.project_id)), v.deleted_at IS NOT NULL, (file_row_is_money(v.is_financial, v.storage_path) AND NOT COALESCE(can_access_project_money(v.project_id)) AND NOT (file_row_is_legal(…) AND COALESCE(can_access_project_legal(v.project_id)))). Refuse a moved row: lower(split_part(v.storage_path,'/',2)) <> v.project_id::text. IF v.storage_path IS DISTINCT FROM p_from_path OR v.thumbnail_url IS DISTINCT FROM p_from_thumb THEN RETURN false. A path change requires v.storage_provider::text <> 's3', the same seg1/seg2, rabbit_file_key_admissible(v.storage_provider::text, p_to_path, v.project_id, p_file, v.folder_id, v.is_financial OR p_financial), (v.storage_provider::text <> 'supabase' OR an object at p_to_path), and Legal status unchanged. Money-ness may change only false→true with p_financial AND lower(split_part(p_to_path,'/',3)) = 'invoices' AND COALESCE(can_access_project_money(v.project_id)). Thumbnail: p_to_thumb IS NULL OR p_to_thumb = p_to_path || '.jpg'. UPDATE files SET storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial = (v.is_financial OR p_financial) WHERE id = p_file; RETURN true. trg_files_legal_fixed and files_money_key_chk stay as backstops. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated.
- **public.can_presign_object(p_path text, p_op text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new (calls 0051's can_presign_project_read/_write unchanged)): SECURITY INVOKER, LANGUAGE sql STABLE, SET search_path = public, COALESCE(…, false). CASE WHEN p_op NOT IN ('get','head','put','del') THEN false WHEN public.storage_object_claimed('byo-s3', p_path) THEN (p_op IN ('get','head') AND public.has_active_membership(public.current_workspace_id()) AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.files f WHERE f.storage_provider = 's3' AND (f.storage_path = p_path OR (right(p_path,4)='.jpg' AND f.storage_path = left(p_path, length(p_path)-4) AND f.thumbnail_url = p_path)))) WHEN p_op IN ('get','head') THEN public.can_presign_project_read(public.fn_try_uuid(split_part(p_path,'/',2))) ELSE public.can_presign_project_write(public.fn_try_uuid(split_part(p_path,'/',2))) END. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role. can_presign_project_read/_write stay as in 0051.
- **storage.objects rabbit_files_select / rabbit_files_insert / rabbit_files_update / rabbit_files_delete_own** (restates from: 0042_money_segments_and_manifest_rewrite.sql): DROP + CREATE each WHOLE from 0042's body (l.121-200), with the CASE project of SB-5 replacing fn_try_uuid((storage.foldername(name))[2]) in the EXISTS-projects hop and in can_write_project. delete_own adds AND NOT public.storage_object_claimed(bucket_id, name). Names unchanged. Post-conditions (comment-stripped pg_policies text): exactly 8 policies LIKE 'rabbit_files%' (0053 pc7, suite 63 count); all 16 rabbit policies name rabbit_money_segment; the 4 base rabbit_files ones name storage_object_claimed and storage_object_current_project; both UPDATE arms carry WITH CHECK; delete_own names 'NOT public.storage_object_claimed'.
- **storage.objects rabbit_thumbnails_select / _insert / _update / _delete_own** (restates from: 0053_thumbnails_bucket.sql): DROP + CREATE each WHOLE from 0053's body with the same CASE (bucket 'rabbit-thumbnails'). delete_own keeps the own-object, one-hour window and has no NOT-claimed arm (runMigration.moveReceipt's thumbs.remove fallback). Bin posters resolve by the prefix fallback, because no files row can own a bin_files key (SB-8).
- **rabbit_files_money_* / rabbit_thumbnails_money_* (8), petal_bin_posters_* and the remote_viewing RESTRICTIVE pair, petal_storage_quota_insert** (restates from: 0092_legal_gate_managers.sql / 0091_bins_on_the_cloud.sql / 0078_quota_exemption_bounded_by_size.sql (unchanged)): NO CHANGE. Post-condition: qual/with_check text equals the 0092 (money), 0091 (posters) and 0078 (quota) definitions, compared to an md5 stored in the migration.
- **public.workspace_petal_committed_bytes(ws uuid)** (restates from: 0073_upload_reservations.sql): CREATE OR REPLACE: 0073's SELECT whole (JOIN projects on segment 2, p.workspace_id = ws) UNION ALL the moved-bodies arm of SB-12, summed with COALESCE(rabbit_object_incoming_bytes(o.metadata),0). LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER search_path public. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT EXECUTE TO service_role (0056 restated). workspace_petal_bytes, rabbit_petal_storage_ok, reserve_upload_bytes and rabbit_quota_exempt_* unchanged.
- **file_events_event_check (CHECK on public.file_events)** (restates from: 0093_file_gateway.sql): Loop-drop as 0093 does, then ADD CONSTRAINT file_events_event_check CHECK (event IN ('uploaded','downloaded','moved','relinked','trashed','restored','purged','upload_abandoned','viewed_remote','moved_out','moved_in')). Post-condition: every term appears exactly once (0093's counting idiom).
- **public.fn_file_events_capture()** (restates from: 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql): CREATE OR REPLACE with 0074's whole body (l.163-254), including the EXCEPTION handler. Inside the UPDATE branch, after the storage_path arm: IF OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id THEN INSERT 'moved_out' (workspace NEW.workspace_id, project_id OLD.project_id, file_id, file_name, storage_provider, old_path = new_path = NEW.storage_path, size, actor, label, is_financial via file_event_is_financial(NEW.is_financial, NEW.storage_path, NULL)) and 'moved_in' (project_id NEW.project_id, otherwise identical); details NULL. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Post-condition: the comment-stripped body contains the five 0074 literals plus 'moved_out' and 'moved_in', file_event_is_financial at least 7 times, and no 'from_project' or 'to_project'.
- **Preflight (top of the first migration)** (restates from: new): Refuse when row security applies to current_user (0088/0092 preflight). LOCK TABLE public.files IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE. Fail-closed counts, RAISE with numbers: (1) duplicate storage_path among key providers; (2) key-provider rows whose lower(seg2) ≠ project_id::text (a pre-existing crafted OSP-07 or latent cross-project row; under the new rules it would hand the prefix project's body to the row's project); (3) key-provider rows whose seg2 project is in another workspace; (4) non-key-provider rows whose storage_path starts with 'projects/'. Reported, not refused (RAISE NOTICE): thumbnail_url non-NULL and ≠ storage_path||'.jpg'; key-shape outliers (the guard is INSERT-only); non-money rows sitting in money-locked folders (INFERRED zero).

### Consumers to change

- **storage.objects rabbit_files_* (0042) and rabbit_thumbnails_* (0053), 8 base policies**: Restated on the claimed/current-project CASE; rabbit_files_delete_own refuses claimed objects (schema_changes).
- **storage.objects money (0092 §4), bin posters (0091), petal_storage_quota_insert (0078); rabbit_petal_storage_ok, reserve_upload_bytes, rabbit_quota_exempt_***: None, pinned by post-condition. Money rows never move; new keys are always under the uploading project.
- **public.workspace_petal_committed_bytes (0073)**: Restated with the moved-bodies arm (SB-12).
- **file_row_is_money / rabbit_money_segment (0088), file_row_is_legal / rabbit_legal_key / file_event_is_legal (0092), rabbit_money_key / file_event_is_financial (0074), files_money_key_chk, files_legal_folder_chk, files_legal_not_core_chk (0088), files_money_provider_chk (0050), files_legal_not_financial_chk (0092), trg_files_legal_fixed (0088), fn_files_gc_enqueue (0054), fn_trash_authz / log_file_downloaded / files_* policies (0092)**: None. They read a path no client can change, and money and Legal rows cannot move. fn_files_gc_enqueue is AFTER DELETE only (MEASURED), so a move never enqueues. Row-based policies follow project_id on their own.
- **public.fn_file_events_capture (0074), file_events_event_check (0093)**: Restated with moved_out/moved_in (SB-15).
- **supabase/functions/storage-presign/index.ts (predicate choice, l.136-138)**: Call rpc('can_presign_object', { p_path, p_op }) as the caller in place of choosing can_presign_project_read/_write(shape.projectId). checkRowShapedPath stays first. Header refusal item 5 rewritten.
- **supabase/functions/_shared/readUrls.ts classifyOwned (l.194-200), handleReadUrls (l.212-284); storage-read-urls/index.ts deps canReadProject and rowPage**: classifyOwned drops the keyProject === row.project_id equality: the guard and admissibility now guarantee that a row vouches only for a key minted under its creation project, and that survives moves. The canReadProject pre-check is removed. rowPage drops .eq('project_id', projectId) and keeps the live s3 filter (folder LIKE, read AS THE CALLER), served by the unique index. 'project' is no longer emitted; a key with no visible row is 'row'. READ_URLS_MAX_PROJECTS becomes a cost bound on distinct key prefixes. Deploy only after the migration is on that environment.
- **supabase/functions/storage-gc/index.ts rowNamesTwin (l.197-201), referencedPaths (l.182-190)**: rowNamesTwin: drop .eq('project_id', projectId), add .in('storage_provider', ['supabase','s3','local_server']). referencedPaths: add the same provider filter. The drain and orphan-scan tenancy are unchanged. Re-measure the deno KNOWN baseline in rls.yml in the same branch (F1-01).
- **supabase/functions/_shared/teardownCollect.ts collectBlobPaths owned()/take/takeThumb (l.188-283)**: For row-derived and queue paths, accept a prefix not in projectIds when no projects row with that id exists anywhere AND file_events ties it to this workspace (SB-14). Unchanged otherwise. Deploy operator-workspaces.
- **electron/projectFilePatch.cjs (Local Server PATCH strip list)**: Also strip project_id, workspace_id, thumbnail_url, is_financial and folder_id (it already strips storage_path, storage_provider and id), so a local PATCH cannot re-home a row inside another project's bundle.
- **electron/localMedia.cjs checkMediaKey; storage/localServerProvider.js; _shared/presignPath.ts, moneySegments.ts, shotKeys.ts, reservedObjects.ts**: None. The key shape is unchanged, and local_server rows never change project.
- **fn_realtime_broadcast files arm (latest 0098) — owned by the realtime area**: Cross-area requirement, not built here: when OLD.project_id ≠ NEW.project_id, emit a removal (id only) on rabbit:project:{OLD.project_id} as well as the UPDATE on the new topic. The money early-return stays first. Without it, the old project's open clients keep a stale row whose body they can no longer read.
- **edit_history_select (0092) — owned by the history/privacy area (S4d-02)**: Cross-area prerequisite: edit_history captures files diffs, so a manager can read a project_id change into a private project or personal space. Add the privacy arm before Move to can target either.
- **.github/workflows/rls.yml (replay list, deno KNOWN) and src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js (l.75 last-five, l.111-115 last-six)**: The new cross-table suite covers no table, so it is added BY HAND to the failure-replay 'for f in' list. Move both tail pins. No RLS_TABLES line (no new table).
- **supabase/tests/rls 05_files (l.126), 33_file_lifecycle (l.88), 78_file_events_money (l.731), 83_private_project_file_gates (l.289), 90_legal_files (l.419, 659, 814-879), 95_legal_gate_managers (l.636, 670, 1226)**: MEASURED by grep: these UPDATE storage_path, project_id or is_financial. Every such probe run as authenticated now meets the guard's 42501 before the CHECK or trigger it was written for. Re-point each: the client probe expects the guard, and a twin through rekey_file_body (or as postgres) keeps the CHECK or trigger pinned. Which probes run as authenticated is INFERRED; settle by reading each probe's role setup. Probes INSERTing files as authenticated with fake paths are re-keyed to admissible keys plus a storage.objects seed. Plans recounted.
- **docs/SYSTEMS_HANDBOOK.md §4.4, §4.7 (a key's prefix is where a file was CREATED, never where it is), §4.9 ordering rows (0042/0053/0073/0074/0093 chains), §5.4, §12.3, §12.4, §12.7a/b, §17; docs/OUTSTANDING.md OSP-01, OSP-07, S4c-12; docs/sessions/PLAN_WEEK_2026-11-04.md F7; docs/PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK.md pre-deploy checks**: Same commit as the behaviour. OSP-01 and OSP-07 closed for claimed keys, residuals stated. F7 drops its planned storage_path index (this one serves) and re-decides thumbnail_url (the drain only). The preflight queries are added to the production runbook (production is at 0063).

### Client changes

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js updateFile (l.3809)**: sanitize(patch, …) also strips project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial and folder_id before .update(). The 'left' tags computation no longer needs its project_id exception. localServerAdapter keeps project_id for its URL. columnAllowlist test updated. No new export. *(production caller: RabbitProvider.patchFile / setFileCore (l.5383-5403, always send project_id); ProjectsPage.jsx l.357; FileEditor through patchFile)*
- **supabaseAdapter.js rewriteFileRow (l.1550)**: client.from('files').update({[column]: to}) becomes client.rpc('rekey_file_body', { p_file, p_from_path, p_to_path, p_from_thumb, p_to_thumb }). The body phase passes the thumbnail unchanged; the picture phase passes the path unchanged; 'a picture at neither key' passes p_to_thumb null. A false result goes down the existing no-row branch (re-read, put back). *(production caller: refileOneObject ← refileOneShot ← adapter.refileShotFolders (S4c re-filing))*
- **src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js moveReceipt (l.2618)**: The direct UPDATE {is_financial, storage_path, thumbnail_url} becomes one rpc('rekey_file_body', { p_file: f.id, p_from_path: already.storage_path, p_to_path: to, p_from_thumb: already.thumbnail_url, p_to_thumb: thumbTo, p_financial: true }). It keeps 'changed by someone else meanwhile' on false. *(production caller: runMigration's receipt step (MigrationPanel run))*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/FileAuditDrawer.jsx FILE_EVENT_META**: Add moved_out ('Moved to another project') and moved_in ('Moved in from another project') entries, tone 'change'. Neither names a project. Check rabbitFilesCss.test.js and rabbitOverlaysRender.test.jsx pins. *(production caller: ProjectFilesExplorer opens FileAuditDrawer)*
- **storage/thumbnails.js thumbnailUrlsAt / FileManager s3 thumbnail refusal handling**: Treat refusal code 'row' exactly as 'project' was treated (icon fallback). No new export. *(production caller: FileManager thumbUrls effect → adapter.thumbnailUrls → thumbnailUrlsAt)*
- **no new client export in this area**: uploadFile's key builder, thumbnailKeyFor, shotObjectPrefix/nestedShotKey, the manifest, rates, bin-poster and connection-check keys, cloudObjectPathFor, isMoneyKey and fileUrl/resolveFileProvider are unchanged (MEASURED: they build or read the locator, which stays valid). The move adapter method (rpc move_files) belongs to the shared-moves area, with Moth's Move to and the Files page as its callers. *(production caller: n/a)*

### Migration order

- **0 — measurement (read-only, before writing SQL)**: On wilson-dev and wilson-staging, run the preflight counts of schema_changes by query, each naming its database (inet_server_addr plus a workspace count). Also measure: the segment-3 values in use among key-provider rows (against the SB-8 allowlist); rows in money-locked folders that are not money; legacy non-derived thumbnails; server_version; postgres rolbypassrls.
  - *Constraints:* Any non-zero refusing count is STOP and report. Those rows need a per-row decision by the controller (re-key, detach or purge), never an automatic repair. Record the numbers in the hand-off.
- **1 — stable file bodies: indexes, helpers, guard, rekey (next free number in Session 1's sequence; after any NAS ALTER TYPE migration; before the moves migration)**: Preflight (row-security check, LOCK TABLE files IN SHARE ROW EXCLUSIVE MODE, fail-closed counts); the four indexes; rabbit_file_key_admissible, storage_object_claimed, storage_object_current_project; fn_files_body_guard and its trigger; rekey_file_body; explicit REVOKE/GRANT. Post-conditions on comment-stripped text: unique and partial index; trigger attached; guard and current_project not prosecdef; every new DEFINER has search_path set and no PUBLIC or anon EXECUTE (suite 35).
  - *Constraints:* One transaction (no enum ADD VALUE here; provider comparisons are ::text where they are CHECK-like). Rolled-back rehearsal (tap-hosted) of the migration plus the new suite and the re-pointed 05/33/78/83/90/95 probes first; collected = planned. Ship the three adapter changes in the same release. Old builds fail loudly: refile puts the object back, moveReceipt says its sentence (the S4c-14 class).
- **2 — storage policies, signer predicate, meter**: DROP + CREATE the 8 base policies from 0042 and 0053; can_presign_object; workspace_petal_committed_bytes restated from 0073. Post-conditions: 16 rabbit policies, 8 rabbit_files%, the money/poster/quota policy text unchanged (md5), the base 8 on the CASE, delete_own NOT-claimed, the meter service_role only.
  - *Constraints:* After step 1 (needs the helpers and the index). EXPLAIN ANALYZE of a 100-object bucket.list and a createSignedUrl on dev, before and after, recorded in the hand-off (the policy is evaluated per object).
- **3 — file_events vocabulary and writer**: file_events_event_check restated from 0093 plus moved_out and moved_in, and fn_file_events_capture restated from 0074 with the project arm, in ONE migration.
  - *Constraints:* A CHECK, not an enum, so no ALTER TYPE separation applies. Must commit before move_files is callable. Suites 33 and 78 extended; suite 66 probe 27 (a term and its writer together) still holds.
- **4 — Edge Functions**: storage-presign (can_presign_object), _shared/readUrls.ts + storage-read-urls (equality and pre-check removed), storage-gc (rowNamesTwin and referencedPaths provider filter), _shared/teardownCollect.ts (owned rule) via operator-workspaces; deno KNOWN re-measured in rls.yml in the same branch.
  - *Constraints:* Deployed by Audrey or the controller on each environment AFTER steps 1–3 are applied there (readUrls without the equality is safe only with the guard in place). Live state is read from `supabase functions list` plus a source comparison, not from CLI success. Rollback in reverse order.
- **5 — moves (shared-moves area) and the Move to UI**: move_files honouring SB-10 (refusals, legacy-thumbnail detach and queue, writes limited to project_id/folder_id/links, FOR UPDATE row locks). Post-condition: the pgTAP allowlist probe now expects exactly {move_files} as a project_id writer and {rekey_file_body, move_files (thumbnail_url only)} as locator writers. Then Moth's and the Files page's Move to.
  - *Constraints:* The Move to UI ships only after step 4's deploys are confirmed on that environment (until then, moved s3 bodies fail closed and teardown rejects bodies from a purged origin). The realtime old-topic removal and the edit_history privacy arm (cross-area) must have landed first.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/NNN_stable_file_bodies.sql (new cross-table suite, next free number at build, registered by hand in rls.yml's replay list)**: Objects inserted as postgres with owner_id. POSITIVE: F uploaded in A, moved by move_files into B (and separately into a personal space), key unchanged. A B-only reader SELECTs F's object and its .jpg (1 row each). After hard-deleting A, B still reads it, and storage_gc_queue holds nothing for F's key. A writer of B can rename (UPDATE) the thumbnail. Row-less objects (PROJECT.json, FINANCE/RATES.json for a money-cleared reader, a bin_files poster, connection-check) read exactly as before. NEGATIVE: an A-only reader gets 0 rows for F and its .jpg; a member of another workspace gets 0. A non-money member gets 0 for an is_financial row under an ordinary key (new closure). A trashed F's object gets 0 for every reader. Purge F as postgres: nobody reads the object, both with and without its queue row deleted (proves the purged-certificate arm). The uploader's delete_own of a claimed rabbit-files object within the hour is refused. An INSERT at a purged claimed key is refused. storage_object_claimed answers false for a key claimed in another workspace. Anon has no EXECUTE on the new functions. Allowlist probe: the prosecdef functions in public whose comment-stripped body assigns files.project_id are exactly {move_files} (empty before step 5), and those assigning storage_path/thumbnail_url/is_financial are within {rekey_file_body, move_files}. PLANTED CONTROLS (each restored by sha1): (1) restore 0042's segment-2 EXISTS in rabbit_files_select, and 'A-only reader gets 0' goes red; (2) drop arms 2 and 3 of storage_object_claimed, and the purged probes go red; (3) drop the workspace scope, and the cross-tenant probe goes red; (4) make storage_object_current_project SECURITY DEFINER, and the is_financial and trashed probes go red; (5) drop NOT claimed from delete_own, and the claimed-delete probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/05_files.sql (extend; existing UPDATE probes re-pointed)**: Client UPDATE of each of project_id (as a writer of both projects), workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial and folder_id → 42501 each. An UPDATE that resends the SAME project_id plus a description change passes. INSERT as a writer of A: a key under B; seg3 bin_files, connection-check or FINANCE; a 3-segment PROJECT.json; a key another row holds; a key named by a queue row; a key named by a purged certificate; a supabase key with no object; thumbnail ≠ path.jpg; provider google_drive; a folder of another project; a money-locked folder for a non-money row: refused, each in its own probe. A valid key with an object → accepted. Re-inserting the same id and path (insertOrSkip) → no error. rekey_file_body: across seg2, across LEGAL, out of INVOICES, into INVOICES without money access, on s3, on a moved row, on a trashed row → refused; a stale p_from_path → false; a valid same-prefix rekey → true and one 'moved' event. PLANTED: drop trg_files_body_guard → the client-update probes go red; make admissible return true → the insert probes go red; drop the seg2 = project arm → 'key under B' goes red; drop the id exclusion → the insertOrSkip probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/33_file_lifecycle.sql and 78_file_events_money.sql (extend)**: A move writes exactly one moved_out under A and one moved_in under B, with old_path = new_path; neither row's details names the other project; an A reader sees only moved_out; a private source's id appears in no B-readable row; a non-moving UPDATE writes neither; no 'purged' event and no queue row. The is_financial flag on a money row's events holds. PLANTED: drop the arm → the count probe goes red; write both under NEW.project_id → the A-reader probe goes red; widen fn_files_gc_enqueue to UPDATE → the no-enqueue probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/61_workspace_storage_s3.sql (extend)**: can_presign_object: get or head on a live s3 body and its derived .jpg → true; a trashed row's key → false (OSP-01); a purged key → false; a moved row → true for destination readers and false for source-only readers; put or del over any claimed key → false; the connection-check key → the legacy can_presign_project_read answer (true for a reader); a fresh unclaimed key → the legacy write answer. PLANTED: route claimed get through can_presign_project_read(seg2) → the source-only probe goes red; drop the claimed put/del refusal → the overwrite probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls 63_thumbnails_bucket, 83_private_project_file_gates, 90_legal_files, 95_legal_gate_managers (extend; existing UPDATE probes re-pointed)**: The 8 rabbit_files% count holds; the money policy text is unchanged; an INVOICES object is still refused to a non-money member; move_files refuses INVOICES, LEGAL, is_financial and expenses.file_ids sources and locked-folder destinations, leaving the row where it was; a local_server body's cross-project move is refused; a body moved INTO a private project is readable only by its creator and admins. PLANTED: strip move_files' money check → the guard still refuses (proves the second lock); then also strip the guard's is_financial/project arm → the probe goes red.
- **the quota-meter suite (66, remeasure)**: Bytes of a body (and its thumbnail) moved out of A, after A is hard-deleted, are still in workspace_petal_bytes(ws), and an upload that would cross the plan is refused; a foreign workspace's moved bytes are never counted; an unmoved body is counted once, never twice. PLANTED: drop the moved-bodies arm → red; drop its NOT EXISTS projects → the double-count probe goes red.
- **vitest: storagePresignBoundary.test.js / readUrls tests**: classifyOwned signs a key whose row's project ≠ the key's prefix (a moved row) and refuses a key no visible row owns; handleReadUrls makes no canReadProject call and refuses with 'row'; a money key is refused 'money' before any read; a failed row read fails closed; storage-presign calls can_presign_object for each op. Red first on the pre-change source. PLANTED: re-add the equality → the moved-row test goes red.
- **vitest: teardownCollect test; storage-gc shot-twin helper test (storageGcShotTwins or its equivalent)**: A moved body whose prefix project is gone but tied by file_events is collected with its .jpg; one whose prefix project exists in another workspace is rejected; rowNamesTwin finds a twin named by a row now in another project, and its query carries the provider filter. PLANTED: put back the projectIds-only owned() → red; put back .eq('project_id') → the moved-twin test goes red.
- **vitest: supabaseAdapter (updateFile, rewriteFileRow), runMigration (moveReceipt), source-scan and lockstep tests**: updateFile never sends project_id, workspace_id, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, is_financial or folder_id to Supabase (payload assertion). rewriteFileRow calls rpc('rekey_file_body') and handles false as the no-row branch with put-back. moveReceipt sends one RPC with p_financial true. A source scan (the writeGate.test.js pattern) fails on any .from('files').update( whose literal names a guarded column. A lockstep test reads uploadContainerFor's segments, cloudObjectPathFor's 'files' and the INVOICES/LEGAL builders and asserts each is in the SB-8 allowlist parsed from the migration text. FILE_EVENT_META carries every term of the CHECK parsed from the migration. PLANTED: re-add project_id to the payload → red; add a container segment → the lockstep test goes red. Full run with --maxWorkers=4, no .env.local.

### Risks

- Per-object policy cost: each storage.objects row listed, signed or read now runs a DEFINER claimed check (up to three index probes: files unique, queue, purged certificate) and, when claimed, an invoker files lookup under files_select and projects_select. Today it only parses the path (INFERRED).
  - *Mitigation:* Every lookup is indexed (SB-2), and the rehearsal EXPLAIN-proves it. Sequential IFs stop at the first hit (real bodies hit arm 1). EXPLAIN ANALYZE of a 100-object list before and after, recorded in the hand-off. F7's policy wave can wrap the constant parts later.
- A purged moved body falls back to its origin project's readers if BOTH fn_files_gc_enqueue and fn_file_events_capture swallow an error on the same purge (both catch every exception, MEASURED).
  - *Mitigation:* The two writers are independent, and the suite pins each arm separately. The residual is recorded in OUTSTANDING. If it is ever measured, the stronger fix is a non-swallowing tombstone for moved rows only.
- Within-project adoption: a writer of P can insert a row vouching for another member's unclaimed in-flight upload under P (seconds-long window; the key holds a millisecond timestamp and name), then move it. The uploader's insert gets 23505.
  - *Mitigation:* No confidentiality gain, because P's writers can already read unclaimed P keys under the prefix rule. The uploader retries and loses nothing. Recorded beside OSP-07 as INFORMATIONAL. A's owner_id check was rejected because it breaks migration resumes under another account.
- Keys stop naming their current project. Anything that reads a bucket prefix as 'this project's files' (Studio browsing, runbook diagnostics, a future per-project byte report) is wrong for moved bodies. storage_path also shows the destination audience the origin project's uuid and the original filename (the S4b-07 class).
  - *Mitigation:* Handbook §4.7/§12.4 rule: 'a key's prefix is where a file was created, never where it is; group by files.project_id'. A bare uuid exposes no title. Hiding storage_path would need signing by file id (later).
- The guard breaks existing pgTAP probes (MEASURED UPDATEs in 05/33/78/83/90/95), and old clients during the deploy window (S4c refile, receipt move).
  - *Mitigation:* Re-point the probes in the same commit and run the whole set. Old-client failures are graceful and named (refile puts the object back; moveReceipt says its sentence); recorded with S4c-14.
- Preflight refusal counts (duplicate keys, prefix ≠ project_id, cross-workspace prefixes) may be non-zero on dev, staging or production.
  - *Mitigation:* Step 0 measures them read-only on each database, and the same queries go into PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK. A non-zero count is stop and report, decided per row by the controller.
- Revocation lag: signed URLs minted before a move (or a trash) stay valid until they expire (thumbnails 1 h, s3 stream 3600 s, read-once 300 s), and the desktop's cached thumbnails survive a move into a personal space.
  - *Mitigation:* Stated in the walkthrough and handbook; no bearer URL can be recalled. Live channels are Session 4's audience-generation work.
- Mirror desync by a Storage API rename: a writer of the CURRENT project can bucket.move a claimed body elsewhere, leaving the row naming a key with no object. The same is possible today.
  - *Mitigation:* USING now requires a writer of the current project, so the old project's writers can no longer do it. A full fix (claimed objects immovable except through rekey) conflicts with S4c's move-then-rekey order. Recorded.
- Trashed bodies become unreadable (SB-16), so Session 1's file trash view cannot preview them.
  - *Mitigation:* MEASURED: no current reader of trashed bodies. The trash view shows names and icons; restore makes the body readable again. Recorded as a deliberate behaviour change that closes OSP-01 for Petal.
- Moved files are never re-keyed, so no S4c re-filing into the destination's shot folders. Legacy non-derived thumbnails are detached on move. Money, Legal and local_server bodies cannot move.
  - *Mitigation:* Stated in the walkthrough and Help. Money files are not in Moth at all. A local-body refusal carries a plain reason on the attempt, with no greyed placeholder.
- Personal-space routing: if the personal-space area reuses projectIsPrivate routing, personal uploads land as local_server, and SB-10 then refuses moving them into a project, against her ruling.
  - *Mitigation:* Hard prerequisite on the personal-space area: a distinct routing arm sends personal-space uploads to the company's chosen provider (fileProviderFor). A probe checks that a personal-space upload row is never local_server.
- Composite links: a moved file can keep asset_id, task_id, scene_id or shot_id, or asset_versions.file_id rows pointing into the source project. There is no composite (id, project_id) FK on files.
  - *Mitigation:* move_files (shared-moves area) clears same-project links on the row and refuses or detaches asset_versions and ingestion references, with a suite probe. Client changes to those links are not in this guard: a cross-project link gives no body access, because authority reads only project_id.
- Hard project deletion plus a client-chosen projects.id (INFERRED: projects_insert does not forbid an explicit id) could let a new project re-take a deleted uuid and inherit the prefix fallback over row-less leftovers. Pre-existing.
  - *Mitigation:* Moved bodies are protected (claimed, current-project answer). Recorded in OUTSTANDING with a probe; the fix (refuse a client-supplied id) is outside this area.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_MOTH §4 New tables: 'file_body_locations | Stable body identity/workspace; verified provider/immutable locator; thumbnail; publication generation/lifecycle; current file association | 123_file_body_locations.sql'; '14 additions would take RLS_TABLES from 61 to 75'
  - *Design:* No file_body_locations table. The immutable locator is the guarded files.storage_path (UNIQUE), the current association is files.project_id (move_files only), and authority comes from storage_object_claimed plus the invoker storage_object_current_project. RLS_TABLES grows by 13. Suite 123 is replaced by a cross-table suite registered in the replay list. The publication generation goes to Session 4. The plan's requirements (current association, not the former prefix; server-controlled locator; no arbitrary adoption) are met.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_MOTH §4 Storage, E19: 'Body stays put, association changes atomically. Old project loses body access; destination gains it; deleting old container cannot collect moved body.'
  - *Design:* Met. Added beyond the plan: a purged moved body becomes nobody's, never the origin project's; a trashed body is unreadable; old-project writers can no longer PUT, DEL or rename a moved body; rowNamesTwin is fixed so an interrupted re-file followed by a move cannot lose the body; the meter and teardown are fixed for a purged origin.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_MOTH §4 Storage, E19 (applies to every provider)
  - *Design:* Only supabase and s3 bodies (and future NAS metadata rows) move by association. local_server bodies, a private project's media on one desktop (MEASURED, uploadFile l.3481), are refused for cross-project moves. Desktop-only (Local Server mode) bundle locators are relative to each project's folder (MEASURED, resolveFileBaseDir), so their cross-project move needs her ruling (questions).
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_WEEK_2026-11-04 F7: 'indexes on files.storage_path and files.thumbnail_url'
  - *Design:* This area creates the storage_path index itself (UNIQUE, text_pattern_ops, partial). Authority lookups are derived and need no thumbnail_url index, so F7 re-decides that one for the drain only. The release plan needs the edit.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* OUTSTANDING OSP-07 recorded fix: a CHECK tying storage_path to projects/<project_id>/
  - *Design:* Wrong under moves (every moved row would violate it). Replaced by INSERT-time admissibility plus client immutability of the locator columns.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Task/plan guard scope: project_id plus storage_path, storage_provider, thumbnail_url
  - *Design:* The guard also covers workspace_id, is_financial (money-ness becomes monotone, so a receipt cannot be flipped and then moved) and folder_id (closes digest fact 12's cross-project and locked-folder route). MEASURED: no client writes folder_id; only moveReceipt writes is_financial after insert.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_MOTH §4 Existing changes: 'Extend 05_files, 90_legal_files/private/storage suites'
  - *Design:* Beyond extending them, the existing authenticated UPDATE probes in 05, 33, 78, 83, 90 and 95 must be re-pointed (MEASURED by grep), and the cross-table suite must be registered by hand.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* PLAN_MOTH (no mention)
  - *Design:* Code finding: a cross-project move emits no file_events today (MEASURED, 0074 keys only on storage_path). moved_out and moved_in are added by widening 0093's CHECK, the writer in the same migration.
- *Plan:* Digest: 'storage-gc (orphan scan, referencedPaths, rowNamesTwin, queue drain)' as consumers
  - *Design:* Only rowNamesTwin needs a correctness change (its project filter); referencedPaths only gains the provider filter so the partial index serves it. The drain and orphan-scan tenancy are correct through moves (MEASURED).
- *Plan:* Digest fact (b): file_events_select / the CHECK latest named as 0092
  - *Design:* The latest file_events_event_check is in 0093 (viewed_remote, MEASURED l.1110-1115); restate from there.

### Questions for her, as drafted (to be put to her in plain words only after the synthesis)

- A private project keeps its pictures and videos only on the computer of the person who added them. If someone tries to move one of those files into a shared project or into their personal space, what should happen?
  - Refuse the move and say plainly that the file lives on that person's computer and has to be added again from there
  - Allow the move only from that computer, and copy the file up to the company's storage as part of the move
  - Allow the move and show the file to everyone else as 'only on that person's computer'
  - *Recommended:* Refuse the move and say plainly that the file lives on that person's computer and has to be added again from there
- Some projects live only on one computer, not in the cloud. When someone moves a file between two of those projects, what should WILSON do?
  - Not in this release: moving files inside one such project still works, and moving between them waits
  - Move the actual file into the new project's folder on that computer (same disk, nothing uploaded), checking it arrived before saying it is done
  - Leave the file in the old project's folder and remember where it really is (if the old project is later deleted, the file goes with it)
  - *Recommended:* Not in this release: moving files inside one such project still works, and moving between them waits

### Review — security (stable file bodies: leaks, privilege escalation, forged authority, weakened gates)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes, not as written. The skeleton is sound: no new table, an immutable locator, the INVOKER current-project resolution under files_select, the eight money policies left untouched, the client guard on seven columns, and the one rekey RPC. But three blockers each leak data people cannot see today. SEC-1: the workspace-scoped 'claimed' answer plus client-chosen project ids lets another company read and rewrite live moved bodies once the origin project is purged. SEC-2: the s3 body/thumbnail key collision lets an origin writer get a moved .jpg body or thumbnail signed. SEC-3: the realtime UPDATE and edit_history diffs still carry the source project's id to the destination. SEC-4 (presign deploy/rollback window), SEC-5 (definers rely on passes_project_privacy, blind to a personal-space arm) and SEC-6 (storage_path exposes the origin id and filename, which needs her ruling) must be settled before move_files is callable on any environment. The minors are small edits to the specified functions and tests.

- **SEC-1 [blocker]** storage_object_claimed only looks at the caller's own workspace (SB-3). Once a moved file's origin project is purged and its uuid is taken again by a project in another company, that other company's members read, list, rename and overwrite the first company's LIVE moved bodies, because the key falls back to the prefix rule there. This breaks SB-3's premise that 'the prefix fallback then denies it through projects_select'.
  - *Scenario:* Company W1: file F is uploaded in project A (key projects/A/...) and moved into W1's private project B (or a personal space). A is trashed and purged by the 30-day cron, which hard-deletes the row. User U belongs to W1 and is also a manager in their own company W2. U knows A's uuid (it was a project U worked on, or it shows in F's storage_path). U signs in to W2 and inserts a projects row with id = A. projects_insert does not check the id, and the default gen_random_uuid() can be overridden. In W2's context storage_object_claimed(F's key) is false, because the claiming row, queue row and certificate are all W1's. The CASE falls back to fn_try_uuid(seg2) = A, which W2 can see under projects_select, so rabbit_files_select returns F's object. U can list projects/A/ to find every moved body and its .jpg. Through rabbit_files_update/insert with can_write_project(A) (true for a W2 manager), U can also rename or overwrite them. Knock-on effects: W1's teardown owned() rule ('no projects row with id seg1 in ANY workspace') now rejects F (stranded), and the SB-12 meter bills W2 for W1's bytes. The design's own test ('storage_object_claimed answers false for a key claimed in another workspace') and planted control 3 pin the vulnerable behaviour.
  - *Evidence:* Design SB-3 / schema_changes storage_object_claimed (v_ws := current_workspace_id(), every arm filtered on workspace). supabase/migrations/0042_money_segments_and_manifest_rewrite.sql rabbit_files_select/_update (EXISTS projects p WHERE p.id = fn_try_uuid(foldername[2])). 0072_private_projects.sql projects_select (workspace_id = current_workspace_id()). 0000_rabbit_base_schema.sql projects.id 'default gen_random_uuid()' with no guard. 0013_project_members.sql projects_insert WITH CHECK (workspace, membership, admin|manager only, no id check). 0029_operator_write_lockdown.sql fn_projects_populate_workspace (sets only workspace_id). Hard delete through purge_soft_deleted (0014/0067) is routine. INFERRED: PostgREST accepts a client id (no column grant found by grep).
  - *Fix:* (1) Decide 'claimed' across ALL workspaces: if any tenant's files row (any trash state), storage_gc_queue row or 'purged' certificate names the key, it is claimed. storage_object_current_project, running under the caller's files_select, then returns NULL to every other tenant, so nobody outside reads it. Keep the boolean-only answer and accept the cross-tenant existence bit, since keys hold uuids, a ms timestamp and a filename. (2) Close project-id reuse: a BEFORE INSERT trigger on projects that, for client roles, forces NEW.id := gen_random_uuid(), or refuses an id that appears in file_events.project_id, storage_gc_queue.object_path seg2 or any files.storage_path seg2. SB-12 and SB-14 depend on 'no projects row anywhere' staying true. (3) Invert the cross-tenant probe and planted control 3, and add a probe in which a W2 project re-taking a purged W1 id reads 0 rows of W1's moved body.
- **SEC-2 [blocker]** In a company's own s3 bucket, bodies and thumbnails share one key namespace (thumbnail = storage_path || '.jpg').
