Admissibility checks uniqueness on storage_path alone, and the guard accepts any thumbnail_url equal to path||'.jpg'. So a writer of the origin project can insert a row whose derived thumbnail key IS another file's body key, or whose body key is another file's thumbnail. can_presign_object and the rewritten classifyOwned then sign the victim's object, because the attacker's own visible row vouches for it. storage_object_current_project's LIMIT 1 is also nondeterministic when two rows match.
  - *Scenario:* s3 workspace. Victim V uploads still.jpg in project A, giving key K = projects/A/assets/<id>/<ts>-still.jpg. V moves it into a personal space or private project P. Attacker X is a writer of A who saw K before the move. X inserts an s3 row R1 in A with storage_path = left(K,-4) and thumbnail_url = K. This is admissible: it is under A, in an allowed segment, no row's storage_path equals it, there is no queue row or certificate, and no object check is made for s3. can_presign_object(K,'get'): K is claimed, and EXISTS visible row with storage_path = left(K,-4) AND thumbnail_url = K is R1, so the answer is true and X gets a signed GET for V's full-size image. storage-read-urls does the same: classifyOwned puts R1's thumbnail_url into thumbs and signs K for 3600 s. The mirror case, a row at storage_path = K'||'.jpg', signs V's thumbnail. This also defeats SB-16 (trashed bodies unreadable) and OSP-07's closure for any file whose name ends in .jpg.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/thumbnails.js thumbnailKeyFor (`${storagePath}.jpg`), putThumbnailTo (s3 thumbnail goes to the same provider/bucket). supabase/functions/_shared/readUrls.ts classifyOwned (bodies.add(r.storage_path); thumbs.add when thumbnail_url === storage_path + '.jpg'; no check against other rows). Design schema_changes can_presign_object (storage_path = p_path OR (storage_path = left(p_path,-4) AND thumbnail_url = p_path)), rabbit_file_key_admissible ('uniqueness excluding p_file'), fn_files_body_guard INSERT (thumbnail_url NULL or storage_path||'.jpg'), storage_object_current_project ('LIMIT 1').
  - *Fix:* Treat each key namespace as one in admissibility and in rekey_file_body. For s3, refuse p_path when any row (any trash state) has storage_path IN (p_path||'.jpg', left(p_path,-4) when p_path ends in .jpg) or thumbnail_url IN (p_path, p_path||'.jpg'). Run the queue and certificate checks on both p_path and p_path||'.jpg'. Refuse a thumbnail_url that another row's storage_path holds. In can_presign_object and classifyOwned, resolve a key to the set of rows that claim it, and sign only when exactly one row claims it and that row is visible. Make storage_object_current_project return NULL when more than one row matches. Add 61_workspace_storage_s3 probes for both collision directions, with a planted control that removes the namespace check.
- **SEC-3 [blocker]** The cross-area requirements still let the source project's id and old state reach the destination audience. By today's code, fn_realtime_broadcast sends a files UPDATE to rabbit:project:{NEW.project_id} carrying the full OLD record. The design asks only to ADD an old-topic removal and keeps 'the UPDATE on the new topic'. edit_history keeps one project_id snapshot per diff, so a privacy arm on that column alone cannot judge a diff that spans two projects. Either channel undoes SB-15's stated purpose (no source id shown to the destination) on the first move.
  - *Scenario:* Owner moves file F from their personal space S (or private project P) into shared project B. Every subscriber of rabbit:project:B receives old_record with project_id = S, plus S-era folder_id, tags, description and name. A manager of B, or any workspace manager, reads the edit_history diff holding before.project_id = S. If the privacy arm keys on the snapshot project_id (the destination B), the diff passes. Moving the other way (B into S) gives B's subscribers nothing on the new topic. But if the removal on B's topic is sent as a normal UPDATE, B's subscribers get NEW.project_id = S.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0098_table_views.sql fn_realtime_broadcast files arm (v_project := COALESCE(NEW,OLD)->>'project_id', i.e. NEW's project, then realtime.broadcast_changes(topic, TG_OP, ..., NEW, OLD)). 0092_legal_gate_managers.sql fn_edit_history_money_snapshot (one NEW.project_id from edit_history_file_class). Design consumers_to_change 'fn_realtime_broadcast files arm' ('emit a removal (id only) ... as well as the UPDATE on the new topic') and 'edit_history_select' prerequisite.
  - *Fix:* Make the cross-area contract exact. When OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id, publish on the NEW topic an INSERT-shaped event with NEW only (old_record NULL), and on the OLD topic a DELETE-shaped event carrying {id} only. For edit_history, either redact project_id and folder_id from move diffs, or have edit_history_select require the caller to pass privacy for BOTH sides of the diff (before.project_id and after.project_id). move_files' migration should hold post-conditions asserting both arms are present (comment-stripped body match), so the RPC cannot be applied before them. A pending UI is not a gate.
- **SEC-4 [major]** The rollout claims that until step 4's deploys, 'moved s3 bodies fail closed'. That is false for storage-presign: the deployed function authorises by the key's prefix project. move_files is a PostgREST RPC that any client can call once step 5's migration lands, whether or not the UI has shipped. Rolling step 4 back 'in reverse order' after any s3 move has the same effect.
  - *Scenario:* Step 5 is applied on an environment where storage-presign is still the current version, or presign is rolled back later. A member who reads only origin project A asks storage-presign for get on a moved s3 body projects/A/...; can_presign_project_read(A) is true, so a URL is signed. A writer of A asks for put or del on the same key and overwrites or deletes a file that now belongs to a private project or personal space. storage-read-urls fails closed (key-project equality), so only presign leaks. Production at 0063 makes this window likely.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/functions/storage-presign/index.ts (predicate = can_presign_project_read/_write, called with p_project: shape.projectId; no row check). Design migration_order step 5 constraint ('The Move to UI ships only after step 4's deploys ... until then, moved s3 bodies fail closed') and step 4 'Rollback in reverse order'.
  - *Fix:* Either have move_files refuse storage_provider = 's3' until a later migration enables s3 moves, applied only after storage-presign's deploy is confirmed on that environment from functions list plus a source comparison, or ship can_presign_object's server change before move_files exists anywhere. State that once any s3 row has project_id ≠ its key prefix, storage-presign must never be rolled back, and add that check to PRODUCTION_RUNBOOK.
- **SEC-5 [major]** The two new SECURITY DEFINER write gates (rabbit_file_key_admissible, rekey_file_body) judge visibility through passes_project_privacy plus can_write_project, not through projects_select. passes_project_privacy restates only the is_private arm. can_write_project lets every member write an unstaffed project and lets every admin or manager write any project. If the personal-space area adds its own arm to projects_select (it cannot simply reuse is_private: digest facts 4 and 9), these definers will admit people projects_select hides. passes_project_privacy also ignores a trashed project.
  - *Scenario:* A personal space S is a project with no project_members rows, so it is unstaffed, and its privacy arm is something other than is_private. Another member M calls rekey_file_body on a file uploaded directly into S (not a moved row). passes_project_privacy(S) is true (is_private false) and can_write_project(S) is true (NOT project_is_staffed), so M re-keys S's file onto another admissible object under S. Calling rabbit_file_key_admissible as an RPC likewise answers for S's keys. The same holds for a file in a trashed project.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0082_private_project_definers.sql passes_project_privacy (only NOT is_private OR created_by OR admin; no deleted_at). 0013_project_members.sql can_write_project (admin|manager OR NOT project_is_staffed OR seat). Design schema_changes rabbit_file_key_admissible and rekey_file_body (caller rights = workspace, has_active_membership, passes_project_privacy, can_write_project).
  - *Fix:* Make the visibility arm of both definers a single helper owned by the privacy/personal-space area, kept word for word with projects_select (including deleted_at IS NULL and the personal-space arm), and pin it with a post-condition. Or require the caller to pass an INVOKER visibility check before calling the definer, for example storage_object_current_project(...) IS NOT NULL for rekey. Add a suite probe: a non-owner member is refused rekey on a personal-space file and on a file in a trashed project.
- **SEC-6 [major]** files.storage_path is immutable and is returned to every reader of the destination project. So a moved file permanently shows the destination the origin project's uuid, the origin entity ids (scene, shot, asset or task uuids), and the original filename. That holds when the origin is a private project or someone's personal space. It contradicts SB-15's rationale and her ruling that ids or existence a person cannot already see never appear. moved_in also writes old_path = new_path = storage_path into the destination's file_events.
  - *Scenario:* Owner uploads 'contract-draft-v2.pdf' in their personal space S (key projects/S/project/S/<ts>-contract-draft-v2.pdf), renames it, then moves it into shared project B. Every reader of B (files_select, the realtime row, FileAuditDrawer moved_in) sees S's uuid and the original filename. A file moved out of a private project shows its scene and shot ids to people who cannot see that project.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0092_legal_gate_managers.sql files_select (whole row, storage_path included). 0027_file_lifecycle.sql fn_file_events_capture (path columns written). Design SB-1 (locator immutable), SB-15 (rationale against showing a private source's id), risks row 'Keys stop naming their current project ... A bare uuid exposes no title'.
  - *Fix:* Ask Audrey to rule, in plain words, whether a moved file may show where it was created, and do not accept it as a risk row. If not, either mint personal-space and private-project uploads under a non-identifying container (for example a per-body uuid segment, keeping seg2 = project only for project-created files), or stop exposing storage_path to clients for rows whose seg2 ≠ project_id: sign by file id in storage-read-urls and an RPC for Petal, and drop the path from moved_in.
- **SEC-7 [minor]** rabbit_file_key_admissible is GRANTed to authenticated, so a client can call it directly as an RPC. As a DEFINER it probes storage.objects and every files row before any money or Legal check. A non-money writer of A can therefore learn whether an object sits at an INVOICES/ or LEGAL/ key of A that has no row: true means object present and no row; false means a row holds it or nothing is there. On INSERT, the guard's 42501 sentence differs from the RLS 'new row violates row-level security policy' sentence, which gives the same bit.
  - *Scenario:* A non-money member of A calls rpc('rabbit_file_key_admissible', {p_provider:'supabase', p_path:'projects/A/INVOICES/<line>/<ts>-<name>', ...}) for a candidate key and reads true or false. The keys are hard to guess, so this is limited to keys the caller already knows, for example from an old listing.
  - *Evidence:* Design schema_changes rabbit_file_key_admissible (order: caller rights, shape, allowlist incl. 'invoices','legal', uniqueness, queue/certificate, storage.objects existence; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated). 0092 files_insert money arm runs after BEFORE triggers.
  - *Fix:* Inside admissible, return false for a money or Legal seg3 unless COALESCE(can_access_project_money(p_project)) OR (Legal AND can_access_project_legal(p_project)), placed before any existence probe. Have the guard raise the same SQLSTATE and sentence the RLS refusal would give, or document the single bit as INFORMATIONAL beside S4b-07.
- **SEC-8 [minor]** storage_object_claimed reads public.files, storage_gc_queue (RLS on, zero policies) and file_events as a definer, and fails OPEN if its owner is ever subject to RLS: every arm reads zero rows, the answer is 'unclaimed', and the prefix fallback hands moved bodies back to the origin project. The preflight checks row security only for the migrating role at apply time.
  - *Scenario:* A future replay, an owner change, or an environment where the owning role lacks BYPASSRLS. storage_gc_queue then returns nothing (no policies) and files returns only rows the caller can see, so an origin-only reader gets 'unclaimed' for a body moved into a private project and reads it by prefix.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0027_file_lifecycle.sql storage_gc_queue ('RLS on with zero policies'). Design schema_changes storage_object_claimed (SECURITY DEFINER, no row_security setting); preflight 'Refuse when row security applies to current_user'.
  - *Fix:* Declare storage_object_claimed (and rabbit_file_key_admissible) with SET row_security = off, so a role that cannot bypass RLS gets an error (fail closed) instead of an empty read. Add a post-condition that the function owner has rolbypassrls, plus a suite probe run under a non-bypass owner if feasible.
- **SEC-9 [minor]** SB-15 specifies 'details NULL' for moved_out and moved_in, but file_events.details is NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'. The whole capture runs inside one EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS block, so every move would silently write no audit rows at all, including a 'trashed' event emitted by the same UPDATE. A control would go missing without any error.
  - *Scenario:* move_files updates project_id. The moved_out INSERT violates NOT NULL, the handler swallows it with a WARNING, and the move leaves no file_events trail under either project.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0027_file_lifecycle.sql file_events.details 'JSONB NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb' and the fn_file_events_capture EXCEPTION handler. 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql (details still listed in the insert column lists). Design SB-15 ('details NULL').
  - *Fix:* Omit details, or write '{}'::jsonb. Add a post-condition or probe that a move writes exactly two events (suite 33 already plans the count probe; make sure it runs against the real column constraint).
- **SEC-10 [minor]** Legacy key-shape outliers are only reported (RAISE NOTICE), and move_files is not required to refuse them. A legacy row keyed projects/A/bin_files/... that is moved keeps its thumbnail under the PERMISSIVE petal_bin_posters_insert/update policies, which grant INSERT and UPDATE on projects/A/bin_files/* to anyone who passes can_edit_shot_lists(A). Those policies OR with the restated base policies, so origin-project reviewers can overwrite a moved file's picture.
  - *Scenario:* A legacy files row at projects/A/bin_files/x/1-a.png (crafted under OSP-07 before the guard) is moved into private P. Reviewer R of A upserts rabbit-thumbnails projects/A/bin_files/x/1-a.png.jpg through petal_bin_posters_update and replaces P's preview with an image of R's choosing.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0091_bins_on_the_cloud.sql petal_bin_posters_insert/_update (permissive, seg2 prefix plus seg3 = 'bin_files'). Design preflight ('key-shape outliers (the guard is INSERT-only)' reported, not refused), SB-10 refused-source list (no shape check).
  - *Fix:* move_files refuses a source whose storage_path fails the SB-8 shape and segment allowlist. Alternatively, make the preflight refuse rows with seg3 outside the allowlist (bin_files, connection-check, finance) so the controller decides each one.
- **SEC-11 [minor]** The CI allowlist probe that replaces the GUC finds definers that write locator columns by matching comment-stripped body text. That misses dynamic SQL (EXECUTE format('UPDATE public.%I SET ...')), the shape soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted already use. A future generic definer could write project_id or storage_path past the guard (current_user = owner) unseen.
  - *Scenario:* A later session adds a generic 'patch_row(table, id, jsonb)' DEFINER built on EXECUTE format. The text probe sees no 'project_id =' assignment, and clients can re-home files rows through it.
  - *Evidence:* Design SB-7 rejected_alternatives / tests ('Allowlist probe: the prosecdef functions in public whose comment-stripped body assigns files.project_id are exactly {move_files}'). supabase/migrations/0014_soft_delete.sql soft_delete_row/restore_soft_deleted (dynamic UPDATE).
  - *Fix:* Extend the probe to list every prosecdef function in public whose body contains EXECUTE together with 'files' or '%I'. Require each to appear on a reviewed allowlist (today soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted, which write deleted_at only), failing on anything new.

### Review — Correctness and house rules: stable file bodies (MO1 design round, hybrid on B's skeleton)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes, not as written. Most of the skeleton checks out against the code: the latest bodies named are the right ones (0042 and 0053 base policies, 0073 meter, 0074 capture, 0093 CHECK and file_events_select). No definer or Edge Function writes files. postgres has BYPASSRLS, so the DEFINER and INVOKER split behaves as claimed. The client-only writers of the locator columns are exactly rewriteFileRow and moveReceipt. The BEFORE-trigger order is safe. RI SET NULL runs as the owner. Suite 95's sixteen-policy text pins survive the CASE restatement.

Four major findings must change the design before code:
- **SFB-01:** the queue and purged-certificate claims are permanent, so desktop files with deterministic migration keys can never be carried again after an orphan sweep or a purge.
- **SFB-02:** the same claims make bin posters (restore of an older poster key) and reserved objects unreadable.
- **SFB-03:** move_files must refuse rows whose object is missing or sits at an S4c twin key, or the old project keeps the body.
- **SFB-04:** suites 79 and 81 go red under the INSERT guard and are missing from the re-point list.

Narrowing arms 2 and 3 to files-born, preferably moved, bodies fixes SFB-01 and SFB-02 together. The minor findings fix the test controls (SFB-06), the event-path claim (SFB-05), the queue reason (SFB-08), the migration-path refusals (SFB-07, SFB-09), the oracles (SFB-10) and the allowlist probe (SFB-11).

- **SFB-01 [major]** Arms 2 and 3 of storage_object_claimed, and SB-8's "never queued, never purged" admissibility rule, claim a key for good. The runMigration body keys are deterministic (they carry no timestamp), so a desktop file whose earlier cloud object was orphan-swept or purged can never be carried to the cloud again.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED code path, INFERRED frequency. (1) Run 1 of a desktop-to-cloud migration uploads projects/<pid>/files/<fid>/<safeName>. (2) insertOrSkip then fails for that file (an RLS, CHECK or network error after the upload). (3) More than 24 hours later an admin runs storage-gc. The orphan scan deletes the object and certify() writes a storage_gc_queue row (reason orphan-scan, status deleted) for that exact key. (4) Run 2 tries again. list() shows nothing, so it uploads to the same key. rabbit_files_insert's CASE now finds the key claimed through the queue arm, storage_object_current_project returns NULL, and the upload is refused. Even if the object did land, the guard's admissibility refuses the row because a queue row names the key. The file fails on every later run. Today run 2 succeeds. The same applies to a file trashed and purged in the cloud and later re-carried from a second bundle copy (the purged-certificate arm), and to moveReceipt's deterministic INVOICES key.
  - *Evidence:* src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js cloudObjectPathFor (projects/${projectId}/${segment}/${f.id}/${safeName}, no Date.now()); the ordinary-files loop (list(objectDir), then upload with upsert:false, then insertOrSkip); supabase/functions/storage-gc/index.ts orphan scan (remove, then certify(ctx, counts, RABBIT_BUCKET, obj.path, 'orphan-scan', 'deleted', …)) and certify() (inserts storage_gc_queue rows); design SB-3 arm 2 (any queue row in the workspace) and SB-8 ("no storage_gc_queue row and no 'purged' file_events row names it").
  - *Fix:* Narrow arm 2 to queue rows with file_id IS NOT NULL. Orphan-scan certificates and bin posters carry a NULL file_id. Narrow both arms to records of bodies that were once a files row's. Better still, narrow them to moved bodies only: a purged certificate whose project_id <> fn_try_uuid(split_part(old_path,'/',2)), since for an unmoved body the prefix project and the current project are the same. Also have runMigration mint unique keys (a Date.now() segment, as uploadFile and the managed carry already do), or treat a claimed key as 'removed'. Add a vitest and a pgTAP probe for orphan sweep then re-carry, with a plant that bites.
- **SFB-02 [major]** The queue arm does not care whose object it names. It therefore claims row-less product objects the design says keep the prefix rule: bin-clip posters, and any reserved object (PROJECT.json, FINANCE/RATES.json) a queue row ever named. Once a key is claimed, the CASE sends reads to storage_object_current_project, which finds no files row and returns NULL. The object becomes unreadable to everyone.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED. A bin clip's poster is replaced. trg_bin_files_poster_replaced queues the old key (kind thumbnail, file_id NULL). 0091 itself names the case where "a restore brings an older key back": poster_path is set back to that key. Poster reads go only through rabbit_thumbnails_select (0091: "Reading stays rabbit_thumbnails_select's"). That policy now sees a claimed key and asks storage_object_current_project('rabbit-thumbnails', key), which matches files rows only. It returns NULL, so the restored poster disappears for every member. Reserved objects: the storage-gc drain marks a queue row naming PROJECT.json or RATES.json as 'skipped' and leaves it there, so after this migration any such historic row makes the manifest or rates file unreadable. SB-5's claim that "Bin posters resolve by the prefix fallback, because no files row can own a bin_files key" overlooks arm 2.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0091_bins_on_the_cloud.sql fn_bin_files_gc_enqueue (INSERT … 'rabbit-thumbnails', OLD.poster_path, …, NULL, 'file-purged', 'supabase', 'thumbnail'), trg_bin_files_poster_replaced and its comment; §9 comment "Reading an existing poster stays on the bucket's project-membership SELECT policy" (no petal_bin_posters_select exists); storage-gc/index.ts drain reserved branch (update status 'skipped', row kept); design SB-3 arm 2, SB-4 (files-only match), SB-5.
  - *Fix:* Scope arm 2 to file_id IS NOT NULL. Better, join it to the file's purged certificate as SFB-01 proposes. Exclude row-less shapes outright: in storage_object_claimed, return false for seg3 bin_files, connection-check, a 3-segment PROJECT.json and FINANCE/RATES.json, in lockstep with _shared/reservedObjects.ts. Add a preflight count of queue rows, purged certificates and files rows naming those shapes. Add suite probes: replace a poster, restore the older key, the poster still reads; plant arm 2 without the file_id filter and the probe goes red.
- **SFB-03 [major]** move_files (SB-10) does not refuse a supabase row whose object is missing from its storage_path. An interrupted S4c re-file leaves exactly that row: its object sits at the nested twin key, which no row names. Moving it breaks E19 in both directions. The origin project keeps reading the only copy of the body, and the destination cannot read it at all.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED code path. A shot re-file is interrupted: the object moved to K' and the row still says K (the rewriteFileRow "run it again" state, S4c-09). The file is then moved from project A to project B. K is claimed by the row but holds no object, so B gets nothing. K' is claimed by nobody, so the prefix rule gives it to A, and A's readers keep reading and writing the body. A re-run of the re-file in A cannot find the row (filesUnderPrefix filters .eq('project_id')), and moved rows are never re-keyed (SB-9). SB-13 keeps storage-gc from deleting K', but the body is stranded and readable only by the old project.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js moveObjectVerified ('landed-earlier'), rewriteFileRow's doc comment ("the object stays at its new key … the next run finds it") and filesUnderPrefix (.eq project_id); storage-gc/index.ts orphan-scan twin comment ("a run that stops between the two leaves an object no row names"); design SB-3 (only the row's own key and its derived .jpg are claimed), SB-9 (moved rows refused), SB-10 (refused-source list).
  - *Fix:* Add a refused source to SB-10, checked inside move_files: for supabase, no storage.objects row in rabbit-files at storage_path (this also covers S4c-03's object-missing rows), or an object at the shot twin (shotKeyTwinPattern) whose own key no row names. Give the refusal a plain sentence ("finish tidying this shot's folder first"). Pin it with a probe and a plant that bites.
- **SFB-04 [major]** The list of pgTAP probes to re-point is incomplete. Suites 79 and 81 insert supabase files rows as authenticated, with invented paths and no storage.objects seed. Under the new INSERT guard (object must exist) these inserts are refused with 42501. Because a BEFORE trigger fires before CHECK constraints, a probe written for a CHECK error code gets 42501 instead.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED. Suite 79 does SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated, then lives_ok INSERT … 'supabase', 'projects/aaaa1111-…/ASSETS/c3/1-brief.pdf' (probe 7). It has no storage.objects row, so admissibility fails and probe 7 goes red. Probe 8 expects '23514' (the 2001-character description cap) and now gets 42501, so it goes red too, and the later money probes lose their fixture rows. Suite 81 sets role authenticated, then lives_ok INSERT … 'supabase', 'projects/…/assets/a1/10-clip.mov'; red, plus its read-back probes. The pgTAP CI job turns red at step 1. The consumers list names only 05, 33, 78, 83, 90 and 95.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/tests/rls/79_file_document_kind.sql (SET LOCAL ROLE authenticated, probes 7 and 8 and the lines after; no storage.objects reference); 81_file_media_metadata.sql (set_config('role','authenticated'), lives_ok INSERT; no storage.objects reference); design consumers_to_change, supabase/tests/rls row.
  - *Fix:* Before the build round, measure every suite that writes files as a client role. Grep each INSERT and UPDATE of public.files against the role switch before it: 79 and 81 are confirmed; 80's local_server insert passes. Re-point each probe: seed storage.objects as postgres, or insert the row as postgres; keep the CHECK-code probes as postgres twins and add a separate guard probe; recount the plans. List the suites in the hand-off.
- **SFB-05 [minor]** SB-15 says that "neither event names the other project" and rejects design B for leaking a private source's id. Yet moved_in carries old_path = new_path = NEW.storage_path, which is projects/<origin uuid>/…. The planned probe "a private source's id appears in no B-readable row" cannot pass against this design, because both the moved_in row and the files row show the origin's uuid. The post-condition (no 'from_project') only checks a word.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED from the design text. A file moves from a private project P, or a personal space, into shared project B. A reader of B sees the moved_in event and the files row, and both show storage_path 'projects/<P uuid>/…'. The 33/78 probe as written goes red, or gets weakened until it no longer bites. Her ruling is that nothing may reveal the existence of something a person cannot see; a hidden project's id exposed this way is that kind of hint.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0074_file_events_money_arm.sql fn_file_events_capture (the 'moved' arm writes OLD and NEW storage_path); design SB-15 (old_path = new_path = NEW.storage_path), its tests row for 33/78, and its risks row ("storage_path also shows the destination audience the origin project's uuid").
  - *Fix:* Write moved_out and moved_in with old_path and new_path NULL. Nothing depends on them: SB-14's tie uses project_id, and arm 3 reads only 'purged' rows. Restate the probe as: "no B-readable file_events row except the files row's own storage_path names P". Record the storage_path exposure in OUTSTANDING beside S4b-07, and stop citing B's leak as a reason against B.
- **SFB-06 [minor]** Two planted controls in the new suite would not bite as written. (a) The probe "uploader's delete_own of a claimed rabbit-files object within the hour is refused" cannot run as SQL, because storage.protect_delete() blocks direct deletes, so plant (5) has nothing behavioural to turn red. (b) The purged-arm probe is listed after A is hard-deleted. With A gone, the prefix fallback already fails closed, so dropping arms 2 and 3 (plant 2) leaves the probe green.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED. (a) Suite 33 #44 says "Functional delete cannot be probed by SQL — storage.protect_delete() blocks direct deletes", and pins delete_own by text only. (b) The suite runs: move F to B, hard-delete A, then "Purge F as postgres: nobody reads". With arms 2 and 3 removed, purged F's key is unclaimed, the prefix fallback asks EXISTS projects(A), A is gone, and nobody reads either way, so the control stays green.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/tests/rls/33_file_lifecycle.sql probe 44 comment; 0042 rabbit_files_select's EXISTS (projects p WHERE p.id = fn_try_uuid(seg2)); design tests row NNN_stable_file_bodies (probe order and plants 2 and 5).
  - *Fix:* (a) Pin delete_own by its qual text ("NOT public.storage_object_claimed"), and evaluate the policy expression directly in a SELECT as the uploader. Cover the functional refusal in the vitest or Playwright layer through the Storage API. (b) Purge F while A still exists, and probe as an A-only reader, with and without the queue row. Run the hard-delete-A positive case separately.
- **SFB-07 [minor]** The INSERT folder rule ("a money-locked folder for a non-money row is refused") is a new refusal on the desktop-to-cloud migration path. The preflight only reports such rows with a NOTICE ("INFERRED zero"). A desktop file the user filed in the INVOICES folder without a money flag, and not named by an expense, migrates today and would fail after this change.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED (depends on desktop data). desktopFolderOfFile returns the desktop folder for any file that has a folder_id. runMigration computes isMoney as f.is_financial === true OR money.receipts.has(id), not from the folder. So an ordinary file in the desktop INVOICES folder is inserted into the cloud INVOICES folder with is_financial false. Admissibility's folder rule refuses it with 42501, and the run reports a failed file with a message the migration does not translate.
  - *Evidence:* src/cloud/migrate/migrationRows.js desktopFolderOfFile and cloudHomeOfFile; runMigration.js isMoney (l.3315) and cloudFolderOfFile; design SB-8 (folder rule) and the Preflight row ("non-money rows sitting in money-locked folders (INFERRED zero)" NOTICE only).
  - *Fix:* Measure it in step 0 on the desktop side as well (bundle files whose folder_id sits under INVOICES, FINANCE or LEGAL without a money flag). Either have runMigration file such a row at the project's top level with a sentence, or treat it as money (put it behind the gate). Do not let the guard's 42501 be the first refusal it meets. Add a migration vitest.
- **SFB-08 [minor]** move_files's detach of a legacy thumbnail (SB-10) inserts a storage_gc_queue row but does not say which reason it uses. The reason CHECK is 0027's closed list ('file-purged', 'orphan-scan', 'avatar-orphan'), and 0054 adds kind and provider CHECKs. The rule against inventing an unlisted term without DROP+ADD is not addressed.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED. If move_files writes reason 'moved' or 'detached', the insert raises 23514 and the whole move aborts (it is not in a swallowing trigger).
  - *Evidence:* 0027_file_lifecycle.sql storage_gc_queue reason CHECK; 0051 storage_gc_queue_provider_chk ('supabase','s3'); 0054 storage_gc_queue_kind_chk; 0091 comment ("storage_gc_queue.reason is 0027's closed list and the drain does not read it: 'file-purged' is the word").
  - *Fix:* State it in the shared-moves contract: reason 'file-purged', kind 'thumbnail', file_id = the file, bucket and provider by 0054's CASE. This also satisfies the file_id IS NOT NULL narrowing in SFB-01 and SFB-02. Otherwise widen the CHECK by DROP CONSTRAINT and ADD, restating the full list, in its own migration step.
- **SFB-09 [minor]** SB-16 has a second effect the design does not record. storage.objects SELECT also governs list(), so an object whose row is trashed disappears from list() for its own project. runMigration's alreadyUploaded and objectAlreadyThere probes then miss it and try to upload into a claimed key. The RLS refusal replaces today's quiet skip.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED. A file that was migrated is then trashed in the cloud, and the desktop's carried record of it is lost (second bundle copy, new computer). On the next run, list(objectDir) no longer shows the object, so the run uploads, the key is claimed and has no current project, and RLS refuses. The file is reported as failed with "storage upload: new row violates row-level security policy". Today the list shows the object, the upload is skipped, and insertOrSkip answers 23505, which counts as skipped.
  - *Evidence:* src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js ordinary-files loop (list then upload, upsert:false, then insertOrSkip) and objectAlreadyThere; design SB-4 and SB-16.
  - *Fix:* Have runMigration ask files (cloudFileRow) before listing, and treat a claimed or invisible key as already there or removed. Record the list() effect in the SB-16 behaviour change and the handbook's §12.4 and §17.
- **SFB-10 [minor]** storage_object_claimed and rabbit_file_key_admissible are granted EXECUTE to authenticated in the exposed public schema, so any member can call them directly through PostgREST. storage_object_claimed is an existence oracle within the workspace, covering trashed and purged files of private projects and personal spaces the caller cannot see. The design rejects B's resolver for being an oracle.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED from the design. A member who knows or guesses a key (project uuid, entity id, millisecond timestamp, name: hard but not impossible, for example from an old link or a log) calls rpc('storage_object_claimed', …) and learns that a hidden private or personal-space file exists or once existed. Her ruling says nothing may reveal the existence of what a person cannot see.
  - *Evidence:* design schema_changes (GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role on both); supabase/tests/rls/35_platform_audit.sql only checks anon on prosecdef functions.
  - *Fix:* Put both helpers in a schema PostgREST does not expose (the storage policies and fn_files_body_guard can still call them, with USAGE granted). Otherwise have storage_object_claimed answer only for keys whose seg2 project passes projects_select or the caller's own files rows. Add a probe that calls each through the API surface as a member and is refused.
- **SFB-11 [minor]** The CI allowlist probe that replaces B's GUC ("prosecdef functions whose comment-stripped body assigns files.project_id are exactly {move_files}") does not define what "assigns" means. A text scan either catches many existing definers (project_id = … appears in every WHERE clause) or misses dynamic SQL such as soft_delete_row's format('UPDATE public.%I …'). The guard's server-role exemption therefore has no control that bites.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED. A future SECURITY DEFINER function does EXECUTE format('UPDATE public.files SET %I = $1 …', col). The naive scan misses it, the guard skips it as postgres, and project_id becomes writable again with the probe still green.
  - *Evidence:* design SB-7 rationale ("Future definers are caught instead by a CI pgTAP allowlist probe") and the tests row; 0014 soft_delete_row's dynamic UPDATE (core fact (a) trash).
  - *Fix:* Define the probe precisely, for example any prosecdef function in public whose stripped body matches '(UPDATE\s+(public\.)?files\b|format\([^)]*UPDATE)', compared against an explicit name list, with a planted definer in the suite that turns it red. Alternatively, have the guard also accept a transaction-local marker set only by move_files and rekey_file_body, so that unknown definers fail closed.

## 2. Personal space and audiences

**Area as the designer framed it:** The personal space, who sees what, and the Moth read path (MO1 design round)

### Summary

Each person's personal space is an ordinary projects row with one new column, personal_owner_id. A CHECK ties that column to is_private = true and created_by = personal_owner_id, so every existing privacy mechanism (projects_select 0072, passes_project_privacy 0082, the live-parent hops, the 0101 workspace-broadcast skip) already gives owner-plus-admins visibility with no policy restated. A partial unique index on (workspace_id, personal_owner_id) and a SECURITY DEFINER ensure_personal_space() (INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING, then SELECT) give exactly one space per person per workspace, race-safe, for any active member, without giving anyone a general right to create projects. Direct inserts that name personal_owner_id are refused: projects_insert is restated from 0013 with one arm added. A new BEFORE UPDATE guard makes the owner immutable, refuses binning a personal space, pins created_by on every project, and lets only the creator or an admin flip is_private. That last part closes a hole I measured: any project writer can make a shared project private and hide it. Media goes to the company's chosen storage because projectIsPrivate in supabaseAdapter stops treating a personal row as 'local disk'. The space stays out of every ordinary list through one filter at listProjects (both adapters) plus a workspace-channel client rule. The Files view gets its own listPersonalSpaces, labelled for the owner and for admins. RLS cannot tell Moth from Files, so Moth reads only through SECURITY INVOKER RPCs (moth_islands, moth_project_folders, moth_project_files). These keep RLS as the floor and narrow every row through one SECURITY DEFINER helper, moth_can_read_target(kind,id). The helper resolves the actual row from the table that kind names and decides only from that row: workspace, live membership, live project, the privacy arm, the personal arm owner-only with no admin leg, and money excluded by classifier for every role. Classifier means file_row_is_money (which includes LEGAL), files filed under a locked folder, locked-folder rows by path, and receipts that expenses.file_ids names, which catches the pre-0076 and customer-bucket residuals. Its write twin moth_can_write_target is mirrored in projectRoleMatrix.js as a new canOnProject action, project.moth.write. Building it on canOnProject keeps it under writeGate.test.js's ready scan. The server supplies the per-project seat inputs through moth_islands, and one MATRIX table in the pgTAP suite is read by both pgTAP and vitest. Moth's last view, appearance, recent folders and first visit live in a bounded user_settings.moth_prefs map keyed by workspace. It is written only through set_moth_prefs, reset when the person's membership row is new, and every stored id is re-checked through the helper on read. Three prerequisites must be committed before the provisioning RPC exists anywhere: the workspace-channel privacy skip (A-R1-04), the task/asset/child-table parent hops (A-R2-03), and the edit_history privacy arm. Without them the new personal row would be broadcast to every member at creation, an orphaned space could be written blind, and managers could read its file history.

### Decisions

- **PS-1** The personal space is a projects row with personal_owner_id uuid (no FK), is_private = true and created_by = personal_owner_id, enforced by CHECK projects_personal_shape_chk. It is not a new table.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: files.project_id is NOT NULL FK projects, and every storage policy, signer, quota, GC and teardown keys on projects/<id>/ (fact 4). A new container would need all of them re-taught. With is_private = true, projects_select (0072), passes_project_privacy (0082), the 0014 live-parent hops, the realtime topic gate (0016, INVOKER over projects_select) and fn_task_items_ws_broadcast (0101: NOT p.is_private) all treat the space as owner-plus-admins without restating a policy. The CHECK makes created_by equal the owner, so the existing arm `created_by = auth.uid()` means 'the owner'. personal_owner_id has no FK, following the 0007/0020 precedent for person ids, so the space survives deactivation and account deletion for the admins' Files reach ('when someone leaves').
  - *Rejected:* (a) A personal_spaces table with its own files: re-keys storage, GC and teardown, the widest change possible. (b) is_private = false with a new personal arm in projects_select and passes_project_privacy: restates two guarded bodies and misses every existing `NOT is_private` suppression. (c) FK (workspace_id, personal_owner_id) to workspace_members: CASCADE deletes the space and its rows when someone leaves, RESTRICT blocks account deletion, and SET NULL silently turns it into an unlabelled private project.
- **PS-2** Provisioning is public.ensure_personal_space() RETURNS uuid, SECURITY DEFINER with search_path pinned, EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC and anon and granted to authenticated. It refuses (42501) without auth.uid(), current_workspace_id() or has_active_membership. It returns the existing row's id if one exists. Otherwise it INSERTs (workspace_id, title 'Personal space', is_private true, personal_owner_id = created_by = auth.uid()) ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, personal_owner_id) WHERE personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING RETURNING id, and re-SELECTs if the insert lost the race. It creates no folder rows: folder_id NULL is the space's root, so SQL never needs fileSlugify, whose two copies are pinned by folderParity.test.js.
  - *Why:* Ordinary members cannot insert projects (projects_insert 0013: admin|manager, MEASURED; suite 18 probes 20-23 pin it). A narrow RPC gives exactly one row without widening project creation. Under READ COMMITTED, the second of two concurrent calls waits on the unique index, does nothing, and its next statement sees the winner. fn_projects_auto_staff (0020) seats the caller as project manager because auth.uid() is the caller even inside DEFINER, so can_write_project works for the owner through the seat.
  - *Rejected:* Widening projects_insert to `app_role user AND personal_owner_id = auth.uid()`: puts creation logic in a permissive policy, ORs with the admin|manager arm, and lets a client choose title, status and folder_root. A client-side 'check then create': not race-safe.
- **PS-3** New BEFORE UPDATE trigger trg_projects_identity_guard runs fn_projects_identity_guard() (a new function; it restates no guard). The service role passes. For everyone else: NEW.created_by := OLD.created_by, a silent pin on every project (the 0086/0098 shape); personal_owner_id IS DISTINCT FROM OLD raises 42501; on a personal row, any change to deleted_at raises 42501 ('a personal space cannot be put in the bin; its files can'); and a change to is_private raises 42501 unless auth.uid() = OLD.created_by or current_app_role() = 'admin'. projects_insert is restated from 0013 verbatim plus `AND personal_owner_id IS NULL`.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: fn_audit_touch (0001) fills created_by only when it is NULL on INSERT and never protects it on UPDATE. No trigger pins projects.created_by (grep shows pins only on shot_lists/edits 0086 and table_views 0098). projects_update (0013) lets any can_write_project caller (a seated member included) UPDATE with WITH CHECK on workspace only. PROJECT_COLUMNS includes is_private and created_by. So a member can set is_private = true and created_by = self and hide a shared project from its team. 0072's header line 'no client can name somebody else as the owner' is wrong for UPDATE. Without the pin, the personal CHECK alone would only stop the change on personal rows. The pin is silent so whole-row re-sends (updateProject carries created_by unchanged) keep working. The unique index includes binned rows, so binning a space would strand the owner (ensure_personal_space would find a hidden row); hence no binning. Hard delete stays admin-only as today (projects_delete 0013): it frees the slot, and the owner gets a fresh space on the next visit.
  - *Rejected:* Pinning only personal rows: leaves the measured hijack open. Refusing (rather than silently reverting) created_by changes: breaks every whole-row re-send. Restating projects_update's WITH CHECK: a permissive WITH CHECK cannot compare OLD and NEW.
- **PS-4** Seats on a personal space: new BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger on project_members, fn_project_members_personal_guard(). On a personal project it refuses any seat except (personal_owner_id, 'manager'), with 42501. Seat DELETE is not guarded.
  - *Why:* The owner holds can_manage_project_roster through the auto-staffed manager seat. Without the guard they could seat a colleague, and the seat row itself is broadcast by the 0018 project_members arm. A deleted seat (the account-deletion CASCADE) leaves the space unstaffed. After the A-R2-03 hops, unstaffed writes are bounded by the projects_select audience (owner plus admins), so that is harmless.
  - *Rejected:* Guarding DELETE: cannot tell a CASCADE from a client delete, and would block account deletion.
- **PS-5** Deactivation and removal. Deactivated (workspace_members.is_active false): the owner reads nothing (has_active_membership is live) and ensure_personal_space refuses. Admins keep Files reach, and the Files label adds '(inactive)'. Reactivation returns the same space (same unique key). Account deletion: the membership and the seat cascade away, the projects row and its files remain, and Files labels it 'former member'. Nobody sees it in Moth. Workspace teardown removes it with the workspace CASCADE, as today.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: admin-set-active only flips is_active plus a ban; no client path deletes workspace_members (grep). The FK project_members_member_fkey cascades seats (0013). Her ruling says admins reach the space 'when someone leaves', so it must outlive the person.
  - *Rejected:* Auto-moving a departed person's files into a project: a write nobody chose. Deleting the space with the membership: loses work the ruling keeps reachable.
- **PS-6** Media routing: supabaseAdapter's projectIsPrivate becomes 'does this project keep media on this computer', selecting is_private and personal_owner_id and answering is_private AND personal_owner_id IS NULL. A personal space therefore takes the workspace-choice branch: petal goes to Supabase, s3 to the customer bucket, money stays pinned to Supabase by fileProviderFor, and a network workspace is refused in the cloud with the existing sentence, like every project there. personal_owner_id is probed once per session (the privateProjectsAvailable pattern) so a client ahead of the database keeps working.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: uploadFile sends every non-money upload of an is_private project to FILE_PROVIDERS.LOCAL_SERVER on whichever desktop uploads. That contradicts her ruling 'company's chosen storage', and an admin's upload would land on the admin's disk. Changing the one predicate keeps the money pin and the network refusal untouched.
  - *Rejected:* A separate personal branch in uploadFile: two decisions where one exists. Leaving personal media on local disk: contradicts the ruling, and a move into a project would then relocate bodies.
- **PS-7** Kept out of ordinary lists by one filter at the source. supabaseAdapter.listProjects adds .is('personal_owner_id', null) when the column is present. localServerAdapter's GET /api/rabbit/projects (main.cjs) skips bundles with project.personal. googleDriveAdapter is unchanged (it has no personal rows). RabbitProvider.handleWorkspaceEvent never merges a projects event whose row (NEW or OLD) carries personal_owner_id or is_private into projectsIndex. Instead it calls scheduleProjectsIndexRefetch() so RLS decides, and removes the id if present. Every projectsIndex consumer inherits this: ProjectsPage, ProjectContextBar, ProjectSummaryView, DeckOutlineGenerator, TeamMembersPage, DashboardTasksView, ConnectionDot, ProjectFilesExplorer, and runAttachmentMigration through listProjects.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: 12 consumers read projectsIndex or listProjects (grep), so filtering one source is smaller than filtering twelve readers. handleWorkspaceEvent merges any projects row with no privacy check (fact client 11), and the owner's own RLS returns their space, so listProjects must filter even after the server fix.
  - *Rejected:* Filtering in each consumer: twelve places to forget. Relying only on the server broadcast fix: the owner's and admins' own listProjects still return the row.
- **PS-8** The Files view shows personal spaces through a separate adapter.listPersonalSpaces(): projects where personal_owner_id is not null, read under RLS, so the owner sees their own and admins see all. ProjectFilesExplorer adds them to its picker: 'My personal space' for the owner; for admins, 'Personal space · <display name>' from useWorkspaceMembers, with '(inactive)' or '(former member)'. Folders and files load through the existing listFolders and listFiles, unchanged. Write controls on another person's space are read-only in this view, behind GatedAction with a reason. The owner's own space follows its seat.
  - *Why:* Her ruling: owner plus workspace admins in Files only. projects_select already admits admins to private rows (0072), and children follow through the hop, so Files needs no new server read. MEASURED: the explorer is 'read-only on purpose' and fails closed for projects that are not open.
  - *Rejected:* An admin-only metadata RPC, like otter_course_index: the files themselves must open for admins, which needs RLS reach anyway.
- **MR-1** Moth reads only through SECURITY INVOKER RPCs: moth_islands(), moth_project_folders(p_project), and moth_project_files(p_project, p_after uuid, p_limit int clamped to 1..1000, keyset on id). Each selects from the base table under the caller's RLS, so the result is never wider than what Files shows, and keeps a row only if moth_can_read_target(kind, id) is true. A hidden or missing project gives an empty result, never an error, and nothing returns a count or total. A vitest scan forbids src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/** from calling .from('projects'|'files'|'folders') or listPersonalSpaces.
  - *Why:* RLS cannot know which page asked, and admins must keep RLS reach for Files. 'Never another person's space in Moth, even for an admin' is therefore a read-path rule. An INVOKER RPC can only narrow. Even a Moth bug that read the base tables would show an admin no more than Files already does: a product-boundary slip, not an escalation.
  - *Rejected:* DEFINER catalogue RPCs: they bypass RLS, so the audience would have to be restated perfectly and the floor is lost. Removing admins from projects_select: breaks Files, storage signing and the 'when someone leaves' ruling.
- **MR-2** One eligibility definition. public._moth_target_eligible(p_kind text, p_id uuid, p_allow_trashed boolean) is SECURITY DEFINER with EXECUTE revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated; only definers call it, such as a future Moth trash index. public.moth_can_read_target(p_kind, p_id) = _moth_target_eligible(p_kind, p_id, false) and is granted to authenticated. Session 1 kinds are 'project', 'folder' and 'file'. Any other kind, a NULL id, or no auth, workspace or active membership returns false. Each later session adds its kind (note, board, task, asset) by restating the latest body with suite probes.
  - *Why:* Policies on later Moth tables must call it (EXECUTE is checked as the caller), and DEFINER avoids RLS recursion. A false answer is the same for missing and hidden, so granting it exposes no oracle. Kind is never authority: it only picks the table to look in, and a wrong kind finds no row, so the answer is false.
  - *Rejected:* A kind-free lookup across tables (UNION by id): slower, and ambiguous if ids ever collide. Trusting the stored kind/id pair: the rule forbids it.
- **MR-3** What the helper checks, from the row only. Folder: folders.id = p_id, workspace_id = current_workspace_id(), NOT rabbit_money_segment(split_part(path,'/',1)), then its project. File: files.id = p_id, workspace match, deleted_at IS NULL unless p_allow_trashed, NOT file_row_is_money(is_financial, storage_path) (which includes LEGAL), NOT 'legal' = ANY(tags), NOT EXISTS a folders row with id = f.folder_id whose first path segment is a money segment, and NOT EXISTS expenses e with e.workspace_id = f.workspace_id AND e.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id]. Then for the project: projects.id, workspace match, deleted_at IS NULL, passes_project_privacy(p.id), AND (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id = auth.uid()). The personal arm has no admin leg.
  - *Why:* Her ruling excludes money for every role, so the classifiers are used and never the access gates (can_access_project_money and _legal admit admins and managers, and S4b-05's manager seat is accepted). MEASURED: file_row_is_money = flag OR third segment in INVOICES|FINANCE|LEGAL (0088). This catches pre-0076 residual 1 (flagged row, blob under an ordinary segment). The expenses arm catches residual 2 and M2-L4 (unflagged receipts on a customer bucket or brought over by migration), and 0076 defines a receipt exactly as 'id in some expenses.file_ids', the only file_ids column. The folder arms cover the canonical locked folders and every descendant (folder paths are relative, and FOLDER_CATEGORIES has INVOICES at the top level). passes_project_privacy keeps unreadable private projects out. The expenses read needs DEFINER, because members cannot see expenses through RLS.
  - *Rejected:* Using can_access_project_money/legal: would show money to admins and managers in Moth. Using the path segment alone: misses residuals 1 and 2. Re-listing folder names in SQL: rabbit_money_segment is the one definition.
- **MR-4** Write twin: public.moth_can_write_target(p_kind, p_id) = moth_can_read_target(p_kind, p_id) AND, on the resolved project, (CASE WHEN personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL THEN personal_owner_id = auth.uid() ELSE can_write_project(project_id) END). SECURITY DEFINER, granted to authenticated.
  - *Why:* Shared projects keep the existing project.entity.write rule (admin and manager app roles, the unstaffed opening, seat manager or member; reviewers out), so Moth and Rabbit agree. The personal space is owner-only whatever the seat or app role.
  - *Rejected:* Reusing can_write_project on personal rows: it admits admins and managers by app role.
- **MR-5** moth_islands() returns TABLE(id, title, is_personal, is_private, my_project_role, is_staffed, can_write, updated_at). my_project_role and is_staffed come from project_role_for() and project_is_staffed(), and can_write from moth_can_write_target('project', id). moth_project_files returns metadata only: id, project_id, folder_id (NULL when the folder belongs to another project), name, mime_type, size_bytes, kind, document_kind, description, tags, storage_provider, storage_path, thumbnail_url, duration_sec, source_modified_at, uploaded_at, updated_at. Post-conditions pin both return types.
  - *Why:* The client holds a seat only for the open project (MEASURED, ctx.myProjectRole). A cross-project graph needs every project's seat inputs, and these come from the server, never from caller-supplied pairs. storage_provider and path are already readable to this caller through files_select and are what FilePreviewDialog needs (E20). A folder_id pointing into another project is possible today (fact 12, no same-project check), so it is cleared.
  - *Rejected:* Loading every project's project_members on the client: a second roster source and wider reads.
- **CL-1** The client predicate is a new PROJECT_ACTIONS entry, 'project.moth.write', in src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js. canOnProject decides it after the ready === false check and before the admin/manager short-circuit: if ctx.isPersonal, return ctx.isPersonalOwner === true; otherwise follow the project.entity.write rule. projectActionDeniedReason gains its sentences: a reviewer may look and comment but not move, rename or delete here; no seat; and 'only the owner of a personal space can change it'. Moth builds ctx inline from an island row and usePermissions: { appRole, projectRole: island.my_project_role, isStaffed: island.is_staffed, isPersonal: island.is_personal, isPersonalOwner: island.is_personal, ready, noRoles }. Every denied control is shown greyed through GatedAction with that reason. roleMatrix.js is unchanged: opening Moth and getting a personal space are open to every role.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: writeGate.test.js scans canOnProject calls only, for `ready`. Building on canOnProject puts Moth under the guard for free and keeps the 'grey never means loading' rule. The same file already carries the lockstep invariant (SYSTEMS_HANDBOOK 'LOCKSTEP INVARIANT'), so the migration's COMMENT names it.
  - *Rejected:* A separate mothMatrix.js: escapes the ready scan unless the scan is widened, and adds a second lockstep file. Using entity.write as is: it admits admins to other people's personal spaces.
- **CL-2** Lockstep is pinned by one table. The new pgTAP suite carries `-- MATRIX project.moth.write | appRole | projectRole | staffed | personal | owner | expected` lines, and executes each against moth_can_write_target with fixture users. projectRoleMatrix.test.js reads the same suite file, parses the MATRIX lines and asserts canOnProject agrees on every row. A client read-eligibility mirror, mothFileHidden(row, { expenseFileIds, localMoneyDirs }) in src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/mothEligibility.js, reuses MONEY_SEGMENTS and isMoneyKey from storage/index.js and adds the desktop money directories ({slug}_RECEIPTS&INVOICES, {slug}_CREWINVOICES, {slug}_TALENTINVOICES) and bundle.expenses[].file_ids. It is what decides on the Local Server and is defence in depth in the cloud. A vitest reads the helper's migration text (comments stripped) and asserts the same arms are present.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: no test today compares SQL with JS (fact 13). One table read by both executors makes drift fail in both suites. On the signed-out desktop there is no RLS, and residual 3 (pre-C4 local receipts with no flag) is caught only by the expenses file_ids arm.
  - *Rejected:* Two hand-kept EXPECTED tables: the drift class the invariant exists to stop.
- **US-1** user_settings gains moth_prefs jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}' with CHECK (jsonb_typeof = 'object' AND octet_length(moth_prefs::text) <= 32768). Shape: { '<workspace uuid>': { member_since: '<workspace_members.created_at>', at: ms, first_visit_at: ms, last_view: { id: '<moth_views uuid>' | 'quick:tags' | 'quick:folders', at: ms }, appearance: { effects: 'auto'|'full'|'light', labels: 'always'|'hover' }, recent_folders: [{ id: uuid, at: ms }] (at most 8) } }, at most 10 workspaces kept by newest `at`. The only writer is set_moth_prefs(p_patch jsonb) RETURNS jsonb, SECURITY INVOKER (the set_table_view_last shape, 0098). It requires has_active_membership(current_workspace_id()), accepts only the keys last_view, appearance, push_recent_folder (one id) and first_visit, refuses anything else with 22023, writes only the current workspace's entry, and replaces that entry wholesale when the stored member_since differs from the live membership's created_at. The reader is moth_prefs() RETURNS jsonb, SECURITY INVOKER STABLE. It returns '{}' when member_since differs; otherwise it returns the entry with recent_folders filtered through moth_can_read_target('folder', id) and last_view kept only if it is a quick token or a moth_views row the caller can SELECT. fetchCloudSettings and saveCloudSettings are unchanged. On the signed-out desktop the same map lives in rabbit-machine-settings.json under 'moth', never in localStorage.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: user_settings has no workspace column; it is one own-row per person (0046), and 0098 set the bounded-map pattern. member_since tells a re-created membership from a reactivation (same row), so reuse after leaving and coming back starts fresh. Re-checking on read means a folder that later went private, into money, or into someone else's personal space is never named back.
  - *Rejected:* A per-workspace prefs table: one more suite and RLS_TABLES line for four small values. Re-checking on write only: authorisation changes after the write.
- **DT-1** Desktop-only company (Local Server adapter, signed out, noRoles): one local personal space per data root. It is a bundle whose project.personal = true, created by a new route, POST /api/rabbit/personal-space, which checks and creates in one synchronous handler with no await between, so it is race-free in the single main process (MEASURED: app.requestSingleInstanceLock in main.cjs). GET /api/rabbit/personal-space returns it. Its bodies live where every local project's do (resolveProjectFilesDir), which is the company's chosen storage there. POST and PATCH /api/rabbit/projects strip personal and personal_owner_id from the body, and GET /api/rabbit/projects skips it. runMigration does not carry a personal bundle as a cloud project in Session 1; it gives a plain sentence instead (recorded in OUTSTANDING). Moth there applies mothFileHidden itself.
  - *Why:* The ruling is one space per person, and a signed-out desktop is one person. POST /api/rabbit/projects spreads req.body (MEASURED), so the flag must be stripped there or any create could mint a space.
  - *Rejected:* No personal space on desktop-only companies: breaks 12a.1's 'desktop-only projects appear in Moth' for the person's own work. Mapping a local personal bundle onto the cloud space in the migration: keys embed the project id, so it belongs to the storage-identity work.

### Schema changes

- **public.projects.personal_owner_id + projects_personal_shape_chk + projects_personal_owner_uniq** (restates from: new): ALTER TABLE public.projects ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS personal_owner_id uuid; -- no FK, person ids are plain uuids (0007/0020)
ALTER TABLE public.projects ADD CONSTRAINT projects_personal_shape_chk CHECK (personal_owner_id IS NULL OR (is_private AND created_by = personal_owner_id));
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS projects_personal_owner_uniq ON public.projects (workspace_id, personal_owner_id) WHERE personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL;
COMMENT ON COLUMN … naming ensure_personal_space and 'owner-only in Moth, owner plus workspace admins in Files'. Post-conditions: the column exists; the CHECK definition (pg_get_constraintdef) contains both arms; the index is unique and partial; zero rows violate.
- **public.fn_projects_identity_guard() + trg_projects_identity_guard (BEFORE UPDATE ON projects)** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public. IF current_setting('role', true) = 'service_role' THEN RETURN NEW; END IF; NEW.created_by := OLD.created_by; IF NEW.personal_owner_id IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.personal_owner_id THEN RAISE ... USING ERRCODE='42501'; IF OLD.personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL AND NEW.deleted_at IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.deleted_at THEN RAISE 42501 ('a personal space cannot be put in the bin; its files can'); IF NEW.is_private IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.is_private AND NOT (auth.uid() = OLD.created_by OR public.current_app_role() = 'admin') THEN RAISE 42501; RETURN NEW. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Post-condition (body with comments stripped) contains 'NEW.created_by := OLD.created_by' and the three refusals. Note: soft_delete_row is DEFINER, so its UPDATE fires this trigger and the binning refusal applies on the RPC path too.
- **policy projects_insert** (restates from: 0013_project_members.sql): DROP and CREATE, restating 0013 verbatim: workspace_id = current_workspace_id() AND has_active_membership(workspace_id) AND current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager') — plus AND personal_owner_id IS NULL. The post-condition compares the qual with comments stripped: all three 0013 arms present, and the new arm present.
- **public.fn_project_members_personal_guard() + trg_project_members_personal_guard (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON project_members)** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, search_path pinned, REVOKEd from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated. SELECT personal_owner_id FROM projects WHERE id = NEW.project_id; if it is not NULL and (NEW.user_id <> it OR NEW.project_role <> 'manager'), RAISE 42501 ('a personal space has one seat, its owner's'). It does not fire on DELETE.
- **public.ensure_personal_space() RETURNS uuid** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE plpgsql VOLATILE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public.
v_ws := current_workspace_id(); v_uid := auth.uid();
IF v_uid IS NULL OR v_ws IS NULL OR NOT has_active_membership(v_ws) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION ... USING ERRCODE='42501';
SELECT id INTO v_id FROM projects WHERE workspace_id=v_ws AND personal_owner_id=v_uid; IF FOUND THEN RETURN v_id;
INSERT INTO projects (workspace_id, title, is_private, personal_owner_id, created_by) VALUES (v_ws,'Personal space',true,v_uid,v_uid) ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, personal_owner_id) WHERE personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL DO NOTHING RETURNING id INTO v_id;
IF v_id IS NULL THEN SELECT id INTO v_id FROM projects WHERE workspace_id=v_ws AND personal_owner_id=v_uid; END IF; RETURN v_id.
REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated. auto-staff (0020) seats the caller as manager. Post-conditions: prosecdef; proconfig carries search_path; no anon or PUBLIC EXECUTE (35_platform_audit also checks).
- **expenses_file_ids_gin** (restates from: new): CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS expenses_file_ids_gin ON public.expenses USING gin (file_ids). It serves the helper's `e.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id]`. MEASURED: 0037 creates only expenses_project_idx, and 0076 notes there is no GIN index.
- **public._moth_target_eligible(p_kind text, p_id uuid, p_allow_trashed boolean) / public.moth_can_read_target(p_kind text, p_id uuid) / public.moth_can_write_target(p_kind text, p_id uuid)** (restates from: new): All three are LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public. _moth_target_eligible: return false if p_id IS NULL, auth.uid() IS NULL, current_workspace_id() IS NULL, or NOT has_active_membership(v_ws). CASE p_kind: 'project' sets v_project := p_id. 'folder' selects project_id from folders WHERE id=p_id AND workspace_id=v_ws AND NOT rabbit_money_segment(split_part(path,'/',1)). 'file' selects project_id from files f WHERE id=p_id AND workspace_id=v_ws AND (p_allow_trashed OR deleted_at IS NULL) AND NOT file_row_is_money(f.is_financial,f.storage_path) AND NOT ('legal' = ANY(f.tags)) AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM folders fo WHERE fo.id=f.folder_id AND rabbit_money_segment(split_part(fo.path,'/',1))) AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM expenses e WHERE e.workspace_id=f.workspace_id AND e.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id]). ELSE return false. Then return EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id=v_project AND p.workspace_id=v_ws AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND passes_project_privacy(p.id) AND (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id = auth.uid())). REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated.
moth_can_read_target = _moth_target_eligible(p_kind,p_id,false); GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, REVOKE from PUBLIC and anon.
moth_can_write_target = moth_can_read_target(...) AND (the resolved project's personal_owner_id = auth.uid() if personal, ELSE can_write_project(project)); same grants. COMMENT ON FUNCTION names src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js 'project.moth.write' (lockstep).
Post-conditions on pg_get_functiondef with comments stripped: the eligible body contains file_row_is_money, rabbit_money_segment(split_part(, passes_project_privacy, e.file_ids @>, and 'personal_owner_id = auth.uid()'; the personal arm contains no current_app_role; no anon EXECUTE on any of the three; authenticated has no EXECUTE on _moth_target_eligible.
- **public.moth_islands() / public.moth_project_folders(p_project uuid) / public.moth_project_files(p_project uuid, p_after uuid DEFAULT NULL, p_limit int DEFAULT 500)** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY INVOKER SET search_path = public. moth_islands: SELECT p.id, p.title, p.personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL, p.is_private, project_role_for(p.id), project_is_staffed(p.id), moth_can_write_target('project',p.id), p.updated_at FROM projects p WHERE moth_can_read_target('project', p.id). moth_project_folders: folders fo WHERE fo.project_id = p_project AND moth_can_read_target('project',p_project) AND moth_can_read_target('folder',fo.id), returning id, parent_id, kind, entity_type, slug, label, path, sort_order, updated_at. moth_project_files: files f WHERE f.project_id=p_project AND (p_after IS NULL OR f.id > p_after) AND moth_can_read_target('file', f.id) ORDER BY f.id LIMIT least(greatest(p_limit,1),1000), returning the MR-5 columns, with folder_id NULLed when the folder's project_id <> f.project_id. No count or total anywhere. REVOKE from PUBLIC and anon; GRANT to authenticated. Post-conditions pin pg_get_function_result for all three (metadata only: no is_financial, no created_by).
- **public.user_settings.moth_prefs + user_settings_moth_prefs_chk + set_moth_prefs(jsonb) + moth_prefs()** (restates from: new (column on the 0046 table; 0098 set_table_view_last is the shape)): ALTER TABLE user_settings ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS moth_prefs jsonb NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb; a guarded DO block adds CHECK (jsonb_typeof(moth_prefs)='object' AND octet_length(moth_prefs::text) <= 32768). set_moth_prefs(p_patch jsonb) RETURNS jsonb, SECURITY INVOKER, search_path pinned: validates keys ⊆ {last_view, appearance, push_recent_folder, first_visit} and value shapes (uuid regex, quick:tags|quick:folders, appearance enums), else 22023. Reads v_since := workspace_members.created_at for (current_workspace_id(), auth.uid()) where is_active, else 42501. Then INSERT … ON CONFLICT (user_id) DO UPDATE merging only the current workspace's entry: the entry is replaced when member_since differs, recent_folders are de-duplicated and capped at 8 newest, at most 10 workspace entries are kept by `at`, and the server clock stamps `at`. Returns the entry. moth_prefs() RETURNS jsonb, SECURITY INVOKER STABLE: the own row's entry for current_workspace_id(); '{}' if member_since differs; recent_folders filtered by moth_can_read_target('folder',id); last_view kept only if it is a quick token or EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM moth_views v WHERE v.id = …) under RLS (that clause lands once moth_views exists in the same session). Both REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon, GRANTed to authenticated and service_role. The 0046 own-row policies are unchanged.
- **public.fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast() (A-R1-04 prerequisite; shape required by this area)** (restates from: 0018_workspace_channel.sql): Restate 0018's whole body (the latest: 0082, 0083 and 0101 name it and leave it alone, MEASURED) and add one arm after the existing filters. v_project := CASE TG_TABLE_NAME WHEN 'projects' THEN (v_row->>'id')::uuid WHEN 'workspace_members' THEN NULL ELSE (v_row->>'project_id')::uuid END. IF TG_TABLE_NAME='projects' AND (COALESCE((to_jsonb(NEW)->>'is_private')::boolean,false) OR COALESCE((to_jsonb(OLD)->>'is_private')::boolean,false)) THEN RETURN NULL. IF v_project IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id=v_project AND p.is_private) THEN RETURN NULL. Personal rows are is_private by CHECK and never flip (guard), so they never reach the workspace topic. What a public-to-private flip sends to the people losing sight of the project stays the realtime owner's and Audrey's call (OUTSTANDING A-R1-04). The post-condition restates 0018's arms word for word.
- **policy edit_history_select (S4d-02 prerequisite for personal spaces)** (restates from: 0092_legal_gate_managers.sql): DROP and CREATE, restating 0092's body whole and adding: AND (project_id IS NULL OR public.passes_project_privacy(project_id)). fn_edit_history_money_snapshot (0092) already sets project_id for files rows, which are the personal space's content. Without this, a workspace manager reads a personal space's file names and full-row diffs. The post-condition pins every 0092 arm plus the new one; re-run order after 0092 is added to handbook §4.9.
- **tasks/assets/folders/scenes/shots/levels/experiences/comments/project_members insert+update WITH CHECK, and files_delete (A-R2-03 and C-R2-01 prerequisite; owned by the privacy-repair migration)** (restates from: 0013_project_members.sql (tasks/assets/comments/project_members), 0040 (scenes/shots/levels/experiences), 0041 (folders), 0092 (files_delete)): Each latest policy is restated with AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = <t>.project_id), in the 0082 §3b milestones shape. Named here only as a hard ordering dependency. An orphaned personal space (seat cascaded on account deletion) is unstaffed, so can_write_project opens it to every member, and only this hop bounds blind writes to the projects_select audience.

### Consumers to change

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js projectIsPrivate**: Select is_private and personal_owner_id (when personalSpacesAvailable says so). Return true only for a private project that is not personal, so personal uploads take the workspace-choice branch of uploadFile. Rename the doc comment to 'keeps media on this computer'.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js listProjects**: Add .is('personal_owner_id', null) when personalSpacesAvailable(client) is true (a new probe in the privateProjectsAvailable shape).
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/localServerAdapter.js listProjects / electron/main.cjs GET /api/rabbit/projects**: Skip bundles whose project.personal is true.
- **electron/main.cjs POST /api/rabbit/projects and PATCH /api/rabbit/projects/:id**: Strip personal and personal_owner_id from req.body before the spread (today the create spreads req.body, MEASURED).
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx handleWorkspaceEvent**: For table 'projects': if NEW or OLD carries personal_owner_id, or is_private is true on either, never merge. Delete the id from projectsIndex if present and call scheduleProjectsIndexRefetch(). Listeners still get the event.
- **src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx ProjectFilesExplorer (project list and Select options)**: Load adapter.listPersonalSpaces() once per mount (showPicker only). Add the entries labelled 'My personal space' or 'Personal space · <name>' (useWorkspaceMembers; '(inactive)', '(former member)'). Make another person's space read-only behind GatedAction with a reason. Leave the R.A.B.B.I.T. Files tab (showPicker=false) unchanged.
- **src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js PROJECT_ACTIONS / canOnProject / projectActionDeniedReason**: Add 'project.moth.write'. Decide it after ready and before the admin/manager short-circuit (personal: isPersonalOwner === true; otherwise the entity.write rule). Add its denied-reason sentences.
- **src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js (project step)**: A bundle with project.personal is not carried as a cloud project in Session 1. The report gives a plain sentence, recorded in OUTSTANDING.
- **src/dev/fixtures/rabbitFixturesAdapter.js + src/dev/fixtures/data/project.js**: Personal spaces for the fixture admin Mara Okonkwo and the member Priya Raman. The new adapter methods are mirrored; listProjects excludes them; ?fixtures=member shows only the member's own.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/googleDriveAdapter.js**: ensurePersonalSpace throws its readOnly() sentence, and listPersonalSpaces returns [], so a Drive company gets no personal-space write path (rule: Drive has no write path).

### Client changes

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js ensurePersonalSpace()**: rpc('ensure_personal_space') returns the id. A missing function (42883) says 'this workspace's database does not have personal spaces yet'. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry, on first entry (acceptance 1))*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js listPersonalSpaces()**: from('projects').select('id, title, personal_owner_id, updated_at').not('personal_owner_id','is',null), read under RLS (owner sees own, admins see all). *(production caller: src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx)*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js listMothIslands() / listMothFolders(projectId) / listMothFiles(projectId, after, limit)**: rpc moth_islands / moth_project_folders / moth_project_files; pages are followed up to a Session 1 cap with no hidden totals. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry (the island list and its personal-space panel))*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js getMothPrefs() / setMothPrefs(patch)**: rpc moth_prefs / set_moth_prefs. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry (first visit opens 'What do you want to map?'; the last view reopens; recent folders))*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/localServerAdapter.js ensurePersonalSpace() / listPersonalSpaces() / listMothIslands() and friends / getMothPrefs() / setMothPrefs()**: The new routes POST and GET /api/rabbit/personal-space in electron/main.cjs. The islands come from the existing project list plus the personal bundle. Prefs go to rabbit-machine-settings.json under 'moth'. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry on the signed-out desktop; ProjectFilesExplorer)*
- **src/tools/moth_v0.1.0/mothEligibility.js mothFileHidden(row, { expenseFileIds, localMoneyDirs })**: The client mirror of the helper's file arms: is_financial, isMoneyKey (third segment), the folder path's first segment against MONEY_SEGMENTS, the 'legal' tag, the expenses file_ids set, and the local money directory names. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry: it filters every file before drawing it (the only filter on the Local Server; defence in depth in the cloud))*
- **src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js 'project.moth.write' (via canOnProject / projectActionDeniedReason)**: See CL-1. The ctx is inlined at each call site so writeGate.test.js's contextTextFor resolves `ready`. *(production caller: src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 Moth entry: GatedAction around 'Add files' (personal space), 'Delete' and 'Move to' (acceptance 6))*
- **src/permissions/GatedAction.jsx (unchanged)**: Used as is. Denied controls stay visible with the reason and the onClick is inert. *(production caller: Moth entry and ProjectFilesExplorer (another person's personal space))*

### Migration order

- **P0 (prerequisite, other owners): privacy repairs**: A-R2-03 and C-R2-01 parent hops on the child write policies and files_delete; A-R1-04's privacy skip in fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast (restating 0018); edit_history_select's privacy arm (restating 0092).
  - *Constraints:* Must be COMMITTED and applied before step P1 runs on any environment.
