Otherwise the first ensure_personal_space INSERT fires trg_projects_ws_realtime with the whole personal row, the auto-staff seat row fires the project_members arm, both reach every active member, and old and new clients merge the project into projectsIndex. Suites 03, 04, 05, 17, 23 and 52 are extended in the same commits; whole pgTAP set; collected = planned.
- **P1: personal space**: projects.personal_owner_id, projects_personal_shape_chk, projects_personal_owner_uniq, fn_projects_identity_guard and its trigger, projects_insert restated, fn_project_members_personal_guard and its trigger, ensure_personal_space(). Post-conditions as in schema_changes.
  - *Constraints:* One transaction, no enum (text kinds avoid the ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE trap). Rehearse rolled back with suites 01_projects, 18_project_members and 80_private_projects extended in the same commit. 35_platform_audit must pass. Ship the client first: listProjects' filter and handleWorkspaceEvent are guarded by the column probe, so they are safe ahead of the database (S4b-08 lesson: old builds show what a new migration exposes). No personal row exists until a new client calls the RPC.
- **P2: Moth read path**: expenses_file_ids_gin, _moth_target_eligible, moth_can_read_target, moth_can_write_target, moth_islands, moth_project_folders, moth_project_files, with return-type and body post-conditions (comments stripped before matching).
  - *Constraints:* After P1 (the bodies read personal_owner_id). It covers no table, so its suite goes into rls.yml's replay list by hand, and the tail pins in rlsSuiteLists.test.js (lines ~75 and ~111-115) are edited in the same commit. The number is provisional: the next free after the table suites 110-123, remeasured at the tip. The helper's kind list is extended later by restating the latest body, each with its own probes.
- **P3: Moth preferences**: user_settings.moth_prefs, its CHECK, set_moth_prefs, moth_prefs.
  - *Constraints:* After P2 (moth_prefs calls moth_can_read_target) and after moth_views exists if last_view is re-checked against it (otherwise only quick tokens are accepted until then). Suite 57_user_settings is extended and its plan count updated; collected = planned.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/80_private_projects.sql (extended) + 01_projects.sql + 18_project_members.sql**: POSITIVE: a member with app_role user calls ensure_personal_space and gets an id; a second call returns the same id; the count for (workspace, owner) is 1; the row has is_private true and created_by = personal_owner_id = owner; the owner holds a manager seat. A row pre-seeded as service gives the ON CONFLICT path its existing id. The owner SELECTs their space; an admin SELECTs it (the Files positive). NEGATIVE: another member SELECTs 0 rows; a workspace manager SELECTs 0; another workspace's admin SELECTs 0; an admin's direct INSERT with personal_owner_id = a colleague raises 42501; UPDATE of personal_owner_id raises 42501; setting is_private false on a personal row raises 23514; created_by changes are silently reverted (read back); soft_delete_row('projects', personal) by an admin raises 42501; a seated member setting is_private = true on a shared project raises 42501, while the creator and an admin succeed; seating a colleague on a personal space raises 42501; a deactivated owner's ensure_personal_space raises 42501 while the admin still SELECTs; after the owner's account is deleted the space remains for the admin and stays at 0 rows for others. PLANTED CONTROLS: drop projects_personal_owner_uniq → the 'second insert refused' probe goes red; restore 0013's projects_insert → the forged-insert probe goes red; remove the created_by pin → the hijack probe goes red; remove the is_private arm of the guard → the member-flip probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/<next>_moth_read_path.sql (new, replay list; no table)**: POSITIVE: the owner's moth_can_read_target('project', own space) is true; an ordinary file in a shared readable project is true for member, reviewer, manager and admin; moth_islands lists the owner's own space, and for an admin it lists others' private projects but not others' personal spaces. NEGATIVE: the admin's moth_can_read_target on another's space, its folder and its file is false (the Moth negative beside the Files positive above); a file with is_financial, INVOICES, FINANCE and LEGAL keys is false for admin, manager and a project-manager seat; an unflagged s3 receipt named in expenses.file_ids is false; a file whose folder_id is the INVOICES folder is false; folders 'INVOICES' and 'INVOICES/x' are false; a binned file is false; another person's private project is false for a manager and true for an admin; forged kinds ('folder', <file id>), ('project', <file id>), ('board', x), NULL and an unknown kind are all false; another workspace's file is false; moth_project_files on a hidden project returns 0 rows, not an error; the pinned return types carry no is_financial and no count. WRITE: the MATRIX lines (appRole × projectRole × staffed × personal × owner) run against moth_can_write_target (reviewer false, member true, no seat on a staffed project false, admin on a shared project true, admin on another's personal false, owner on own personal true). PLANTED: remove the personal arm → the admin Moth-negative goes red; swap file_row_is_money for can_access_project_money → the admin money probe goes red; remove the expenses arm → the receipt probe goes red; remove the folder-ancestry arm → the INVOICES-folder probe goes red; remove passes_project_privacy → the manager private probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/57_user_settings.sql (extended)**: set_moth_prefs writes only the current workspace's key; recent_folders are capped at 8 and workspace entries at 10; an unknown key raises 22023; oversize is refused by the CHECK; the admin and another user read 0 rows; moth_prefs drops a folder id once its project turns private for someone else, and drops one moved under INVOICES; an entry whose member_since differs from the live membership comes back as '{}'; an inactive member's set raises 42501. PLANTED: remove the re-check filter → the private-folder probe goes red; remove the member_since reset → the reuse probe goes red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/35_platform_audit.sql (unchanged; must stay green)**: Every new SECURITY DEFINER function (fn_projects_identity_guard, fn_project_members_personal_guard, ensure_personal_space, _moth_target_eligible, moth_can_read_target, moth_can_write_target) is not executable by anon. PLANTED: GRANT anon on moth_can_read_target → suite 35 goes red.
- **src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.test.js**: EXPECTED rows for 'project.moth.write', including the personal arms; ready === false returns true; a new test parses the MATRIX lines of the moth read-path suite and asserts canOnProject agrees on every row. PLANTED: route moth.write through the admin short-circuit → the 'admin on another's personal' row goes red.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/views/writeGate.test.js**: The existing ready scan now also asserts that at least one canOnProject site is found under src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 (the instrument proves a presence). PLANTED: drop ready from the Moth call → the offender is listed.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters (supabaseAdapter tests)**: listProjects sends .is('personal_owner_id', null) when the column exists and not when the probe says it is absent. uploadFile on a personal project (petal) calls the Supabase provider, not LOCAL_SERVER; on a network workspace it throws the existing NAS sentence; a private non-personal project still routes to LOCAL_SERVER. PLANTED: restore the old projectIsPrivate → the personal routing test goes red.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state (RabbitProvider workspace-event test)**: A projects event carrying personal_owner_id, or is_private true, never enters projectsIndex and triggers a refetch; a public row still merges. PLANTED: remove the check → red.
- **src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer test (mounted, fixtures)**: The owner sees 'My personal space'; the fixture admin sees 'Personal space · Priya Raman' and its files, read-only, with a reason; a member sees no other person's space; the R.A.B.B.I.T. Files tab shows no personal entry.
- **src/tools/moth_v0.1.0 tests**: A source scan: no .from('projects'|'files'|'folders') and no listPersonalSpaces under the Moth folder (PLANTED: add one → red). mothFileHidden hides is_financial, INVOICES/FINANCE/LEGAL keys, the INVOICES folder, the 'legal' tag, expense receipts and the local {slug}_RECEIPTS&INVOICES directory; a vitest reads the helper's migration text and asserts the same arms. A mounted entry: a reviewer sees Delete greyed with its reason and no adapter write is called; the first visit shows the 'What do you want to map?' window; the last view reopens on the second visit.
- **electron route tests (main.cjs local projects)**: POST /api/rabbit/projects with personal: true creates an ordinary project; GET /api/rabbit/projects omits the personal bundle; POST /api/rabbit/personal-space twice returns one id.

### Risks

- The personal row is broadcast on the workspace channel at creation (0018 sends whole projects and project_members rows with only a workspace filter), so every member's client would list it.
  - *Mitigation:* Hard ordering: the A-R1-04 privacy skip is committed and applied before ensure_personal_space exists, and the client's handleWorkspaceEvent refuses private and personal rows. A suite 23 probe, where realtime exists, pins it.
- An old client (pre-change build) lists personal spaces in R.A.B.B.I.T., D.O.G. and Team for their owners, and for admins lists everyone's.
  - *Mitigation:* Ship the client before the migration on staging and the beta; no personal row exists until a new client provisions one. Old admin builds show only what the admin's RLS already admits.
- Admins can still read other people's personal spaces through raw REST or any non-Moth surface; 'not in Moth' is a read-path rule, not RLS.
  - *Mitigation:* Required by the Files ruling and stated as such in the handbook §4.4 deviations table. The Moth source scan and the INVOKER-only Moth RPCs keep Moth itself honest. Whether an admin's look is recorded is asked.
- Per-row DEFINER calls in moth_project_files cost one lookup each across files, folders, expenses and projects.
  - *Mitigation:* PK lookups plus the new GIN index; pages are clamped to 1000. Measure on the fixtures-sized seed and on dev; the bundle and query timings go in the hand-off.
- Hiding receipts from Moth tells a member that a file they can open in R.A.B.B.I.T. is attached to an expense.
  - *Mitigation:* Minor: they can already open the receipt body itself. Recorded in OUTSTANDING with the measurement, and no indicator or count is shown.
- Workspace storage totals include personal-space bytes (S4b-06), so the size of a person's uploads shows in the company total.
  - *Mitigation:* The same as private projects today; recorded. No new per-space usage function is added (none would carry the workspace check S4b-06 lacks).
- Pinning created_by on every projects UPDATE could change a writer that legitimately reassigns ownership.
  - *Mitigation:* MEASURED: no writer changes created_by (PROJECT_COLUMNS re-sends it unchanged, runMigration's project UPDATE patches budget pointers only). Silent revert, not refusal; service_role exempt.
- files.folder_id may point at a folder in another project (fact 12), so a personal-space file could appear filed under a foreign folder.
  - *Mitigation:* moth_project_files NULLs a foreign folder_id, the helper still excludes locked folders by id, and the missing same-project check goes to OUTSTANDING.
- Bodies of a personal space in an s3 or petal company are row-reached only, so GC and teardown treat them like any project's.
  - *Mitigation:* Nothing new to teach: they keep projects/<personal id>/… keys. Body identity on a later move is the storage area's job.
- On an office-server (network) company, personal-space files on the share are readable by anyone with share access outside WILSON.
  - *Mitigation:* Asked as a question; the default UI sentence says so plainly.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Projects, E9: 'Protected personal-space owner/classification; exactly one private backing project … Moth owner-only; Files owner/admin.'
  - *Design:* The plan does not say which existing paths break. Measured: is_private alone routes media to the uploading desktop (projectIsPrivate in uploadFile), so the design changes that predicate. It keeps is_private = true and adds personal_owner_id with a CHECK, so no privacy policy needs restating. 'Moth owner-only' cannot live in RLS. It is enforced by INVOKER Moth RPCs plus the helper's personal arm, and admins keep RLS reach for Files.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* 0072 header: 'created_by is stamped by fn_audit_touch … no client can name somebody else as the owner of a private project.'
  - *Design:* Wrong for UPDATE, and partly for INSERT. fn_audit_touch fills created_by only when NULL on INSERT and never protects it on UPDATE. projects_update lets any project writer change is_private and created_by (WITH CHECK is workspace only), so a seated member can hide a shared project as their own private one (INFERRED exploit; a pgTAP probe settles it). The design pins created_by and limits is_private flips to the creator or an admin. A forged created_by on INSERT by an admin or manager stays open and goes to OUTSTANDING.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Tasks/assets, E12 and A-R2-03: 'Add missing parent-privacy checks as explicit Moth write prerequisite.'
  - *Design:* It is also a prerequisite for the personal space itself. After account deletion the owner's seat cascades away, the space becomes unstaffed, and can_write_project opens to every member. Only the parent hop bounds those writes. Measurement widened A-R2-03 to folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, comments and project_members as well.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Realtime, E12/E13: 'Suppress private/personal workspace payloads.'
  - *Design:* It must land before the provisioning RPC: the very first provision would otherwise broadcast the personal row and its seat to every member.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Trash/history: 'no broadened private readership.'
  - *Design:* edit_history_select (0092) has no privacy arm (S4d-02), so workspace managers would read a personal space's file history. This design names restating it from 0092 with a passes_project_privacy arm as a prerequisite.
- *Plan:* Shared rules: 'moth_can_read_target … validates actual type …; caller-supplied kind/identifier pairs are never authority.'
  - *Design:* Specified as a DEFINER helper whose kind only picks the table, with every decision taken from the row found. Session 1 kinds are project, folder and file. A private _moth_target_eligible with a trashed switch serves the later trash index. Money exclusion uses classifiers plus an expenses.file_ids arm and folder ancestry, which needs a new GIN index on expenses.file_ids.
- *Plan:* Coverage: 'Extend 01_projects, 80_private_projects and definers'
  - *Design:* Kept, plus one new table-less suite for the Moth read path, registered in the replay list and in rlsSuiteLists.test.js's tail pins. Its MATRIX lines are also read by projectRoleMatrix.test.js, giving the first SQL-to-JS lockstep pin.
- *Plan:* User settings, E10: 'Bounded workspace-keyed … reset and re-authorization on reuse.'
  - *Design:* Made concrete: the reset is keyed on the membership row's created_at (so reactivation keeps the prefs and re-joining resets them), and every stored id is re-checked through moth_can_read_target on read. The single writer RPC follows set_table_view_last.
- *Plan:* Local scopes / desktop: personal space on a desktop-only company (not stated)
  - *Design:* Adds one local personal bundle per data root through a dedicated route; the generic create and patch routes strip the flag. runMigration does not carry it in Session 1 (OUTSTANDING).

### Questions for her, as drafted (to be put to her in plain words only after the synthesis)

- Some companies keep their files on an office server instead of in the cloud. For those companies, the files in someone's personal space would sit in a personal folder on that office server. WILSON hides them from everyone else inside WILSON, but anyone who can open the office server directly (outside WILSON) could still find them. Is that acceptable?
  - Yes: keep them on the office server, and tell people this in plain words when they add files to their personal space (Recommended)
  - No: for those companies, keep personal-space files in WILSON's own cloud storage instead
  - No: for those companies, personal spaces hold maps and boards only, with no files, in this release
  - *Recommended:* Yes: keep them on the office server, and tell people this in plain words when they add files to their personal space (Recommended)
- When a workspace admin opens someone else's personal space from the Files page (for example after that person has left), should WILSON keep a record of who looked and when?
  - Yes: keep a record that other admins can see in the admin activity list (Recommended)
  - Yes: keep that record and also tell the owner the next time they sign in
  - No: do not record it
  - *Recommended:* Yes: keep a record that other admins can see in the admin activity list (Recommended)

### Review — Security: personal space and audiences (MO1 design round). Looked for leaks past what a person can already see, privilege escalation, authority a client can forge, and broken existing gates. I read 0013, 0018, 0020, 0037, 0041, 0072, 0076, 0082, 0097, 0098 and 0101, plus TeamMembersPage.jsx, WorkspaceTakeout.jsx, supabaseAdapter.js, main.cjs and localDemoRoot.cjs.

**Verdict:** Buildable once two blockers are fixed. SEC-1: project_members_select is open to the whole workspace, and TeamMembersPage reads seat rows directly, so every member sees an 'Untitled' entry per person's personal space and every admin sees 'Personal space' outside Files. P0 must restate the policy (latest in 0020) with a projects hop. SEC-2: the prescribed workspace-broadcast arm fails open on cascades, so hard-deleting a space or a private project broadcasts its tasks, assets and seats to everyone. It must take 0101's fail-closed shape. The majors (SEC-3 to SEC-7) also need fixing before P1 ships: scope the expenses arm to the file's own project; exclude personal spaces from Workspace Takeout; gate provisioning against stale client builds; either decide with Audrey or enforce owner-only writes at the database; and key the desktop personal space per machine. The rest of the core holds up under attack. It reuses is_private with a CHECK. It pins created_by and limits is_private flips. ensure_personal_space is narrow and its EXECUTE is revoked from anon. Moth reads go through INVOKER RPCs with a DEFINER helper that decides from the row it finds, never from the caller's kind and id. The personal arm has no admin leg. No function grants anything for 'a manager seat on any project', so the owner's auto-staffed seat on their space escalates only inside that space.

- **SEC-1 [blocker]** Seat rows are readable by the whole workspace, so every person's personal space shows up for everyone. That covers its existence, its project id and its owner. The design hides the space at listProjects and at the workspace broadcast, but not at project_members_select, and the Team page reads that table directly rather than through projectsIndex.
  - *Scenario:* Priya, an app-role user, opens Moth, and ensure_personal_space seats her as manager of her space through fn_projects_auto_staff. Tom, another plain member, opens Team Members. The Assigned-projects column reads project_members joined to projects(title, deleted_at). The embedded project is null for Tom (projects_select hides it), so the code adds 'Untitled' to Priya's list, and Tom now knows the space exists. An admin on the same page sees 'Personal space' listed under every person, which is a surface other than Files (the ruling allows admins Files only). Through plain REST, any member can run select project_id,user_id from project_members and get every personal space id, and every private project id too.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0020_admin_grants_and_alignment.sql project_members_select (L255-259): workspace_id = current_workspace_id() AND has_active_membership. It has no projects hop, and 0013 L167 says 'Rosters are workspace-visible'. src/components/TeamMembers/TeamMembersPage.jsx L228-239: .from('project_members').select('user_id, project_id, projects(title, deleted_at)'), then `if (row.projects?.deleted_at) continue` (a null embed is not skipped) and `list.push(row.projects?.title || 'Untitled')`. Design PS-7 lists TeamMembersPage as covered 'through projectsIndex', which is wrong for this column.
  - *Fix:* Add a P0 prerequisite: restate project_members_select from 0020 word for word, plus `AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = project_members.project_id)`, in the 0082 §3b hop shape under the caller's RLS. The can_* helpers and fn_projects_auto_staff are DEFINER, so they are unaffected. TeamMembersPage should also skip rows whose embed is null or carries personal_owner_id, and admins should get personal spaces from listPersonalSpaces only. Add suite 18 probes: a member reads 0 seat rows of another person's personal or private project, and an admin reads them. Plant: drop the hop and the member probe goes red. Add a mounted Team test: no 'Untitled' and no 'Personal space' entry.
- **SEC-2 [blocker]** The workspace-broadcast privacy arm the design prescribes (A-R1-04 shape) fails open when the parent project has been deleted. 'Skip if EXISTS a private project' lets a row through when the project row is gone. That is the cascade case, and the design itself names it as the admin's way to free a space slot. 0101's measured shape is the opposite: skip unless a non-private project exists.
  - *Scenario:* An admin hard-deletes Priya's personal space (projects_delete, the path PS-3 offers to 'free the slot'), or any private project. The projects DELETE is skipped (OLD.is_private). The cascaded children are not. tasks rows assigned to Priya pass 0018's dashboard filter, assets DELETE passes the asset filter, and project_members passes too. By the time their AFTER triggers run, the parent row is already deleted, so `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id = v_project AND p.is_private)` is false and the whole OLD row (task title, description, asset name) is broadcast on rabbit:workspace:<ws> to every active member.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. Design schema_changes fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast: 'IF v_project IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id=v_project AND p.is_private) THEN RETURN NULL'. 0101_versions_and_items_realtime.sql fn_task_items_ws_broadcast L185-192: `IF NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = v_project AND NOT p.is_private) THEN RETURN NULL` with the comment 'A project gone (a cascade) is skipped the same way'. 0018 L102-137 broadcasts NEW and OLD whole.
  - *Fix:* Write the arm fail-closed in the 0101 shape: for every table with a project (tasks, assets, project_members), `IF v_project IS NOT NULL AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = v_project AND NOT p.is_private) THEN RETURN NULL`. Keep the projects arm on NEW or OLD is_private. Add a suite 23 probe where realtime exists: hard-delete a private project holding an assigned task, and no workspace message carries the task. Plant: invert to the EXISTS-private form and the probe goes red. Add the cascade case to the post-condition text.
- **SEC-3 [major]** The new expenses arm trusts a client-writable uuid[] across the whole workspace. A personal space also gives every person the money gate on their own space. Together, any member can hide any file in the company from everyone's Moth. The DEFINER helper also works as an oracle on expense rows the caller cannot read.
  - *Scenario:* Priya is an app-role user and her space's auto-staffed manager seat gives her can_access_project_money on it. She inserts into expenses (project_id = her space) with file_ids naming twenty files from a shared project. The design's arm, `e.workspace_id = f.workspace_id AND e.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id]`, is not tied to the file's project, so moth_can_read_target('file', x) becomes false for those files for every role, and they disappear from every colleague's Moth. In the other direction, a member can call moth_can_read_target on a file they can see. A false answer tells them an expense somewhere, possibly in a private project or someone's personal space, names that file.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0037_budget_system.sql: expenses.file_ids UUID[] (L299) with no FK. expenses_insert WITH CHECK is workspace + can_access_project_money(project_id) (L493-498). can_access_project_money admits `project_role_for(p_project) = 'manager'` (L99-112). 0076_receipts_are_money.sql L193-196: 'expenses.file_ids is a plain uuid[] with no FK and no trigger and it sits in supabaseAdapter's write allowlist, so a client CAN point an expense at a file'. 0020 fn_projects_auto_staff seats the creator as manager. Design MR-3 file arm.
  - *Fix:* Scope the arm to the file's own project: `e.project_id = f.project_id AND e.workspace_id = f.workspace_id AND e.file_ids @> ARRAY[f.id]`. Then only that project's money audience can mark its files. Keep the GIN index, and add (project_id) to the predicate or a composite index if the plan needs it. Record the remaining same-project oracle in OUTSTANDING with the existing risk line. Probe: an expense in project B naming a file of project A does not hide that file. Plant: drop the project equality and the probe goes red.
- **SEC-4 [major]** Workspace Takeout gives admins every person's personal-space content, a surface other than Files. The rule set says owner-only personal content is excluded from takeout the way Notes are. The design never mentions takeout.
  - *Scenario:* An admin runs Workspace Takeout in the Admin Terminal. It pages `select('*')` under the admin's RLS over projects, files, comments, edit_history, file_events, project_members and task tables. projects_select and the child hops admit admins to private rows, so the zip holds every personal space: titles, file names, descriptions, tags, comments and full-row edit_history diffs. The zip can then be passed around off-platform.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/components/AdminTerminal/WorkspaceTakeout.jsx TAKEOUT_TABLES (L35-58) and fetchAll (L79-107). The header excludes Notes because they are 'owner-only personal content with NO admin bypass' (L15). moth1_rules.md item 28 → 'Owner-only personal-space content … must be excluded like Notes'. 0072 projects_select admin arm.
  - *Fix:* Exclude personal-space rows and their children from takeout: filter projects with .is('personal_owner_id', null) and drop child rows whose project_id is in the set listPersonalSpaces returns. Name the exclusion in the manifest's `excluded` list. Or ask Audrey whether takeout counts as the 'when someone leaves' reach, and if it does, put those rows in a separate labelled folder. Add a test: a fixture personal space is absent from the takeout zip, and the manifest says so.
- **SEC-5 [major]** Keeping personal spaces out of every list except Files and Moth depends on every client running the new build. Nothing enforces a minimum version. Any installed older desktop build shows an admin every person's personal space as an ordinary project, which they can open and write. An older build also sends uploads to the uploader's own disk.
  - *Scenario:* P1 is applied after the new web build ships. Mara, an admin, still runs last month's Electron build, which loads its bundled renderer with base './', so it is not updated by the web deploy. Her listProjects has no personal_owner_id filter. RLS returns Priya's space, so it appears in R.A.B.B.I.T.'s project list, D.O.G.'s project picker and the Dashboard, outside Files, where she can open it and edit or upload. Her old projectIsPrivate sends every upload to LOCAL_SERVER on her own disk. Priya, also on an old desktop build, gets the same misrouting for her own uploads.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabaseAdapter.js listProjects (L2686-2695) has no personal filter, and projectIsPrivate is at L336-339. A grep for min_client_version|minClientVersion|MIN_APP_VERSION|forceUpdate|required_version across the app finds nothing. Design P1 constraint: 'Ship the client first … old admin builds show only what the admin's RLS already admits'.
  - *Fix:* Gate provisioning on the server. Option one: ensure_personal_space takes a p_client_contract int and refuses (42501, with a plain 'update WILSON to use your personal space' sentence) below the contract that carries the PS-6 and PS-7 changes. Option two: a workspace-level switch that Audrey turns on after the rollout. Also record in OUTSTANDING that rows already provisioned remain visible to stale admin builds, and ask whether a minimum-version check belongs in this release.
- **SEC-6 [major]** Admin write access to another person's personal space is blocked only in the interface. At the database, can_write_project, the P0 parent hops (which run under projects_select, which admits admins) and fn_trash_authz's privacy arm all let an app admin write. Any admin session can therefore upload, rename, trash or delete in someone's personal space, and can edit the space's own row.
  - *Scenario:* Mara, an admin, or anyone holding her token, PATCHes files and folders in Priya's space, calls soft_delete_row('files', …) on Priya's files, inserts tasks there, or updates projects.title and folder_root on the space. All of it passes RLS. PS-8 makes the Files view read-only through GatedAction, and MR-4 makes Moth owner-only, but neither stops REST, R.A.B.B.I.T. (see SEC-5) or a future screen.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0013 can_write_project L115-122 (current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager') first), projects_update L215-222, files_insert L293-298, folders_insert and folders_update in 0041 L296-313. 0072 projects_select admin arm, which every P0 hop evaluates under. Design PS-8: 'read-only in this view, behind GatedAction'.
  - *Fix:* Ask Audrey whether admins may change a person's personal space or only look at it (for example, move files out when someone leaves). If look-only: add RESTRICTIVE write policies on files, folders, tasks, assets, comments and the personal row's projects_update: `NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id = <t>.project_id AND p.personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL AND p.personal_owner_id IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid())`. Restrictive policies AND together, so this genuinely narrows. Pin it in fn_trash_authz by restating the 0092 body. Probes: an admin write on another person's space raises 42501 and the owner's succeeds. Plant: drop the restrictive policy and the admin-write probe goes red. If writes are allowed, keep them and record each admin write in app_events.
- **SEC-7 [major]** DT-1 assumes one signed-out data root means one person. The data root can be a UNC share or a copied folder, so several people share one 'personal space'. The single-instance lock works per machine, not per folder, so 'race-free' is also false.
  - *Scenario:* Two editors in an office both open \\nas\studio\wilson-demo as their demo folder. Each machine's GET /api/rabbit/personal-space returns the same bundle under <folder>/.wilson/rabbit-data, so each sees the other's 'personal' files in Moth. If both machines POST at once, each main process checks and creates on its own, giving two personal bundles, and later GETs pick one at random. A copied folder also carries one person's personal space to the next person.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. electron/localDemoRoot.cjs checkDemoFolderShape (L77-107) accepts '\\server\share\folder'. getRabbitDataDir in main.cjs (L151-155) resolves under <folder>/.wilson/rabbit-data. app.requestSingleInstanceLock (main.cjs L96) is per userData. localDemoRoot.cjs header L14-16 keeps files-config.json per machine for the same copied-folder reason (review round 2, M4).
  - *Fix:* Key the local personal space per machine and per operating-system user, not per data root. Keep its pointer in userData (as files-config.json does) and its bodies under the per-machine local-media root. Or refuse to create it when the active root is UNC, with a plain sentence. Test: two data roots on one machine give one space, and a copied folder brings no personal space with it.
- **SEC-8 [minor]** The Moth source scan is narrower than MR-1's rule. It bans .from('projects'|'files'|'folders') and listPersonalSpaces, but not the adapter readers that query the same tables under admin RLS. A Moth caller holding a personal-space id could then show an admin another person's space.
  - *Scenario:* A later Moth change calls adapter.listFiles(id) or loadProject(id) for an island whose id came from projectsIndex, a stored preference or a link. For an admin, RLS returns another person's personal-space files and the scan stays green.
  - *Evidence:* INFERRED from the design tests ('no .from(...) and no listPersonalSpaces under the Moth folder'). supabaseAdapter.js listFiles/listFolders (L1221-1232, 1466) and loadProject (L2739-2741) read the base tables directly.
  - *Fix:* Widen the scan to forbid listFiles, listFolders, listManagedFiles, loadProject, listProjects, listProjectMembers and useRabbit().projectsIndex under src/tools/moth_v0.1.0, with a planted-call test for each.
- **SEC-9 [minor]** Clearing folder_id in moth_project_files must be written in the positive form. An INVOKER comparison `fo.project_id <> f.project_id`, run against a folder the caller cannot see under RLS, evaluates to NULL and passes the foreign folder id through. That id can belong to a folder in someone's personal space.
  - *Scenario:* Priya files a shared-project file under a folder in her own space (fact 12: no same-project check). Tom calls moth_project_files on the shared project. His folders subquery returns no row, the `<>` test is NULL, and Tom's Moth gets Priya's personal folder id.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. moth1_core.md fact 12 (no folder same-project check). 0041 folders_select (L290-294) hops under RLS. Design MR-5: 'folder_id NULLed when the folder's project_id <> f.project_id'.
  - *Fix:* Specify `CASE WHEN EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM folders fo WHERE fo.id = f.folder_id AND fo.project_id = f.project_id) THEN f.folder_id END`. Probe: a file whose folder sits in another person's personal space comes back with folder_id NULL. Plant: switch to `<>` and the probe goes red.
- **SEC-10 [minor]** The folder-ancestry money arm trusts folders.path, which any project writer can change. Renaming the INVOICES category row, or giving a child folder a path that disagrees with its parent chain, brings receipts back into every role's Moth. Rows filed by folder_id with no flag (the receipts that predate 0076) are the ones exposed. This never goes beyond what RLS already shows, but it weakens the 'money excluded for every role' rule.
  - *Scenario:* A seated member edits the category folder row so its path reads 'RECEIPTS' (folders_update needs only can_write_project). Unflagged receipts whose folder_id points at it now appear in the admin's and manager's Moth.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0041 folders_update (L304-313) with a free-text path checked only by folders_path_shape_chk. Design MR-3: 'NOT EXISTS folders fo WHERE fo.id=f.folder_id AND rabbit_money_segment(split_part(fo.path,'/',1))'.
  - *Fix:* Base the folder arm on the root category as well: walk parent_id up to the kind='category' ancestor (bounded recursive CTE) and test its slug with rabbit_money_segment, or also exclude entity_type = 'invoice'. Alternatively add a guard that pins the path and slug of kind='category' rows for non-service callers. Probe: a renamed INVOICES category still hides the receipts filed under it.
- **SEC-11 [minor]** moth_prefs can be written directly, so 'written only through set_moth_prefs' is not a control. The 0046 own-row UPDATE policy and table-wide grants leave the column open, as 0098 says about table_view_last. The member_since reset, the 8 and 10 caps and the key shapes can all be forged by the owner. The only safe authority is the read-time re-check.
  - *Scenario:* A person PATCHes user_settings.moth_prefs with member_since copied from the live row and recent_folders full of guessed ids. Reads then rely on moth_prefs() alone. If an implementer someday returns the stored map without re-checking it, forged ids come straight back.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0098_table_views.sql L752-758: 'its grants are table-wide, so the column is not fenced: the client writes it only through set_table_view_last'. Design US-1: 'The only writer is set_moth_prefs'.
  - *Fix:* Say so in the migration comment and the handbook. Make moth_prefs() treat the stored map as untrusted: ignore any non-uuid id, cap the counts on read, and re-check every id (already in the design). Probe: a directly written entry naming a hidden folder comes back filtered.

### Review — Correctness and house rules: personal space, who sees what, and the Moth read path (MO1 design round)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes, not as written. Core: the personal space as a projects row with personal_owner_id is sound, and the CHECK, partial unique index, DEFINER provisioning, identity guard and INVOKER Moth RPCs all hold against the code. Specifically: no FK on created_by since 0007; fn_audit_touch only fills a NULL created_by; postgres has BYPASSRLS, so the DEFINER insert passes FORCE RLS; auto-staff seats the caller; expenses.file_ids is uuid[]; folders.path is relative. One blocker in P0: the edit_history_select restatement calls passes_project_privacy, which authenticated cannot execute, so it would break every history read. Coverage gaps the design claims to close but leaves open: edit_history covers files only (project_id is NULL for other entity types); project_members_select has no hop, so Team shows 'Untitled' for every personal space; the admin takeout exports every personal space. Desktop paths that delete the space: runMigration and Archive-and-clear (via the GET filter), and the unguarded DELETE route. The network-company model contradicts itself. Smaller items: a project-patch writer missed, the inverted realtime skip, the Files owner gate, the PGRST202 check, the comments hop template, the suite-list wording, and the MATRIX input.

- **PSA-01 [blocker]** The P0 edit_history_select restatement calls public.passes_project_privacy(project_id) inside a policy. 0082 REVOKEs EXECUTE on that function from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated, and a function in a policy is EXECUTE-checked as the querying role. Once the restatement lands, every SELECT on edit_history by an admin or manager fails with permission denied, which breaks the shipped history drawer. The design's own MR-2 states the rule ('EXECUTE is checked as the caller') and then breaks it here.
  - *Scenario:* A workspace admin opens a task's history after P0 is applied. PostgREST evaluates edit_history_select as authenticated, the call to passes_project_privacy raises 42501, and the drawer shows an error for every entity in every project.
  - *Evidence:* supabase/migrations/0082_private_project_definers.sql passes_project_privacy: 'REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.passes_project_privacy(UUID) FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated' and COMMENT 'Callers: milestones_trash_index, fn_trash_authz … No client role executes it'. Every policy in the repo uses the EXISTS projects hop instead (0082 §3b milestones_insert/update). The design's schema_changes entry 'policy edit_history_select'.
  - *Fix:* In the policy, use a predicate authenticated can execute: the file_events_select shape from 0092, `public.can_read_project_topic(project_id)` (INVOKER over projects_select), or `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = edit_history.project_id)`. Keep the admin arm the way file_events_select keeps it if the history of binned or purged projects must stay readable. Add a suite 17 probe that the admin and manager reads still succeed (lives_ok), next to the manager-negative.
- **PSA-02 [major]** The edit_history fix covers files only. edit_history.project_id is filled only for entity_type 'files', so the `project_id IS NULL OR …` arm leaves every other history row of a personal or private project readable by every workspace manager. That includes the personal space's own 'projects' create row, whose diff.new holds the whole row (title, personal_owner_id, created_by), plus its phases, assets, tasks, comments and rate cards.
  - *Scenario:* Priya's first Moth visit runs ensure_personal_space. fn_edit_history_capture writes a 'projects' create row with project_id NULL. Every app_role manager reads it through edit_history_select and learns that Priya's personal space exists, along with its id. A comment on that project is exposed the same way.
  - *Evidence:* 0088_legal_files.sql COMMENT ON COLUMN edit_history.project_id: 'the file's project, for files rows only … NULL for every other entity type'. 0092 fn_edit_history_money_snapshot runs only `WHEN (NEW.entity_type = 'files')`. The entity allowlist (0012/0061) includes 'projects', 'phases', 'assets', 'tasks', 'comments' and others.
  - *Fix:* Snapshot project_id for every entity type at capture. Restate the latest capture body (fn_edit_history_capture, 0061), using the v_project CASE that fn_realtime_broadcast (0088) already carries: entity_id for 'projects', fn_comment_project_id for comments, the joins for the dependency and link tables. Backfill the existing rows, then gate on a non-NULL project with a client-executable predicate (PSA-01). Add suite 17 probes: a manager reads 0 history rows of a private project and of a personal space, for both the projects row and a task, with a planted control.
- **PSA-03 [major]** Consumer missed. Every active member can read project_members across the workspace: project_members_select (0020) checks workspace and membership only, with no projects hop. TeamMembersPage reads the seats directly with an embedded projects(title) and pushes 'Untitled' when the embed comes back null. So once the auto-staff seat exists, each person's Team row shows an extra 'Untitled' assignment, a placeholder that reveals the space exists. PS-7 claims TeamMembersPage inherits the projectsIndex filter, but it does not read projectsIndex for this list.
  - *Scenario:* An ordinary member opens Team. For every colleague who has opened Moth, the assignments list shows 'Untitled', which is the colleague's personal space: RLS hides the projects embed, so it reads null and `row.projects?.title || 'Untitled'` fills in the name. Raw REST /project_members also returns the owner's manager seat with the personal project_id.
  - *Evidence:* 0020_admin_grants_and_alignment.sql project_members_select: `workspace_id = current_workspace_id() AND has_active_membership(workspace_id)`. src/components/TeamMembers/TeamMembersPage.jsx load(): `.from('project_members').select('user_id, project_id, projects(title, deleted_at)')` and `list.push(row.projects?.title || 'Untitled')` (it skips only rows whose `deleted_at` is set). supabaseAdapter.listProjectMembersByProjects reads the same table.
  - *Fix:* Add a P0 item: restate project_members_select from 0020 verbatim plus `AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = project_members.project_id)`, with probes in suite 18. Make TeamMembersPage, and the fixture listProjectMemberships, drop rows whose projects embed is null. Add a mounted test in which a member sees no 'Untitled' entry for a colleague's personal space.
- **PSA-04 [major]** Scope missed. The workspace takeout reads 'projects' and 'files' through the admin's own RLS, so it exports every person's personal space and its file metadata (names, descriptions, tags, storage paths). Her ruling allows admins to reach a personal space from the Files view only, and the takeout already excludes owner-only Notes for exactly this reason. The design does not mention the takeout.
  - *Scenario:* An admin runs Workspace Takeout from the Admin Terminal. The ZIP's projects.csv holds a 'Personal space' row for each member, and files.csv holds every file in every personal space. That is a bulk admin backdoor outside Files.
  - *Evidence:* src/components/AdminTerminal/WorkspaceTakeout.jsx: the table list includes 'projects' and 'files', and the header says 'Notes (S8): owner-only personal content with NO admin bypass' is excluded. moth1_rules.md item 28: 'Owner-only personal-space content … must be excluded like Notes'.
  - *Fix:* Exclude personal rows from the takeout: projects filtered with `.is('personal_owner_id', null)`, and files, folders and file_events filtered to non-personal project ids. If Audrey wants departed members' spaces exported, ask her as a question. Pin it with a mounted or source test that has a planted control.
- **PSA-05 [major]** Local Server data loss through the migration path. DT-1 makes GET /api/rabbit/projects skip the personal bundle, and runMigration discovers local projects only through that route. The promised 'plain sentence' therefore can never be produced, and the report reads clean. MigrationPanel's 'Archive and clear local' then runs archiveLocalRabbitData, which deletes rabbit-data/projects/ with rmSync. The personal bundle's rows survive only inside the archive JSON. When the bundle has no project folder, its file bodies live under projects/<pid>/files (the getRabbitFilesDir fallback), so they are deleted even though they were never carried.
  - *Scenario:* A signed-out desktop user keeps files in their local personal space, signs in and runs the cloud migration. The report shows nothing about the space, they press 'Archive and clear local', and the space with its files is gone from the desktop and was never uploaded.
  - *Evidence:* src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js fetchLocalProjectsList: `localFetch(`${RABBIT_BASE}/projects`)`. electron/main.cjs archiveLocalRabbitData: `fs.rmSync(projectsDir, { recursive: true, force: true })`, whose header says 'minus binary files; they're already in Storage'. main.cjs resolveProjectFilesDir falls back to `getRabbitFilesDir(projectId)` = projects/<pid>/files.
  - *Fix:* Give runMigration its own read of GET /api/rabbit/personal-space, so it reports the space as not carried and the report is not 'clean'. Also make archiveLocalRabbitData keep (not rmSync) the personal bundle's directory, or refuse to clear while a personal bundle holds files. Add a test in runMigrationBins.test.js, which already lifts archiveLocalRabbitData, with the control 'the personal dir survives'.
- **PSA-06 [major]** Writer missed on the desktop. DELETE /api/rabbit/projects/:id permanently removes any bundle directory with rmSync, and the design guards only POST and PATCH. The cloud refuses to bin a personal space (PS-3), but on the Local Server a single route call destroys the space and its internal bodies, with no trash or restore. Her ruling routes deletes through the soft-delete and restore paths.
  - *Scenario:* Any caller holding the local token (the renderer's localServerAdapter.deleteProject, or a stale project id in some list) sends DELETE /api/rabbit/projects/<personal id>. The directory, including files/ under the internal fallback, is gone.
  - *Evidence:* electron/main.cjs `expressApp.delete('/api/rabbit/projects/:id', …)`: `if (fs.existsSync(dir)) fs.rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true })`. The design's DT-1 and consumers_to_change name only the POST and PATCH strips.
  - *Fix:* Refuse DELETE when bundle.project.personal is true (409 with a sentence, the local twin of the cloud 42501), and add an electron route test: POST personal-space, DELETE it, assert a refusal and that the directory still exists. Have the dedicated route own the personal flag on every path, including any import that writes a project.json.
- **PSA-07 [major]** The office-NAS ('network') company is self-contradictory. PS-6 keeps the existing refusal, so a cloud personal space in a network workspace can never hold a file. DT-1 separately creates a local personal bundle per data root on the signed-out desktop. One person in a network company therefore gets two spaces, one in the cloud that cannot hold files and one on the local bundle, which breaks 'ONE personal space per workspace'. The question to Audrey describes files 'on that office server', which neither path implements.
  - *Scenario:* A network-storage company. In the cloud app, Moth provisions the personal row, and Add files throws 'this workspace stores media on its own server or NAS…'. On the desktop in Local Server mode, POST /api/rabbit/personal-space creates a second, unrelated space.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js uploadFile: `if (activeProvider === WORKSPACE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK && !moneyGated) throw new Error('this workspace stores media on its own server or NAS …')`. moth1_core fact 8: network bodies live only in the desktop Local Server bundle, and the cloud has no files rows for them. The design's questions_for_audrey[0].
  - *Fix:* Before building, decide one model for network companies. Either the cloud space holds only metadata and maps, with files explicitly unsupported in this release (one of the question's options), or there is a defined mapping between the cloud row and the desktop bundle. Reword the question to describe what the design actually does. Then pin the chosen behaviour with an uploadFile test and a Local Server route test.
- **PSA-08 [minor]** The 'one filter at the source' misses a third projectsIndex writer: the project channel's 'project-patch' effect merges any projects row into projectsIndex with no privacy check. If a personal space is ever the provider's active project (for example, Moth's 'Add files' going through the provider's upload path, which acts on the open project), the space enters projectsIndex. ProjectsPage, ProjectContextBar, ConnectionDot and the rest would then list it.
  - *Scenario:* The owner adds a file to their personal space from Moth through RabbitProvider. The project channel delivers a projects UPDATE (updated_at), and the effect at RabbitProvider.jsx's project-patch branch adds the space to projectsIndex. R.A.B.B.I.T.'s project list now shows 'Personal space'.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx: `} else if (eff.type === 'project-patch' && eff.record?.id) { setProjectsIndex(idx => … { ...(cur || {}), ...eff.record } …` (around l.1139). The design's PS-7 names only listProjects and handleWorkspaceEvent.
  - *Fix:* Apply the same refusal (personal_owner_id present → never merge) in the project-patch branch. Alternatively state and enforce that a personal space is never opened as the active project: Moth uploads through the adapter with an explicit projectId. Add a RabbitProvider test with a planted control.
- **PSA-09 [minor]** The workspace-channel skip is inverted compared with the 0101 precedent. The design writes 'IF v_project IS NOT NULL AND EXISTS (… p.is_private) THEN RETURN NULL'. When a private or personal project is hard-deleted, the cascaded project_members and tasks DELETEs fire after the projects row is gone, the EXISTS is false, and the seat's OLD row (user_id, project_id) is broadcast to every member. 0101 uses the fail-closed form: skip unless a non-private parent exists.
  - *Scenario:* An admin hard-deletes an orphaned personal space (projects_delete). The projects event is skipped (OLD.is_private), but the cascaded seat DELETE reaches rabbit:workspace:<ws>, and TeamMembersPage, listening to project_members events, reloads.
  - *Evidence:* 0101_versions_and_items_realtime.sql fn_task_items_ws_broadcast (moth1_core fact 17): 'skip unless EXISTS (projects p WHERE p.id = v_project AND NOT p.is_private)'. 0013 project_members project_id FK ON DELETE CASCADE. 0018 trg_project_members_ws_realtime is AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OR DELETE.
  - *Fix:* For rows with a project, broadcast only `IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id = v_project AND NOT p.is_private)`, and otherwise RETURN NULL. Keep the projects-row arm on NEW/OLD.is_private. Pin the shape with a body post-condition, since CI has no realtime and suite 23's broadcast probes skip there.
- **PSA-10 [minor]** The Files view cannot deliver 'the owner's own space follows its seat'. ProjectFilesExplorer decides writes for a project that is not open as app admin or manager only, and money as admin only. A personal space is filtered out of projectsIndex, so it can never be the open project. The owner (app_role user) is therefore read-only on their own space, and cannot see their own invoices there. An app-manager owner gets write through the app-role branch. Meanwhile an admin gets write controls on someone else's space unless the new override lands first.
  - *Scenario:* Priya (user) picks 'My personal space' in Files and sees every control greyed with 'Open this project in R.A.B.B.I.T. to change its files', but she cannot open it in R.A.B.B.I.T.
  - *Evidence:* src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx: `const canWrite = backendWrites && (noRoles || (isOpenProject ? access.canWrite : (appRole === 'admin' || appRole === 'manager')))` and `canSeeMoney = noRoles || (isOpenProject ? … : appRole === 'admin')`.
  - *Fix:* Decide personal entries from the listPersonalSpaces row. For the owner (personal_owner_id === me), write and money follow the owner. For anyone else, read-only with a reason (and money as today for admins in Files, if that is intended). Add these as mounted fixture tests next to the design's ProjectFilesExplorer probes.
- **PSA-11 [minor]** The missing-function check names only 42883. A missing RPC reaches supabase-js as PostgREST's PGRST202 (schema cache), so on a database without the migration ensurePersonalSpace would show a raw error instead of 'this workspace's database does not have personal spaces yet'. The same applies to listMothIslands, getMothPrefs and the rest.
  - *Scenario:* The client is shipped ahead of P1 (the design's own deploy order). Moth's first entry calls rpc('ensure_personal_space') and gets PGRST202, so the friendly sentence never shows.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js around l.1751-1758: 'A missing 0084 FUNCTION: PGRST202 (PostgREST's schema cache), or a 42883 that names THIS function', and `error?.code === 'PGRST202' || (error?.code === '42883' && …includes(fnName))`. Also useWorkspaceMembers.js MISSING_RPC_CODES = {'PGRST202','42883'}.
  - *Fix:* Reuse the existing missing-function predicate factory for every new Moth and personal-space RPC, and add an adapter test for each of PGRST202 and a named 42883.
- **PSA-12 [minor]** The A-R2-03 template '<t>.project_id' does not apply to comments: comments has no project_id column (it is polymorphic entity_type/entity_id), and its write policies resolve the project through fn_comment_project_id. As written, the P0 restatement fails to create the policy for comments.
  - *Scenario:* The privacy-repair migration restates comments_insert with `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = comments.project_id)` and fails with 42703 (column does not exist), aborting the P0 transaction that P1 hard-depends on.
  - *Evidence:* 0000_rabbit_base_schema.sql `create table if not exists comments (id, entity_type, entity_id, body, …)`. 0013_project_members.sql comments_insert: `can_comment_project(public.fn_comment_project_id(entity_type, entity_id))`.
  - *Fix:* For comments, hop with `EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = public.fn_comment_project_id(entity_type, entity_id))`, and name that variant explicitly in the dependency so the privacy-repair owner restates it correctly.
- **PSA-13 [minor]** The CI registration wording is misleading. P2 says the table-less suite 'goes into rls.yml's replay list by hand' because it covers no table, but rlsSuiteLists.test.js requires every suite file in the replay list, in number order. It also hard-pins the last five replay entries and the last six picked suites, ending at 109. Every new suite in this area (the moth read-path suite, and any table suites 110 to 123 landing earlier) must be added, and the two tail pins edited, in the same commit. The pins move again with each landing, so the read-path suite's tail depends on landing order.
  - *Scenario:* Suite 110_<table>.sql lands with only its RLS_TABLES line, on the belief that only table-less suites need the replay list. rlsSuiteLists 'names every suite file … and nothing else' fails, and so do the slice(-5) and slice(-6) pins.
  - *Evidence:* src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js: `expect([...listed].sort()).toEqual(files)`, `expect(listed.slice(-5)).toEqual([... '109_s3_endpoint_public_hosts.sql'])`, `expect(picked.slice(-6)).toEqual([... '109_s3_endpoint_public_hosts.sql'])`.
  - *Fix:* State the rule as: every suite file goes into the replay list, and table suites also get RLS_TABLES. In the commit that adds each suite, update both tail pins to that commit's real last entries, re-measured at the tip.
- **PSA-14 [minor]** The lockstep pin for the personal arm guards the predicate, not the input to it. CL-1 builds ctx with `isPersonalOwner: island.is_personal`, so in production canOnProject can never see a personal island with owner false. The MATRIX row 'admin on another's personal false' bites canOnProject, but no test fails if moth_islands or the helper ever started returning another person's personal space, because the client would then grant write.
  - *Scenario:* A later restatement of _moth_target_eligible loses the personal arm. moth_islands returns colleagues' spaces to an admin, and the client sets isPersonalOwner = true for them and shows Delete and Move enabled. The SQL write twin still refuses, but no client test goes red.
  - *Evidence:* The design's CL-1 ctx: `isPersonalOwner: island.is_personal`. MR-5's moth_islands columns carry no owner field.
  - *Fix:* Return a server-computed `is_mine` (personal_owner_id = auth.uid()) from moth_islands, pin it in the return-type post-condition, and set isPersonalOwner from it. Add a pgTAP probe that an admin's moth_islands never carries is_personal AND NOT is_mine.

## 3. Moves, deletes, trash and undo

**Area as the designer framed it:** Shared checked moves, restorable deletes, trash view and file undo (cloud and desktop)

### Summary

The cloud move is one SECURITY DEFINER RPC, public.moth_move_items(file_ids[], folder_ids[], dest_project, dest_folder). It takes a workspace-wide transaction advisory lock, locks the affected files and folders rows FOR UPDATE, and validates the whole selection before it writes anything. The checks are: read gate (workspace, live project, passes_project_privacy, personal space owner-only with no admin bypass), Moth exclusion (file_row_is_money, file_row_is_legal, expense membership, all answered as not_found so the RPC is no oracle), can_write_project on source and destination, locked destination folder, custom-only folder sources, cycles, path collisions and a movable body provider. Then it commits atomically or raises. A cross-project move clears the seven entity links and is_core_definer. It never touches storage_path or thumbnail_url, so no 'moved' event, no GC enqueue and no Legal trigger fire. The body follows through the body-location designer's same-transaction hook. Each move is journalled server-side in a new definer-only table, file_moves (S4c-09). Undo and redo are both moth_undo_move(move_id): it replays the journal's recorded origins, links included, through the same validation core, never through caller-supplied destinations. It refuses if anything changed since. MEASURED: no foreign key references files(id, project_id). The only FKs into files are three single-column SET NULL links (asset_versions.file_id, rate_cards.source_file_id, ingestion_chunks.file_id). files.folder_id and folders.parent_id have no same-project check, so I make both composite (id, project_id) FKs, DEFERRABLE. A trigger allows a project_id change only inside the checked move (transaction-local GUC). file_events gains a 'refiled' event, written in each project without naming the other. fn_realtime_broadcast's files arm splits a project change into a DELETE on the old topic and an INSERT on the new one. Cloud delete and restore stay on soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted (fn_trash_authz unchanged), wrapped in atomic Moth batch RPCs (moth_trash_files, moth_restore_files) that add the Moth exclusions. A definer moth_trash_index is the Moth trash view's cloud source. It returns metadata only, money is excluded for everyone, and other people's personal spaces are excluded even for admins. On the desktop, a files-row delete becomes a 30-day trash. The bundle row moves to a new trashedFiles key and the body moves to <baseDir>/.trash/, so it stays on the same volume and under the same ACLs. Restore routes cover both files and managedFiles, and expired items are purged when the project is opened. All of this lives in a new electron/fileTrash.cjs behind the existing launch-token lock, with no new IPC. Moth's undo is its own in-memory stack (src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.js). It is not RabbitProvider's per-project stack, so R.A.B.B.I.T.'s active project is never switched, and it is guarded against the S3b-10, S5c-05, S5d-01 and S5d-02 traps. RabbitProvider.deleteFile and deleteManagedFile gain undo steps, which gives restoreFile its first production caller. Moves between desktop-local projects, and between local and cloud projects, are refused in Session 1. Within one local project a move changes only folder_id, in one atomic bundle write.

### Decisions

- **MV-1** Cloud checked move = public.moth_move_items(p_file_ids uuid[], p_folder_ids uuid[], p_dest_project uuid, p_dest_folder uuid) RETURNS jsonb {move_id, files, folders, links_cleared}, SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE PUBLIC/anon, GRANT authenticated. Its work is done by an internal fn_moth_apply_moves(p_moves jsonb, p_undo_of uuid), which is REVOKEd from every client role. The arguments are typed arrays (no kind/id pairs). Every id is resolved by the server against its own table.
  - *Why:* One server operation, so the whole selection is validated and committed in one transaction, or nothing changes. Postgres atomicity replaces the S4c multi-step client orchestration. Moving the body is not needed because the stable-body design keeps it in place.
  - *Rejected:* Client-side loop of adapter.updateFile(project_id): no atomicity, and files_update cannot see the source/destination pair. A polymorphic items jsonb [{kind,id}] from the client: violates the never-trust-kind/id rule. Moving via patchFile's foreign-project branch (RabbitProvider.patchFile sends project_id straight to updateFile): unguarded today.
- **MV-2** Validation order inside fn_moth_apply_moves, all before any write: (1) caller: auth.uid() set, current_workspace_id(), has_active_membership. (2) Selection between 1 and 500 items, deduplicated, and items inside a selected folder ignored (they move with it). (3) Read gate on every source and destination project: same workspace, deleted_at IS NULL, passes_project_privacy (0082), and if it is a personal space, owner = auth.uid() with no admin arm. Failure gives not_found. (4) Moth exclusion on every file, including trashed files in a moved subtree: file_row_is_money OR file_row_is_legal OR EXISTS expenses e WHERE f.id = ANY(e.file_ids). Failure gives not_found, the same message as an unknown id, refused before anything about existence is answered (rule 48). (5) can_write_project on source and destination (not_allowed_source / not_allowed_destination). (6) Destination folder must be in the destination project, and rabbit_money_segment(split_part(path,'/',1)) must be false (destination_locked). (7) Folder sources: kind = 'custom' only, not locked, and the destination's ancestor chain (recursive CTE over parent_id) must not contain the source (into_itself). Every recomputed subtree path must be free in the destination project, per folders_project_path_uniq (name_taken). (8) Cross-project moves only: storage_provider must be on the relocatable-by-association list (Session 1: 'supabase', 's3', plus whatever the body designer marks); local_server and google_drive are refused (body_on_a_computer). A file referenced by asset_versions.file_id is refused (is_an_asset_version).
  - *Why:* This carries out her rulings: no money or Legal for anyone, admins included; private projects stay with their creator and admins; personal spaces are owner-only in Moth. It also follows the house rule that a definer is never an existence oracle. Refusing local_server bodies follows from the fact that a private project's media lives on the uploading desktop (MEASURED, uploadFile l.3481). A move into a shared project would leave members with a NOT_HERE body.
  - *Rejected:* Using can_access_project_money / can_access_project_legal as the exclusion: they admit admins and managers, and her ruling excludes money for everyone. A different refusal code for 'exists but money': an oracle. Clearing asset_versions.file_id silently: a hidden side effect on another table.
- **MV-3** Concurrency (S4c-12): PERFORM pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('wilson.file_move:'||v_ws, 0)) first, then SELECT the affected files rows ORDER BY id FOR UPDATE, then the moved folders and their subtrees FOR UPDATE, with the destination ancestry FOR SHARE.
  - *Why:* Row locks alone cannot stop two crossed folder moves (A into B, B into A) from each passing its own cycle check. A workspace-level transaction lock serializes them. Moves are paced by people and capped at 500 items, so contention is negligible. soft_delete_row and the S4c rewriteFileRow UPDATEs serialize behind the row locks.
  - *Rejected:* A lock row table: needs cleanup when a client dies. Per-project advisory locks: deadlock-prone when two moves name the same projects in opposite order, and still blind to cycles across projects.
- **MV-4** Journal = a new table public.file_moves: one row per operation, items jsonb holding per item kind, id, from/to project, from/to folder or parent, from path, the seven from-links and was_core_definer. It is written only by fn_moth_apply_moves. There is no client SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE policy, ALL is REVOKEd from anon and authenticated, and ENABLE + FORCE RLS are on. purge_file_moves() runs after 30 days under a guarded cron 'wilson-purge-file-moves'.
  - *Why:* S4c-09: this is the server's own record, so undo restores exact links without trusting the client. Nobody can read it, so recording private project ids in it leaks nothing. The 30 days match the trash window.
  - *Rejected:* Undo by client-supplied origins: technically validated, but caller-held links are weaker evidence and can go stale. Reading edit_history: admin|manager only, and it has no privacy arm (S4d-02). moth_history: owned by another designer, read-shaped for people, not for replay.
- **MV-5** Undo and redo of a move are both public.moth_undo_move(p_move_id uuid) RETURNS jsonb {move_id}. It requires journal row workspace = v_ws, actor_user_id = auth.uid() and undone_at IS NULL (otherwise not_found or already_undone). Every item must currently sit at its recorded to_project/to_folder and be live (otherwise changed_since, and nothing moves). It replays the reverse moves through fn_moth_apply_moves with the full validation, restoring links and is_core_definer from the journal. A link target that no longer exists in the origin project is restored as NULL. Then it stamps undone_at, and the undo's own journal row carries undo_of. Redo = moth_undo_move(<the undo's row id>).
  - *Why:* One function, and a server-held record in both directions. The caller must still have write rights on the origin, so an undo cannot reach a project they have lost access to. An all-or-nothing refusal keeps 'commits atomically or changes nothing' true for undo too.
  - *Rejected:* Redo by re-calling moth_move_items with client arguments: works, but it is a second path, and the selection could have been re-scoped since. Partial undo: leaves a half-restored selection.
- **MV-6** Cross-project row effects: project_id goes to the destination. folder_id goes to the destination folder for directly selected files, and stays the same (folder moved with them) for subtree files. phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id and experience_id go to NULL, and is_core_definer goes to false, all journalled. Within one project only folder_id (or a folder's parent_id and path) changes, and links are kept. The UI confirmation lists the links that will be cleared, which it reads from rows the person can already see. storage_path, thumbnail_url, storage_provider, is_financial, tags, document_kind and description are never written.
  - *Why:* An entity link into the old project would be a dangling cross-project reference. MEASURED: entity links have no same-project FK, only single-column SET NULL FKs (0000, 0043), and task_items etc. use their own composite keys, not files. Clearing is_core_definer stops a moved file silently steering the destination project's agent context. Leaving the key alone keeps every money CHECK (files_money_key_chk, files_legal_folder_chk), trg_files_legal_fixed and fn_files_gc_enqueue (AFTER DELETE only) untouched.
  - *Rejected:* Remapping links to same-named entities in the destination: invents relationships. Refusing moves of linked files: blocks the main use (personal space to project).
- **MV-7** Integrity guards that apply to every writer, not only the RPC. (a) folders gets UNIQUE (id, project_id). files.folder_id gets a composite FK (folder_id, project_id) -> folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id) DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE, replacing files' single-column folder_id FK. folders.parent_id gets the same composite FK (parent_id, project_id) -> folders(id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE DEFERRABLE. (b) Trigger trg_files_move_guard (BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF project_id, folder_id, is_financial, storage_path) refuses a project_id change unless current_setting('wilson.checked_move', true) = 'on', which only fn_moth_apply_moves sets via set_config(..., true) and clears before returning. It also refuses a money or Legal row whose folder_id is not under a locked folder. (c) trg_folders_move_guard does the same for folders.project_id.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: files.project_id is client-writable (FILE_COLUMNS, patchFile, files_update WITH CHECK only checks the new row), and folder_id has no same-project check (fact 12, settled by reading 0043/0041). The composite FK makes a cross-project folder_id impossible for every writer. The deferrable form lets the RPC move a folder subtree and its files in one transaction. The money-folder guard means a custom folder can never contain a money row, so a folder move never meets a hidden file and never needs a revealing refusal. PostgREST cannot set arbitrary GUCs, and this is the 0064 otter_nomination_apply precedent.
  - *Rejected:* A same-project trigger instead of a composite FK: weaker, and it misses folder project changes. ON UPDATE CASCADE from folders to files: hides a project move inside an FK action. A policy-only fix in files_update: policies cannot compare OLD with NEW.
- **MV-8** Lifecycle and history records. Edit history is captured by the existing trg_files_edit_history (0012). The definer UPDATE carries the caller's JWT, so updated_by and the actor are the caller. file_events: one new term, 'refiled'. fn_file_events_capture (restated from 0074) writes it twice on a project change: in the source project (old_path, details {direction:'out', folder_id}) and in the destination (new_path, details {direction:'in', folder_id}). It writes once {direction:'within', from_folder_id, to_folder_id} for a folder change inside one project. Neither row names the other project.
  - *Why:* file_events_select reads by can_read_project_topic(project_id), so each project's readers see their half and the admin arm sees both. Leaving the other project's id out keeps a private project's id away from the other side's readers. MEASURED: no event exists today for a project or folder change, and 'moved' means a storage_path change, which a body-preserving move never makes.
  - *Rejected:* Reusing 'moved' with old_path = new_path: breaks the meaning that 'moved' carries a path pair. One event with from_project_id in details: leaks a private project's id.
- **MV-9** Realtime: fn_realtime_broadcast (restated from 0098). In the files arm, after the money return, a project_id change broadcasts a DELETE carrying OLD on rabbit:project:{OLD.project_id} and an INSERT carrying NEW on rabbit:project:{NEW.project_id}. Folders stay off the channel, as today.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the arm routes by COALESCE(NEW, OLD).project_id, so today the old project's open windows would keep a stale row. realtimeMerge already handles both INSERT and DELETE, so no client change is needed. can_read_project_topic is SECURITY INVOKER, so a private destination's topic stays private.
  - *Rejected:* Old topic only plus a refetch effect: the destination's windows would miss the arrival.
- **MV-10** Cloud delete and restore from Moth: public.moth_trash_files(p_file_ids uuid[]) and public.moth_restore_files(p_file_ids uuid[]) RETURNS integer, SECURITY DEFINER. Each checks at most 500 ids, the Moth read gate (personal spaces owner-only, admins excluded) and the Moth exclusion (not_found), then for each id in id order PERFORMs public.soft_delete_row('files', id) or public.restore_soft_deleted('files', id), which keep fn_trash_authz's checks. Any raise rolls the whole batch back. fn_trash_authz is not restated. The 30-day purge is the existing purge_soft_deleted.
  - *Why:* Her ruling says 'through the existing soft-delete and restore paths'. The wrappers add only what Moth must add on top: money excluded for admins too, and personal spaces owner-only. Admins keep Files-view reach through the generic RPCs. A single transaction closes the S5c-04 class (a bulk delete stopping part way) on the cloud.
  - *Rejected:* A client loop of adapter.deleteFile: partial failures. Extending fn_trash_authz with Moth rules: it also serves R.A.B.B.I.T. and Files, where admins must keep money and personal-space reach.
- **MV-11** Moth trash view, cloud source: public.moth_trash_index(p_limit int DEFAULT 200, p_before timestamptz DEFAULT NULL) RETURNS TABLE (id, project_id, project_title, folder_id, name, mime_type, size_bytes, kind, deleted_at, deleted_by_label, purges_at, can_restore). Rows: trashed files of the caller's workspace in live projects that pass passes_project_privacy and the personal-space owner check, not money, Legal or expense rows, deleted within the last 30 days, newest first, capped at 500. can_restore = can_write_project. There are no storage_path or thumbnail_url columns and no previews.
  - *Why:* The milestones_trash_index (0082) shape: trashed rows are invisible through files_select by design, so a read needs a definer index. It is gated on READ so reviewers can see the trash. Restore is gated by the batch RPC and GatedAction. Returning metadata only means a trashed body is never signed (OSP-01).
  - *Rejected:* A per-project index: Moth spans projects. Returning locators: widens what a trashed row can open.
- **MV-12** Desktop restorable delete: DELETE /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId/files/:id becomes a trash. In ONE writeRabbitBundle, the row leaves bundle.files for a new bundle key trashedFiles as {row, trashed_at, trash_name} and a local 'trashed' event is logged. AFTER that write, the body is renamed to <baseDir>/.trash/<fileId>-<safe basename>, where baseDir = resolveFileBaseDir(bundle, pid, row), so it stays in the same directory tree, volume and ACLs as <slug>_FILES, INVOICES or LEGAL. If the rename fails (EPERM after retries), the body stays where it is. Restore (POST …/files/:id/restore) renames the body back first and then writes the row back into bundle.files whole, with a 'restored' event. The body is always located by checking BOTH the .trash name and the origin (locateTrashedBody), so a crash between the two steps on either side is recoverable without a journal. Managed files keep their existing deleted_at soft delete and gain POST …/managed-files/:id/restore. The 30-day purge (purgeExpiredLocalTrash) runs when the project opens (GET /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId) and on the trash read. It unlinks the contained body (and a managed thumbnail), logs 'purged' with blob_removed taken from what unlink actually did, and writes once. All of this lives in a new electron/fileTrash.cjs.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the DELETE unlinks the body and removes the row (permanent), and managedFiles are soft with no restore. A separate trashedFiles key means every existing reader of bundle.files (download, the stream in fileReads.cjs, the makeRowLocator open path, relink, listFiles, the runMigration files step) keeps today's 'gone' behaviour with no edits. A .trash inside the base dir avoids EXDEV and avoids moving a money body out of an INVOICES or LEGAL directory whose NAS ACLs may be tighter. The walkers already skip dot entries (rabbitBins.cjs, main.cjs import walks), so .trash is never imported.
  - *Rejected:* deleted_at on bundle.files rows (the milestones shape): every files reader in main.cjs, fileReads.cjs, openPath.cjs and runMigration.js would need filtering, a wide surface. The project-root .trash/: can cross volumes when files_dir is overridden, and moves money bodies out of their folder. Leaving bodies in place: the file stays visible in Explorer after a 'delete'.
- **MV-13** The desktop gates stay as they are. The new routes are /api paths, so applyLocalServerLock (localToken.cjs) covers them with the launch-token header or cookie, and its 401 has no body. No new IPC channel is added: nothing repoints a root, and the ipcGate allowlist is unchanged. Every fs path is joined through resolveContainedFilePath against resolveFileBaseDir, and the .trash target is contained against the base dir. trash_name is derived from the row's id plus a basename-sanitised storage_path and is never taken from the request. The routes read and write JSON only and never serve trashed bytes. openPath's makeRowLocator already resolves only live bundle.files rows.
  - *Why:* These are the house's existing gates. A trash route that served bytes or took a path from the page would reopen the S14/S35 containment class.
  - *Rejected:* An IPC restore channel: no root is repointed, so HTTP behind the token is the established route family.
- **MV-14** Desktop-local projects: Session 1 supports moves WITHIN one local project only, through POST /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId/files/move {moves:[{kind:'file'|'folder', id, to_folder_id}]}. It validates every id against that bundle's files and folders, custom folders only, no locked destination (money segment as the path's first segment, plus legacy {slug}_RECEIPTS&INVOICES / _CREWINVOICES / _TALENTINVOICES names), no cycles and no path collisions. It changes folder_id, or parent_id and path, in one writeJsonAtomic bundle write, logs local 'refiled' events, and answers the from-folders so the client can undo by posting the inverse moves. Moves between two local projects, and between a local and a cloud project, are refused with a plain reason. projectFilePatch.cjs refuses a project_id different from the route's and a folder_id not in bundle.folders.
  - *Why:* A local files row's storage_path is a bare name relative to its own project's base dir.
