A cross-project move would therefore have to move the body between project folders, possibly across volumes, and write two bundles non-atomically. Her ruling that a move never relocates the body describes company storage, which local projects are not. A within-project move needs no body move: fileTree places by folder_id, and S4c-11 says rows move, not directories. This keeps parity with the cloud's within-project arm.
  - *Rejected:* Cross-bundle moves with a journal and a body copy: a large new failure surface, beyond Session 1's foundations. Moving managedFiles: their bodies live under folder_path directories, so a move is a body relocation. Refused in Session 1.
- **MV-15** Moth undo is its own module: src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.js (in the lazy Moth chunk). It is an in-memory inverse-operation stack, capped at 10 each way, serialized by a queue that copies inHistoryQueue's per-step wait. Entries are self-addressed: they carry move_id, file ids and project ids, never the open project. It is cleared on account, workspace or adapter change (a generation counter; an operation that finishes under an older generation is dropped, not pushed), and NOT on an R.A.B.B.I.T. project switch or a page change. While a replay runs, new Moth mutators are refused, not just left unrecorded. A failed undo puts the entry back. It never uses runBatch, because one RPC is one step. Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y are bound only while currentPage === 'moth'. The toast goes through useRabbit().showUndoToast with onUndo = mothHistory.undoToken(token).
  - *Why:* RabbitProvider's stack is per project visit (projectVisitRef; setActiveProject replaces historyRef), so pushing Moth's cross-project steps onto it would either be dropped or force a project switch, and a switch clears it. This guards the traps: S3b-10 (one global batch slot, the replay records nothing), S5c-05 (undo across a project switch), S5d-01 (no refusal during a replay), S5d-02 (no visit check). The tableViewsState offerUndo precedent shows toast-only undo outside the keyboard stack.
  - *Rejected:* Reusing pushHistory with a pseudo visit: clearHistory on any remote project trash wipes it, and the S5c-05 class returns. Switching R.A.B.B.I.T.'s active project per operation: clears history (plan §4 says so).
- **MV-16** R.A.B.B.I.T. parity. RabbitProvider.deleteFile pushes one history step with a visit guard. The inverse is adapter.restoreFile(id, projectId) followed by putting the snapshot row back into bundle.files, and there is an undo toast. deleteManagedFile(id, false) pushes the inverse adapter.restoreManagedFile. Both are registered in mutationsRef for the mutationsRegistry pin. A new ctx verb noteFilesChangedElsewhere(projectIds) reloads the active project when it is involved and realtime is not live (always so on the Local Server).
  - *Why:* restoreFile has zero callers (MEASURED). The local DELETE route's meaning changes from permanent to trash, so FileManager's copy has to change anyway, and the parity rule (M1-L6) asks for the same undo on both backends. Moth needs R.A.B.B.I.T.'s open bundle to follow its moves where no broadcast arrives.
  - *Rejected:* Leaving R.A.B.B.I.T. deletes undo-less: the R.A.B.B.I.T. Files delete would silently become restorable with no way back except Moth's trash.

### Schema changes

- **public.folders** (restates from: new (0041 created the table and the single-column FK)): ADD CONSTRAINT folders_id_project_key UNIQUE (id, project_id). Replace the single-column parent_id FK, found by catalogue (conrelid folders, contype f, conkey = {parent_id}), never by name, with: ALTER TABLE public.folders ADD CONSTRAINT folders_parent_same_project_fk FOREIGN KEY (parent_id, project_id) REFERENCES public.folders (id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE NOT VALID; ALTER TABLE public.folders VALIDATE CONSTRAINT folders_parent_same_project_fk. Post-conditions: exactly one FK on parent_id; it is composite and CASCADE ('c'); the key exists.
- **public.files (folder FK)** (restates from: new (replaces 0043's files_folder_id FK)): Drop the single-column folder_id FK, found by catalogue. ADD CONSTRAINT files_folder_same_project_fk FOREIGN KEY (folder_id, project_id) REFERENCES public.folders (id, project_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (folder_id) DEFERRABLE INITIALLY IMMEDIATE NOT VALID; then VALIDATE CONSTRAINT. Before applying, the rehearsal counts SELECT count(*) FROM files f JOIN folders d ON d.id=f.folder_id WHERE d.project_id<>f.project_id on dev and staging. Post-conditions: 0043's post-conditions 2 and 3 still hold (folder_id carries an FK, confdeltype 'n'); the FK is composite with confdelsetcols = {folder_id}; condeferrable is true.
- **public.fn_files_move_guard() + trg_files_move_guard; public.fn_folders_move_guard() + trg_folders_move_guard** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Body: IF TG_OP='UPDATE' AND OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id AND COALESCE(current_setting('wilson.checked_move', true),'') <> 'on' THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'moth_move:use_checked_move' USING ERRCODE='42501'; END IF; IF NEW.folder_id IS NOT NULL AND public.file_row_is_money(NEW.is_financial, NEW.storage_path) AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.folders d WHERE d.id = NEW.folder_id AND public.rabbit_money_segment(split_part(d.path,'/',1))) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'a locked file is filed only in its locked folder' USING ERRCODE='23514'; END IF; RETURN NEW;. Trigger: BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE OF project_id, folder_id, is_financial, storage_path ON public.files FOR EACH ROW. The folders twin applies only the project_id arm, BEFORE UPDATE OF project_id. Rehearsal first counts money rows filed outside a locked folder on dev and staging (INFERRED zero: invoicesFolderHook files financial uploads into INVOICES).
- **public.file_moves** (restates from: new): CREATE TABLE public.file_moves (id uuid PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(), workspace_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES workspaces ON DELETE CASCADE, project_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES projects ON DELETE CASCADE /* destination; feeds the populate trigger */, actor_user_id uuid NOT NULL DEFAULT auth.uid(), items jsonb NOT NULL CHECK (jsonb_typeof(items)='array' AND jsonb_array_length(items) BETWEEN 1 AND 500), undo_of uuid REFERENCES public.file_moves(id) ON DELETE SET NULL, undone_at timestamptz, created_at/created_by/updated_at/updated_by). trg_file_moves_populate_workspace BEFORE INSERT -> fn_populate_workspace_from_project(). trg_file_moves_touch BEFORE UPDATE -> fn_audit_touch(). ENABLE + FORCE RLS. NO policies. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT ALL TO service_role. Index (workspace_id, created_at). Post-conditions: relrowsecurity and relforcerowsecurity are true; zero policies; authenticated holds no privilege.
- **public.fn_moth_apply_moves(p_moves jsonb, p_undo_of uuid) RETURNS jsonb** (restates from: new): Internal, SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Steps: advisory xact lock on 'wilson.file_move:'||ws; FOR UPDATE locks (files ORDER BY id; folder subtrees by recursive CTE); the MV-2 validation, raising 'moth_move:<code>' with ERRCODE P0001 and the code set {not_found, not_allowed_source, not_allowed_destination, destination_locked, not_custom_folder, into_itself, name_taken, body_on_a_computer, is_an_asset_version, too_many, empty, no_root_folder}; PERFORM set_config('wilson.checked_move','on',true); SET CONSTRAINTS files_folder_same_project_fk, folders_parent_same_project_fk DEFERRED; UPDATE the folder subtrees (project_id; the top's parent_id = the destination folder or the destination root row; path = CASE WHEN parent.path='' THEN slug ELSE parent.path||'/'||slug END from stored slugs); UPDATE files (MV-6; for undo moves, links from the journal re-validated by EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM tasks t WHERE t.id=link AND t.project_id=to_project) else NULL, likewise for the other six); SET CONSTRAINTS … IMMEDIATE; PERFORM set_config('wilson.checked_move','',true); INSERT file_moves; RETURN jsonb. The personal-space owner clause reads the schema designer's column (provisionally projects.personal_owner_id): (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id = auth.uid()). The relocatable-provider list and the body association are the body designer's hook, called in the same transaction (see risks).
- **public.moth_move_items(uuid[], uuid[], uuid, uuid) RETURNS jsonb; public.moth_undo_move(uuid) RETURNS jsonb** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated. moth_move_items builds p_moves with every item going to (dest_project, dest_folder) and calls fn_moth_apply_moves(…, NULL). moth_undo_move: lock; SELECT … FROM file_moves WHERE id=p AND workspace_id=current_workspace_id() AND actor_user_id=auth.uid() FOR UPDATE (missing gives not_found; undone_at set gives already_undone); every item at its to_* and live, else changed_since; reverse moves through fn_moth_apply_moves(rev, p); UPDATE file_moves SET undone_at=now(). Post-conditions (pg_get_functiondef with comments stripped): the core contains pg_advisory_xact_lock, FOR UPDATE, file_row_is_money, file_row_is_legal, passes_project_privacy, can_write_project and the expenses membership clause; anon holds no EXECUTE on any of the three; authenticated holds none on fn_moth_apply_moves.
- **public.moth_trash_files(uuid[]) RETURNS integer; public.moth_restore_files(uuid[]) RETURNS integer** (restates from: new (calls 0014 soft_delete_row / restore_soft_deleted and 0092 fn_trash_authz unchanged)): SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE PUBLIC/anon, GRANT authenticated. Body: membership; 1 to 500 ids; FOR v_id IN SELECT unnest ORDER BY 1: read the files row with RLS bypassed (no deleted_at filter for restore); the project must be live, in the workspace, pass passes_project_privacy and the personal-space owner clause; Moth exclusion as in MV-2 (else RAISE 'moth_trash:not_found'); IF public.soft_delete_row('files', v_id) THEN n := n+1 (restore: public.restore_soft_deleted). RETURN n. fn_trash_authz is NOT restated (its latest body, 0092, is called unchanged through the 0014 RPCs).
- **public.moth_trash_index(integer, timestamptz)** (restates from: new (shape of 0082 milestones_trash_index)): RETURNS TABLE (id uuid, project_id uuid, project_title text, folder_id uuid, name text, mime_type text, size_bytes bigint, kind file_kind, deleted_at timestamptz, deleted_by_label text, purges_at timestamptz, can_restore boolean). STABLE SECURITY DEFINER, search_path=public, REVOKE PUBLIC/anon, GRANT authenticated. v_keep INTERVAL '30 days' (tracks purge_soft_deleted). WHERE f.workspace_id=v_ws AND f.deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND f.deleted_at > now()-v_keep AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND passes_project_privacy(p.id) AND (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id=auth.uid()) AND NOT file_row_is_money(f.is_financial,f.storage_path) AND NOT COALESCE(file_row_is_legal(f.is_financial,f.storage_path),false) AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM expenses e WHERE f.id = ANY(e.file_ids)) AND (p_before IS NULL OR f.deleted_at < p_before) ORDER BY f.deleted_at DESC LIMIT least(coalesce(p_limit,200),500). can_restore = can_write_project(p.id). Post-condition: no result column named storage_path, thumbnail_url or storage_provider.
- **public.purge_file_moves(interval) + cron 'wilson-purge-file-moves'** (restates from: new): DELETE FROM file_moves WHERE created_at < now() - p_retention (default 30 days). SECURITY DEFINER, REVOKE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT service_role. Scheduled under the 0014 guarded DO block (pg_cron absent in CI raises a NOTICE, not a failure).
- **public.file_events vocabulary (file_events_event_check)** (restates from: 0093_file_gateway.sql): 0073/0093's discovery drop (every CHECK on file_events whose definition mentions 'uploaded'), then ADD CONSTRAINT file_events_event_check CHECK (event IN ('uploaded','downloaded','moved','relinked','trashed','restored','purged','upload_abandoned','viewed_remote','refiled')), the full list restated. Post-condition: exactly one such CHECK, with ten terms.
- **public.fn_file_events_capture()** (restates from: 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql): 0074's whole body restated, plus one UPDATE branch after the storage_path branch: IF OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id THEN two INSERTs of event 'refiled' (source row: OLD.project_id, old_path OLD.storage_path, details {direction:'out', folder_id: OLD.folder_id}; destination row: NEW.project_id, new_path NEW.storage_path, details {direction:'in', folder_id: NEW.folder_id}), is_financial = file_event_is_financial(NEW.is_financial, OLD.storage_path, NEW.storage_path); ELSIF OLD.folder_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.folder_id THEN one 'refiled' row {direction:'within', from_folder_id, to_folder_id}. The EXCEPTION idiom is kept. Post-condition (comments stripped): the function still sets is_financial in every INSERT (0074's post-condition) and contains 'refiled'.
- **public.fn_realtime_broadcast()** (restates from: 0098_table_views.sql): 0098's whole body restated. The files arm: after the money RETURN NULL, IF TG_OP='UPDATE' AND OLD.project_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.project_id THEN PERFORM realtime.broadcast_changes('rabbit:project:'||OLD.project_id::text,'DELETE','DELETE','files','public',NULL,OLD); PERFORM realtime.broadcast_changes('rabbit:project:'||NEW.project_id::text,'INSERT','INSERT','files','public',NEW,NULL); RETURN NULL; END IF. Every table arm named in 0098's post-conditions is kept. Must be one restatement shared with the realtime / A-R1-04 designer if they also restate this function (they own fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast).

### Consumers to change

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/editHistoryRevert.js (files revert)**: A revert whose diff includes project_id or folder_id is not sent as a plain updateFile, which the guard trigger and the composite FK now refuse. It answers 'Use Move to put it back' instead. Other columns revert as before.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/FileAuditDrawer.jsx (event labels)**: Add 'refiled' with direction-aware words ('Moved in from another place' / 'Moved out' / 'Moved to another folder'), and the local 'trashed' / 'restored' events now written by the desktop.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx patchFile / markFileCoreDefiner (foreign-project branch)**: No code change. MEASURED: they send project_id = the row's own project, so the guard never fires. A stale or wrong projectId now fails loudly instead of silently moving the row (the latent cross-project move found by measurement).
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/realtimeMerge.js applyEventCore**: No change, verified by test: the files DELETE on the old topic removes the row, and the INSERT on the new topic upserts a live row. A test pins both.
- **electron/main.cjs reconcileRabbitBundle / emptyBundle**: Add a trashedFiles: [] default (dirty on first load) and trashedFiles: [] at birth.
- **electron/projectFilePatch.cjs mountProjectFilePatch (files PATCH)**: Refuse a body project_id that differs from req.params.projectId (409), and a folder_id that is not null and not in bundle.folders (400). This is the local twin of the guard trigger and the composite FK.
- **src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js NOT_CARRIED**: trashedFiles is not carried (the desktop trash stays local). Counted into NOT_CARRIED_SENTENCE as 'file in this computer's trash'.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/FileManager.jsx confirmDelete copy**: The Local Server confirm no longer promises a permanent delete. It reads 'Moves it to the trash for 30 days', the same promise as the cloud's. The stale comment 'Managed deletes go to the OS trash' is corrected, and the RELEASE_TESTING.md strings are found by grep.
- **.github/workflows/rls.yml (RLS_TABLES, failure-replay list) and src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js (tail pins)**: RLS_TABLES gains file_moves in the same commit as its suite. The replay list gains the file_moves suite and the table-less moth_trash suite. The last-five and last-six pins move.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/mutationsRegistry.test.js**: restoreFile and restoreManagedFile join the registry pin, as mutators named by history ops.
- **supabase/functions/storage-gc, storage-read-urls, storage-presign, _shared/teardownCollect.ts**: Owned by the body-location designer. They must treat the body as wanted, and as readable by the file's CURRENT project, after a move. My move never changes storage_path, so their key-shape assumptions hold. Their authority source changes, and that is the body designer's work.

### Client changes

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js: moveFiles({fileIds, folderIds, destProjectId, destFolderId}), undoFileMove(moveId), trashFiles(ids), restoreFiles(ids), listTrashedFiles({before})**: rpc('moth_move_items' | 'moth_undo_move' | 'moth_trash_files' | 'moth_restore_files' | 'moth_trash_index'). 'moth_move:<code>' / 'moth_trash:<code>' errors are mapped to {code} for the sentence table. listTrashedFiles turns only PGRST202, or a 42883 naming moth_trash_index, into [] (the narrowed listTrashedMilestones precedent). The existing restoreFile keeps its boolean contract. *(production caller: src/tools/moth/state/mothFileOps.js moveSelection / deleteSelection / restoreSelection, and src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.js entries (undo and redo), called from the Moth entry's Move to picker, Delete confirm and Trash view)*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/localServerAdapter.js: restoreFile(id, projectId), restoreManagedFile(id, projectId), trashFiles(ids, projectId), restoreFiles(ids, projectId), listTrashedFiles(projectId), moveFiles({projectId, moves})**: POST …/files/:id/restore, POST …/managed-files/:id/restore, POST …/files/trash {ids}, POST …/files/restore {ids}, GET …/trash, POST …/files/move. deleteFile keeps its URL, now a trash. One call per project: the caller loops over projects and records which went (err.doneIds), following the S5c-04 rule. *(production caller: src/tools/moth/state/mothFileOps.js (desktop-local projects), src/tools/moth/components/MothTrashView.jsx (local section), RabbitProvider.deleteFile / deleteManagedFile undo)*
- **src/dev/fixtures/rabbitFixturesAdapter.js: the same six method names in memory**: An in-memory twin, with the trash index honouring money exclusion for the fixture admin Mara Okonkwo, so the walkthrough can show it. *(production caller: Dev fixtures only (DEV-gated selectAdapter); exercised by the Moth walkthrough pictures)*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx: deleteFile, deleteManagedFile, new restoreFile / restoreManagedFile mutators (mutationsRef), new noteFilesChangedElsewhere(projectIds) on the context**: deleteFile: optimistic removal plus pushHistory({undoOps:[restore + re-insert snapshot], redoOps:[deleteFile]}, visit) and showUndoToast('File moved to the trash', () => undoHistoryEntry(token)). deleteManagedFile(soft) gets the same with restoreManagedFile. noteFilesChangedElsewhere reloads the active project when it is listed and realtimeLiveRef is false. *(production caller: FileManager.jsx confirmDelete (existing), and mothFileOps.js after each move, delete or restore)*
- **src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.js (new, lazy chunk)**: createMothHistory({cap: 10}) → {push, undo, redo, undoToken, clear, canUndo, canRedo, replaying}. Serialized queue, generation drop, refusal during replay, a failed undo re-inserts the entry. Keys are bound only while the Moth page is current. *(production caller: src/tools/moth/state/mothFileOps.js, and the Moth entry's key handler)*
- **src/tools/moth/state/mothFileOps.js (new, lazy chunk)**: moveSelection(selection, dest), deleteSelection(selection), restoreSelection(items). Each runs the confirm (the links that will be cleared; 'everyone on <project> will see it' when the audience widens), calls the adapter (cloud or local per item scope), pushes a mothHistory entry, shows the toast through useRabbit().showUndoToast, and calls noteFilesChangedElsewhere. Gating: canOnProject(ctx with ready, entity.write) per project, plus the owner-only personal-space predicate from the schema designer; denied controls go through GatedAction with the reason. *(production caller: Minimal Moth entry: MothMoveTo picker, the Delete button, MothTrashView)*
- **src/tools/moth/components/MothTrashView.jsx and MothMoveTo.jsx (new, lazy chunk)**: Trash view: cloud rows from listTrashedFiles plus local rows from listTrashedFiles(projectId) for each desktop-local project. Money is already excluded at the source. Shows the purges-in-N-days countdown, 'Could not look' kept apart from 'Nothing here', and Restore (single item restoreFile, several restoreFiles) gated by can_restore. Move to: a picker of projects and folders the person can write to, plus their personal space. Locked folders are never listed. *(production caller: The Moth page entry (Session 1's minimal lazy entry; another designer owns the route and lazy load))*
- **electron/fileTrash.cjs (new) + electron/main.cjs route mounts**: New module, so the routes run for real in tests (projectFilePatch.cjs / legalFiling.cjs precedent; main.cjs inline routes cannot be served in vitest): trashFileRow, restoreFileRow, locateTrashedBody, purgeExpiredLocalTrash, listLocalTrash (money excluded: is_financial, isLegalRow, legacy money folder names), moveWithinProject. main.cjs mounts it in place of the inline files DELETE route and calls purgeExpiredLocalTrash in GET /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId. *(production caller: localServerAdapter methods above, which Moth and RabbitProvider call)*

### Migration order

- **MV-A (first free number after the schema designer's allocation, from 0107)**: folders_id_project_key; composite FKs files_folder_same_project_fk and folders_parent_same_project_fk (NOT VALID, then VALIDATE); fn_files_move_guard and fn_folders_move_guard with their triggers; post-conditions; suite extensions 05_files, 52_folders.
  - *Constraints:* The rehearsal first counts cross-project folder_id rows and cross-project folder parents, and money rows filed outside locked folders, on dev and staging (a rolled-back query). If any are non-zero, the session stops and handles them before VALIDATE. 0043's post-conditions 2 and 3 are re-asserted, so a replay of 0043 is caught. No enum change. The two suite extensions go in the same commit. Whole pgTAP set run before push.
- **MV-B**: file_events_event_check widened (full list, restated from 0093) and fn_file_events_capture restated from 0074 with 'refiled'. fn_realtime_broadcast restated from 0098 with the files project-change split, or this arm handed to whichever migration the realtime / A-R1-04 designer writes, so only ONE restatement exists.
  - *Constraints:* A CHECK, not an enum, so one transaction is fine. Post-conditions: one vocabulary CHECK with ten terms; the capture body sets is_financial in every INSERT; the broadcast table-arm list exactly equals 0098's. Suites 33_file_lifecycle, 78_file_events_money, 20_realtime and 104 updated in the same commit.
- **MV-C**: file_moves table (ENABLE + FORCE RLS, no policies, populate and touch triggers) and its suite NNN_file_moves.sql. The RLS_TABLES line goes into rls.yml in the same commit, and the replay list and rlsSuiteLists.test.js tail pins are updated.
  - *Constraints:* Must land after the schema designer's personal-space column migration (provisionally projects.personal_owner_id) and before MV-D. The table must be in RLS_TABLES before anything depends on it.
- **MV-D**: fn_moth_apply_moves, moth_move_items, moth_undo_move, moth_trash_files, moth_restore_files, moth_trash_index, purge_file_moves plus the guarded cron. REVOKE and GRANT. Post-conditions on the comment-stripped definitions. Suites NNN_file_moves.sql (RPC probes) and NNN_moth_trash.sql (table-less, replay list only).
  - *Constraints:* Depends on MV-A (FKs, GUC guard), MV-B (refiled events the suites assert), MV-C, and the body designer's association hook. The cross-project arm must not be callable before that hook exists: a post-condition checks to_regprocedure of the hook and the core refuses cross-project moves with 'not_yet' when it is absent. 35_platform_audit must stay green: anon has EXECUTE on none of them. Rolled-back rehearsal of migration plus suite, then a file-by-file apply to wilson-dev only, with the history row md5.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/05_files.sql (extended) and 52_folders.sql (extended)**: Positive: an upload with a same-project folder_id inserts; the cascade SET NULL on a folder delete keeps the file row. Negative: a writer of both projects A and B runs a plain UPDATE files SET project_id=B and gets 42501 'use_checked_move' (planted control: DROP TRIGGER trg_files_move_guard and this probe goes red); a plain UPDATE setting folder_id to a B folder on an A file throws an FK violation (planted: restore the single-column FK and it goes red); a folder re-parented under another project's folder by a plain UPDATE is refused; a financial row filed into a non-locked folder is refused, while the same row in the INVOICES folder passes.
- **supabase/tests/rls/NNN_file_moves.sql (table and move RPCs)**: Table: authenticated SELECT/INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on file_moves all refused or zero rows; anon has no privilege; relforcerowsecurity is true. Moves, positive: a member who writes A and B moves a file A→B: row project_id=B, folder=dest, all seven links NULL, is_core_definer false, one journal row, two 'refiled' events (out in A, in B) with no foreign project id in details, an edit_history row; a within-project move keeps task_id; a custom folder subtree moves with recomputed paths and its files; undo returns project, folder, task_id and core flag exactly; redo (undo of the undo) works. Negative: a reviewer on A gets not_allowed_source; a manager moving into another member's private project gets not_found; an admin moving into another person's personal space gets not_found (planted: remove the owner clause and it goes red); an admin moving an INVOICES-path or is_financial file gets not_found with the same message text as a random uuid (planted: remove the money clause and it goes red); a workspace manager moving a Legal file gets not_found; a file whose id is in expenses.file_ids gets not_found; destination folder 'INVOICES' gets destination_locked; a destination folder in another project than the destination is refused; a folder into its own child gets into_itself; a path collision gets name_taken; a cross-workspace destination gets not_found; a trashed file gets not_found; a trashed destination project gets not_found; a local_server file gets body_on_a_computer; a file referenced by asset_versions gets is_an_asset_version; 501 items gets too_many. Atomicity: a selection of three good files plus one money file leaves all four unchanged (planted: move the UPDATE above the validation loop and it goes red). Undo by another user gets not_found; a second undo gets already_undone; undo after the file was moved again gets changed_since with nothing moved. Lock: a post-condition-style probe that the comment-stripped definition contains pg_advisory_xact_lock and FOR UPDATE (planted: strip the lock and it goes red).
- **supabase/tests/rls/NNN_moth_trash.sql (table-less, replay list)**: moth_trash_files trashes the caller's project files and returns n; a batch containing one invoice id trashes nothing even for an admin (planted: drop the money clause and it goes red); a reviewer is refused through fn_trash_authz; an admin is refused on another person's personal-space file through moth_trash_files, while soft_delete_row('files', id) still succeeds for that admin (the Files-view arm; both sides pinned). moth_trash_index: shows the caller's trashed files with purges_at = deleted_at + 30 days; hides invoices and Legal for the admin (planted: remove the clause and it goes red); hides another person's personal space from the admin; hides a private project's trash from a non-creator manager; hides files of a trashed project; hides rows older than 30 days (negative interval because now() is frozen); exposes no storage_path column; can_restore is false for a reviewer. moth_restore_files restores; an already-live row adds 0.
- **supabase/tests/rls/33_file_lifecycle.sql, 78_file_events_money.sql, 20_realtime.sql / 104_versions_and_items_realtime.sql (extended)**: The vocabulary CHECK accepts 'refiled' and still refuses an unknown term; the money flag rule is unchanged; the capture body sets is_financial (0074's pin); the broadcast arm list equals 0098's plus the files split present (definition pin, since CI has no realtime).
- **electron/fileTrash.test.js (new; served for real against a temp dir)**: Delete: the row leaves files for trashedFiles, the body is at <baseDir>/.trash/<id>-<name>, a 'trashed' event is logged. Restore: bundle.files gets back a row deep-equal to the original and the body bytes equal the original sha1 (planted: replace trashFileRow with today's unlink and this test goes red, the permanent-delete substitution). Crash windows: bundle written but rename skipped, then restore still works; rename back done but bundle not written, then a retry works. Containment: a trashed entry whose storage_path is '..\\..\\evil' is never unlinked or renamed outside baseDir (planted: bypass resolveContainedFilePath and it goes red). Purge: an entry 31 days old is unlinked with a 'purged' certificate blob_removed=true; a missing body gives blob_removed=false, never true; a 29-day entry is kept. Money: listLocalTrash omits is_financial, LEGAL and legacy money-folder rows. Within-project move: changes folder_id in one write; another bundle's folder is refused; a locked destination is refused; a cycle is refused. Token: the new routes answer 401 with no body and no token (applyLocalServerLock, localToken test pattern).
- **electron/projectFilePatch.test.js (extended)**: A PATCH with project_id ≠ the route param gets 409 and the bundle is unchanged; a folder_id not in bundle.folders gets 400; the existing Legal and tag rules are unchanged.
- **src/tools/moth/state/mothHistory.test.js (new)**: Cap of 10 each way; two quick undos run one at a time; a mutator during a replay is refused; an operation finishing after a generation bump pushes nothing (planted: remove the generation check and it goes red); a failed undo re-inserts the entry at its index; an R.A.B.B.I.T. project switch (setActiveProject) leaves the Moth stack intact.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/fileVerbsProvider.test.jsx and mutationsRegistry.test.js (extended)**: deleteFile pushes one step and Ctrl+Z calls adapter.restoreFile and puts the row back (red on the pre-change source); a delete that finishes after a project switch pushes nothing (visit guard); deleteManagedFile(soft) undo calls restoreManagedFile; the registry names restoreFile and restoreManagedFile.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter test file for the new methods; src/tools/moth writeGate coverage**: moveFiles sends typed arrays and never a kind/id list; a refusal code maps to its sentence; listTrashedFiles degrades only on a missing function, while a 42501 reaches the panel. writeGate.test.js finds a ready key in every Moth canOnProject ctx (the scan extended to src/tools/moth).

### Risks

- The body-location interface (another designer). After a cross-project move the body still sits under projects/<old>/…. The storage.objects policies, storage-read-urls, presign, storage-gc's orphan scan and teardown all derive authority or tenancy from that key, so a moved file could be unreadable in B, still readable in A, or collected when A is purged.
  - *Mitigation:* The interface is fixed here: the move never changes storage_path or thumbnail_url and calls the body designer's association hook inside the same transaction (a trigger AFTER UPDATE OF project_id on files, or an explicit PERFORM). Every key consumer must move to the CURRENT association. Until that hook exists, the core refuses cross-project moves with 'not_yet' (post-condition on to_regprocedure) and the client hides them by capability. Suites assert 'moved body survives old-project purge' once the hook lands.
- VALIDATE of the composite FKs fails on existing data (a cross-project folder_id or folder parent), or the money-folder guard refuses a legitimate upload path (for example a receipt filed into a non-locked folder by some hook).
  - *Mitigation:* Rolled-back count queries on dev and staging before MV-A. If non-zero, stop, then null the stray folder_ids in a recorded repair with counts in the hand-off. Grep the uploadFile / invoicesFolderHook / legal filing paths for the folder they set, and add a suite probe for each real upload shape.
- fn_realtime_broadcast and fn_file_events_capture are shared guard bodies. Another Session 1 designer (A-R1-04 realtime, history capture) may restate the same function, and the later restatement silently drops the earlier arm.
  - *Mitigation:* One owner per function per session. The plan's build step merges the arms into a single restatement from the latest body (0098, 0074), with post-conditions naming every arm, and suites 20 and 104 pin the definitions.
- Workspace-wide advisory lock: a large folder move (up to 500 items) briefly blocks other moves in the same company.
  - *Mitigation:* The 500-item cap; the transaction holds no network calls; moves are paced by people. Measure one 500-item move in the rehearsal and record it.
- Local trash: a crash between the bundle write and the body rename leaves the body at its origin, or an EPERM on a NAS leaves the body in place. The 30-day purge runs only when a project is opened, so bodies of never-opened projects linger.
  - *Mitigation:* locateTrashedBody always checks both places; certificates count only what unlink did; the limit is stated in the handbook §3.1 and in OUTSTANDING if it is not closed. Purge also runs on the trash read.
- An R.A.B.B.I.T. undo entry recorded before a Moth move (for example a patchFile on that file) replays updateFile with the old project_id.
  - *Mitigation:* The guard trigger refuses it (42501) and RabbitProvider's undo swallows per-op errors. Add a test that the replay fails closed and leaves the row in B. noteFilesChangedElsewhere reloads the open project.
- edit_history captures the project_id diff, and edit_history_select has no privacy arm (S4d-02), so a manager can read a private origin project's id in a moved file's history.
  - *Mitigation:* Already recorded as S4d-02, not widened beyond an id. The history privacy repair is the schema designer's (moth_history needs a privacy arm anyway). Flag it in OUTSTANDING if it stays open.
- The clearHistory call inside RabbitProvider (a remote project trash, a project switch) clears the shared undo toast, which may hold Moth's toast.
  - *Mitigation:* Moth's stack keeps the entry, so Ctrl+Z in Moth and the trash view still recover it. Documented; a test pins that the Moth stack survives clearHistory.
- Folder paths recomputed server-side contradict 0041's 'path computed client-side' parity rule.
  - *Mitigation:* Only concatenation of stored slugs (no slugify), the same rule folderPaths.js uses, done identically by fileTrash.cjs moveWithinProject. A vitest compares both against folderPaths.js output for the same tree.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* §4 New tables: 14 additions taking RLS_TABLES from 61 to 75
  - *Design:* Adds a 15th table, file_moves: a definer-only move journal for S4c-09, undo and redo. RLS_TABLES would go to 76, and suite numbers shift past the plan's 110–123 allocation.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Risk (handbook 3 (i)): composite same-project FKs (task_items, shot_list_items, bins) conflict with cross-project file moves
  - *Design:* MEASURED wrong: no FK references files(id, project_id) and no files_id_project key exists. task_items, shot_list_items and bins use composite keys onto tasks, assets, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, shot_lists and bins, never files. The only FKs into files are asset_versions.file_id, rate_cards.source_file_id and ingestion_chunks.file_id, all single-column SET NULL. The real cross-project hazard is files' OWN seven entity links and folder_id, which have no same-project check.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Brief §4.3 / plan: every move goes through the same mutators (R.A.B.B.I.T. adapter layer) so undo applies
  - *Design:* Moth moves and deletes go through adapter methods (new RPCs) and Moth's own undo stack, not RabbitProvider's per-project mutators and history. Those are per visit and are cleared by a project switch. The plan's 'without switching Rabbit active project' is met this way.
- *Plan:* Folders, E8: validate cycles, parents, scope changes/reserved folders
  - *Design:* Also adds composite (id, project_id) FKs on files.folder_id and folders.parent_id, plus a guard on project_id changes for files and folders. MEASURED: neither had a same-project check, and files.project_id is client-writable. Folder moves are limited to kind='custom' in Session 1.
- *Plan:* Restorable local delete via the project's .trash/
  - *Design:* Trashed bodies go to <baseDir>/.trash/ inside <slug>_FILES, INVOICES or LEGAL, not the project-root .trash/ (which remains for asset folders). This keeps the same volume (no EXDEV under a files_dir override) and the same NAS ACLs for money bodies. Rows move to a new bundle key trashedFiles.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Plan is silent on moves between desktop-local projects
  - *Design:* Session 1 refuses local-to-local and local-to-cloud moves; within one local project folder_id moves are supported. Managed files are not movable in Session 1.
- *Plan:* OUTSTANDING S4c-12: a lock against two moves at once
  - *Design:* Moth moves are locked (workspace advisory lock plus row locks). S4c's client-orchestrated shot refile remains unlocked against another refile, narrowed rather than closed, because a transaction lock cannot span its client steps.
- *Plan:* Trash/history: extend explicit allowlists (fn_trash_authz)
  - *Design:* fn_trash_authz and the 0014 RPCs are NOT restated. Moth wraps them in atomic batch RPCs that add the Moth-only exclusions (money for admins, owner-only personal spaces), so R.A.B.B.I.T. and Files keep admin reach.
- *Plan:* Files: a guard on project_id is needed regardless (measurement notes)
  - *Design:* Implemented as a transaction-local GUC guard set only by the checked-move core, the 0064 otter_nomination_apply precedent.

### Review — security (moves, deletes, trash and undo)

**Verdict:** Buildable once the fixes are in, but not as drafted. Three blockers have to be fixed before the cross-project arm can be switched on.

SEC-MV-01: the stable storage_path names the origin project. Destination readers see a private project's or personal space's id through the row, the INSERT broadcast and the 'refiled' event's new_path. Readers of the origin project can still read the moved body, because rabbit_files_select (0042) authorises by the key's second segment. The 'not_yet' switch tests only that the body designer's hook exists, not that storage authority moved.

SEC-MV-02: the claim that no custom folder holds a money row is false. Financial uploads are filed into entity folders or the folder they are dropped in (uploadFile l.3404). 0076 flipped receipts in place. Custom folders sit under INVOICES. Expense links can be written by the client. So step (4)'s not_found on a visible folder reveals that hidden money exists, and guard (b) as written would also break receipt uploads.

SEC-MV-03: a folder move takes trashed subtree files into a wider audience, where they become listable and restorable.

Six majors follow. ingestion_chunks keep a moved file's text in the origin project. moth_trash_index has no membership guard. The generic trash RPCs rely on is_private and created_by for personal spaces. Move diffs land in edit_history, which has no privacy arm (S4d-02). The desktop routes skip the Moth money exclusion. The Move to picker inherits canOnProject's admin bypass.

The core shape is sound: typed arrays, a definer-only journal, undo that replays the server's own record, an atomic batch over soft_delete_row, the composite FKs, and the existing desktop launch-token gate. Within-project moves and the delete, restore and trash paths can ship once SEC-MV-02's re-home has run and the majors are fixed. Cross-project moves stay refused until SEC-MV-01, SEC-MV-04 and SEC-MV-07 are fixed.

- **SEC-MV-01 [blocker]** A body-preserving cross-project move leaves storage_path (and thumbnail_url) naming the ORIGIN project, so the origin project's uuid reaches every reader of the destination. It arrives three ways: the files row (files_select hands storage_path to every reader), the realtime INSERT carrying NEW on rabbit:project:{dest}, and the destination's 'refiled' event, whose new_path = NEW.storage_path. MV-8 drops the other project's id from details to protect a private origin's id, but new_path carries the same id. A second problem: the 'not_yet' switch that enables cross-project moves tests only that the body designer's hook exists (to_regprocedure). It does not test that body authority stopped coming from the key. Until it does, rabbit_files_select still lets every reader of the ORIGIN project read the moved body by its key.
  - *Scenario:* (a) An owner moves 'pitch_v3.pdf' from her personal space PS (or a private project P) into shared project S. Every reader of S now sees storage_path 'projects/<PS uuid>/project/<PS uuid>/…' in the row, in the INSERT broadcast and in file_events. They learn that the personal space or private project exists and its id. (b) Reverse case: a manager moves a file from shared S into private P. S readers saw the row before the move (and the DELETE broadcast on S's topic carries OLD.storage_path). With storage.from('rabbit-files').download(key) they can still read the body, because the key's segment 2 is S and S passes projects_select.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0042_money_segments_and_manifest_rewrite.sql rabbit_files_select (authority = projects row for fn_try_uuid(foldername[2]), no files-row check); key shape projects/<pid>/<seg>/… in supabaseAdapter.js uploadFile l.3427-3446; 0098 fn_realtime_broadcast v_row := COALESCE(to_jsonb(NEW), to_jsonb(OLD)) (the whole row is broadcast); 0092 file_events_select (can_read_project_topic(project_id), no path redaction); design MV-8 'refiled' destination row new_path NEW.storage_path; design risk 1's 'not_yet' gated on to_regprocedure of the hook.
  - *Fix:* (1) Enable the cross-project arm on a post-condition over the storage policies, not on the hook's existence. rabbit_files_select/_insert/_update/_delete_own (0042), rabbit_thumbnails_* (0053) and the eight money policies (0092 §4) must resolve authority through the body association / current files.project_id. They must no longer read foldername[2]. Suite probe: after a move from S to P, a reader of S downloading by the old key gets 0 rows (planted: restore 0042's policy and it goes red). (2) A key must never name a project the reader cannot see. Either the body designer gives personal-space uploads a project-neutral key from day one (for example bodies/<body_id>/<name>), and the core refuses (not_yet) a cross-project move whose source key names a project other than the destination, or the move re-keys inside the same bucket. Re-keying is not relocation to another store, but it needs her confirmation as a reading of her ruling. (3) 'refiled' rows carry no path: drop new_path from the 'in' row (and old_path from the 'out' row, for symmetry). Probe: a destination reader's 'refiled' row contains no origin uuid anywhere (row_to_json text LIKE '%'||origin||'%' is false).
- **SEC-MV-02 [blocker]** MV-7's premise ('a custom folder can never contain a money row, so a folder move never meets a hidden file') is false. Step (4) then makes the folder move an existence oracle for hidden money rows: a visible folder is refused with not_found because of rows the caller cannot see. Three facts break the premise. First, uploadFile files a financial upload into its entity folder, or into the folder it is dropped in, and uses INVOICES only when neither exists. Second, 0076 flipped receipts to is_financial in place without touching folder_id. Third, custom folders may sit under INVOICES (path 'INVOICES/x'), and the design's guard admits money rows there. The guard as drafted also fires only on INSERT/UPDATE, so existing rows keep breaking the invariant. Expense membership makes it worse. expenses.file_ids is a client-writable uuid[] with no trigger, and a receipt on a BYO-S3 workspace could not be flipped (0076 TRAP 1). Such a file is visible to every member through files_select but excluded by Moth, so a refusal reveals that it is attached to an expense (manager-only money data). Separately, as written, guard (b) would refuse today's receipt uploads onto a task or asset (entity folder).
  - *Scenario:* A plain member sees custom folder 'Locations/Permits' in Moth, containing 3 visible files. It also holds a receipt uploaded from a task's budget line, or a file a manager attached to an expense. The member drags the folder to another project and gets 'not_found' for a folder they are looking at. Repeating this with sub-selections finds exactly which folder holds hidden money and which visible file is an expense receipt. Likewise, moving 'INVOICES/2024' (a custom folder) is refused differently from an empty custom folder.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js uploadFile l.3375-3385 (folderRow from container.key for non-Legal, financial included) and l.3404 ('if (scope.financial && !scope.folderId && !folderRow)': INVOICES only as the fallback), l.3427 folderId; supabase/migrations/0076_receipts_are_money.sql statement 1 (UPDATE files SET is_financial = true, folder_id untouched) and TRAP 1 (non-supabase receipts stay unflagged); 0037 expenses.file_ids UUID[] with no FK; projectFileStream.test.js l.427 (custom folders 'INVOICES/x', 'INVOICES/x/y'); src/cloud/migrate/migrationRows.js cloudHomeOfFile (money with a desktop home folder keeps that folder).
  - *Fix:* Make the invariant true before relying on it, and refuse by folder shape, never by content. (1) Re-home in one recorded repair: every money row (file_row_is_money, or expense-linked) whose folder_id is not under a locked first segment gets the project's INVOICES category folder (a non-Legal row) or NULL (Legal). Count it on dev and staging first and post-condition the count to zero. (2) Change uploadFile and cloudHomeOfFile so that financial files always take INVOICES. That is the production caller the guard needs; otherwise guard (b) breaks receipt uploads on entities. (3) Extend guard (b) to expense membership through a trigger on expenses.file_ids, or have the source check below decide alone. (4) Step 7 refuses any source folder whose path first segment satisfies rabbit_money_segment, and any subtree that contains one, with destination_locked or not_custom_folder. This check runs BEFORE any per-file content check. (5) If a content check must remain as a backstop, it uses the same not_movable code for every hidden cause and is tested to be unreachable after the repair. Probes: a member moving a custom folder that holds a planted hidden receipt gets the same answer as for a folder that holds none, or the receipt has already been re-homed (planted: skip the re-home and it goes red).
- **SEC-MV-03 [blocker]** A folder move carries the subtree's TRASHED files into the destination project, links cleared and journalled. From then on they appear in moth_trash_index to every reader of the destination, and every destination writer can restore them through moth_restore_files or restore_soft_deleted. Their names are broadcast on the destination topic (the INSERT payload of a trashed row reaches the topic even though the client ignores it), and they get 'refiled' rows in the destination's file_events. The mover never sees trashed files in Moth, so the confirmation ('everyone on <project> will see it') counts only live items.
  - *Scenario:* An owner trashes 'salary_counteroffer.pdf' inside her personal-space folder 'Pitch', then moves 'Pitch' into shared project S. Every S reader sees the file's name in the Moth trash, with deleted_by_label = her name. Any S writer presses Restore, and the file comes back live in S with its body readable to everyone on S.
  - *Evidence:* Design MV-2 step (4) 'including trashed files in a moved subtree' and MV-6 (subtree files keep folder_id and change project_id); design MV-11 moth_trash_index (rows are files of readable live projects, deleted in the last 30 days, can_restore = can_write_project); MEASURED supabase/migrations/0014_soft_delete.sql restore_soft_deleted → 0092 fn_trash_authz (can_write_project + passes_project_privacy on the row's CURRENT project only); 0092 file_events_select has no deleted_at filter.
  - *Fix:* Choose one: refuse a cross-project folder move whose subtree holds any trashed file (code has_trashed_items; the person empties or restores them first in their own trash), or detach those files at the origin before the move. The composite FK requires a same-project folder, so detaching means setting folder_id to NULL in the origin project and leaving the rows there, journalled. Never let them change project. Probe: a subtree containing one trashed file moved from PS to S leaves moth_trash_index for an S reader with 0 rows and S's file_events with no row for that file (planted: drop the clause and it goes red).
- **SEC-MV-04 [major]** The design refuses files referenced by asset_versions but not files referenced by ingestion_chunks. Those rows hold the file's extracted raw_text and parsed_json under an ingestion_runs row tied to the ORIGIN project, and ingestion_chunks_select admits any reader in the workspace through the run's project. After a cross-project move into a private project or a personal space, the document's text stays readable through the origin project.
  - *Scenario:* An ingested script in shared S is moved into a member's personal space. Every S reader can still list ingestion_chunks for S's runs and read the script's raw_text, linked by file_id to a file they can no longer see. INFERRED: no production writer of chunks exists today (upsertIngestionChunk has callers only in adapters and fixtures), so the exposure covers historical rows and any future intake writer.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0000_rabbit_base_schema.sql ingestion_chunks (run_id → ingestion_runs.project_id, file_id SET NULL, raw_text, parsed_json); 0004_rls_rabbit.sql ingestion_chunks_select (EXISTS ingestion_runs JOIN projects WHERE p.workspace_id = current_workspace_id(): workspace only, no privacy arm); design MV-2 step (8) refuses only asset_versions.
  - *Fix:* Add a step (8) refusal 'has_extracted_text' when EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM ingestion_chunks c WHERE c.file_id = f.id), which matches the existing is_an_asset_version rule, or delete the chunks in the same transaction, journalled, so undo cannot restore them. Add a probe with a planted chunk, red when the clause is removed. Record that ingestion_chunks_select has no privacy arm in OUTSTANDING if it is not closed here.
- **SEC-MV-05 [major]** moth_trash_index, as specified, filters by f.workspace_id = current_workspace_id() with no has_active_membership check. current_workspace_id() reads the JWT's app_metadata, so a deactivated member whose token has not yet expired can list trashed file metadata (names, sizes, project titles, who deleted them) across every non-private project. The 0082 precedent the design names (milestones_trash_index) raises not_a_workspace_member first. MV-2 and MV-10 state the membership check; MV-11's WHERE clause leaves it out.
  - *Scenario:* An admin deactivates a contractor at 10:00. Until the contractor's access token expires, a call to rpc('moth_trash_index') still returns up to 500 trashed file names and project titles for the company.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0082_private_project_definers.sql milestones_trash_index ('IF v_ws IS NULL OR NOT public.has_active_membership(v_ws) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION not_a_workspace_member'); 0001 current_workspace_id reads the JWT claim; design schema_changes moth_trash_index WHERE clause (no membership term).
  - *Fix:* Open the function body with 0082's exact guard, IF v_ws IS NULL OR NOT has_active_membership(v_ws) THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'not_a_workspace_member'. Do the same in moth_undo_move before the journal lookup, so it does not depend on the order inside fn_moth_apply_moves. Probe: an is_active=false member gets the exception (planted: remove the guard and it goes red). Add a post-condition that the comment-stripped body contains has_active_membership.
- **SEC-MV-06 [major]** The design keeps fn_trash_authz unchanged and lets admins reach personal spaces through the generic soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted. But fn_trash_authz knows a personal space only through passes_project_privacy (is_private, created_by = auth.uid(), or admin) and through can_write_project (admin, manager, any member of an unstaffed project). The Moth arms read a separate column, projects.personal_owner_id. Unless a personal space is always is_private = true with created_by = its owner, and both can never change, the generic RPCs let managers, and every member when the space is unstaffed, trash and restore another person's personal-space files by id. Moves also hand those ids around: everyone who could read a file before it was moved into a personal space knows its id.
  - *Scenario:* A personal space is provisioned by a definer, so created_by is the provisioner or NULL, or it is not is_private. A manager who saw 'notes.docx' in shared S before its owner moved it into her personal space calls rpc('soft_delete_row', {p_table:'files', p_id}). fn_trash_authz admits the manager (can_write_project's manager arm; privacy passes) and her file is trashed. restore_soft_deleted likewise un-trashes her deleted files.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0092_legal_gate_managers.sql fn_trash_authz (v_allowed := can_write_project(v_project); privacy only through passes_project_privacy); 0082 passes_project_privacy (NOT is_private OR created_by = auth.uid() OR admin); core fact: can_write_project 0013 (admin|manager OR unstaffed OR seat). Design MV-10 ('fn_trash_authz is not restated') and its test pins only the admin-success side.
  - *Fix:* Choose one and pin it. (a) The schema designer guarantees that a personal space is is_private = true with created_by = personal_owner_id, enforced by a CHECK plus an immutability trigger on is_private, created_by and personal_owner_id for personal rows. (b) Restate fn_trash_authz from 0092 with one added arm: a personal-space row is the owner's or an admin's. Add probes on the generic path: a manager is refused soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted on another person's personal-space file, and so is a member of an unstaffed space (planted: drop the arm and it goes red).
- **SEC-MV-07 [major]** The move writes an edit_history diff that names the origin project and the seven cleared link ids from the origin (task, asset, scene and so on). edit_history_select admits every admin and manager with no privacy arm (S4d-02). The design's risk entry says the leak is 'not widened beyond an id'. In fact moves make it the route by which ids of personal spaces and private projects, and their entities, land on a destination row's history, and personal spaces are a new audience that her ruling closes to managers entirely.
  - *Scenario:* An owner moves a file from her personal space into S. A workspace manager opens the file's history in S and reads project_id {old: <personal space uuid>, new: S}, together with the old task_id and asset_id from inside her space.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0092_legal_gate_managers.sql edit_history_select (current_app_role IN admin|manager + membership + money arm, no projects hop and no privacy term); fn_edit_history_money_snapshot sets NEW.project_id (0092 l.962); 0012 trg_files_edit_history captures full diffs.
  - *Fix:* Treat S4d-02 as a blocker for the cross-project arm: add the privacy and personal-owner arm to edit_history_select keyed on the snapshot project_id, and also on the diff's old project_id for files rows, before 'not_yet' lifts. Or, as a minimum, the move core writes its history row with the origin-side values redacted when the origin fails the destination audience's privacy. Probe: a manager reads no history row naming a personal space they do not own.
- **SEC-MV-08 [major]** The desktop twin does not enforce the Moth money exclusion or the money-folder invariant on its new write routes. Only listLocalTrash filters money. POST …/files/trash, …/files/restore and …/files/move (moveWithinProject) accept any id in the bundle, so Moth can move an invoice's or receipt's folder_id out of INVOICES into a custom folder, which the cloud guard forbids. On a later desktop-to-cloud migration, cloudHomeOfFile files the money row into that custom folder: either guard (b) refuses the insert, or the money row ends up in a movable custom folder (the SEC-MV-02 class).
  - *Scenario:* Moth on the Local Server posts {moves:[{kind:'file', id:<invoice id>, to_folder_id:<custom>}]}. The route checks only that the destination is not locked, writes the bundle, and the invoice now sits in 'Locations'. The cloud migration later inserts it with folder_id = that folder.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: src/cloud/migrate/migrationRows.js desktopFolderOfFile / cloudHomeOfFile (a money file keeps its desktop folder); electron/main.cjs resolveFileBaseDir (money-ness locally = is_financial / isLegalRow). Design MV-14 (destination checks only) and MV-12 (money exclusion only in listLocalTrash).
  - *Fix:* fileTrash.cjs gets a single isMothExcluded(row, bundle) check: is_financial, isLegalRow, a legacy money-folder name, or the row id in any bundle expense's file_ids. The Moth batch routes (trash, restore, move) answer 404 for an excluded id before anything else. The R.A.B.B.I.T. single-file DELETE and restore routes keep money for the desktop user. moveWithinProject and the projectFilePatch twin refuse moving a money row's folder_id to a folder outside a locked first segment. Test with a planted invoice id, red when the check is removed.
- **SEC-MV-09 [major]** The Move to picker is specified as 'projects and folders the person can write to, plus their personal space', gated by canOnProject(entity.write). canOnProject returns true for every app admin and manager on every project, and projectsIndex holds other people's private projects and personal spaces for admins, because projects_select admits admins. Unless personal spaces are filtered out BEFORE canOnProject runs, the picker shows an admin the names of other people's personal spaces inside Moth. Her ruling allows admins to reach personal spaces from the Files view only. The server's not_found does not help, because the leak is the list itself.
  - *Scenario:* An admin opens Move to in Moth and the searchable list includes 'Dana's space' and 'Lee's space'.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js canOnProject (the 'current_app_role() IN (admin, manager) bypasses every gate below' short-circuit after l.440; ready === false → true at l.402); core client fact 3 (projectsIndex includes private projects for admins) and the measurement that the workspace channel handler does not suppress private rows.
  - *Fix:* Build the picker from a single source: projects where (personal_owner_id IS NULL OR personal_owner_id = me) AND canOnProject(ready, entity.write). Do the same for the Moth trash view's local section and any project chooser. Add a test with the fixture admin and another user's personal space in projectsIndex that asserts it is absent from MothMoveTo's options (planted: drop the filter and it goes red). Never render the list while ready is false.
- **SEC-MV-10 [minor]** Step 7 checks kind = 'custom' on the selected folder only. The schema lets entity and category folders sit under a custom folder (folders_kind_chk, no parent-kind rule), and an entity folder carries an asset_id, scene_id or similar FK with no same-project check. A cross-project subtree move would carry an origin-project entity link and slug (often the entity's name) into the destination.
  - *Scenario:* A custom folder in private P holds an entity folder for asset 'Untitled Villain'. Moving the custom folder to S shows S readers a folder named after P's asset, and deleting the asset in P cascades into S's tree.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0041_folders.sql folders_kind_chk, the folders_entity_link_chk entity FKs (ON DELETE CASCADE, no project check), parent_id with no kind rule.
  - *Fix:* Refuse with not_custom_folder unless every folder in the recursive subtree is kind = 'custom' with all entity FKs NULL. Add a probe for it.
- **SEC-MV-11 [minor]** The bypass GUC is a boolean 'on'. Every house precedent binds the bypass to a specific id (wilson.otter_cr_apply = cr id, otter_nomination_apply = nomination id, shot_list_activate = project id), and the guard compares that id with the row. With 'on', any project_id change to any files or folders row in the same transaction passes, for example a future trigger or a nested call.
  - *Scenario:* A later migration adds a trigger that, under some condition, updates another files row's project_id during a move. The guard admits it silently.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0025_otter_cr_apply.sql l.591, 0064 l.800-832, 0084 l.956-1068 (set_config(name, <id>, true)); design MV-7(b) uses 'on'.
  - *Fix:* Set wilson.checked_move to the move id, and stage the allowed (id, to_project) pairs in a temporary table or a jsonb GUC. The guard admits NEW.id only when it is in the set with NEW.project_id equal to the recorded destination. Probe: a second row updated in the same transaction is refused.
- **SEC-MV-12 [minor]** Several refusal paths are not pinned as oracle-free. Step (6) 'destination folder must be in the destination project' has no code in the published set; it must be not_found, so that a folder id from another workspace or a private project cannot be told apart from an unknown one. moth_undo_move checks changed_since (every item at its to_* and live, read with RLS bypassed) BEFORE fn_moth_apply_moves runs the read gate. A caller who has lost access to a destination therefore learns whether the file has been moved or trashed since.
  - *Scenario:* A member passes dest_folder = a folder uuid from a private project they cannot see and gets something other than not_found. Or they call undo on an old move into a project that has since become private and learn from changed_since versus not_found whether its files are still there.
  - *Evidence:* Design MV-2 step (6) and its code set; design MV-5 order of checks.
  - *Fix:* Step (6) answers not_found (and a probe compares its message with that of a random uuid). moth_undo_move runs the read gate on every to_project and from_project, answering not_found, before the changed_since comparison.
- **SEC-MV-13 [minor]** The local trash recomputes the base directory with resolveFileBaseDir. For a financial row, that function picks INVOICES or _FILES by checking where the body currently is (fs.existsSync), and it falls back to the canonical INVOICES dir when the body is in neither place. Once a body has moved into <dir>/.trash, restore and purge recompute the canonical dir and look in INVOICES/.trash. A legacy invoice body trashed from <slug>_FILES/.trash is therefore never found: restore fails, and the 30-day purge certifies blob_removed=false while the money body stays on disk indefinitely.
  - *Scenario:* A desktop invoice still in its legacy _FILES location is deleted. Thirty days later the purge logs 'purged', blob_removed=false, and the invoice PDF remains in <slug>_FILES/.trash, invisible to every view.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/main.cjs resolveFileBaseDir l.2187-2202 (existsSync selection and the canonical fallback); legalFiling.baseDirFor has the same 'where the body actually is' fallback.
  - *Fix:* Record the resolved base directory kind (files | invoices | legal | internal) in the trashedFiles entry when the row is trashed. Restore, locateTrashedBody and purge use the recorded kind, and the result stays contained by resolveContainedFilePath. Test: a legacy-location invoice trashed and then purged has blob_removed=true and is gone from disk.
- **SEC-MV-14 [minor]** The design makes GET /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId destructive: it runs purgeExpiredLocalTrash, which unlinks bodies. The launch token is accepted from a cookie set with sameSite 'lax', which browsers send on cross-site top-level GET navigations. A safe method gains a side effect that a navigation from any page can trigger. The impact is limited to items already past 30 days.
  - *Scenario:* A web page the user visits navigates to http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/rabbit/projects/<pid>. The cookie is sent and expired trashed bodies are unlinked earlier than the user's own open would have removed them.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/main.cjs l.5060-5065 (cookies.set ... sameSite: 'lax'); electron/localToken.cjs applyLocalServerLock accepts the cookie (l.198-200); design MV-12 purges in the project GET.
  - *Fix:* Run the purge on the POST trash routes and on the trash read made with the header token, or require the header (not the cookie) for any route that unlinks. Leave the project GET side-effect free.

### Review — Correctness and house rules (moves, deletes, trash and undo; cloud and desktop)

**Verdict:** It can be built once the fixes are in, but not as written. One finding blocks: the money-folder arm of trg_files_move_guard refuses rows that the shipped desktop-to-cloud migration creates (receipts and Legal files filed in their desktop folder) and its receipt repair. Every later edit of such a row is refused too, because patchFile always sends project_id. The fn_realtime_broadcast restatement also turns five pinned pgTAP probes red (95 #170, 99 #14, 100 #7, 101 #16 and #18), and the design names none of them. The desktop trash finds a body's directory by checking where it exists, and that check gives a different answer once the body sits in .trash. The client cannot gate Move to for projects other than the open one. The exclusion misses files under locked folders. Folder trash, which the plan requires, is missing. Separately confirmed sound: the latest bodies named (0098, 0074, 0093) are the latest; no foreign key references files(id, project_id); the composite FKs pass 0043's post-conditions on PG 17 (confdeltype 'n'); the CHECK widening by discovery drop is safe beside 0093's shape CHECKs; soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted return booleans, as the wrappers assume; realtimeMerge handles the DELETE/INSERT split with no change; the new routes sit behind applyLocalServerLock and none collides with an existing one; 35_platform_audit and the RLS_TABLES, replay and tail-pin plan are correct.

- **MV-R1 [blocker]** The money-folder arm of trg_files_move_guard (MV-7b) refuses rows that shipped writers create, and it does so for every later UPDATE of those rows. The trigger refuses a money or Legal row whose folder_id is not under a locked folder, and it fires on UPDATE OF project_id. Every client file patch sends project_id, so a single stray row cannot even have its tags edited. The rehearsal count only cleans existing data; the writers keep producing new stray rows after it.
  - *Scenario:* (1) An admin runs the desktop-to-cloud migration on a bundle with a receipt (or a Legal file) filed in an asset or custom folder on the desktop. cloudHomeOfFile returns that desktop folder, because it returns `desk` before it would choose INVOICES. The INSERT is_financial=true with folder_id=<non-locked folder> fails with 23514, so the files step fails. (2) The M2-L4 repair moveReceipt runs UPDATE files SET is_financial=true, storage_path=<INVOICES key> without touching folder_id. A receipt that an earlier run filed in its desktop folder is refused, so the receipt stays outside the money gate. (3) Any such row afterwards: patchFile sends project_id, the trigger fires, and the tag or description edit fails.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/cloud/migrate/migrationRows.js cloudHomeOfFile (`if (desk || !money || … isLegalFile(file)) return desk`); src/cloud/migrate/runMigration.js cloudFolderOfFile, the row build `folder_id: cloudFolderOfFile(f, bundle, folders, isMoney)` with `if (isMoney) row.is_financial = true`, and moveReceipt's `.update({ is_financial: true, storage_path: to, thumbnail_url: thumbTo })`; src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx patchFile / markFileCoreDefiner (they always send project_id); supabaseAdapter.uploadFile (a financial upload takes the container's entity folderRow before INVOICES).
  - *Fix:* Drop the money-folder arm from the trigger. The Moth exclusion already refuses money and Legal rows for moves. For a folder move, refuse with the same not_found if the subtree holds any money, Legal or expense row. If the arm is wanted anyway: fire it only when folder_id actually changes (IF OLD.folder_id IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.folder_id), and in the same commit change cloudHomeOfFile/cloudFolderOfFile so money and Legal rows get INVOICES (or NULL), and make moveReceipt set folder_id. Add suite probes for the migration's insert shape and moveReceipt's update shape.
- **MV-R2 [major]** Restating fn_realtime_broadcast as designed turns pgTAP red in five pinned probes that the design does not list. The design names only suites 20 and 104. The proposed PERFORM realtime.broadcast_changes(…,'files','public',…) adds two more literal 'files' to the body.
  - *Scenario:* CI replays from 0000 after MV-B: 101_table_views #18 (md5 = 'e5abdcf2…', 0098's body) fails; 100_no_main_item #7 (md5 IN 0096/0098) fails; 101_table_views #16 and 99_task_items #14 ('files' named exactly once) fail on the two new 'files' literals; 95_legal_gate_managers #170 fails if the split is placed between the money END IF and `v_project := (v_row ->> 'project_id')::uuid;` (it matches the files arm's whole text up to that line). 0098's §0 pre-flight also refuses any later replay of 0098 once the md5 changes.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/tests/rls/101_table_views.sql probes 16 and 18; 100_no_main_item.sql probe 7; 99_task_items.sql probe 14; 95_legal_gate_managers.sql probe 170 (substring from 'WHEN ''files'' THEN' to the v_project assignment); supabase/migrations/0098_table_views.sql §0 pre-flight (md5 guard) and the post-condition naming §10's md5.
  - *Fix:* Put the split AFTER the v_project assignment and use TG_TABLE_NAME / TG_TABLE_SCHEMA, so the body still names 'files' once. In the same commit, update 100 #7, 101 #18 (new md5), 95 #170 if the arm text changes, and count 99 #14 and 101 #16 again. Add the new md5 to the restating migration's own post-condition and record that 0098 cannot be replayed after it (handbook §4.9 row).
- **MV-R3 [major]** The desktop trash works out where a trashed body is by calling resolveFileBaseDir again at restore and purge time. That function, and legalFiling.baseDirFor, choose the directory by checking where the body exists. Once the body has been renamed into .trash, it exists at neither origin, so they answer with the canonical home and not the directory used at trash time. locateTrashedBody then searches the wrong .trash. A relink that changes files_dir strands every trashed body the same way, because relink-apply's stranded check only looks at bundle.files.
  - *Scenario:* An invoice was uploaded while the project had no folder, so its body sits in <slug>_FILES. The project folder is set later. A person deletes the invoice in R.A.B.B.I.T.'s FileManager: the body goes to <slug>_FILES/.trash/<id>-<name>. On Undo or Restore, resolveFileBaseDir finds the body in neither INVOICES nor FILES and returns INVOICES, so the restore searches INVOICES/.trash and fails. After 30 days the purge records blob_removed=false and the body stays on disk for good. The same happens to any trashed file after a Files & Storage relink moves files_dir.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. electron/main.cjs resolveFileBaseDir (the `here`/`legacy` existsSync checks, falling back to invoicesDir); electron/legalFiling.cjs baseDirFor (`holds(filesDir, …)` falling back to legalDirs[0]); main.cjs relink-apply (`stranded = bundle.files.filter(…)`, `bundle.project.files_dir = path.resolve(baseDir)`).
  - *Fix:* At trash time, record in the trashedFiles entry the base dir that was used, as a contained path relative to the project root or a tag (files | invoices | legal | internal). Restore and purge resolve from that record, with resolveContainedFilePath against the recorded base. relink-apply must also move or refuse while trashedFiles have bodies under the old files_dir. Add fileTrash.test.js cases for the legacy invoice dir and for a relink between trash and restore.
- **MV-R4 [major]** Moth's client gate cannot be computed for any project other than the open one. canOnProject(entity.write) needs projectRole and isStaffed, and the client holds the seat only for R.A.B.B.I.T.'s open project. The Move to picker ('projects and folders the person can write to') and the per-project Delete and Restore gates therefore either deny members who can write, or follow app role alone.
  - *Scenario:* A plain member staffed as member on project B, who is not R.A.B.B.I.T.'s open project, picks Move to. canOnProject gets projectRole undefined. The ProjectFilesExplorer precedent fails closed to admin/manager, so B is not offered or is greyed, although can_write_project(B) is true on the server. Her main use, a personal space into a project, fails for ordinary members.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/permissions/projectRoleMatrix.js canOnProject (destructures appRole, projectRole, isStaffed); moth1_core E14 (the client holds ctx.myProjectRole for the open project only; ProjectFilesExplorer fails closed to admin/manager for other projects); useProjectAccess as the single assembler.
  - *Fix:* Add a definer read, e.g. moth_writable_projects() returning (project_id, can_write) from can_write_project plus the personal-space owner clause, REVOKEd from anon. Feed the per-project ctx from it, with `ready` set until it settles. Keep projectRoleMatrix in lockstep and extend writeGate.test.js's scan to src/tools/moth.
- **MV-R5 [major]** The Moth exclusion misses one part of the plan's shared rule: canonical locked-folder descendants. MV-2(4), moth_trash_index, moth_trash_files and listLocalTrash test only the row classifiers (file_row_is_money, file_row_is_legal) and expenses.file_ids. A file that is not flagged but is filed inside the INVOICES folder is listed in Moth's trash and can be moved out.
  - *Scenario:* A row has is_financial=false, a storage_path whose third segment is not a money segment, and folder_id = the project's INVOICES category folder. Such a row can come from uploadFile, which honours scope.folderId, or from migration placement through the desktop folder home. A reviewer sees it in Moth's trash index, and a writer moves it out of INVOICES with Move to. Her ruling excludes INVOICES, FINANCE and LEGAL folders and files for everyone.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: docs/sessions/PLAN_MOTH.md §4 'Shared rules' ('Exclude financial files/canonical locked-folder descendants, including Legal'); supabaseAdapter.uploadFile (`folderId = legal ? null : (scope.folderId || folderRow?.id || null)`); 0041 folders (the INVOICES category, path 'INVOICES'). INFERRED: whether such rows exist today (no current UI caller passes folderId).
  - *Fix:* Write one internal SQL helper, e.g. fn_moth_file_excluded(file row), that ORs the classifiers, expense membership and the file's folder sitting under a folder whose split_part(path,'/',1) is a money segment. REVOKE it from client roles and call it from fn_moth_apply_moves, moth_trash_files/moth_restore_files and moth_trash_index. Add a planted control. Mirror it in listLocalTrash (bundle.folders ancestry).
- **MV-R6 [major]** Scope gaps around folders. Session 1's Folders row (E8) asks for a reversible, independent trash for folders, and boards are real folders. Her delete ruling also covers deleting from Moth. The design has no folder delete, trash or restore: folders hard-delete, and the new composite parent FK keeps ON DELETE CASCADE. Separately, nothing creates kind='custom' folders today, so the folder-move arm (cloud MV-2(7) and local MV-14) has no production input.
  - *Scenario:* A person deletes a folder (or later a board-folder) in Moth with confirmation. There is no soft-delete path, so it either cannot be offered or it hard-deletes the subtree with no undo or trash entry, against her ruling. Meanwhile the folder-move code can only be reached from pgTAP.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. PLAN_MOTH.md §4 'Existing changes' Folders row ('Board-folder identity/reversible independent trash; preserve children/bodies'); 0041_folders.sql (no deleted_at; parent_id ON DELETE CASCADE; COMMENT 'custom is unused until S27'). Grep: no writer of kind 'custom' in src/ or electron/ (main.cjs has folders/ensure, ensure-entity, refile-shots and DELETE only).
  - *Fix:* Either add folders.deleted_at with folders_select filtering it, restate fn_trash_authz from 0092 and purge_soft_deleted from 0067 with folders on their allowlists, and give Moth a folder trash. Or record the cut as a material plan difference for her. Name which session or designer writes custom folders, or drop folder sources from Session 1.
- **MV-R7 [major]** Moves out of the personal space depend on a precondition the design does not state. MV-2(8) refuses local_server bodies cross-project with body_on_a_computer. uploadFile sends every non-money upload of an is_private project to LOCAL_SERVER. If the schema designer builds the personal space on is_private (her ruling 7 names 0072 as a possible base), every personal-space file has a local_server body, and every personal-to-project move is refused.
  - *Scenario:* Plan acceptance 3, 'Move test file from personal space into writable project; Files agrees and it still opens', fails with body_on_a_computer for every file in the personal space.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabaseAdapter.uploadFile (`if (!moneyGated && await projectIsPrivate(...)) storageProvider = LOCAL_SERVER`); projectIsPrivate; the design's MV-2(8) refusal list.
  - *Fix:* Name it as an interface requirement on the schema and storage designers: a personal-space upload routes to the company's chosen provider, not projectIsPrivate's arm. Add a suite probe: upload into the personal space, then the row's storage_provider is the workspace's provider, then move to a project. Until that lands, keep the cross-project arm behind 'not_yet'.
- **MV-R8 [minor]** Moving the inline files DELETE route out of main.cjs breaks a source pin the design does not list.
  - *Scenario:* After main.cjs mounts fileTrash.cjs in place of the inline route, pathContainment.test.js 'the delete route containment-checks before unlink' gets indexOf("expressApp.delete('/api/rabbit/projects/:projectId/files/:id'") = -1 and fails. The route also no longer calls unlinkSync.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/pathContainment.test.js ('the delete route containment-checks before unlink'); electron/main.cjs the files DELETE route.
  - *Fix:* Repoint the pin at fileTrash.cjs: it contains the route string and resolveContainedFilePath before every renameSync and unlinkSync. Keep main.cjs's not-redefined checks.
