- **MV-R9 [minor]** Two consumers are misidentified. editHistoryRevert.js has no files revert at all, so that consumer change is a phantom. NOT_CARRIED lives in binsMigration.js, not runMigration.js, and notCarriedOf counts only keys in BUNDLE_COUNTED.
  - *Scenario:* buildRevertPlan for a files entry returns 'no revert support for files'; the planned 'Use Move to put it back' branch never runs. Adding 'trashedFiles' only to NOT_CARRIED leaves the report silent, because notCarriedOf skips keys outside BUNDLE_COUNTED. binsMigration.test.js's key lists also need the entry.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/editHistoryRevert.js REVERTABLE_TABLES (projects, phases, assets, tasks); src/cloud/migrate/binsMigration.js NOT_CARRIED, BUNDLE_COUNTED, notCarriedOf; binsMigration.test.js lines listing keys.
  - *Fix:* Drop the editHistoryRevert change. Add 'trashedFiles' to NOT_CARRIED and BUNDLE_COUNTED in binsMigration.js, with a binsMigration.test.js assertion.
- **MV-R10 [minor]** file_moves stamps no creator on insert. The design attaches fn_audit_touch BEFORE UPDATE only, so fn_audit_touch's INSERT arm, which sets created_by, updated_by, created_at and updated_at, never runs.
  - *Scenario:* Every journal row has created_by NULL. The house rule's audit columns are empty on the only write the table ever gets.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/migrations/0001 fn_audit_touch (TG_OP='INSERT' arm); 0098 precedent trg_item_groupings_audit BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE.
  - *Fix:* Make the trigger trg_file_moves_audit BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE … fn_audit_touch(), and add a suite probe that created_by equals the caller.
- **MV-R11 [minor]** The privacy rationale of MV-8 ('Neither row names the other project') is false. storage_path keeps projects/<origin uuid>/…, and it is written as new_path in the destination's 'refiled in' event. It also stays on the moved files row that every destination reader selects.
  - *Scenario:* A file moves from private project A into shared project B. Every reader of B reads A's uuid from files.storage_path and from the 'in' event's new_path.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabaseAdapter.uploadFile key shape (`projects/${projectId}/${entity}/${entityId}`); 0027 file_events columns old_path/new_path; files_select.
  - *Fix:* Correct the claim in the design and the handbook. Write the 'refiled' rows with old_path/new_path NULL (the body never moves), and record in OUTSTANDING that the origin id is carried in the key until the body designer's association lands.
- **MV-R12 [minor]** Undo cannot work on a folder move whose subtree held trashed files. The composite FK forces trashed subtree files to change project too, so they must be journalled. moth_undo_move requires every item to be live and otherwise refuses changed_since. The 'not_yet' code is also missing from MV-2's code set. And a post-condition that asserts the hook exists contradicts 'refuse when absent'.
  - *Scenario:* A person moves a folder containing one trashed file and presses Undo straight away. The refusal is changed_since, and nothing moves.
  - *Evidence:* INFERRED from the design text; MEASURED that folders have no deleted_at and that files.deleted_at rows still need to carry the composite FK (0041, 0043).
  - *Fix:* For undo, check position only (to_project/to_folder) for items that were trashed when journalled, and liveness only for items that were live. Add 'not_yet' to the code set. Make the hook check a body-time branch, not a migration post-condition.
- **MV-R13 [minor]** The new managed-file restore and the 30-day purge meet managed rows that an asset delete soft-deleted. That route moves the asset's directory to <root>/.trash/<slug>_<ts> and stamps deleted_at on its managed files.
  - *Scenario:* A desktop asset is deleted. Its managed files then appear in the local trash and are restored by POST …/managed-files/:id/restore. Each comes back as a live row with a dangling asset_id, and its stream answers 410 because the body is in the asset's .trash. The purge later resolves ASSETS/<slug>/<stored_name>, finds nothing, and records blob_removed=false each time, leaving the bodies on disk.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. electron/main.cjs DELETE /api/rabbit/projects/:projectId/assets/:id (renameSync into root/.trash, `mf.deleted_at = now` for mf.asset_id); resolveManagedFileDiskPath.
  - *Fix:* listLocalTrash and the managed restore leave out (or refuse) rows whose asset_id no longer exists in bundle.assets. The purge either handles the asset .trash directories or the gap is stated in OUTSTANDING.
- **MV-R14 [minor]** Folders are off the channel, and noteFilesChangedElsewhere reloads only when realtime is not live. A live cloud window on the destination therefore receives file INSERTs whose folder_id names a folder it never loaded.
  - *Scenario:* A teammate has B open with realtime live. A folder subtree moves A→B, and its files arrive on rabbit:project:B. fileTree cannot place them under the unknown folder until the window reloads.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0098 fn_realtime_broadcast (no folders arm); realtimeMerge applyEventCore INSERT upsert; the design's noteFilesChangedElsewhere condition.
  - *Fix:* Have the split emit a thin 'refetch folders' signal on the destination topic, or have the client refetch folders when an incoming file names an unknown folder_id. Pin it with a realtimeMerge test.

## 4. Office-nas catalogue and desktop-only projects

**Area as the designer framed it:** MO1 design round: the metadata-only office-NAS catalogue, and desktop-only projects in Moth

### Summary

Files on the office NAS (the company's network share) get listed in the cloud as ordinary public.files rows. They use a new body provider value, 'network_share', which is added in its own migration. Its name is deliberately different from the workspace choice 'network' so the two vocabularies stay apart, as storage/index.js requires. Each row's storage_path is 'nas:' followed by a forward-slash path relative to the company drive (workspace_storage.root_path, which only admins can set, 0048). Migration 0049 already forces every project folder strictly inside that drive, so a move between projects changes only project_id and folder_id, never the locator. The 'nas:' namespace can never equal a rabbit-files key. That matters because storage-gc treats any row's storage_path as a live reference (MEASURED). Clients can never insert these rows or change their locator, size, modified time or missing flag. A guard trigger refuses that; the only writer is one SECURITY DEFINER RPC, network_share_catalogue_apply. That RPC checks the caller's write right on a live, visible project in their own workspace. It confirms the walked root is the company's current drive and takes the folder from the project row, never from the caller. It refuses money, Legal, the desktop's invoice folders, `_DATABASES` folders and dot-folders at any depth. It also silently skips anything under another project's folder, private and trashed projects included. Rows keep their ids across checks through a unique index per workspace and locator, a size-and-time match for files renamed on the server, and a missing timestamp instead of deletion. 'Last checked' lives in a new owner-less table, network_share_checks, written only after a walk that actually reached the server. The desktop walks the folder when a project opens and on Refresh. It asks the root first through the shared rootReachable pattern (async, 2 s limit, 15 s cache), then walks in a worker thread capped at 5,000 files, depth 8 and 60 s, recording metadata only. The web lists the same rows and says 'Preview available on the desktop app'. Nothing in the catalogue ever becomes gateway authority. For desktop-only projects (Local Server bundles), Session 1 does not need moth_local_scopes, and I recommend removing it. Moth reads through the active adapter, the cloud never learns that a local project exists, and later local Moth state stays on that computer.

### Decisions

- **NS-D1** NAS files are public.files rows with storage_provider = 'network_share', a new value of the public.storage_provider enum. The workspace configuration vocabulary (workspace_storage_provider_chk: petal, network, s3; 0051) is unchanged, and NEW_BODY_GOES_TO[network] stays 'local_server' because no body is ever uploaded.
  - *Why:* Moth, the Files page, trash, tags, moves and history all target files rows, so a NAS file needs a files identity to take part. PLAN_MOTH §4 'Files … company-network catalogue' and 'Add distinct network body provider' say the same. 'local_server' already means 'bytes on the computer that added it' (localServerProvider NOT_HERE, checkMediaKey under the local media root, MEASURED) and would send these rows to the wrong resolver. The value is not called 'network' because storage/index.js's header says the two axes must never merge, and with one word for both, `row.storage_provider === WORKSPACE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK` would silently type-check.
  - *Rejected:* (a) A separate catalogue table (the INFERRED line in the rules digest): every Moth feature would then need a second file-like target kind, plus its own trash, tags and move paths. (b) Reusing 'local_server': wrong resolver and wrong sentence. (c) Value 'network': collides with the workspace axis. (d) Widening workspace_storage_provider_chk: not needed, since 'network' is already admitted.
- **NS-D2** The locator is the company drive (workspace_storage.root_path) plus a contained, forward-slash relative path, stored as storage_path = 'nas:' || <relative path>, e.g. 'nas:Salt-Hours/Salt-Hours_FILES/rushes/A001.mov'. The project's folder prefix comes from projects.folder_root, relative to the root when it sits strictly inside it (0049's exact comparison), else from projects.folder_slug. One pure SQL helper, network_share_folder_of(folder_root, folder_slug, root), is the only definition.
  - *Why:* 0049 (fn_project_folder_root_guard) and main.cjs folderRootRefusal put every project folder strictly inside the one admin-set drive, so a root-relative locator does not depend on which project owns the row. A move between projects or into a personal space therefore needs no relocation. A NAS renamed by an admin keeps every locator. The 'nas:' prefix keeps the namespace apart from cloud keys. storage-gc's referencedPaths and its queue drain read files.storage_path with no provider filter (MEASURED storage-gc/index.ts), so a bare locator equal to a real rabbit-files key would keep that object from being collected. The rabbit_files_* storage policies require foldername[1]='projects' (0042), so no object can ever be written under 'nas:' (INFERRED). The locator travels in storage_path so the resolveFileProvider(row).getUrl(row.storage_path) seam stays as it is.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Paths relative to the project folder: a move would have to rebase the locator. (b) Anchoring on bin_locations: reviewers can write it (can_edit_shot_lists, 0091 §7a), it holds UNC paths only, and it is the gateway's vetting domain (TPN-AUTH-012). (c) A new nas_roots table: there is only one root to hold, and 0049 already binds folders to it. (d) A bare relative path in storage_path: it could collide with cloud keys under a share folder literally named 'projects'.
- **NS-D3** Only the server writes catalogue rows. A BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger, fn_files_network_share_guard, refuses three things unless the transaction-local GUC wilson.network_share_writer = 'on', which only network_share_catalogue_apply sets. (1) Any INSERT with storage_provider='network_share'. (2) Any change of storage_provider to or from 'network_share'. (3) On a network_share row, any change to storage_path, size_bytes, source_modified_at, thumbnail_url, mime_type or share_missing_since. WILSON's own metadata (name, description, tags, document_kind, is_core_definer, entity links) stays writable through the existing files_update policy. So do folder_id and project_id through the checked-move path, and deleted_at through soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted.
  - *Why:* OSP-07 (MEASURED) shows a project writer can make a row vouch for any key. A catalogue row must not let a client choose what it points at. The GUC is the 0064/0069 otter_nomination_apply precedent, and PostgREST cannot set it. The trigger leaves every files_* policy (latest 0092) untouched, so nothing has to be restated.
  - *Rejected:* (a) A narrow INSERT policy beside files_insert: permissive policies OR together, so a narrow one changes nothing. (b) A current_user test: every SECURITY DEFINER function would pass, including future ones that copy client input. (c) Adding the columns to FILE_COLUMNS: that would make them client-writable.
- **NS-D4** Stable identity is a UNIQUE index on files (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) WHERE storage_provider='network_share', with trashed rows included. The apply inserts with ON CONFLICT … DO NOTHING. It updates only rows of the project being checked, and only when size, modified time or the missing flag actually changed. It never resurrects a trashed row and never touches another project's row. On a complete walk, an exact match keeps the row's id. A match means a vanished row of this folder and one new entry with the same leaf name (case-folded), size and modified time, unique on both sides. The row's locator then moves to the new path, and fn_file_events_capture records it as 'moved'.
  - *Why:* Ids survive every check, so Moth positions, tags and relationships keyed on file id persist. Keeping trashed rows in the index means a deleted listing does not reappear while it is in the bin. The exact match is the first rung of the relink matcher (components/relinkMatcher.js), done in SQL so the server stays the only authority. It keeps ids when someone renames a folder on the server, which leaves file times alone. No file id ever comes from the caller.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Delete-and-reinsert on each check: ids would churn and layouts would be lost. (b) Fuzzy name-only matching: wrong adoptions. (c) The client sending rename pairs or file ids: that trusts caller-supplied identities.
- **NS-D5** A file that has vanished from the server is marked, not deleted. A new column, files.share_missing_since timestamptz NULL, is set by a COMPLETE walk on live rows of the walked folder that the walk did not see. It is also set through p_gone for this project's rows that sit outside its folder (rows moved in from another project) and that the desktop stat'ed as absent. It is cleared when the file is seen again. Truncated walks (limits or deadline) and failed walks mark nothing.
  - *Why:* Her rulings call for 'vanished files marked missing rather than hard-deleted' and say 'a failed refresh must not read as checked'. One timestamp per row is enough, because a row seen by the last check is present as of network_share_checks.last_checked_at. A per-row 'seen at' column would update every row on every check, flooding edit_history and updated_at.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Hard delete: loses ids. (b) Soft-deleting via deleted_at: confuses the trash and undo semantics. (c) A per-row last_seen_at: write amplification.
- **NS-D6** 'Last checked' lives in a new table, public.network_share_checks, with one row per project (project_id PK). Its columns are workspace_id, folder_locator, last_checked_at, last_checked_by, complete, entries_seen, missing_count and the audit columns. It is written only by the apply RPC after a walk that reached the server. Failures (unreachable, slow, offline) are shown on that computer only and never written to the cloud.
  - *Why:* A column on projects would put a projects UPDATE (and an edit_history entry) on every open, and would broadcast the projects row on the workspace channel. Handbook §17 rule 33 says to keep the last-known-good value and show its age. One laptop off the VPN must not tell the whole company that the server is down.
  - *Rejected:* (a) projects.nas_checked_at: churn, history noise, and workspace-channel exposure (A-R1-04). (b) Recording failures in the cloud: misleads other machines.
- **NS-D7** The desktop main process walks the folder. The new module electron/networkShare.cjs exposes POST /api/rabbit/network-share/walk behind the per-launch token gate. The root comes from main's memory only (workspaceRootDir, pushed by rabbit:set-workspace-root). The project folder from the body passes checkFolderRootShape and isPathInside(root) first. The walk then asks the root through rootReachable, extracted unchanged from rabbitBins.cjs mountCloudBins into a new electron/rootReachable.cjs, with one shared instance and one cache for bins and the catalogue. The walk itself runs in a worker_threads Worker using the eval-code precedent (askRootInWorker), with the refusal lists passed as workerData. Limits: 5,000 files, 2,000 folders, depth 8, 60 s. Links and junctions are never followed, and the folder's real path must be inside the root's real path. Excluded at any depth and never counted: rabbit_money_segment's INVOICES, FINANCE and LEGAL (case-folded); names ending _RECEIPTS&INVOICES, _CREWINVOICES, _TALENTINVOICES and _DATABASES; every dot-segment; node_modules, desktop.ini and Thumbs.db. Metadata only: name, folder, size, modified time and an extension-derived MIME type (localMedia.cjs mimeForLeaf). No hashing, no media probing and no thumbnail. The same route stats up to 500 of this project's moved-in locators.
  - *Why:* Sync fs on a dead SMB share froze the window for 5 to 42 s (OUTSTANDING bins freeze, MEASURED), and cloud-bins/prepare's main-thread walk is an open defect, so neither pattern may be repeated. The `<slug>_DATABASES` mirrors hold budget.json, which is money (MEASURED: main.cjs mirrorProjectDatabases). `.wilson/rabbit-data` holds whole project bundles. The old split invoice folders are money by name (main.cjs resolveProjectInvoicesDir's comment). INFERRED: a Worker loaded from a file inside the packaged asar is unproven here, so the eval form follows the measured precedent.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Reusing resolveProjectFilesDir or resolveProjectFolder: they existsSync and mkdirSync on main, which would write to the NAS and stall. (b) A walk on the libuv pool: four shared threads (rabbitBins measured). (c) A continuous watcher: ruled out for this release. (d) An IPC channel: a read under the token needs none, and the handbook's IPC count stays the same.
- **NS-D8** The server skips any entry under another project's folder prefix in the same workspace, private and trashed projects included, computed with network_share_folder_of. Such entries are not inserted and not counted in the RPC's answer. Inserts are allowed only under the checked project's own folder. Locators outside it can only be updated or marked gone for rows this project already owns.
  - *Why:* 0049 does not forbid nested project folders. Without this, a private project's file names nested in a public project's folder would become visible to that project's members. That is a privacy leak the caller's own RLS cannot see, which is why it is done in the SECURITY DEFINER body. A slug-only project's folder is always top-level under the root, so it can never be nested inside another project's folder (a project folder can never be the root itself).
  - *Rejected:* (a) The client sending the folders to skip: it cannot see private projects. (b) Reporting 'skipped' counts: that is an existence oracle.
- **NS-D9** When checks run: on the desktop, with the cloud adapter, when storesMediaOnServer(workspace_storage) is true and the person passes canOnProject('entity.write', { ready, … }). An automatic check runs when a project is opened in R.A.B.B.I.T. or picked in the Files page, unless the project was checked less than 2 minutes ago. Refresh is always available (GatedAction with its reason for people who cannot edit). Each computer runs at most one walk per project at a time, and the server serializes concurrent applies with pg_advisory_xact_lock per project. Nothing runs while an offline sign-in is held.
  - *Why:* This follows her NAS ruling (open plus a clear Refresh, show last checked). The cool-down stops repeated opens from hammering a share, and the label keeps it honest about when the check happened. Reviewers do not hold entity.write (projectRoleMatrix, MEASURED), which matches the lockstep with can_write_project.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Checks from the web: a browser cannot reach a NAS. (b) A new project action: entity.write already mirrors can_write_project.
- **NS-D10** Realtime: inside the apply, the files arm of fn_realtime_broadcast returns early when wilson.network_share_writer is on. network_share_checks joins the project_id arm, so each check sends exactly one signal. realtimeMerge maps it to a new effect, { type: 'files-refetch' }, which RabbitProvider handles by re-reading the project's files and its check row.
  - *Why:* A first check can insert 5,000 rows. One broadcast per row would amplify writes into realtime.messages and hit the channel's rate limits. The budget_versions 'signal only' path (0101, realtimeMerge) is the precedent.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Broadcasting every row: flood. (b) No signal: other windows would stay stale until reload.
- **NS-D11** file_events: a catalogued INSERT records the event 'catalogued' instead of 'uploaded'. A purge certificate for a network_share row adds details.body = 'left on the company server'. file_events_event_check is widened by DROP and ADD, restating 0093's nine terms plus 'catalogued'.
  - *Why:* The file_events stream is a TPN trail (TPN-CONT-002, -008). Recording 'uploaded by X' for a file X only listed would make the trail false. trg_files_gc_enqueue's WHEN clause (0054) already skips these rows, because the provider is not supabase or s3 and thumbnail_url is forced NULL.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Keeping 'uploaded': a false audit record. (b) Suppressing events: loses the record of when a file was first listed.
- **NS-D12** Provider and web behaviour: a new storage/networkShareProvider.js is registered on every surface beside supabase, s3 and local_server. put and del always refuse ('WILSON lists this file from the company server and does not change it there.'). get, exists and getUrl call the desktop route GET/HEAD /api/rabbit/network-share/file/<encoded locator>. Off the desktop they throw 'Preview available on the desktop app'. describe reports root reachability. unavailableSentence and FilePreviewDialog say that sentence on the web without making a request. uploadFile's refusal for network workspaces stays. SERVER_MEDIA_SENTENCE is reworded to say the files are listed from each project's folder on the server.
  - *Why:* The registry's REQUIRED contract (all six functions) holds, and resolveFileProvider never throws 'no storage provider registered'. Brief §9: 'Preview available on the desktop app', never a blank dot. Network rows carry no thumbnail_url, so every thumbnail dispatcher skips them.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Leaving the value unregistered: every listing would throw. (b) Gateway previews: post-release by her ruling.
- **NS-D13** Opening a file on the desktop: GET/HEAD /api/rabbit/network-share/file/* and a new 'share' source in rabbit:open-path (electron/openPath.cjs resolveOpenTarget). Both apply the same steps in order: the token gate; the locator shape and refusal check (the CJS twin of network_share_locator_ok); awaiting rootReachable(workspaceRootDir); resolveContainedFilePath, then insideByRealPath against the root; async stat; then res.sendFile with ranges and safeMediaContentType. The open IPC handler in main.cjs awaits reachability before resolving. The renderer names a row (locator), never a path.
  - *Why:* This reuses localMedia.cjs and openPath.cjs's three containment checks. The person's own SMB rights on the share are the access authority on the desktop. WILSON adds the launch token, containment to the company drive and the money refusal, and nothing more. The remaining risk of a share dying after it answered is the accepted limit from §17.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Main reading the row from the cloud: main has no Supabase client. (b) Reusing the local-media route: different root, different key shape.
- **NS-D14** Catalogue rows never authorize the file gateway. The catalogue migration's post-condition refuses unless pg_get_functiondef of gateway_clips_for_tickets and gateway_sync_apply (latest 0104), comments stripped, mentions neither 'public.files' nor 'network_share'. Suite 96_gateways gains a probe for this.
  - *Why:* TPN-AUTH-012 (fixed by 0104): a catalogue row once made the gateway serve any file on the share. Vetted bin_files stay the gateway's only authority.
  - *Rejected:* Trusting future gateway sessions to remember the rule, with nothing that turns red when it is broken.
- **NS-D15** Session 1 builds no moth_local_scopes table, and I recommend removing it from PLAN_MOTH, not deferring it in its current form.
  - *Why:* No Session 1 feature writes Moth state for a local item. Views and layouts (S2), tags and relationships (S3) and boards (S4) are all later. Her ruling 12a.1 says local data 'shows its latest state' from that computer and that a desktop-only project has no live features, so nothing needs a cloud identity. A signed-out desktop has no cloud, so a local store is needed anyway. A cloud 'technical backing project' would be a projects row that projects_select shows to admins (0072, MEASURED), and it would appear in projectsIndex, the Files picker and the workspace channel (A-R1-04). Hiding it would mean chasing every consumer, the same trap the personal-space measurement found. Writing local ids to the cloud also leaks that local projects exist to the company and risks impersonation, because the desktop to cloud migration keeps ids (cloudMigration record, MEASURED main.cjs mergeCloudMigrationRecord).
  - *Rejected:* (a) Building it in Session 1 as planned: a table with no Session 1 caller breaks the caller rule. (b) Deferring it unchanged to Session 2: it keeps the leak and impersonation surface.
- **NS-D16** Moth's identity and scope rules for desktop-only projects. Every Moth node is keyed (scope, id), with scope 'cloud' or 'local'; a bare id is never enough. In Session 1, Moth reads through the active adapter like every other tool. In Local Server mode it lists the bundle's projects (with folders, files and managedFiles), marked 'On this computer only'. In cloud mode it lists cloud projects, the personal space and NAS catalogue rows. A local project whose bundle.cloudMigration[<signed-in workspace>].carried.projects lists its id is never shown beside its cloud copy. Mixing local islands into a signed-in cloud graph is Session 2's to build under these rules. Nothing about a local project ever reaches a cloud table, channel, count or the web.
  - *Why:* This satisfies 'cannot impersonate a cloud identity' by construction. The cloud copy wins after a migration. The web and other machines show nothing, which matches 'no placeholder that reveals existence'.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Showing local and cloud copies side by side under one id. (b) Keying local items by bare ids.
- **NS-D17** Moth state about local projects (S2 to S4: last view, layout overlay, local tags and relationships, local boards) stays on that computer. It goes in a new 'moth' key in each project.json and a per-machine rabbit-data/moth-local.json, written through writeJsonAtomic and reconcileRabbitBundle. The desktop to cloud migration does not carry it unless a later ruling says otherwise. The company tag catalogue and workspace notes are read from Supabase when signed in ('data already in Supabase still comes from Supabase'). Shared cloud text never takes a mention or link to a local item: the [[ picker does not offer local items in cloud text.
  - *Why:* Her rule that all database data lives in Supabase (2026-09-11) was about cloud projects. A desktop-only project's data is local by definition. F3's one atomic writer (jsonFile.cjs) and the bundle reconciler are the house rules for any new local store.
  - *Rejected:* (a) localStorage: the origin changes every launch (handbook §17 41). (b) The cloud: leak and impersonation.

### Schema changes

- **TYPE public.storage_provider** (restates from: new (enum created in 0000_rabbit_base_schema.sql, last widened in 0051_s3_storage_provider.sql)): ALTER TYPE public.storage_provider ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'network_share'; in its own migration and nothing else. Post-condition reads pg_enum (comments stripped where function text is matched): EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_enum e JOIN pg_type t ON t.oid=e.enumtypid WHERE t.typname='storage_provider' AND e.enumlabel='network_share'). No use of the value in this transaction.
- **FUNCTION public.network_share_locator_ok(text) RETURNS boolean** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE sql IMMUTABLE PARALLEL SAFE SET search_path = public, pg_temp. True iff: p NOT NULL, <> '', length <= 1024, p IS NFC NORMALIZED; p !~ '(^/|/$|//|\\|:|[*?"<>|])'; no C0/C1/format/bidi/zero-width/line-separator/BOM/variation-selector/TAG code point (explicit ranges \x01-\x1f \x7f-\x9f ­ ؜ ᠎ ​-‏ ‪-‮     ⁠-⁯ ︀-️ ﻿ \U000e0000-\U000e007f, the SQL twin of UNC_FORBIDDEN_CHARS_RE); p !~ '(^|/)\.' (no dot-led segment: ., .., .wilson, .trash); p !~ '[. ](/|$)'; and NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM unnest(string_to_array(p,'/')) s WHERE public.rabbit_money_segment(s) OR lower(s) ~ '_(receipts&invoices|crewinvoices|talentinvoices|databases)$'). rabbit_money_segment (0088, IMMUTABLE) stays the one money vocabulary. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role (pure).
- **FUNCTION public.network_share_folder_of(p_folder_root text, p_folder_slug text, p_root text) RETURNS text** (restates from: new): IMMUTABLE sql. CASE WHEN p_folder_root IS NOT NULL AND p_root IS NOT NULL AND lower(left(p_folder_root, length(p_root)+1)) = lower(p_root)||'\\' THEN replace(substr(p_folder_root, length(p_root)+2), '\\', '/') WHEN p_folder_root IS NULL AND p_folder_slug IS NOT NULL THEN p_folder_slug END, then NULL unless network_share_locator_ok(result). Uses 0049's comparison exactly. The project folder is folder_root itself (MEASURED main.cjs resolveProjectFolder), not its parent, whatever 0041's column comment says. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon.
- **COLUMN public.files.share_missing_since** (restates from: new): ALTER TABLE public.files ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS share_missing_since timestamptz NULL; COMMENT: set by network_share_catalogue_apply only. Not added to supabaseAdapter FILE_COLUMNS. No files_* policy is restated (latest 0092): a nullable column changes no policy.
- **CONSTRAINTS on public.files: files_network_share_shape_chk, files_network_share_namespace_chk** (restates from: new): Pre-flight RAISEs unless count(*) of files WHERE storage_path LIKE 'nas:%' = 0. ADD CONSTRAINT files_network_share_shape_chk CHECK (storage_provider <> 'network_share' OR (left(storage_path,4)='nas:' AND public.network_share_locator_ok(substr(storage_path,5)) AND thumbnail_url IS NULL AND NOT is_financial AND NOT ('legal' = ANY(tags)))); ADD CONSTRAINT files_network_share_namespace_chk CHECK ((storage_provider = 'network_share') = (left(storage_path,4) = 'nas:')). Wrapped as DROP IF EXISTS + ADD. No existing files CHECK needs widening, because none enumerates providers (MEASURED): files_money_provider_chk (latest text pinned in 0092) is `storage_provider='supabase' OR NOT money`, and catalogue rows are never money by construction. files_money_key_chk, files_legal_folder_chk, files_legal_not_core_chk and files_legal_not_financial_chk all hold, because no segment is a money one and the legal tag is refused.
- **INDEXES on public.files** (restates from: new): CREATE UNIQUE INDEX files_network_share_locator_key ON public.files (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) WHERE storage_provider = 'network_share';  (trashed rows included on purpose) CREATE INDEX files_network_share_project_idx ON public.files (project_id, lower(storage_path)) WHERE storage_provider = 'network_share' AND deleted_at IS NULL.
- **FUNCTION public.fn_files_network_share_guard() + TRIGGER trg_files_network_share_guard BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.files** (restates from: new): plpgsql, not SECURITY DEFINER. v_on := current_setting('wilson.network_share_writer', true) = 'on'. INSERT: IF NEW.storage_provider='network_share' AND NOT v_on → RAISE 'network_share: catalogue rows are written by the server only' ERRCODE 42501. UPDATE: IF (OLD.storage_provider='network_share') <> (NEW.storage_provider='network_share') AND NOT v_on → RAISE. IF OLD.storage_provider='network_share' AND NOT v_on AND (NEW.storage_path, NEW.size_bytes, NEW.source_modified_at, NEW.thumbnail_url, NEW.mime_type, NEW.share_missing_since) IS DISTINCT FROM (OLD.…) → RAISE. Everything else passes, so files_update (0092) still decides. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Name sorts after trg_files_legal_fixed (0088) so Legal refusals keep their words.
- **TABLE public.network_share_checks** (restates from: new): CREATE TABLE (project_id uuid PRIMARY KEY REFERENCES public.projects(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, workspace_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES public.workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE, folder_locator text NOT NULL CHECK (public.network_share_locator_ok(folder_locator)), last_checked_at timestamptz NOT NULL, last_checked_by uuid, complete boolean NOT NULL, entries_seen integer NOT NULL CHECK (entries_seen BETWEEN 0 AND 5000), missing_count integer NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (missing_count >= 0), created_at, created_by, updated_at, updated_by). Triggers: trg_network_share_checks_populate_workspace BEFORE INSERT (fn_populate_workspace_from_project, 0004); trg_network_share_checks_audit BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE (fn_audit_touch, 0001); trg_network_share_checks_realtime AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE (fn_realtime_broadcast). ENABLE + FORCE RLS. One policy: network_share_checks_select FOR SELECT USING (workspace_id = public.current_workspace_id() AND public.has_active_membership(workspace_id) AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = network_share_checks.project_id)), the 0014 live-parent hop, so privacy and trash follow projects_select (0072). No INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE policy. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT SELECT TO authenticated; GRANT ALL TO service_role. The only writer is the RPC below (postgres has rolbypassrls, MEASURED 0055 and 0057).
- **FUNCTION public.network_share_catalogue_apply(p_project_id uuid, p_root text, p_folder text, p_entries jsonb, p_complete boolean, p_gone text[] DEFAULT '{}') RETURNS jsonb** (restates from: new): SECURITY DEFINER, SET search_path = public, pg_temp. Steps:
(1) Authority. v_ws := current_workspace_id(). Read v_p from projects by id. Raise one message ('network_share: not allowed', 42501) when auth.uid() IS NULL, membership is not active (has_active_membership), the project is absent, in another workspace, trashed (deleted_at IS NOT NULL), not visible (passes_project_privacy, 0082) or not writable (can_write_project, 0013). The single message keeps the error from becoming an existence oracle.
(2) Drive. workspace_storage must have mode='byos', provider='network' and lower(root_path) = lower(btrim(p_root)), else 'network_share: drive_changed'.
(3) Folder. v_folder := network_share_folder_of(v_p.folder_root, v_p.folder_slug, root). It must be non-NULL and equal p_folder case-folded, else 'network_share: no_folder'.
(4) Size limits. p_entries must be an array of at most 5000; cardinality(p_gone) at most 500.
(5) Lock. pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtextextended('network_share:'||p_project_id, 0)), then set_config('wilson.network_share_writer','on',true).
(6) Valid entries. Build temp _ns ON COMMIT DROP from jsonb_to_recordset(p_entries) AS e(p text, s bigint, m timestamptz, t text). Keep only rows where network_share_locator_ok(e.p), the path is under this folder (left(lower(e.p), length(v_folder)+1) = lower(v_folder)||'/'), and NOT EXISTS another project o in v_ws (any deleted state) whose network_share_folder_of(o.folder_root, o.folder_slug, root) prefixes e.p. Normalize: s clamped to 0..2^53; m NULL unless between 1980-01-01 and now()+1 day; t kept only if it matches '^[a-z0-9.+-]{1,127}/[a-z0-9.+-]{1,127}$'.
(7) Updates. UPDATE this project's live network_share rows whose lower(storage_path) = lower('nas:'||p) when size, time or share_missing_since differ; share_missing_since := NULL.
(8) Exact match (only when p_complete). Pair a vanished live row of this folder with an unmatched entry that has the same lower(leaf), size and m, each unique on both sides (count(*) OVER per key = 1). UPDATE that row's storage_path to the new locator.
(9) Inserts. INSERT the remaining entries into files (project_id, name, mime_type, size_bytes, source_modified_at, storage_provider, storage_path, kind, uploaded_at, folder_id) with name = the leaf with a leading '<uuid>-' Local Server prefix stripped, provider 'network_share', path 'nas:'||p, kind 'source', and folder_id = the live folders row of this project whose lower(path) equals the entry's directory relative to the folder (INFERRED that folders.path mirrors the on-disk layout; else NULL). ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) WHERE storage_provider = 'network_share' DO NOTHING.
(10) Missing (only when p_complete). Set share_missing_since := now() on this project's live rows under the folder, not under another project's folder, not in _ns, and share_missing_since IS NULL. Also set it on this project's rows outside the folder whose lower(storage_path) = ANY(lower('nas:'||p_gone)).
(11) Record. INSERT INTO network_share_checks … ON CONFLICT (project_id) DO UPDATE.
(12) Return jsonb {added, updated, renamed, found_again, newly_missing, complete, checked_at}, counts of this project's rows only, with no skipped count. REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated. Neither the network_share_checks row nor the files rows are ever returned whole.
- **FUNCTION public.fn_realtime_broadcast()** (restates from: 0098_table_views.sql): Restate the whole latest body from 0098_table_views.sql with exactly two additions. (a) The WHEN 'files' arm first does IF current_setting('wilson.network_share_writer', true) = 'on' THEN RETURN NULL; END IF; ahead of the money test, which stays as it is. (b) 'network_share_checks' joins the project_id arm list ('phases','assets',…,'table_views','item_groupings','network_share_checks'). Post-condition (comments stripped): the body contains file_row_is_money twice, 'network_share_checks' and 'wilson.network_share_writer', and every table name 0098's post-condition pins. The trigger list on network_share_checks is pinned.
- **FUNCTION public.fn_file_events_capture()** (restates from: 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql): Restate the whole latest body from 0074_file_events_money_arm.sql. Additions: (a) the INSERT branch writes event := CASE WHEN NEW.storage_provider::text = 'network_share' THEN 'catalogued' ELSE 'uploaded' END. (b) the DELETE branch's details gains 'body', CASE WHEN OLD.storage_provider::text='network_share' THEN 'left on the company server' END via jsonb_strip_nulls. The EXCEPTION handler and the is_financial arms are unchanged. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated, as 0074 does.
- **CONSTRAINT public.file_events file_events_event_check** (restates from: 0093_file_gateway.sql): Use the 0093 DO-block that drops every CHECK whose definition LIKE '%uploaded%', then ADD CONSTRAINT file_events_event_check CHECK (event IN ('uploaded','downloaded','moved','relinked','trashed','restored','purged','upload_abandoned','viewed_remote','catalogued')). file_events_subject_chk and file_events_viewed_remote_shape_chk are left as 0093 made them. Post-condition reads pg_get_constraintdef back and RAISEs unless all ten terms are present.
- **Post-conditions of the catalogue migration (load-bearing; function text matched after stripping comments)** (restates from: new): The migration RAISEs unless all of the following hold.
- The enum label exists.
- Both files CHECKs exist with 'network_share_locator_ok' and 'nas:' in their definitions.
- files_network_share_locator_key is unique and its predicate names network_share.
- trg_files_network_share_guard is attached BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE.
- network_share_checks has relrowsecurity and relforcerowsecurity, exactly one policy (SELECT), and no INSERT, UPDATE or DELETE privilege for authenticated.
- network_share_catalogue_apply is prosecdef with search_path pinned, and has_function_privilege('anon', …, 'EXECUTE') is false.
- The text 'wilson.network_share_writer' appears in exactly three public function bodies: the apply, the guard and fn_realtime_broadcast.
- gateway_clips_for_tickets and gateway_sync_apply (latest 0104) mention neither 'public.files' nor 'network_share'.
- trg_files_gc_enqueue's WHEN clause still reads IN ('supabase', 's3').
- A probe of network_share_locator_ok refuses 'a/INVOICES/x', 'a/Proj_CrewInvoices/x', 'a/P_DATABASES/b.json', 'a/.wilson/x', 'a/../b', 'a\\b', 'C:/x', 'a/x\u202e.mov' and a non-NFC string, and admits 'Salt-Hours/Salt-Hours_FILES/rushes/A001.mov' (the 0091 probe style).

### Consumers to change

- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/index.js FILE_PROVIDERS, SERVER_MEDIA_SENTENCE, header comment**: Add FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE = 'network_share', with a comment that it is the body axis, not WORKSPACE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK. Add NETWORK_SHARE_PREFIX = 'nas:', DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE = 'Preview available on the desktop app', and isNetworkShareRow(row) / networkShareLocator(row). Reword SERVER_MEDIA_SENTENCE to say the files found in each project's folder on the server are listed, and are opened and added in the desktop app. Correct the stale 'getStorageProvider(local_server) will throw' comment (MEASURED wrong: supabaseAdapter registers it). NEW_BODY_GOES_TO and uploadFile's network refusal stay unchanged.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js module registrations, FILE_COLUMNS, new methods refreshNetworkShare and getNetworkShareCheck**: Call registerStorageProvider(FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE, createNetworkShareStorageProvider()) beside the three existing registrations. Add refreshNetworkShare({ projectId, root, folder, entries, complete, gone }), which calls client.rpc('network_share_catalogue_apply') and maps 42501, drive_changed, no_folder and too_many to sentences. Add getNetworkShareCheck(projectId), which reads network_share_checks through RLS (null when the table is absent, 42P01). FILE_COLUMNS deliberately leaves out share_missing_since.
- **src/components/Resources/filePreview.js unavailableSentence; src/components/Resources/FilePreviewDialog.jsx SourcedStage**: For a network_share row off the desktop, return DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE before any fileUrl call. On the desktop, a 404 or 503 from the share route becomes 'WILSON did not find this file on the server at the last check' or 'This computer cannot reach the company server right now'.
- **src/components/Resources/fileActions.js diskSourceFor**: On the desktop with adapterMode 'supabase', a network_share row returns { source: 'share', locator: networkShareLocator(row) }, so Show in folder and Open in default app go through rabbit:open-path.
- **src/components/Resources/fileTree.js buildFileTree, fileMeta**: fileMeta gains missing: !!row.share_missing_since and onServer: provider === 'network_share'. buildFileTree takes an optional shareFolder (from network_share_checks.folder_locator). It places a network_share row that has no folder_id under read-only virtual folder nodes (id 'nas:<dir>') built from its locator below shareFolder, and otherwise places it as today.
- **src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx mediaOnServer banner**: For a network workspace on the cloud backend, the banner says 'Listed from the company's server · last checked <time> by <name>'. Add a 'partly checked' note when complete is false, and a Refresh GatedAction on the desktop (canOnProject 'entity.write' with ready), or 'Refresh from the desktop app' on the web. Rows marked missing show a 'Not found on the server at the last check' chip. Pass shareFolder to buildFileTree.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/FileManager.jsx empty state and thumbnails**: The empty state for a network workspace uses the reworded SERVER_MEDIA_SENTENCE. Network rows are skipped by the thumbnail batch (thumbnail_url is NULL). Add files stays refused with the existing sentence.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/realtimeMerge.js applyEventCore**: A 'network_share_checks' event returns { bundle, effects: [{ type: 'files-refetch' }] } as a signal only, on the budget_versions precedent. It gets no TABLE_TO_COLLECTION entry and does not merge into the bundle.
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/RabbitProvider.jsx project-open effect and realtime effect handler**: After the active project's bundle loads on the desktop, call refreshOnOpen(project) from the new networkShareCatalogue.js. It is fire-and-forget, does not block the UI, skips under the cool-down and skips while an offline sign-in is held. Handle the 'files-refetch' effect by re-reading listFiles and getNetworkShareCheck for the open project.
- **electron/rabbitBins.cjs mountCloudBins rootReachable / askRootInWorker**: Move rootReachable, its caches, slot cap and askRootInWorker unchanged into electron/rootReachable.cjs (createRootReachability(deps)). mountCloudBins takes the shared instance from deps, and still honours its existing test seams (deps.cloudBinsAskRoot, cloudBinsRootWaitMs, cloudBinsRootTtlMs and the rest), so the rabbitBins tests stay green.
- **electron/openPath.cjs resolveOpenTarget; electron/main.cjs rabbit:open-path handler**: Add a 'share' source to resolveOpenTarget. It checks the locator with networkShare.cjs checkShareLocator, resolves it against deps.shareRoot() (= workspaceRootDir) with resolveContainedFilePath and insideByRealPath, and keeps the existing regular-file, reveal and open refusals. In main.cjs, rabbit:open-path awaits the shared rootReachable(workspaceRootDir) before resolving a 'share' request, and answers 'This computer cannot reach the company server right now' when the root is off.
- **electron/main.cjs startLocalServer**: Mount require('./networkShare.cjs').mountNetworkShare(expressApp, { gate, getWorkspaceRoot: () => workspaceRootDir, reach, checkFolderRootShape, isPathInside, resolveContainedFilePath, insideByRealPath, safeMediaContentType, mimeForLeaf }). Create the shared rootReachable instance once and pass it to both mountCloudBins and mountNetworkShare. No new IPC channel.
- **src/dev/fixtures/rabbitFixturesAdapter.js and src/dev/fixtures/install.js variants**: A '?storage=network' variant gives Salt Hours a byos+network workspace_storage, a dozen network_share rows (one missing, one in a virtual folder) and a check row. getNetworkShareCheck answers from the fixtures. refreshNetworkShare throws devWriteRefused. These rows are for the walkthrough pictures.
- **Personal-space area (interface)**: In a network workspace, the personal space's folder on the drive is catalogued by the same RPC when its owner opens it on the desktop. The RPC's write predicate must use the personal-space owner-only helper that area defines instead of can_write_project, because admins reach a personal space from Files read-only. network_share_folder_of must know the personal-space folder so project walks skip it.
- **Checked-move and file-body-identity areas (interface)**: A network_share row moves by changing project_id and folder_id only. storage_path, provider and the body never change, and the destination must be in the same workspace. The guard trigger already allows this. If file_body_locations is built, the catalogue apply writes the row's body-location record in the same transaction (provider network_share, immutable locator, no thumbnail, never gc-enqueued). Moves into a locked folder are refused by the move path.
- **Moth entry area (interface)**: Moth file nodes for network_share rows show the 'On the company server' state and the missing state. On the web they say DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE and never a blank dot. Session 2's island-open calls the same refreshOnOpen. In Local Server mode the entry shows 'On this computer only' projects and the personal-space sentence (see NS-D16).

### Client changes

- **NEW src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/networkShareProvider.js — createNetworkShareStorageProvider({ available = hasLocalServer, fetchImpl }), NETWORK_SHARE_BASE = '/api/rabbit/network-share', networkShareUrl(locator, { download })**: Implements the six REQUIRED functions. put and del always throw 'WILSON lists this file from the company server and does not change it there.' get, exists and getUrl use localFetch or the same-origin URL of /file/<encoded locator> on the desktop, and throw DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE elsewhere. exists treats only 404 as false, the localServerProvider rule. describe reports { provider: 'network_share', label: 'Company server', reachable }. Why a new file: localServerProvider.js is keyed to the local media root and checkMediaKey's projects/… shape, and holds NOT_HERE's different sentence. *(production caller: supabaseAdapter.js registerStorageProvider(FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE, createNetworkShareStorageProvider()) at module load, then resolveFileProvider(row) inside supabaseAdapter.fileUrl, downloadUrl and downloadFile, which FilePreviewDialog and ProjectFilesExplorer call)*
- **NEW src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/networkShareCatalogue.js — refreshOnOpen(project, deps), refreshNow(project, deps), useNetworkShareCatalogue(projectId), shouldAutoRefresh(check, now)**: Eligibility: hasLocalServer(), adapterMode 'supabase', storesMediaOnServer(getWorkspaceStorageCached()), canOnProject('entity.write', { ready, … }) and no held offline session. Flow: POST the walk to the desktop route, then adapter.refreshNetworkShare, then the check is re-read. Keeps one walk per project at a time and a 2-minute cool-down for automatic checks only. Exposes { check, checking, error, refresh } for the banner. Failures stay in component state and are never written. Why a new file: no existing module owns the desktop-to-cloud catalogue flow, and RabbitProvider.jsx is already over 5,000 lines. *(production caller: RabbitProvider.jsx project-open effect (refreshOnOpen) and ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx banner (useNetworkShareCatalogue, the Refresh GatedAction))*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js refreshNetworkShare, getNetworkShareCheck**: New adapter methods (see consumers). They are absent from localServerAdapter, and networkShareCatalogue.js checks typeof before calling, the existing supports* pattern. *(production caller: networkShareCatalogue.js refreshNow and useNetworkShareCatalogue)*
- **src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/index.js FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE, NETWORK_SHARE_PREFIX, DESKTOP_PREVIEW_SENTENCE, isNetworkShareRow, networkShareLocator**: The new vocabulary entries and helpers. *(production caller: supabaseAdapter.js (registration), filePreview.unavailableSentence, fileActions.diskSourceFor, fileTree.fileMeta, ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx banner, networkShareProvider.js)*
- **NEW electron/networkShare.cjs — mountNetworkShare(expressApp, deps), checkShareLocator(locator), SHARE_REFUSED_EXACT, SHARE_REFUSED_SUFFIX, walkInWorker**: Routes, all behind gate: POST /api/rabbit/network-share/walk (the folder from the body passes checkFolderRootShape and isPathInside(workspaceRootDir); then the root is asked through rootReachable; then the Worker walk; answers { ok, root, folder, complete, truncated, reason, entries:[{p,s,m,t}], gone:[…] }); HEAD and GET /api/rabbit/network-share/file/*locator (shape, refusal, reach, contain, real-path, async stat, sendFile with ranges, safeMediaContentType, nosniff). checkShareLocator is the CJS twin of network_share_locator_ok. Why a new file: rabbitBins.cjs serves bin locations (a different root and authority), and main.cjs's resolvers do sync fs and mkdir. *(production caller: electron/main.cjs startLocalServer mount; openPath.cjs 'share' arm (checkShareLocator); the renderer's networkShareProvider.js and networkShareCatalogue.js call the routes)*
- **NEW electron/rootReachable.cjs — createRootReachability({ askRoot, waitMs, ttlMs, offTtlMs, maxAsking })**: The bins machinery moved unchanged: askRootInWorker, the 2 s limit, the 15 s on-cache, the 60 s off-cache, a 16-question cap, and rootKnownOffFor and markRootOffFor. Why a new file: two mounts now need one cache, and the subtract-before-add rule favours a move over a second copy. *(production caller: electron/main.cjs, which builds one instance and passes it to rabbitBins.cjs mountCloudBins and networkShare.cjs mountNetworkShare)*
- **src/components/Resources/ProjectFilesExplorer.jsx, fileTree.js, fileActions.js, filePreview.js, FilePreviewDialog.jsx; src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/realtimeMerge.js, RabbitProvider.jsx; src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/components/FileManager.jsx; electron/openPath.cjs, rabbitBins.cjs, main.cjs; src/dev/fixtures/rabbitFixturesAdapter.js**: As listed in consumers_to_change. There is no new IPC, no new page and no Edge Function change, so the F1-01 KNOWN count is untouched. Bundle: provider about 3 KB, catalogue hook about 4 KB and Files changes about 5 KB in the main chunk (INFERRED; to be measured). The walker is in the main process and outside the bundle. *(production caller: Each is the caller or callee of the new exports above; the Files page and R.A.B.B.I.T.'s Files tab mount ProjectFilesExplorer)*

### Migration order

- **NS-1: <next free>_storage_provider_network_share.sql**: ALTER TYPE public.storage_provider ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS 'network_share', plus its pg_enum post-condition. Nothing else.
  - *Constraints:* This migration contains the ADD VALUE and nothing that uses it (55P04). On wilson-dev, apply it for real and commit before rehearsing NS-2. It is additive and harmless, but follow procedure (2): drift read, LF copy, history row and md5. It has no suite of its own; NS-2's suites exercise it after the commit. CI applies each migration file in its own transaction from 0000, so NS-2 sees the value committed. Remeasure free numbers across every ref first: 0107 and suite 110 are claimed on paper by three OUTSTANDING fix shapes (the UNC CHECK, bin_files audio, avatars), so record the number taken in the header.
- **NS-2: <next>_network_share_catalogue.sql + suites, one commit**: In order: pre-flight (NS-1's label present; 0098's fn_realtime_broadcast, 0074's fn_file_events_capture and 0093's event CHECK are the deployed bodies, matched on anchor text with comments stripped; zero 'nas:%' paths); the helpers network_share_locator_ok and network_share_folder_of; files.share_missing_since; the two CHECKs; the two indexes; the guard function and trigger; the network_share_checks table with triggers, ENABLE+FORCE RLS, its policy and grants; network_share_catalogue_apply with REVOKE and GRANT; fn_realtime_broadcast restated from 0098; fn_file_events_capture restated from 0074; file_events_event_check widened by DROP and ADD; post-conditions. Same commit: supabase/tests/rls/<n>_network_share_checks.sql (one table, exact name), its RLS_TABLES line in .github/workflows/rls.yml, supabase/tests/rls/<n+1>_network_share_catalogue.sql (cross-table, added by hand to rls.yml's replay 'for f in' list in number order), and rlsSuiteLists.test.js's two tail pins (l.75 last five replay entries, l.111-115 last six picked suites).
  - *Constraints:* Rehearse as a rolled-back transaction (tap-hosted.py with NS-2 and both suites; collected must equal planned) only after NS-1 commits. Every other Session 1 migration that restates fn_realtime_broadcast (A-R1-04's workspace function is a different function; the Moth tables joining the project arm are not) or fn_file_events_capture (moves, body identity) must restate from whichever of them lands last. Their post-conditions must pin every arm, including 'network_share_checks' and the GUC early return, so no restatement silently drops another's lines. Run the whole pgTAP set before push: suite 35 (DEFINER anon revoke), 33 (the event vocabulary), 20 (realtime) and 96 (gateway) must pass. Budget: rehearse a 5,000-entry apply and record its time, the edit_history rows and the file_events rows. If it exceeds 3 s on dev, split the walk into 1,000-entry calls, with p_complete set only on the last call of a run, before building the client.
- **NS-3: desktop and client commits after NS-2 is applied on dev**: (a) electron/rootReachable.cjs extraction, with the rabbitBins tests green and unchanged; (b) electron/networkShare.cjs and the openPath.cjs 'share' arm with their tests; (c) networkShareProvider.js and the storage/index.js vocabulary and registration with their tests; (d) networkShareCatalogue.js, the adapter methods, realtimeMerge and RabbitProvider; (e) the Files page, fileTree, fileActions, filePreview and FileManager; (f) the fixtures variant; (g) docs. Handbook §3.1 route families, §4.4 inventory, §4.5 feeds, §12.1 provider table and §12.1a, §12.3 'catalogued', §12.7 catalogue, §12.7b and §12.7c ('no cloud row' sentences) and §17 workspace drive go in the same commits as the behaviour. RELEASE_TESTING strings are quoted from the code. OUTSTANDING's L1107 network-workspace entry and walkthrough 85 Q1 are answered by her NAS ruling of 2026-11-01 for metadata, with previews still post-release.
  - *Constraints:* No export lands before its caller (rule 19): the provider and its registration ship in one commit, and so do the hook and its two callers. writeGate.test.js must see a ready key in the Refresh GatedAction's canOnProject ctx. Run the full vitest suite with --maxWorkers=4 before the merge. Measure the bundle before and after, and account for any growth over 20 KB.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/<n>_network_share_checks.sql (table suite)**: Positive:
- A row written by the apply for a public project is read by an active member of the workspace.
- The private project's creator reads its row, and a workspace admin reads it too (0072 arm).
- workspace_id is filled from the project even when the RPC runs for a caller whose claim names it (populate trigger).
- updated_at advances on a second check (fn_audit_touch).
Negative:
- Another tenant reads 0.
- An inactive member reads 0.
- A non-creator member reads 0 for a private project.
- After the project is trashed, the row reads 0 (live-parent hop).
- authenticated INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE each throw (no grant, no policy).
- relforcerowsecurity is true.
Planted controls:
- Add a permissive FOR INSERT policy TO authenticated WITH CHECK (true): the INSERT-refused probe goes red.
- Replace the SELECT policy's EXISTS hop with true: the private-project and trashed-project probes go red.
- **supabase/tests/rls/<n+1>_network_share_catalogue.sql (cross-table; in rls.yml's replay list by hand)**: Positive:
- A member who can write applies 3 entries: 3 rows with provider network_share, storage_path 'nas:<folder>/…', thumbnail_url NULL and is_financial false.
- A repeat apply keeps the same ids and reports 0 added.
- A size change updates the row and keeps its id.
- A complete walk without one entry sets share_missing_since, and the row still exists.
- The entry seen again clears it (found_again 1).
- A folder renamed on the server (same leaf, size and m; complete walk) keeps the id with the new path, and file_events gains 'moved'.
- An incomplete walk marks nothing missing.
- p_gone marks a moved-in row outside the folder.
- A client UPDATE of description and tags on a network row succeeds (positive control for the guard).
- soft_delete_row and restore_soft_deleted work on a network row.
- file_events gets 'catalogued' and not 'uploaded'.
- Purging a network row writes a 'purged' certificate with body 'left on the company server' and enqueues nothing in storage_gc_queue.
Negative (each raises or skips as stated):
- A reviewer seat: 42501.
- A project in another workspace: 42501, same message.
- A private project as a non-creator member: 42501, same message as a nonexistent id.
- A trashed project: 42501.
- Root mismatch, or mode central with network retained: drive_changed.
- p_folder different from the row's folder: no_folder.
- 5,001 entries: too_many.
- An entry outside the folder: skipped.
- An entry under a PRIVATE project's folder nested inside this one: skipped, not counted, and no row exists afterwards.
- Entries with INVOICES, legal (lower case), X_CrewInvoices, P_DATABASES, .wilson, '..', a backslash, a colon, U+202E or a non-NFC name: skipped.
- A trashed row's locator in the walk: not resurrected and no duplicate.
- A direct client INSERT of a network_share row: guard 42501.
- A client UPDATE of storage_path, share_missing_since or size_bytes on a network row: guard.
- A client UPDATE turning a supabase row into network_share: guard.
- A network row with thumbnail_url, or a supabase row with storage_path 'nas:x': CHECK violation.
- Two rows with the same locator case-folded: unique violation.
- has_function_privilege('anon', apply, 'EXECUTE') is false.
Planted controls, each turning its named probe red:
- Drop trg_files_network_share_guard: the direct-INSERT probe.
- Remove the other-project prefix test from the apply: the nested-private-folder probe.
- Remove the deleted_at handling: the resurrection probe.
- Revert the 'catalogued' CASE: the events probe.
- Drop NOT is_financial from the shape CHECK, together with a crafted INSERT under the GUC: the money probe.
- **supabase/tests/rls/96_gateways.sql (extend), 33_file_lifecycle.sql (extend), 20_realtime.sql (extend), 60_workspace_storage_provider.sql (extend)**: 96:
- gateway_clips_for_tickets never returns a network_share files row whose locator matches a vetted clip's relative path.

### Risks

- Anyone who can write a project can feed fabricated entries, or a 'complete' empty walk, to the apply. That lists fake files or marks real ones missing in their own project.
  - *Mitigation:* Scope is the caller's own writable, visible project and its own folder only. Every locator is shape- and money-checked by the server, and other projects' folders are skipped. No row is ever deleted by a check. The actor is recorded in edit_history and file_events, and the next honest check repairs the marks. Catalogue rows grant no body access anywhere: the gateway has a post-condition, and the desktop route uses the person's own SMB rights within the company drive. This is the same trust level as OSP-07 today, and narrower.
- An SMB share that dies after answering can still hold a desktop thread during a preview or open stat.
  - *Mitigation:* The walk runs only in a Worker, after rootReachable answered within 2 s. The file route and open-path ask reachability first and use async stat. The residual stall is the accepted §17 limit, now confined to one request instead of a scan, and it is written in the handbook.
- Write amplification on the first check of a big folder: 5,000 files rows, 5,000 edit_history full-row diffs and 5,000 'catalogued' events in one transaction.
  - *Mitigation:* Realtime is suppressed to one signal. The dev rehearsal records the time and row counts for a 5,000-entry payload, and the apply is split into 1,000-entry calls if it runs over 3 s. Later checks write only rows whose metadata changed. edit_history's 90-day purge bounds the history. The Moth history area decides whether catalogue observations feed moth_history.
- Several Session 1 migrations restate fn_realtime_broadcast and fn_file_events_capture, and a later one silently drops this design's lines.
  - *Mitigation:* One chain per function, each restating from the latest file. Each migration's post-conditions pin every arm by name (the GUC early return, 'network_share_checks', the 'catalogued' CASE) with comments stripped, so a dropped line fails the migration, not a later probe.
- Pre-C4 Local Server receipts filed in <slug>_FILES on the NAS (residual 3) carry no flag and sit in no money folder, so the catalogue lists them as ordinary files.
  - *Mitigation:* This cannot be detected from metadata. The handbook §12.9 residual 3 already states it; extend that sentence to the catalogue. The INVOICES, FINANCE, LEGAL, old split invoice folder, _DATABASES and dot-folder exclusions cover every money place WILSON itself writes on the desktop (MEASURED main.cjs resolveProjectInvoicesDir and legalFiling).
- A nested folder layout. If a project folder is set inside another project's already-catalogued folder later, the outer project's rows there are no longer seen by its walks.
  - *Mitigation:* The missing sweep ignores rows under another project's prefix, so they are left stale rather than falsely missing. Record this in OUTSTANDING with its measurement. 0049 does not forbid nesting, and Admin guidance should advise against it.
- Path identity misses moves made on the server outside WILSON, other than exact renames. The old row is then marked missing and a new id appears, and Moth loses that file's layout and tags.
  - *Mitigation:* Ids are kept for exact folder or file renames (same leaf, size and time). Anything else is labelled honestly ('Not found on the server at the last check'). A wider relink-style match is a later improvement, written in OUTSTANDING.
- The company drive is repointed by an admin, or the computer still holds the old root until relaunch (§17 rule 33).
  - *Mitigation:* The apply refuses with drive_changed when the root walked differs from workspace_storage. The UI says to restart WILSON, and nothing is written. Rows remain. Locators are relative to the root, so a NAS rename keeps them valid.
- No end-to-end desktop proof against wilson-dev: no session can sign in there (house procedure 9).
  - *Mitigation:* Prove the server half with pgTAP on dev and the desktop half with vitest on real temp folders and the Electron module. Walkthrough pictures use the '?storage=network' fixtures. RELEASE_TESTING gains a desktop check for Audrey on a real share.
- Local-only Moth state (NS-D17) diverges between two computers holding copies of the same local project.
  - *Mitigation:* That is by definition: desktop-only projects are per machine (her ruling: one person working alone). Moth labels the island 'On this computer only', and nothing claims to sync.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* moth_local_scopes | Owner/workspace; machine-origin and opaque local project identity; technical backing project; last observation. | Owner-only Moth; no live channels/absolute paths; cannot impersonate cloud identity. | 121_moth_local_scopes.sql / moth_local_scopes
  - *Design:* Not built in Session 1, and recommended for removal. No Session 1 feature needs a cloud identity for a local item. A cloud backing project would show to admins through projects_select (0072) and reach projectsIndex, the Files picker and the workspace channel. Local ids in the cloud leak that local projects exist and invite impersonation, because the migration keeps ids. Local Moth state goes to the bundle and a per-machine file (NS-D15 to NS-D17). The RLS_TABLES total stays at 75, but the set differs: network_share_checks replaces moth_local_scopes.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Local scopes, E1/E18 | Keep legacy production source local. For new file-backed Moth data needing cloud identities, map owner-only technical catalogue parent through moth_local_scopes. No implicit full migration. Hide technical parents from normal lists.
  - *Design:* Replaced by (scope, id) node identity, 'migrated copy hidden by the cloudMigration record', and local storage of local Moth state. Session 1 reads through the active adapter, and mixed islands are Session 2's. Nothing about a local project reaches the cloud.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* NAS catalogue, E18 | Add distinct network body provider alongside all existing providers. Containment-checked relative locator; metadata only. Refresh on desktop project open and Refresh; show last checked. | Explicit complete constraint widening; 05_files, 58_workspace_storage, 60_workspace_storage_provider/provider contracts.
  - *Design:* MEASURED: no files CHECK enumerates providers. files_money_provider_chk is 'supabase OR not money', and workspace_storage_provider_chk already admits 'network'. So the only widenings are the enum value (its own migration) and file_events_event_check ('catalogued', restated from 0093). The design adds the new table network_share_checks (where 'last checked' lives), a new files column (share_missing_since), two new CHECKs, a guard trigger, one DEFINER RPC, and restatements of fn_realtime_broadcast (0098) and fn_file_events_capture (0074). Suite 58 needs nothing. Suite 60 needs only a no-change pin. The value is named 'network_share', not 'network'.
- *Plan:* (rules digest, not the plan) 'That needs Audrey's answer and a new table (not files rows). The catalogue must not become cloud files rows, which uploadFile's S36 refusal assumes do not exist.'
  - *Design:* The catalogue is files rows (plan §4 agrees). The S36 refusal is about uploading bodies and stays unchanged. Her NAS ruling of 2026-11-01 answers walkthrough 85 Q1 for metadata, so the 'no cloud row' sentences in storage/index.js, filePreview.js and handbook §12.7b, §12.7c and §17 are rewritten in the same commits.
- *Plan:* file_body_locations | Stable body identity/workspace; verified provider/immutable locator; thumbnail; publication generation/lifecycle; current file association.
  - *Design:* Catalogue rows are location-stable already: the locator never changes on a move, and there is no thumbnail and no gc. If file_body_locations is built, the catalogue apply writes its row in the same transaction. Nothing in this design depends on it.
- *Plan:* Proposed: one reusable eligibility helper, proposed name moth_can_read_target … validates actual type, existence, workspace, parent and audience.
  - *Design:* The apply uses can_write_project + passes_project_privacy + workspace + has_active_membership + not trashed, all inlined with one refusal message. A personal-space target must swap in that area's owner-only write helper. MEASURED: can_read_project does not exist and can_write_project has no workspace or privacy check, so all four checks are needed.
- *Plan:* (storage/index.js comment) getStorageProvider('local_server') will throw
  - *Design:* MEASURED stale: supabaseAdapter registers local_server on every surface. Corrected while storage/index.js is edited for FILE_PROVIDERS.NETWORK_SHARE.

### Questions for her, as drafted (to be put to her in plain words only after the synthesis)

- When someone deletes, in WILSON, a file that lives on the office server, WILSON takes it off its list and never touches the file on the server. What should happen once the 30-day bin empties and the file is still on the server?
  - WILSON lists it again at the next check, because it is still on the server. The delete message says so. (Recommended)
  - WILSON remembers that it was taken off the list and never lists it again on its own. Someone would have to bring it back by hand.
  - *Recommended:* WILSON lists it again at the next check, because it is still on the server. The delete message says so. (Recommended)

### Review — security (MO1 design round: office-NAS catalogue and desktop-only projects)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes, not as is. Three blockers leak data people cannot see today. NS-SEC-01: projects.folder_slug can be written by any project writer and has no limits, so any member can catalogue any folder on the company drive, which gets around the 0049 rule that only admins and managers set project folders. NS-SEC-02: the rule that skips other projects' folders fails open for any project whose folder cannot be placed under the current drive (no folder name set, an alias or an old drive, a service-role write), so private files get listed inside another project. NS-SEC-03: every catalogue write lands in edit_history, which any manager can read with no privacy check, so private-project and personal-space file names reach managers. Four major issues follow: a 'local' drive is accepted, which publishes each person's own disk; the checking person's server permissions decide what everyone can list; a private folder nested later stays listed in the outer project; the personal-space skip has no mechanism. Each has a cheap, fail-closed fix that can be shown red first. What holds up: the write guard and two CHECKs stop clients forging locators or money and Legal flags; the 'nas:' prefix cannot collide with storage-gc or teardown (teardownCollect filters storage_provider='supabase'); the gateway post-condition matches 0104 (neither function mentions public.files today); the open-path allowlist refuses programs; network_share_checks follows privacy through the live-parent hop, can_read_project_topic and file_events_select; rabbit_money_segment is case-insensitive (upper()), so the 'legal' lower-case probe is correct; fn_realtime_broadcast (0098) and fn_file_events_capture (0074) are the latest bodies, as the design says.

- **NS-SEC-01 [blocker]** Through projects.folder_slug, any project writer can point the catalogue at ANY folder on the company drive. That bypasses 0049's admin/manager folder seat. The design's claim that 'a slug-only project's folder is always top-level' is false, because folder_slug is unconstrained, member-writable text and network_share_locator_ok admits '/'.
  - *Scenario:* Maya is an ordinary member (app_role user) of an unstaffed project U whose folder_root is NULL. can_write_project's unstaffed arm admits her, so through PostgREST she runs UPDATE projects SET folder_slug='Accounts' (or 'HR/Payroll'). No guard fires: 0049 checks folder_root only. On the desktop she opens U, and the walk route accepts '\\nas\share\Accounts' because it is inside the root. The apply then computes v_folder = network_share_folder_of(NULL,'Accounts',root) = 'Accounts', which equals p_folder, so every file name and size in the company's Accounts folder (not INVOICES/FINANCE/LEGAL by name) becomes a files row in U. Every U member, reviewers included, sees those rows on the web and in Moth, though none of them could see the folder before.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/migrations/0013_project_members.sql projects_update: USING can_write_project(id), WITH CHECK only workspace_id. 0013 can_write_project: `NOT project_is_staffed(p_project)` admits every caller. 0041_folders.sql adds folder_slug TEXT with no CHECK. The only projects triggers (grep CREATE TRIGGER trg_projects_) are auto_staff, populate_workspace, folder_root_guard, active_shot_list_guard, open_budget_version_guard and budget_settings_guard, so none guards folder_slug. 0049 fn_project_folder_root_guard examines NEW.folder_root only, and its header names devtools callers as the threat. In the design, network_share_folder_of has the arm `WHEN p_folder_root IS NULL AND p_folder_slug IS NOT NULL THEN p_folder_slug`, and network_share_locator_ok permits multi-segment paths.
  - *Fix:* In the catalogue migration, add a NEW guard trigger on projects (do not rewrite 0049's function). (a) Changing folder_slug once it is set requires COALESCE(current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager'), false), with service_role passing, as in 0049. (b) A CHECK makes folder_slug a single segment in fileSlugify's output shape: no '/' or '\', no dot-led segment, not a money segment, NFC. Before adding it, pre-flight that existing rows comply. In network_share_folder_of, return NULL unless the slug is one segment. Probes: a member UPDATE of folder_slug throws; slug 'a/b' violates the CHECK; a planted control dropping the guard turns the member-slug probe red. Mirror the seat in canSetProjectFolder.
- **NS-SEC-02 [blocker]** The 'skip other projects' folders' rule (NS-D8) fails open. It skips only projects whose folder network_share_folder_of can place under the CURRENT root. A project the function returns NULL for is silently never skipped, even though the desktop resolves a real folder for it on the share. That covers a NULL folder_slug (the desktop uses fileSlugify(title)), a folder_root under an alias or a previous root after an admin repoints the drive, and a folder_root written by service_role, which 0049 lets through. That project's private files are then catalogued into whichever project's folder contains them.
  - *Scenario:* (1) Private project P (creator Ana) has folder_slug NULL. The desktop resolves its folder as root\\Salt-Hours-Secret (fileSlugify of its title). A manager sets public project A's folder_root to root\\Salt-Hours-Secret, the folder they see on the share. network_share_folder_of(P) is NULL, so A's walk skips nothing, and P's file names, including P's own <slug>_FILES, become rows in A that every A member reads. (2) P's folder_root is \\nas\share\Proj\Private, nested in slug-only project 'Proj'. An admin then repoints root_path to \\nas.corp.local\share, the same server. P's folder_of becomes NULL, and Proj's next walk lists Proj/Private/** into Proj.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED electron/main.cjs resolveProjectFolder (l.1848-1860): folder_root when it exists and storedRootUsable, else path.join(rootBase, folder_slug || fileSlugify(title)). The design's network_share_folder_of returns NULL in both cases. Apply step (6) keeps an entry unless another project's folder_of prefixes it, so a NULL never matches. 0049 fn_project_folder_root_guard: `IF current_setting('role', true) = 'service_role' THEN RETURN NEW` and 'Only a CHANGE is examined', so a repointed root never re-validates existing folder_root values. 0048 lets an admin change root_path freely.
  - *Fix:* Fail closed. In apply step (6), if ANY project in the workspace (any deleted state) has a non-NULL folder_root that network_share_folder_of cannot place, or has both folder_root and folder_slug NULL, refuse with 'network_share: folders_unplaced' (after the authority step, so it is no oracle) and write nothing. Backfill folder_slug NOT NULL from the desktop's ensureProjectFolders or a SQL twin of fileSlugify, held equal by a parity test, so the second case disappears. Probes: a NULL-slug private project whose derived folder sits inside the walked folder makes the apply refuse; a folder_root under a different root string makes it refuse; the planted control 'treat NULL as no folder' turns both red.
- **NS-SEC-03 [blocker]** Catalogue writes put full-row diffs of private-project rows (and, through the personal-space interface, personal-space rows) into edit_history. Every workspace manager can read them, because edit_history_select has no project-privacy arm. File names, paths, sizes and folder ids of a private project, or of someone's personal space, reach managers. That breaks 'private projects visible only to creator and admins' and 'personal space owner-only'.
  - *Scenario:* Ana, creator of private project P (or the owner of a personal space catalogued by the same RPC), opens it on the desktop. The apply inserts 800 network_share rows, and fn_edit_history_capture writes 800 'create' entries holding the whole files row (name, storage_path 'nas:P/…', size). Ben, a workspace manager who is not P's creator and cannot read P, runs SELECT * FROM edit_history WHERE entity_type='files'. edit_history_select admits him (app_role manager, not is_financial) and he reads every file name in P.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/migrations/0092_legal_gate_managers.sql edit_history_select (l.1017-1028): workspace + current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager') + has_active_membership + money/legal arm, with no projects hop and no passes_project_privacy. 0012_edit_history.sql attaches trg_files_edit_history, and its diff holds full rows on create and delete (core fact 6).
