Core fact 7 records the gap as INFERRED and not in OUTSTANDING. The design's own risk list counts '5,000 edit_history full-row diffs' per first check.
  - *Fix:* In NS-2, restate edit_history_select from 0092, keeping every arm verbatim, and add `AND (project_id IS NULL OR EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = edit_history.project_id))`. That is the live-parent hop under the caller's projects_select, which carries 0072's privacy arm. Add the personal-space owner-only exclusion that area defines. Post-condition: the policy text contains the hop and all of 0092's arms. Probes in the catalogue suite: a manager reads 0 history rows for a private project's catalogue, and the creator and an admin read them; the planted control of removing the hop turns the probe red. Do not rely on 'admins only' for edit_history: that gate is app_role, not privacy.
- **NS-SEC-04 [major]** The apply accepts a 'local'-kind drive. With a root such as C:\\Projects or D:\\Work, the same locator names a DIFFERENT folder on every computer. Each walker publishes their own machine's folder contents to the cloud, and another member who opens the row on the desktop opens a different file on their own disk under the same name.
  - *Scenario:* An admin sets a network workspace's root to 'D:\\Work' (root_kind 'local'). On Leo's laptop, D:\\Work\\Salt-Hours exists and holds his personal files. main.cjs applies the root there because the path exists. When Leo opens Salt Hours, his walk lists them and every project member sees Leo's local file names. Mia's complete walk on her machine then marks them all missing and lists hers instead. Opening a row runs the opener's own local file.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0048_workspace_storage.sql workspace_storage_kind_chk admits root_kind IN ('unc','local'), and kind_shape_chk ties 'local' to a non-UNC path. 0050 workspace_storage_root_provider_path_chk only requires provider='network' for a path. electron/main.cjs rabbit:set-workspace-root (l.5436-5445): 'A local-kind root … names a DIFFERENT folder on every machine. Apply it only where it actually exists', so workspaceRootDir is set on any machine where the path exists. The design's apply step (2) checks only mode='byos', provider='network' and root equality.
  - *Fix:* Apply step (2) also requires root_kind = 'unc', else 'network_share: drive_changed' (or a sentence of its own). networkShare.cjs refuses to walk or serve unless workspaceRootDir is a UNC path. The client eligibility in networkShareCatalogue.js checks the same. Probes: a byos+network workspace with a local root refuses the apply, and a walk-route test with a drive-letter root answers refused before any fs call.
- **NS-SEC-05 [major]** The catalogue republishes the WALKER's SMB view to every project member, reviewers and web users included. Any subfolder of a project folder that the company restricts with NAS permissions has its names and sizes exposed to people the share itself would refuse. The design states that SMB rights are the authority for opening a file (NS-D13), but lets any project writer's rights decide what everyone can list. Walkers with different rights also flip each other's rows between missing and found.
  - *Scenario:* Salt Hours' folder holds a 'Talent_Contracts' subfolder that the studio's NAS permissions limit to producers, and its name is not a money segment. Ola, a producer and project member, opens the project, and her walk lists the contracts. Every member and external reviewer of Salt Hours now sees the contract file names on the web and in Moth. Next, Raj, a member without that NAS permission, opens the project. His complete walk does not see them and marks them all 'Not found on the server'.
  - *Evidence:* Design NS-D9 (eligibility is canOnProject entity.write, mirroring can_write_project) and NS-D13 ('The person's own SMB rights on the share are the access authority on the desktop'). MEASURED 0013 can_write_project admits app admin/manager, every caller on an unstaffed project and every manager or member seat. The files_select policy (latest 0092) has no seat arm, so reviewers read every non-money row. Before this design a network workspace had no cloud rows at all (storage/index.js SERVER_MEDIA_SENTENCE, core fact 8).
  - *Fix:* Make it a decision for Audrey, in plain words: whose view of the server becomes the list everyone sees. Until she answers, (a) restrict the apply to a named seat (workspace admin/manager or the project's manager seat, can_manage_project_roster), not can_write_project; (b) record last_checked_by (already present) and let a complete walk mark missing only rows that a walk by the same person, or a person holding the same seat, last saw; (c) the Admin storage page says the catalogue publishes whatever the checking person can open on the server. Add a probe that a member seat cannot apply, if (a) is chosen.
- **NS-SEC-06 [major]** Creating or re-pointing a private project's folder INSIDE an already-catalogued project folder leaves that private project's files listed in the outer project, visible to all of its members. The outer project's existing rows under the new prefix are neither removed nor hidden, because the missing sweep ignores them. The private project's own walk cannot take them over, because the per-workspace unique index plus ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING keeps the outer project's rows.
  - *Scenario:* Public project A ('Salt-Hours/') has been catalogued, including Salt-Hours/Pitch/*.pdf. A manager creates private project P with folder_root root\\Salt-Hours\\Pitch, which 0049 allows because it is strictly inside the root. P's walk inserts nothing for those locators, since A's rows already hold them. A's walks now skip the prefix and leave A's rows untouched. Every A member keeps reading P's pitch files, and the names of files added later stay invisible to P too.
  - *Evidence:* Design apply steps (6) (skip entries under another project's prefix), (9) (ON CONFLICT (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) DO NOTHING) and (10) (the missing sweep excludes rows 'under another project's folder'), and its risk 'left stale rather than falsely missing'. MEASURED 0049 fn_project_folder_root_guard checks containment in the root only, never against other projects' folders.
  - *Fix:* Refuse nesting in a network workspace. Add a new BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE OF folder_root, folder_slug trigger on projects that refuses a folder inside, equal to, or containing another project's folder in the same workspace, any deleted state, computed with network_share_folder_of. Pre-flight existing rows. If nesting must stay, the inner project's apply must instead reassign (UPDATE project_id) or hide the outer project's rows under its prefix, in the same transaction and under the advisory locks of both projects. Probe: create P inside A's catalogued folder, then the A member reads 0 rows under P's prefix. Planted control: remove the guard or reassignment and the probe goes red.
- **NS-SEC-07 [major]** The skip list covers only projects rows. A personal space's folder on the drive, owner-only by her ruling, is protected only if the unbuilt personal-space area remembers to extend network_share_folder_of. Nothing in this design turns red if it does not. Any personal-space folder physically inside a project folder (or a project walk whose folder contains it) would be catalogued into the project.
  - *Scenario:* The personal-space area places Ana's space at root\\Salt-Hours\\_people\\ana, or a manager later sets a project folder that contains the personal-space directory. Salt Hours' walk lists Ana's personal files into Salt Hours, readable by every member, in violation of 'owner-only in Moth' and 'a single folder that counts as just them'.
  - *Evidence:* Design NS-D8 and apply step (6) build the skip test from `another project o in v_ws` only. The consumers_to_change 'Personal-space area (interface)' entry says 'network_share_folder_of must know the personal-space folder' but specifies no mechanism, probe or post-condition.
  - *Fix:* Define ONE SQL function now, for example network_share_owned_prefixes(p_ws), returning every folder-owning space's prefix (projects in any deleted state, plus personal spaces once built). The apply's skip and the nesting guard (NS-SEC-06) read only it. Its post-condition and a suite probe pin it. Alternatively, forbid any personal-space folder inside, or containing, a project folder through the same guard. Add a probe with a personal-space folder nested in a project folder before the personal-space migration can merge.
- **NS-SEC-08 [minor]** Path-identity uniqueness across the whole workspace makes the catalogue an existence oracle for rows held by spaces the caller cannot see. A file the walker can see on the share that is held by another project (private, trashed or a personal space, after a move) is silently not listed, and the RPC's 'added' count comes back smaller than the new entries sent.
  - *Scenario:* Ana moves a NAS row from public project A into her personal space. Its locator stays under A's folder. Raj, an A member, sees the file on the share and runs Refresh: the file is absent from A's list and 'added' is one short. He learns that someone's private space or project holds it.
  - *Evidence:* Design NS-D4 (UNIQUE (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)), trashed rows included, ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING) and step (12) (counts of this project's rows). The ruling: 'no count, no placeholder that reveals existence'.
  - *Fix:* Have 'added' report only entries that became visible to the caller, and do not let the client compare it against entries sent. Better, drop the counts from the RPC answer and let the client re-read through RLS. Write the residual (a file visible on the share but held elsewhere is not listed) into the handbook as accepted. Or put the trade-off to Audrey: per-project uniqueness, where a moved row could be listed twice.
- **NS-SEC-09 [minor]** The writer GUC is a bare 'on' that is never cleared and not tied to a project. Every house precedent binds the GUC to the target id and resets it to '' before returning. As designed, any later statement in the same transaction passes the guard for every network_share row of any project, which includes a future DEFINER that calls the apply and then copies client input.
  - *Scenario:* A later session writes a DEFINER that calls network_share_catalogue_apply and then updates files from caller-supplied rows in the same transaction. The guard sees wilson.network_share_writer='on' and lets it rewrite storage_path on another project's catalogue rows.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED 0069_otter_nomination_self_approval_audit.sql l.230/237, 0084 l.956/958, 0086 l.232/238 and 0100 l.665/674, each `set_config('wilson.<x>', <id>::text, true)` … `set_config('wilson.<x>', '', true)`. The design's step (5) is set_config('wilson.network_share_writer','on',true) with no reset.
  - *Fix:* Set the GUC to p_project_id::text and reset it to '' at the end of the apply. Make the guard require COALESCE(current_setting('wilson.network_share_writer', true) = NEW.project_id::text, false), checking OLD.project_id as well on UPDATE. Make the realtime early return compare to NEW.project_id. Add a post-condition (comments stripped) that the apply contains the reset.
- **NS-SEC-10 [minor]** The new desktop share routes list only the launch-token gate. The sibling file-serving routes also require a fail-closed same-origin check, because the httpOnly token cookie rides requests from other loopback ports (same-site). NS-D13 also names insideByRealPath, which does synchronous realpath and existsSync calls on the main thread against the NAS.
  - *Scenario:* A page loaded in WILSON's Electron session from another 127.0.0.1 port embeds <img src=http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/rabbit/network-share/file/…>. The cookie is sent and the route answers, so onload and onerror probe file existence on the share. Separately, a share that dies after rootReachable answered blocks the window inside insideByRealPath's realpathSync.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED electron/fileReads.cjs isSameOriginRequest (l.29-36) and header ('the bins' gate: same-origin only, failing closed … another port is same-site'). electron/localToken.cjs: the cookie is sent on a plain <img> and <video>. electron/localMedia.cjs insideByRealPath (l.116-128) uses fs.realpathSync.native and fs.existsSync.
  - *Fix:* mountNetworkShare refuses unless isSameOriginRequest(req) on GET, HEAD and the POST walk (403, fail closed), ahead of the token check's success path. Use an async realpath twin (fs.promises.realpath) for the share routes. Tests: a cross-origin request answers 403; a same-site Sec-Fetch-Site answers 403.
- **NS-SEC-11 [minor]** Caller-supplied entries create permanent rows with no cap. Any writer, which on an unstaffed project means any active member, can call the apply repeatedly with 5,000 fabricated locators each time. Rows are never deleted by checks, so files, edit_history and file_events grow without bound, and every member sees invented 'files on the company server'.
  - *Scenario:* A member scripts 200 calls with distinct fake names ('Payroll_2026_final.xlsx', …) under an unstaffed project's folder. That is 1,000,000 files rows, 1,000,000 history diffs and 1,000,000 'catalogued' events, all shown to the project as real server files until someone deletes them by hand.
  - *Evidence:* Design step (4) caps one call at 5,000 entries. Steps (9)/(10) insert, and rows are only ever marked missing. The design's risk 1 accepts fabrication 'at the OSP-07 trust level'. MEASURED 0013 can_write_project's unstaffed arm.
  - *Fix:* Add a server-side ceiling on live network_share rows per project (for example 20,000; refuse 'too_many' beyond it) and a minimum interval between applies per project, read from network_share_checks.last_checked_at under the advisory lock. Probe both.
- **NS-SEC-12 [minor]** NS-D17 puts local Moth state 'in a new moth key in each project.json' without saying WHERE. If it lands on bundle.project, mirrorProjectDatabases copies it verbatim to <slug>_DATABASES/project.json inside the project folder. That folder is on the shared drive whenever the root is a NAS, so personal tags and private local notes would become readable on the share.
  - *Scenario:* A desktop-only project lives under a NAS root. Moth stores Leo's personal tags under bundle.project.moth, and the next mirror pass writes them to \\\\nas\\share\\Proj\\Proj_DATABASES\\project.json, where anyone with access to the share reads them.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED electron/main.cjs mirrorProjectDatabases (l.2173-2182): writeMirror(path.join(dbDir,'project.json'), bundle.project) with dbDir = <projectFolder>/<slug>_DATABASES.
  - *Fix:* Specify a top-level bundle key (bundle.moth, a sibling of bundle.project), never a field of bundle.project. Add a test that mirrorProjectDatabases' project.json carries no 'moth' key. Keep moth-local.json under the app's userData rabbit-data, not under an open demo folder's .wilson.

### Review — Correctness and house rules: the office-NAS catalogue and desktop-only projects (MO1 design round)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes. The overall approach (files rows with a separate 'network_share' body value added in its own migration, a 'nas:' namespace, a guard trigger, one SECURITY DEFINER writer, a check table with no owner) fits the code. It never needs a files policy restated, and the provider registry, the gc WHEN clause, storage-gc, teardown and the thumbnail batches all handle the new rows as claimed. As written, two things stop it. Restating fn_realtime_broadcast and adding a trigger turns six realtime pins red (two md5 pins in 100 and 101, three trigger-set pins in 72, 99 and 101, and the whole-arm pin in 95), and the temp table makes a second apply in the same transaction fail, so the cross-table suite cannot run. Before the build: bind and clear the writer GUC (0069's precedent), stop renames from colliding with locators held by trashed or other-project rows, design the batching instead of the fallback, keep NAS stats off the main thread, plan for frame sequences against the 5,000 cap, and fix the gc post-condition text. Two of her rulings need her answer rather than the design's reading: whether names from SMB-restricted folders may be shown to everyone on the project, and whether Moth state for local projects may live on that computer. The edit_history privacy gap also has to be closed for catalogue writes.

- **NS-X01 [blocker]** Restating fn_realtime_broadcast as designed turns six registry pins red, and none of them is in the design's test list. The design says it follows the budget_versions precedent, but that precedent (0101) deliberately left fn_realtime_broadcast untouched and used its own thin trigger function.
  - *Scenario:* NS-2 lands as designed. Then 101_table_views probe 18 (md5 'e5abdcf2…') and 100_no_main_item probe 7 (md5 IN 0096/0098) fail on the new body. 95_legal_gate_managers probe 170 compares the whole 'files' arm, whitespace-collapsed, to 0088's text, so the GUC early-return line fails it. 72_milestone_realtime, 99_task_items probe 16 and 101_table_views probe 17 compare the exact trigger set of fn_realtime_broadcast against eighteen tables, so trg_network_share_checks_realtime makes it nineteen and fails all three. The pgTAP job goes red on the first push.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/tests/rls/101_table_views.sql probes 17 and 18 (l.330-343); 100_no_main_item.sql probe 7 (l.218-221); 95_legal_gate_managers.sql probe 170 (l.1493-1498); 72_milestone_realtime.sql l.202-211; 99_task_items.sql l.329-335. 0101_versions_and_items_realtime.sql header l.37-40 ('fn_realtime_broadcast stays 0098's (md5 e5abdcf2…)') and fn_budget_versions_broadcast (realtime.send, a thin record).
  - *Fix:* Give network_share_checks its own thin trigger, fn_network_share_checks_broadcast, in 0101's shape (realtime.send with {id, project_id}, SECURITY DEFINER, a catalog probe, a catch-all WARNING, REVOKE from every client role). That keeps the eighteen-table trigger-set pins true. The GUC early return in the files arm is unavoidable, so the same commit must restate the md5 in 101 probe 18 and 100 probe 7 and the expected arm text in 95 probe 170, each with a dated comment. Name all three in the tests section, and add a pin that fn_network_share_checks_broadcast never hands broadcast_changes a whole row.
- **NS-X02 [blocker]** Apply step 6 creates `temp _ns ON COMMIT DROP`. A second call to network_share_catalogue_apply in the same transaction fails with 42P07 (relation already exists). The cross-table suite calls apply many times inside one BEGIN…ROLLBACK, so it cannot run.
  - *Scenario:* In suite <n+1>, probe 1 applies 3 entries, which creates _ns. Probe 2 ('a repeat apply keeps the same ids') calls apply again in the same transaction, and CREATE TEMP TABLE _ns raises 42P07. Every later probe aborts and the plan count fails. tap-all.mjs and the dev rehearsal hit the same thing, because both run each suite as one transaction.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: the design's step (6) text in network_share_catalogue_apply. Pgtap convention 'BEGIN; SELECT plan(N); … ROLLBACK' (moth1_core facts schema 15; scripts/tap-all.mjs runs one transaction per suite). INFERRED from Postgres semantics: ON COMMIT DROP drops only at commit.
  - *Fix:* Use CTEs or a VALUES-backed subquery instead of a temp table. Or use CREATE TEMP TABLE IF NOT EXISTS … ON COMMIT DELETE ROWS followed by TRUNCATE at entry, with DROP TABLE at exit. Add a probe that calls apply twice in one transaction.
- **NS-X03 [major]** The writer GUC is set to a bare 'on' and never cleared. It stays live for every later statement in the caller's transaction. The precedent the design cites does the opposite: it binds the GUC to an id and clears it straight after the write. Because it stays on, the guard's negative probes cannot bite after the first apply, and the GUC also suppresses realtime for later files writes in the same transaction.
  - *Scenario:* In the cross-table suite, any probe after the first apply that does a 'direct client INSERT of a network_share row' or a 'client UPDATE of storage_path' passes the guard, because wilson.network_share_writer is still 'on'. The probe goes red, or a reordered suite goes green with the guard dropped, so the planted control 'drop trg_files_network_share_guard' proves nothing. In production, any future SECURITY DEFINER function that calls the apply and then writes files rows from caller input does so with the guard off.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0069_otter_nomination_self_approval_audit.sql otter_nomination_apply l.205-237. It sets 'wilson.otter_nomination_promote' to v_course.id, then set_config(…, '', true), with the comment 'must not still be live for any later statement in the caller's transaction'. Design: steps (5) and (12) never reset the GUC.
  - *Fix:* Set the GUC to p_project_id::text around each write block and reset it to '' before RETURN. Have fn_files_network_share_guard and the files arm of fn_realtime_broadcast compare it to NEW/OLD.project_id::text, not to 'on'. Add a probe in which a direct INSERT after a successful apply in the same transaction is still refused 42501.
- **NS-X04 [major]** Step 8, the exact-match rename, can move a vanished row's storage_path onto a locator that another files row already holds: a trashed row of this project, or a row moved into another project. The unique index includes trashed rows, so the UPDATE raises 23505 and the whole apply rolls back. Every later complete check fails the same way until that row is purged 30 days later.
  - *Scenario:* Copies A/x.mov and B/x.mov have the same size and mtime. Someone trashes the B listing in WILSON, then deletes A/x.mov on the server. The next complete walk sees B/x.mov, which no live row of this project matches. Live row A is now missing, with the same leaf, size and m, unique on both sides, so step 8 pairs them. UPDATE storage_path='nas:…/B/x.mov' collides with the trashed row in files_network_share_locator_key, and the project can never be checked again until the purge. The same happens when the held locator belongs to a row moved into a private project Q, and the error then also confirms that the row exists.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: the design's index files_network_share_locator_key (workspace_id, lower(storage_path)) with trashed rows included, and steps (7)-(9), where step 8 only checks uniqueness within this project's vanished rows and the entries. 0014 purge_soft_deleted runs on a 30-day cron.
  - *Fix:* In step 8, exclude every entry whose lower('nas:'||p) already exists in any files row of the workspace (any project, any deleted_at). Do the same in step 7 for updates. Add a probe for a trashed row plus a twin rename, and one for a row moved into another project plus a rename. Both must apply cleanly and keep the trashed row untouched.
- **NS-X05 [major]** The 'split into 1,000-entry calls with p_complete only on the last call' fallback breaks the missing sweep and the rename match. Both read only the current call's _ns, and the server keeps no record of which earlier calls belong to the same walk.
  - *Scenario:* The rehearsal exceeds 3 s, so the client sends five 1,000-entry calls. On the fifth call (p_complete=true), step 10 marks share_missing_since on every live row under the folder that is not in that call's 1,000 entries, which is about 4,000 files that are present. Step 8 pairs vanished rows with entries from this call only. The next walk then clears 4,000 marks, so edit_history and realtime churn on every check.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: the design's migration_order NS-2 constraint (the split fallback) against apply steps (8) and (10) ('not in _ns').
  - *Fix:* Choose the batching design now. Either stage the entries per run (a run id in an unlogged staging table keyed by project and run, swept at completion), or have the RPC mark missing only from a server-side set accumulated across calls in one run. Otherwise drop the fallback and paginate by folder with per-subtree completeness.
- **NS-X06 [major]** The file route and the open-path 'share' arm still run synchronous filesystem calls on the main thread against the NAS. The design says 'async stat', but the helpers it names are synchronous. insideByRealPath runs up to 64 existsSync calls plus two realpathSync.native calls, and resolveOpenTarget runs existsSync, statSync and realpathSync. Each range request of a video preview repeats them on the main thread.
  - *Scenario:* A preview of a 'missing' row, which the design labels and expects people to click, walks ancestors with existsSync over SMB. Or the share dies after rootReachable cached 'on' (a 15 s TTL): every range request of the VideoStage then holds the main process for the SMB timeout. That is the 5 to 42 s window freeze OUTSTANDING records for bins.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/localMedia.cjs insideByRealPath l.116-131 (sync), electron/openPath.cjs resolveOpenTarget l.87-92 (existsSync, statSync, realpathSync), and electron/rabbitBins.cjs l.1692-1703 (the measured freeze) with statKind l.1780-1794, the async bounded precedent.
  - *Fix:* Write an async containment for the share routes. Await fs.promises.realpath of the root once per reachability window, run the candidate's realpath and stat through statKind's pattern (STAT_MAX slots, the ROOT_WAIT_MS race, markRootOff on timeout), and refuse a missing target without walking ancestors. Give resolveOpenTarget an async 'share' path, or resolve the share target before calling openOrReveal. Add a test that spies on fs sync calls during GET and the open IPC.
- **NS-X07 [major]** The 5,000-file and depth-8 cap lists every frame of an image sequence as its own files row. A project with EXR, DPX or TIFF sequences is truncated on every walk, in the same directory order, so complete never becomes true. Files past the cap are never listed, nothing is ever marked missing, and the rename match never runs. Bins already treats sequences as one row.
  - *Scenario:* One VFX shot with three 1,200-frame EXR plates exceeds 5,000 entries. Every check returns complete=false with the same first 5,000 entries. The banner says 'partly checked' forever, deliverables beyond the cap never appear in Moth, and a renamed folder churns ids because step 8 is skipped.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/rabbitBins.cjs row.is_sequence (l.1055), where bins collapse sequences, and the design's NS-D7 limits with step (8)/(10) 'only when p_complete'. INFERRED: production folders hold sequences, as the VIDEO and pipeline extension lists in main.cjs and openPath.cjs OPEN_ALLOWED_EXT (.exr, .dpx) show.
  - *Fix:* Collapse numbered sequences into one entry, reusing bins' sequence detection, before counting. Make the walk resumable: a cursor per subtree, with completeness recorded per walked subtree so the missing sweep runs per complete subtree. State the measured cost in the risks.
- **NS-X08 [major]** Her privacy ruling says nothing may appear that a person cannot already see. The catalogue breaks it. Its contents are whatever the walking person's own SMB rights can see, and the rows then go to every reader of the project, on the web included, through files_select. People whose NAS permissions hide a subfolder see its file names.
  - *Scenario:* An HR or legal subfolder inside a project folder is NTFS-restricted to two people, under a name that is not a money segment, such as 'Contracts_Talent'. One of those two opens the project on the desktop, and the walk lists the subfolder. Every member of the project, and every admin through projects_select, now sees those file names, sizes and dates in the Files page and in Moth, where before only the two could see them.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: the design's NS-D13 ('The person's own SMB rights on the share are the access authority on the desktop') and NS-D3/D9 (any entity.write member walks). 0092 files_select grants by project only. The task text's ruling ('nothing appears in Moth that a person cannot already see').
  - *Fix:* Add this to questions_for_audrey in plain words: whether a file on the server that only some people may open there may be listed for everyone on the project. Until she answers, either limit the catalogue to the project's own WILSON-made subfolders (<slug>_FILES, ASSETS, SCENES and the like from FOLDER_CATEGORIES) or record it as an accepted risk in the handbook §17 and OUTSTANDING.
- **NS-X09 [major]** NS-D17 decides on its own to keep Moth state for local projects in local JSON stores. That contradicts Audrey's measured rule ('all database data lives in Supabase at all times; local storage holds only media files and assets') and PLAN_MOTH's row 'new Moth databases stay Supabase'. The design reinterprets her rule as applying to cloud projects only, and does not ask her.
  - *Scenario:* S2 to S4 build local layouts, tags and boards into project.json under 'moth' and into rabbit-data/moth-local.json. Those are a local database of record, which the rule and the rules digest (#43: 'Local trash must not become a local-only database of record') forbid. Her later review then finds the house rule broken in three sessions' worth of work.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: moth1_rules.md item 43 (handbook §12.8 L2915-2924, §12.8a); PLAN_MOTH.md l.115 ('new Moth databases stay Supabase'); the design's NS-D17 rationale.
  - *Fix:* Move the NS-D15 to D17 choice into questions_for_audrey in everyday words: whether a project kept only on one computer may keep its Moth layout and tags on that computer, or whether they wait until it moves to the cloud. Session 1 needs no answer to build, so record it as open, and do not write the 'moth' bundle key until she rules.
- **NS-X10 [major]** Every catalogue insert and update writes a full-row edit_history diff. edit_history_select (latest 0092) has no privacy or projects hop. A private project's NAS catalogue therefore becomes readable by every workspace manager, against her ruling that private projects are visible only to their creator and admins. The gap already exists, but the catalogue adds up to 5,000 new rows per check.
  - *Scenario:* A creator opens a private project on the desktop. 5,000 files rows are inserted, which writes 5,000 edit_history diffs with names and paths. A manager who is not the creator opens history, or queries edit_history over PostgREST, and reads all of them.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: moth1_core facts schema 6/7 (edit_history_select 0092: workspace + app_role admin|manager + membership + money arm, no projects or privacy hop); 0012 fn_edit_history_capture on files.
  - *Fix:* In the same session, either add the passes_project_privacy / projects-hop arm to edit_history_select (restating 0092's latest body whole), or skip edit_history capture for catalogue writes while the project-bound GUC is set (restating 0061's fn_edit_history_capture). Add a probe: a manager reads 0 history rows for a private project's catalogued file.
- **NS-X11 [major]** The post-condition that trg_files_gc_enqueue's WHEN clause 'still reads IN ('supabase', 's3')' can never match the catalog. pg_get_triggerdef renders the IN list as `= ANY (ARRAY['supabase'::text, 's3'::text])`, so if it is written as a text match the migration RAISEs everywhere.
  - *Scenario:* NS-2 runs on dev and the post-condition aborts the migration. A hurried fix then loosens or drops the check.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0054_thumbnail_follows_its_source.sql trg_files_gc_enqueue WHEN (OLD.storage_provider::text IN ('supabase', 's3') OR OLD.thumbnail_url IS NOT NULL) l.150-157. 0054's own post-condition matches only '%thumbnail_url%' (l.241-247) for this reason. INFERRED: Postgres deparses IN lists as = ANY(ARRAY[…]).
  - *Fix:* Match the normalized form (`'supabase'::text` and `'s3'::text` both present and no 'network_share'), or prove it by behaviour: the suite purges a network row and asserts that storage_gc_queue gained nothing (the design already has this probe), and the post-condition only asserts the trigger exists.
- **NS-X12 [minor]** Three of the named planted controls cannot turn their probes red, so they prove nothing. (1) A permissive INSERT policy on network_share_checks: authenticated has no INSERT grant, so the INSERT still fails 42501. (2) Dropping NOT is_financial from the shape CHECK: files_money_provider_chk still refuses a financial row that is not on supabase. (3) Removing deleted_at handling in step 7: a trashed row is updated but never undeleted, and the unique index still stops the duplicate.
  - *Scenario:* A reviewer plants each control and sees the probe stay green. The 'the guard bites' claim then rests on nothing.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0050 files_money_provider_chk (storage_provider='supabase' OR NOT money, 0092 text); the design's network_share_checks grants (SELECT only) and steps (7)/(9).
  - *Fix:* (1) Plant GRANT INSERT plus the policy together, or pin the privilege and the policy count separately. (2) Plant the drop of both CHECKs, or retarget the probe to the new shape CHECK by name (conname in the error). (3) Plant 'step 7 also sets deleted_at = NULL', or assert updated_at and size unchanged on the trashed row.
- **NS-X13 [minor]** The table-suite probe 'updated_at advances on a second check' cannot pass. fn_audit_touch sets updated_at := now(), and now() is fixed for the whole pgTAP transaction.
  - *Scenario:* Two applies inside one BEGIN…ROLLBACK both stamp the same updated_at and last_checked_at, so the probe is red on correct code.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0001_workspaces_and_users.sql fn_audit_touch l.201-217 (now()); suites run as one transaction.
  - *Fix:* Probe what changes instead (entries_seen, missing_count, updated_by after a second caller's apply), or set last_checked_at from clock_timestamp() in the RPC and compare that.
- **NS-X14 [minor]** Both new suites must be in rls.yml's replay 'for f in' list, not only the cross-table one. rlsSuiteLists.test.js requires every .sql file in supabase/tests/rls to appear there, in number order. The tail pins at l.75 and l.111-115 must name both.
  - *Scenario:* Only <n+1> is added by hand. rlsSuiteLists' 'names every suite file' test fails on <n>_network_share_checks.sql.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js l.53-58 and l.75, l.111-115.
  - *Fix:* State that both files join the replay list, and that the two tail pins become […'109_s3_endpoint_public_hosts.sql', '<n>_network_share_checks.sql', '<n+1>_network_share_catalogue.sql'] (slice(-5)/slice(-6) adjusted).
- **NS-X15 [minor]** Step 9 maps folder_id to 'the live folders row', but public.folders has no deleted_at column. A deleted_at filter fails at run time (42703) on the first insert.
  - *Scenario:* The apply is written with `AND f.deleted_at IS NULL` and every call raises 'column f.deleted_at does not exist'. plpgsql does not check this at CREATE time.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0041_folders.sql CREATE TABLE public.folders l.116-204 (no deleted_at; no later ALTER TABLE public.folders adds one).
  - *Fix:* Join folders by (project_id, path) with no deleted filter, using folders_project_path_uniq. Note that folders rows cascade on entity delete.
- **NS-X16 [minor]** The walk route checks the body folder with isPathInside(root), which returns true for the root itself. A request can walk the whole company drive, other projects' private folders included, up to the caps. folderRootRefusal refuses equality, and the route should too.
  - *Scenario:* The renderer, or any script holding the launch cookie, POSTs folder = workspaceRootDir. The worker lists 5,000 names across every project folder and returns them to the renderer. The RPC later refuses no_folder, but the names have already been returned.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/pathContainment.cjs isPathInside l.51-56 (a === b passes); main.cjs folderRootRefusal l.365-372 (equality refused).
  - *Fix:* Use folderRootRefusal's rule (strictly inside: refuse equality), or call folderRootRefusal itself, and test the equality refusal.
- **NS-X17 [minor]** The design claims rootReachable can move 'unchanged' into rootReachable.cjs, but it cannot. rootKnownOffFor and markRootOffFor iterate the bins' cloudLocations closure, and mountCloudBins' test seams (cloudBinsAskRoot, cloudBinsRootWaitMs, cloudBinsRootTtlMs, cloudBinsRootOffTtlMs, cloudBinsRootMaxAsking) configure the instance inside mountCloudBins. One shared instance built in main.cjs conflicts with those seams.
  - *Scenario:* Extracted as written, rabbitBins tests that pass cloudBinsAskRoot to mountCloudBins get main's shared instance instead of their stub, or markRootOffFor loses cloudLocations. Either the bins tests change, which the design says they will not, or the shared cache silently ignores the seams.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/rabbitBins.cjs mountCloudBins l.1704-1819 (seams read from deps; rootKnownOffFor and markRootOffFor loop over cloudLocations.values()).
  - *Fix:* Extract only the question machinery (askRootInWorker, slots, rootAnswers, rootReachable, markOff(root)), injected with ask, wait and TTL values. Keep the location-to-root mapping in rabbitBins. Have mountCloudBins build its own instance when any cloudBins* seam is present, and use the shared one otherwise.
- **NS-X18 [minor]** network_share_folder_of uses folder_slug only. The desktop's resolveProjectFolder falls back to fileSlugify(title) when folder_slug is NULL, so such projects always fail with no_folder and are never catalogued. The design also says a renamed NAS 'keeps every locator', but any admin repoint of root_path to a different depth changes every relative locator: all rows go missing and new ids appear. 0049 does not re-validate stored folder_root values after a repoint.
  - *Scenario:* A project created on the web, so ensureProjectFolders never wrote folder_slug, is opened on the desktop and the walk is refused no_folder with no sentence. Separately, an admin changes the drive from \\nas\share\Projects to \\nas\share, and the next checks churn every id, losing Moth layouts.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: electron/main.cjs resolveProjectFolder l.1848-1860 (folder_slug || fileSlugify(title)); 0049 fn_project_folder_root_guard examines only a change (l.77-84).
  - *Fix:* Either have the client write folder_slug before the first walk (through the existing ensureProjectFolders path) and say so, or give the helper the slug fallback the desktop uses, computed by the client and checked against folders' root row. Reword the repoint claim. Have StorageSection warn with a count of catalogued rows before a root change, as countFilesAtProvider does for s3.
- **NS-X19 [minor]** Some consumers of files bodies are not in the design's list. D.O.G.'s references (useReferences readBody → adapter.downloadFile), InvoiceAttachment and BudgetView all call downloadFile on any row. On the web, a network row throws the desktop sentence from inside D.O.G. On the desktop, D.O.G. pulls the whole NAS body into renderer memory and may send its text to the AI.
  - *Scenario:* A user adds a NAS-listed script as a D.O.G. reference. On the web the reference shows a raw thrown error. On the desktop a 2 GB .mov reference is described, but a large PDF is read whole across the SMB link.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: src/tools/deck-outline-generator_v0.514/references/useReferences.js readBody l.476-488; supabaseAdapter.downloadFile l.3680-3705 (resolveFileProvider(file).get).
  - *Fix:* List D.O.G. references in consumers_to_change. Mark network rows 'on the company server' there with the desktop sentence, and bound reads by size_bytes before get. Pin it with a test.
- **NS-X20 [minor]** Step 6 casts inside jsonb_to_recordset (m timestamptz, s bigint) before the 'normalize' step can clamp anything. One malformed value raises 22007 or 22003 and rolls back the whole apply. Step 6 also recomputes network_share_folder_of for every project for every entry, and that function cannot be inlined because of its SET clause, so the cost is 5,000 × projects calls.
  - *Scenario:* An NTFS file whose mtime the walker emits as an out-of-range ISO string, or a size above the bigint range from a buggy client, makes every check of that project fail with a Postgres error. A workspace with 300 projects runs 1.5M helper calls per check.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: the design's step (6) text; Postgres casts in a recordset column definition raise on a bad value (INFERRED, standard behaviour).
  - *Fix:* Read p, s, m and t as text, then validate them with regex or TRY-style CASE before casting and drop bad entries. Compute the other projects' prefixes once into a CTE and anti-join against it.

## 5. The moth tables

**Area as the designer framed it:** The new Moth tables (moth_views, moth_layout_positions, moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships, workspace_notes, boards, board_elements, sticky_locks, and how moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations fit) and Moth history (moth_history: capture, baseline, 90-day retention, gaps, the two-audience reader)

### Summary

Session 1 creates one table of this family itself: moth_history, the server-captured, append-only record of graph structure and metadata. moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations also land in Session 1, but their mechanics belong to other areas. Every other table is created by the session that first writes it, because its shape depends on that session's protocol: moth_views and moth_layout_positions in Session 2, the four tag tables, moth_relationships and workspace_notes in Session 3, and boards, board_elements and sticky_locks in Session 4. Each later table is specified here so those sessions start from a reviewed shape. moth_history copies no baseline. The baseline is a per-workspace, per-type 'recording since' mark written when the migration is applied. Every structural change after that point stores the full prior and current metadata projection, so the graph at any time T is rebuilt backwards from today's live graph through the changes after T. A purge can therefore delete rows older than 90 days with no compaction, and nothing is ever re-baselined. Bodies, descriptions, notes, storage paths and locators never enter the table. An IMMUTABLE key-allowlist CHECK and a no-projection-on-a-locked-side CHECK enforce this. A money or Legal side is stored only as a flag, and a row whose sides are all locked is never written. Clients cannot touch the table at all: RLS is enabled but not forced (the edit_history and file_events precedent, because the only writers are definers), it has no policies and no grants. History is read only through the SECURITY DEFINER RPCs moth_history_read and moth_history_window. The reader shows each side of a row only when the reader is in that side's original audience (scope class and owner snapshotted at capture, no admin bypass for personal or local) and in its present audience (the side's project passes the Moth scope gate now). The whole entity is hidden when it fails the live gate now: money or Legal now, or a project the reader cannot see now. A side that is hidden turns the row into a plain 'added' or 'removed', so no hidden project id, name or count ever comes back. A DEFINER purge_moth_history runs nightly through a guarded pg_cron job, 'wilson-purge-moth-history' at 04:45. The later tables reuse house patterns: the 0014 live-parent hop, the 0017 owner-only arms with no admin bypass for anything personal, the O.T.T.E.R. tiers with live-admin-only decisions, 0098's revision, cap, pin and column grants and its bounded config, and 0096's generated typed foreign-key columns. The last one means a target's kind is proven by a real foreign key and never taken on the caller's word.

### Decisions

- **D1** Session 1 creates moth_history, and moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations through their own areas. It creates none of the other eleven tables. moth_views and moth_layout_positions go to Session 2. moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships and workspace_notes go to Session 3. boards, board_elements and sticky_locks go to Session 4.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: none of Session 1's six acceptance items reads or writes a view, layout, tag, relationship, workspace note, board or lease (PLAN_MOTH §5, Session 1 checklist). Their shapes depend on protocols that later sessions design: S2's graph model sets view config and dot/edge kinds; S3's normalisation, merge and fold rules set the tags; S4's lease and fencing protocol and the board channel set sticky_locks. House precedent (0041 header on files_dir): a column or table with no reachable writer is schema debt, and it 'arrives with its feature'. moth_history must start in Session 1, because history runs only 'from Moth's first recorded baseline onward', and starting it early gives the most history by release. Session 1's tests also already list 'History baseline/expiry/gaps/no bodies'.
  - *Rejected:* (a) Create all 14 tables in Session 1, as PLAN_MOTH §5 'In scope: All section 4 schemas' reads. This was rejected because it freezes eleven shapes before their protocols exist, and it leaves tables with no writer and later shape-changing migrations on live tables. (b) Defer moth_history to Session 2, its first reader. This was rejected because every day of delay is a day of history lost for good.
- **D2** moth_history keeps no copy of the baseline. The baseline is a mark_recording row per (workspace, entity_type), written at apply time for every existing workspace. A workspace with no mark postdates the migration, so its recording starts at workspaces.created_at. Every captured change stores full prior and state projections, and the state at T is rebuilt backwards from the live graph through the rows after T.
  - *Why:* Copying every existing project, folder, file and entity at apply time costs O(rows) in one migration on production. The baseline would then have to be rolled forward by the purge every night, either by rewriting it at the cutoff or by keeping rows older than 90 days, and either breaks the 90-day retention or costs O(N) writes a night. Backwards rebuilding needs only rows inside the window, so the purge is a plain DELETE WHERE observed_at < now() - 90 days. edit_history already stores full rows on create and delete (0012 diff), and this extends the same idea to updates.
  - *Rejected:* A full snapshot plus forward replay, with nightly compaction into a new baseline at the cutoff. A per-project lazy baseline taken the first time Moth opens a project, which makes the recorded start differ between projects and adds a first-open write path.
- **D3** moth_history is ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY without FORCE, with zero policies, REVOKE ALL from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated (the identity sequence included), and reads only through DEFINER RPCs. This is a deliberate exception, written into the handbook's FORCE-exception list beside edit_history, file_events, storage_gc_queue and auth_attempt_log.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: 0012 and 0027 set the precedent, because a DEFINER capture runs as the owner and FORCE would make its inserts need a policy. The rules digest (rule 17 and its note) names moth_history as the candidate for this exception. Putting the reader in an RPC keeps the two-audience logic in one body. Any helper that a SELECT policy calls must be executable by authenticated, and a 'locked?' helper over arbitrary ids would be an existence oracle; the RPC needs no such helper. Rule 22 says a time window inside a SELECT policy forces a DEFINER prune; the RPC avoids that too. Workspace takeout reads through the admin's RLS, so it does not reach this table, and moth_history stays out of takeout.
  - *Rejected:* FORCE plus an INSERT policy for the owner role, which adds a policy that only exists to admit the trigger. A SELECT policy with a DEFINER locked-classifier granted to authenticated, which is an oracle and spreads the logic across policy text. Joining edit_history, which is R.A.B.B.I.T.-only, admin/manager-read and has no privacy arm (MEASURED S4d-02 gap); Moth must not copy that.
- **D4** Capture is one SECURITY DEFINER AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE trigger function, fn_moth_history_capture, on eleven tables: projects, phases, assets, tasks, files, folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences and task_items. Each UPDATE trigger carries a WHEN clause listing that table's structural columns, so a description, notes or updated_at edit never reaches the function. The projection is done by an IMMUTABLE helper, moth_history_project(table, row). Later sessions add their own tables (S3 tags, assignments, relationships and note metadata; S4 board existence) by restating the latest projector and the entity CHECK. View, layout, board-element and lease churn is never captured.
  - *Why:* These eleven tables are the graph Session 2 draws: hierarchy plus R.A.B.B.I.T. items and their derived task_items edges. MEASURED: scenes, shots, levels, experiences and folders have no history today, and edit_history leaves them out (0012/0061 allowlist), so Moth needs its own capture. The WHEN clauses keep the write cost on hot tables (tasks, files) to changes Moth actually draws. Geometry churn is not graph structure, and capturing it would swamp the 90 days.
  - *Rejected:* Adding the Moth fields to fn_edit_history_capture, which would rewrite a 14-table guard to add tables and mix retention semantics. A diff-only storage like edit_history's updates, which cannot rebuild backwards past an entity's first captured change.
- **D5** Money, Legal and locked-folder content is never projected. At capture, a side is classified locked by the canonical Moth exclusion for files: file_row_is_money (0088) OR a locked folder_id. A folder is locked when rabbit_money_segment applies to the first segment of folders.path. A row is skipped when every side is locked. A row with one locked side keeps only that side's flag and project id, and its projection is NULL (CHECK moth_history_locked_no_projection_chk).
  - *Why:* The ruling excludes money from Moth for every role, leadership included. Rules digest line 174: a second readable surface over money is the defect class. MEASURED: file_events.is_financial is a snapshot, and a file that becomes money later keeps unflagged earlier events (0074 column comment). So the reader also checks the live gate (D6), and the stored flag is only the at-the-time half.
  - *Rejected:* Storing locked projections and filtering them on read, which puts a money surface in a 90-day dump. Classifying only by the snapshot, the file_events limit this design must not copy.
- **D6** The reader, moth_history_read(p_scope_project uuid NULL, p_from, p_to, p_after_id, p_limit ≤ 5000), checks three things. First, the caller: authenticated, current_workspace_id(), has_active_membership, and the scope passes moth_passes_scope or p_scope is NULL for every scope the caller can read; one identical refusal covers 'nonexistent', 'other tenant', 'private' and 'someone else's personal space'. Second, the entity: if its live row exists, it is not Moth-excluded now and its current project passes moth_passes_scope; if not, the last captured side was not locked and its project passes now. Third, each side separately: that side is not locked, its snapshotted audience admits the caller (shared means any member; private means the snapshot owner or current_app_role() = 'admin'; personal and local mean the snapshot owner only), and its project passes moth_passes_scope now. The row's overlay_owner_id must be NULL or auth.uid(). If both sides are visible, the action is mapped to changed, moved, trashed, restored or removed. If only the state side is visible, the row is 'added' with the prior side NULL. If only the prior side is visible, the row is 'removed' with the state side NULL. If neither is visible, the row is omitted. No count of omitted rows is ever returned.
  - *Why:* This implements 'satisfy both the present and the original audience' for every side, so neither a hidden prior location nor a hidden present location is revealed. Mapping to added and removed means a move out of a personal space or a private project reads as an ordinary arrival, with no placeholder that reveals the earlier place existed (ruling: nothing appears that a person cannot already see). The admin arm for private projects matches projects_select (0072). Personal and local scopes have no admin bypass in Moth (ruling 12a.9).
  - *Rejected:* Checking only the present audience, which leaks the private origin of a moved file. Checking only the original audience, which leaks things the reader can no longer see. Returning a redacted 'from somewhere hidden' marker, which reveals that the hidden place exists.
- **D7** Gaps are labelled by marks inside the same table, not by a second table. mark_recording rows hold the start per type. mark_gap rows hold period_from and period_to. A mark_gap is written in three places: by the capture's exception handler (best effort, an instant gap), by any later migration that has to suspend capture, and by the local-scope observe path for the unobserved interval between two desktop observations. moth_history_window(p_scope) returns, per type, recording_since = GREATEST(mark or workspaces.created_at, now() - 90 days), and the gaps whose scope passes.
  - *Why:* Ruling: 'periods with no record labelled'. Keeping one table holds RLS_TABLES at the plan's count and gives the purge one sweep. The capture must never block a user's write, like edit_history's WARNING-and-continue. A silent miss would corrupt the backwards rebuild, so the handler records an instant gap. The post-condition comparing the trigger list with the CHECK list removes the one systematic way the handler itself could fail.
  - *Rejected:* A separate moth_history_marks table, a 16th table with its own suite and no extra safety. Aborting the user's write when capture fails, which would let a Moth bug break every R.A.B.B.I.T. write.
- **D8** Retention: purge_moth_history(p_retention interval DEFAULT '90 days') is SECURITY DEFINER with search_path pinned. It refuses a retention under 1 day. It DELETEs rows with observed_at < now() - p_retention, and mark_gap rows whose period_to falls before the cutoff. EXECUTE is revoked from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated and granted to service_role. It is scheduled as 'wilson-purge-moth-history' at '45 4 * * *' inside the 0012 guarded DO block, so a missing pg_cron never fails CI. It never touches file_events.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: the house's 90-day sweeps (0012 at 04:43, 0021 at 04:51) and the slot table (04:39, 04:43, 04:47, 04:51, 04:55 and 04:59 are taken; 04:45 is free). file_events purged rows are deletion certificates, and rule 34 says they are never purged. The 1-day floor stops a typo from wiping the window.
  - *Rejected:* A retention window inside a policy, which forces a DEFINER prune anyway (rule 22). A GitHub cron, which the rules exclude on feature branches.
- **D9** The Moth tables share one workspace-stamping rule. A table whose parent always carries project_id uses the existing fn_populate_workspace_from_project trigger: boards, board_elements, sticky_locks, and project-scoped views. A table whose scope can be workspace-level (moth_views aggregates, layout positions, tag types, tags, assignments, nominations, relationships, workspace_notes) uses workspace_id DEFAULT current_workspace_id(), the 0017 and 0064 precedent, and leaves workspace_id out of the INSERT column grant. Its parent is checked by a composite foreign key to (id, workspace_id), so a mismatched parent is refused with 23503. Every table also gets fn_audit_touch, BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: fn_populate_workspace_from_project fills only when project_id is present (0004). 0017 and 0064 rely on the DEFAULT and are both pinned by suites. A composite foreign key turns 'same workspace' into a constraint, not a trust. updated_at must exist on every table or realtimeMerge.isStaleIncoming has nothing to compare (moth1_core E13).
  - *Rejected:* A new populate function with arms for each Moth table, which adds code with no gain. A client-sent workspace_id checked by policy alone.
- **D10** Polymorphic targets (layout positions, tag assignments, relationship endpoints) use 0096's task_items shape: target_type text CHECK plus target_id, and STORED GENERATED typed columns (file_id, folder_id, project_id_t, phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, and later tag_id, note_id, board_id). Each typed column has a real foreign key with ON DELETE CASCADE, on id alone. There is no project composite, so a body-preserving file move between projects never breaks it. Workspace and visibility are checked as the caller by the SELECT and WITH CHECK hops.
  - *Why:* Rule: never trust a caller-supplied kind/id pair. With this shape, a file id sent as 'asset' fails its foreign key. Rules digest line 131 prefers typed foreign keys over a bare polymorphic pair. MEASURED: task_items (0096) already does this, and core note (i) warns that same-project composite foreign keys conflict with cross-project file moves. A purge cascades the link away, so the item_groupings-style sweep is not needed.
  - *Rejected:* An untyped (type, id) pair with a validation trigger only, which leaves dangling targets after a purge. A separate link table per target kind, about 13 tables per relation.
- **D11** Write gates. A shared project-scoped write requires moth_can_write_scope(project). That gate is personal_owner_id = auth.uid() for a personal space, and can_write_project(p) AND passes_project_privacy(p) otherwise, so an admin or manager is admitted to a personal space nowhere in Moth. A company-tier decision (create, rename, merge or retire a company tag or tag type, decide a nomination, move a note into or out of company_standard) requires is_live_workspace_admin(workspace_id) (0093), never manager and never the JWT claim. A personal-tier write is owner_id = auth.uid() with no admin arm.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: can_write_project (0013) admits every app admin and manager and every member of an unstaffed project, and knows nothing of privacy (0082 says so). moth1_core E14: project.entity.write cannot express 'owner-only, admins excluded'. Ruling: nomination decisions belong to admins, not O.T.T.E.R.'s managers. Rule 20: anything that acts re-checks a live row.
  - *Rejected:* Reusing can_write_project alone, which admits admins to personal spaces. Using current_app_role() for decisions, which lets a demoted admin act for up to an hour.
- **D12** A tier change that is not allowed RAISEs; it is never silently reverted. This applies to workspace_notes tier transitions, tag tier and owner changes, and nomination stamps.
  - *Why:* MEASURED: 0064's header shows that fn_otter_pin_course_identity's silent NEW.visibility := OLD.visibility made an unauthorised change report success, which forced the GUC workaround. A refusal a person can read is better.
  - *Rejected:* Copying the O.T.T.E.R. silent pin.
- **D13** Deleting and restoring. workspace_notes and moth_relationships with origin 'manual' get deleted_at and deleted_by, and join the soft-delete allowlists in their session. That session restates fn_trash_authz from its latest body (0092 today) and purge_soft_deleted from its latest (0067 today), adds a DEFINER trash-index RPC like milestones_trash_index (0067/0082), and adds partial live indexes. boards carry no deleted_at: a board is trashed and restored through its folder. moth_views, layout positions, tag assignments, inline relationships and board elements are hard-deleted, with the client's re-insert-by-id undo (0014 leaf rule, 0098 views). Tags and tag types use reversible retired_at instead.
  - *Why:* Ruling 12a.5 asks for confirmation, undo and a trash view through the existing soft-delete and restore paths, for things a person 'throws away'. MEASURED 0014: link and leaf rows stay hard-delete because unique keys collide with recreated rows and per-row trash is noise. A board that is a folder needs one trash entry, not two: 'the trash entry is the parent' (0014 §4).
  - *Rejected:* deleted_at on every Moth table, which multiplies trash entries for one action and causes unique-key collisions on undo.

### Schema changes

- **public.moth_history (table, Session 1)** (restates from: new): CREATE TABLE public.moth_history (
  id BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY PRIMARY KEY,
  workspace_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES public.workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  entity_type TEXT NOT NULL,      -- CHECK moth_history_entity_type_chk IN ('projects','phases','assets','tasks','files','folders','scenes','shots','levels','experiences','task_items')
  entity_id UUID NOT NULL,        -- no FK: rows outlive their subject (0012/0027 convention); marks use the workspace id
  action TEXT NOT NULL,           -- CHECK moth_history_action_chk IN ('create','update','move','trash','restore','delete','observe','mark_recording','mark_gap')
  observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),   -- order ties by id
  actor_user_id UUID,             -- no FK, no label (directory resolves; minimises the 90-day dump)
  source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'cloud',             -- CHECK IN ('cloud','local')
  local_scope_id UUID,            -- no FK; required iff source='local'
  prior_project_id UUID, prior_class TEXT, prior_owner_id UUID, prior_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, prior JSONB,
  state_project_id UUID, state_class TEXT, state_owner_id UUID, state_locked BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false, state JSONB,
  overlay_owner_id UUID,          -- private overlay (S3: personal tags, private-note links): owner-only row
  period_from TIMESTAMPTZ, period_to TIMESTAMPTZ    -- mark_gap only
);
CHECKs, named in sort order so a fixture breaks exactly one (rule 29): moth_history_a_class_chk (each *_class NULL or IN ('shared','private','personal','local'); a class of private, personal or local requires its *_owner_id), moth_history_b_shape_chk (create ⇒ prior and prior_project_id NULL; delete ⇒ state and state_project_id NULL; mark_* ⇒ prior, state and both project ids NULL; mark_gap ⇔ period_from and period_to NOT NULL and period_from ≤ period_to), moth_history_c_locked_chk (NOT (prior_locked AND prior IS NOT NULL) AND NOT (state_locked AND state IS NOT NULL)), moth_history_d_projection_chk (prior IS NULL OR moth_history_projection_ok(prior), and the same for state), moth_history_e_source_chk ((source='local') = (local_scope_id IS NOT NULL)), plus moth_history_entity_type_chk and moth_history_action_chk. Indexes: (workspace_id, observed_at, id); (workspace_id, state_project_id, observed_at) and (workspace_id, prior_project_id, observed_at) WHERE their column IS NOT NULL; (entity_type, entity_id, id DESC); (observed_at) for the purge. ALTER TABLE ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY (NOT FORCE: header and post-condition say why). No policies. REVOKE ALL ON TABLE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT ALL TO service_role; REVOKE ALL ON SEQUENCE (pg_get_serial_sequence) FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. COMMENT ON TABLE states: metadata only, 90 days, RPC-read, two audiences.
- **public.moth_history_projection_ok(jsonb) and public.moth_history_project(text, jsonb)** (restates from: new): moth_history_projection_ok(p JSONB) RETURNS BOOLEAN, LANGUAGE sql, IMMUTABLE PARALLEL SAFE, search_path pinned. It is true when p is an object of at most 4096 octets (octet_length(p::text)), every key is in the allowlist {name, title, kind, type, status, document_kind, mime_type, size_bytes, categories, is_core_definer, parent_id, folder_id, phase_id, parent_phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, level_ids, experience_ids, item_type, item_id, position, sort_order}, and every value is a scalar or an array of at most 200 scalars. Never NULL. It is written with jsonb_object_keys inside the function, because a CHECK cannot hold a subquery. moth_history_project(p_table TEXT, p_row JSONB) RETURNS JSONB, IMMUTABLE: a CASE per table picks the allowlisted structural keys, and files.tags is renamed to 'categories'. It never emits storage_path, thumbnail_url, thumbnail_image, description, notes, body_preview, ydoc_state, folder path or folder_root. REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role (pure functions, no table read, no oracle).
- **public.fn_moth_history_capture() and triggers trg_<t>_moth_history on projects, phases, assets, tasks, files, folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, task_items** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public. Steps: (1) v_old := to_jsonb(OLD) and v_new := to_jsonb(NEW), with the unassigned side read as NULL on PG14+. (2) workspace from the row's workspace_id, then RETURN NULL when no workspaces row exists, which covers a teardown cascade. (3) Each side's scope: projects uses the row itself; every other table uses the side's project_id joined to projects for is_private, created_by and personal_owner_id. class = personal when personal_owner_id is set, private when is_private, otherwise shared; owner = personal_owner_id or created_by. (4) Each side's locked flag: files use the canonical Moth exclusion (file_row_is_money(is_financial, storage_path) OR the folder_id's folder locked); folders use rabbit_money_segment(split_part(path,'/',1)); everything else is false. (5) prior := moth_history_project(TG_TABLE_NAME, v_old), and state the same from v_new; a locked side gets a NULL projection. (6) Action: INSERT→create; DELETE→delete; UPDATE with deleted_at going NULL→set → trash, set→NULL → restore; a change of project_id, folder_id, parent_id or parent_phase_id → move; otherwise update. Skip when prior = state (projection unchanged) or when every present side is locked. (7) INSERT the row with actor auth.uid(). (8) EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS: RAISE WARNING, then a nested BEGIN that inserts a mark_gap (period_from = period_to = now(), entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME, entity_id = workspace id) and swallows its own failure; RETURN NULL. The user's write never fails. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated. Triggers: AFTER INSERT OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW; AFTER UPDATE FOR EACH ROW WHEN (structural columns IS DISTINCT FROM), with one per-table list (files: name, project_id, folder_id, kind, mime_type, size_bytes, document_kind, tags, is_core_definer, phase_id, asset_id, task_id, scene_id, shot_id, level_id, experience_id, deleted_at, is_financial, storage_path; folders: project_id, parent_id, slug, label, kind, path; projects: title, status, is_private, personal_owner_id, deleted_at; and so on). storage_path and is_financial are in the WHEN only so a reclassification is caught; they are never projected.
- **mark_recording backfill (data, Session 1 migration)** (restates from: new): INSERT one row per (existing workspace × each of the 11 entity types): action 'mark_recording', entity_id = workspace id, observed_at = now(), source 'cloud', and nothing else. It is idempotent: WHERE NOT EXISTS a mark for the same (workspace, type). A later migration that adds a type writes that type's marks the same way.
- **public.moth_history_read(uuid, timestamptz, timestamptz, bigint, int) and public.moth_history_window(uuid)** (restates from: new): Both are LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public; REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon; GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated, service_role. moth_history_read(p_scope_project UUID, p_from TIMESTAMPTZ, p_to TIMESTAMPTZ, p_after_id BIGINT DEFAULT 0, p_limit INT DEFAULT 1000) RETURNS TABLE(id BIGINT, entity_type TEXT, entity_id UUID, action TEXT, observed_at TIMESTAMPTZ, actor_user_id UUID, prior_project_id UUID, prior JSONB, state_project_id UUID, state JSONB). Checks run first. If auth.uid(), current_workspace_id() or has_active_membership is missing, or p_scope_project is given and NOT moth_passes_scope(p_scope_project), it raises one message, 'that history is not available', the same for every cause. p_limit is clamped to 1..5000. p_from is clamped to the window start. Each row then needs: workspace_id = current_workspace_id(); action NOT LIKE 'mark_%'; overlay_owner_id IS NULL OR = auth.uid(); the entity check (a LEFT JOIN LATERAL to the live row by entity_type: when it exists, NOT Moth-excluded now AND moth_passes_scope(live project); otherwise the entity's newest row's last non-NULL side was not locked AND moth_passes_scope(its project)); and at least one side visible. A side is visible when: NOT side_locked AND side_project IS NOT NULL AND moth_passes_scope(side_project) AND CASE side_class WHEN 'shared' THEN true WHEN 'private' THEN side_owner = auth.uid() OR current_app_role() = 'admin' WHEN 'personal' THEN side_owner = auth.uid() WHEN 'local' THEN side_owner = auth.uid() ELSE false END. Output mapping: both sides visible → changed, moved, trashed, restored or removed (update, move, trash, restore, delete, observe→changed); only state → 'added' with prior and prior_project_id NULL; only prior → 'removed' with state and state_project_id NULL. moth_history_window(p_scope_project UUID) applies the same opening checks and RETURNS TABLE(kind TEXT, entity_type TEXT, recording_since TIMESTAMPTZ, period_from TIMESTAMPTZ, period_to TIMESTAMPTZ): one 'recording' row per type, GREATEST(mark or workspaces.created_at, now() - 90 days), and the 'gap' rows whose prior_project_id is NULL (workspace-wide) or passes moth_passes_scope. A local gap is returned only to the scope's owner.
- **public.purge_moth_history(interval) and cron job 'wilson-purge-moth-history'** (restates from: new): LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public. IF p_retention < INTERVAL '1 day' THEN RAISE. DELETE FROM moth_history WHERE observed_at < now() - p_retention OR (action = 'mark_gap' AND period_to < now() - p_retention); RETURN the row count. REVOKE EXECUTE FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT TO service_role. The 0012 guarded DO block runs cron.schedule('wilson-purge-moth-history', '45 4 * * *', $$SELECT public.purge_moth_history();$$), with NOTICE when pg_cron is absent and WARNING on any error.
- **Session 1 history migration: pre-flight and post-conditions** (restates from: new): Pre-flight refuses to run unless these exist: projects.personal_owner_id (or whatever the personal-space area names it), moth_passes_scope(uuid), the canonical Moth file exclusion, file_row_is_money(boolean, text), rabbit_money_segment(text), is_live_workspace_admin(uuid), has_active_membership(uuid), current_workspace_id(). It also refuses a re-run once a later migration is recorded in supabase_migrations.schema_migrations (0098's guard), and compares the md5 of the capture body (carriage returns stripped) with this file's own constant. Post-conditions run on comment-stripped prosrc: relrowsecurity true and relforcerowsecurity false on moth_history; zero rows in pg_policies for it; has_table_privilege('authenticated' and 'anon', ..., 'SELECT,INSERT,UPDATE,DELETE') all false; the set of tables with a trigger calling fn_moth_history_capture equals, both ways, the set the entity CHECK admits (the 0061 lesson); every new DEFINER function lacks EXECUTE for anon and PUBLIC and has proconfig search_path set; moth_history_projection_ok('{"storage_path":"x"}') is false and moth_history_projection_ok('{"name":"x"}') is true; the capture body contains moth_history_project( and no reference to storage_path inside the projector; the cron job exists when pg_cron does.
- **public.moth_views (Session 2)** (restates from: new): id UUID PK DEFAULT gen_random_uuid(); workspace_id UUID NOT NULL DEFAULT current_workspace_id() REFERENCES workspaces ON DELETE CASCADE; scope_kind TEXT NOT NULL CHECK IN ('project','aggregate'); project_id UUID NULL; owner_id UUID NULL (pinned); name TEXT (0098's table_views_name_chk shape); position INT ≥ 0; revision BIGINT and revised_by (moth_views_revision_seq, pinned as in fn_table_views_pin); config JSONB object ≤ 32768 octets; audit columns. CHECK moth_views_scope_chk: (scope_kind='project') = (project_id IS NOT NULL), and (scope_kind='aggregate') = (owner_id IS NOT NULL). CHECK moth_views_config_refs_chk: (config - 'refs')::text !~* the uuid pattern, so every id lives in config.refs (an array of at most 200 {type, id}). UNIQUE (id, workspace_id); FOREIGN KEY (project_id, workspace_id) REFERENCES projects (id, workspace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE. Triggers, by name: audit (fn_audit_touch) → cap (≤ 200 per project or per owner aggregate; advisory xact lock; 54000) → pin (owner_id := auth.uid() for aggregate and NULL for project; created_* kept; revision) → validate (SECURITY INVOKER: each refs entry visible to the caller under RLS and not Moth-excluded; in a project view, refs must not be personal-tier: no personal tag, no personal note). Policies, four, never FOR ALL. SELECT: workspace AND has_active_membership AND ((scope_kind='project' AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM projects p WHERE p.id = project_id AND (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id = auth.uid()))) OR (scope_kind='aggregate' AND owner_id = auth.uid())). INSERT, UPDATE (WITH CHECK = USING) and DELETE: workspace AND membership AND ((project AND moth_can_write_scope(project_id) AND the same hop) OR (aggregate AND owner_id = auth.uid())). Grants: REVOKE ALL FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated; GRANT SELECT, DELETE; INSERT (id, scope_kind, project_id, name, position, config); UPDATE (name, position, config); USAGE on the sequence; ALL to service_role. Indexes: (project_id, position, created_at, id) WHERE project; (workspace_id, owner_id, position) WHERE aggregate. No history capture, no trash (hard delete with re-insert undo). Realtime: S2 decides; a project view may join fn_realtime_broadcast's project_id arm by restating its latest body; an aggregate view never does.
- **public.moth_layout_positions (Session 2)** (restates from: new): id UUID PK; workspace_id DEFAULT current_workspace_id() FK CASCADE; view_id UUID NOT NULL, with FOREIGN KEY (view_id, workspace_id) REFERENCES moth_views (id, workspace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE; target_type TEXT CHECK IN ('project','folder','file','phase','asset','task','scene','shot','level','experience'), widened by S3 and S4 through DROP + ADD with the full list; target_id UUID NOT NULL; GENERATED STORED typed columns, each with an FK ON DELETE CASCADE (D10); x and y DOUBLE PRECISION NOT NULL CHECK finite AND abs ≤ 1e7; pinned BOOLEAN NOT NULL DEFAULT false; owner_id UUID NULL (pinned to auth.uid() when the view is aggregate or the target is personal-tier); audit columns. UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (view_id, target_type, target_id, owner_id). Cap: 20000 per view. SELECT: workspace AND membership AND EXISTS (the view, under caller RLS) AND the typed target hop under caller RLS with the Moth exclusion (for a file: EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM files f WHERE f.id = file_id AND NOT <canonical exclusion>); for a folder: NOT rabbit_money_segment(split_part(path,'/',1))) AND (owner_id IS NULL OR owner_id = auth.uid()). Writes add: owner_id = auth.uid(), or the view is a project view with moth_can_write_scope(view.project_id). A reviewer cannot persist rearrangement. Grants: SELECT, DELETE; INSERT (id, view_id, target_type, target_id, x, y, pinned); UPDATE (x, y, pinned). An optional SECURITY INVOKER bulk RPC, moth_save_positions(p_view, p_positions jsonb ≤ 1000), is the S2 writer. Not history-captured.
- **public.moth_tag_types and public.moth_tags (Session 3)** (restates from: new): moth_tag_types: id; workspace_id DEFAULT current_workspace_id() FK CASCADE; tier CHECK IN ('company','personal'); owner_id (CHECK (tier='personal') = (owner_id IS NOT NULL); pinned to auth.uid()); name TEXT 1..60, trimmed, no control characters; name_norm TEXT GENERATED STORED (lower(normalize(btrim(regexp_replace(name,'\s+',' ','g')),NFKC))); shape TEXT CHECK in S3's list; appearance JSONB object ≤ 2048 octets; retired_at and retired_by; audit; UNIQUE (id, workspace_id). Partial uniques: (workspace_id, name_norm) WHERE company AND retired_at IS NULL; (workspace_id, owner_id, name_norm) WHERE personal AND retired_at IS NULL. moth_tags: id; workspace_id; type_id with FOREIGN KEY (type_id, workspace_id) REFERENCES moth_tag_types (id, workspace_id); tier and owner_id as above (validate trigger: a company tag needs a company type; a personal tag needs a company type or its owner's own personal type); label TEXT 1..80; label_norm GENERATED; canonical_id UUID NULL REFERENCES moth_tags(id) ON DELETE SET NULL (a merged tag is retired and points at its canonical tag); retired_at; revision; audit. Live partial uniques by (workspace_id, type_id, label_norm), and by owner for personal. Policies on both tables. SELECT: workspace AND membership AND (tier='company' OR owner_id = auth.uid()); no admin bypass for personal. INSERT and UPDATE: (company AND is_live_workspace_admin(workspace_id)) OR (personal AND owner_id = auth.uid()); tier and owner_id immutable (trigger RAISEs, D12). DELETE: personal by owner only; a company tag or type is retired, never deleted (no DELETE arm for company). Grants by column: no workspace_id, owner_id or tier on UPDATE. Merge, rename-everywhere and fold run in DEFINER RPCs (S3): live admin, a transaction lock, exact normalised and type-compatible matches only. A folded personal assignment keeps its owner_id overlay and is never published. Each RPC is REVOKEd from PUBLIC and anon. moth_tags joins moth_history in S3 (company tier class 'shared'; personal tier carries overlay_owner_id). moth_tag_types is not captured.
- **public.moth_tag_assignments (Session 3)** (restates from: new): id; workspace_id DEFAULT current FK CASCADE; tag_id with FOREIGN KEY (tag_id, workspace_id) REFERENCES moth_tags (id, workspace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE; target_type and target_id with GENERATED typed foreign keys ON DELETE CASCADE (D10; targets: project, folder, file, phase, asset, task, scene, shot, level, experience, workspace_note, and S4 board); owner_id UUID NULL (forced to auth.uid() when the tag is personal; optionally a personal annotation of a company tag); assigned_by pinned to auth.uid(); audit. UNIQUE NULLS NOT DISTINCT (tag_id, target_type, target_id, owner_id). SELECT: workspace AND membership AND EXISTS (the tag, under caller RLS) AND the typed target hop with the Moth exclusion and the personal-space arm AND (owner_id IS NULL OR owner_id = auth.uid()). INSERT and DELETE: the same, plus (owner_id = auth.uid()) OR (owner_id IS NULL AND moth_can_write_target(target), the company assignment). No UPDATE grant (made and removed whole). This table is never folded into files.tags; the nine 0085 categories stay separate. History: S3 adds it to capture, with overlay_owner_id = owner_id.
- **public.moth_tag_nominations (Session 3)** (restates from: new): Mirrors otter_course_nominations (0064): id; workspace_id DEFAULT current FK CASCADE; tag_id UUID NULL REFERENCES moth_tags ON DELETE SET NULL (the nominator's personal tag); proposed_label, proposed_type_name, proposed_shape (the consented snapshot, so an admin decides without reading the private tag or any of its assignment targets); proposed_by DEFAULT auth.uid(); summary (0064 summary CHECK); status ('open','changes_requested','approved','rejected','withdrawn'); reviewed_by and reviewed_at; review_note (≤ 4000, required for changes_requested); revision ≥ 1; applied_at and applied_by (CHECK (status='approved') = (applied_at IS NOT NULL)); result_tag_id REFERENCES moth_tags ON DELETE SET NULL; audit. One live nomination per tag_id: partial unique WHERE status IN ('open','changes_requested'). SELECT: workspace AND membership AND (proposed_by = auth.uid() OR is_live_workspace_admin(workspace_id)); not manager. INSERT: proposed_by = auth.uid(), status open, revision 1, every review and apply column NULL, the tag owned by the caller and of the personal tier.
