UPDATE: USING proposer OR live admin; WITH CHECK live admin OR (proposer AND status IN ('open','withdrawn','rejected')). Transitions are enforced by a 0064-style review trigger with approver = is_live_workspace_admin. Approval happens only through a DEFINER moth_tag_nomination_apply, which writes a 0069-style self-approval app_events line when proposer = approver. No DELETE grant. Not history-captured.
- **public.moth_relationships (Session 3)** (restates from: new): id; workspace_id DEFAULT current FK CASCADE; source_type and source_id, dest_type and dest_id, each with GENERATED typed foreign keys ON DELETE CASCADE (D10); CHECK source ≠ dest; label TEXT NULL ≤ 120, with label_norm GENERATED; origin CHECK IN ('manual','inline'); host_field TEXT NULL and occurrence_key TEXT NULL ≤ 64 (both required iff origin='inline'); owner_id UUID NULL (forced to the note owner when the source is a personal note; personal overlays are owner-only); deleted_at and deleted_by (manual only; an inline row is hard-deleted by its writer when the mention goes); revision; audit. Uniques: manual (source, dest, label_norm) WHERE deleted_at IS NULL; inline (source_type, source_id, host_field, occurrence_key). SELECT: deleted_at IS NULL AND workspace AND membership AND both endpoint hops under caller RLS with the Moth exclusion AND (owner_id IS NULL OR owner_id = auth.uid()); a backlink therefore needs a visible source. INSERT: the source writable (moth_can_write_target) AND the destination eligible. UPDATE: USING the source writable; WITH CHECK the source writable AND the destination eligible, so a changed destination is checked again. DELETE of a manual row goes through soft_delete_row, after S3 restates fn_trash_authz from its then-latest body with a moth_relationships arm resolving the workspace and the source's project; S3 also restates purge_soft_deleted. History: S3 captures it.
- **public.workspace_notes (Session 3)** (restates from: new): id; workspace_id DEFAULT current FK CASCADE; owner_id DEFAULT auth.uid() (pinned); tier CHECK IN ('personal','shared','company_standard') DEFAULT 'personal'; title ≤ 200; ydoc_state TEXT (base64 Yjs, with a CHECK bound S3 measures); body_preview ≤ 500; version BIGINT (0017's snapshot-merge-write: UPDATE … WHERE version = v); deleted_at and deleted_by; audit. SELECT: deleted_at IS NULL AND workspace AND membership AND (owner_id = auth.uid() OR tier IN ('shared','company_standard')); personal is owner-only with no admin bypass. INSERT: owner_id = auth.uid() AND tier IN ('personal','shared'). UPDATE: the content columns by the owner, or by a live admin for company_standard. A tier trigger RAISEs unless the move is personal↔shared by the owner, or into or out of company_standard by is_live_workspace_admin. Soft delete through the trash RPCs (allowlist restated in S3). Never on any realtime topic, never in edit_history. Takeout excludes the personal tier, like Notes. S3 captures its metadata (title and tier only, never the body) in moth_history, with class 'shared' for shared and company_standard, and overlay_owner for personal. Kept distinct from public.notes (0017), which is unchanged.
- **public.boards and public.board_elements (Session 4)** (restates from: new): boards: id; folder_id UUID NOT NULL UNIQUE; project_id UUID NOT NULL, with FOREIGN KEY (folder_id, project_id) REFERENCES folders (id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE (S4 adds folders UNIQUE (id, project_id)); workspace_id by the populate trigger; colour_key TEXT CHECK in the fixed semantic token list; channel_generation BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 1 (bumped on an audience change, S4); revision; audit. No deleted_at: trash goes through the folder. SELECT: workspace AND membership AND EXISTS (folders under caller RLS) AND the projects hop with the personal arm. Writes: moth_can_write_scope(project_id) plus the hops. board_elements: id; board_id with FOREIGN KEY (board_id, project_id) REFERENCES boards(id, project_id) ON DELETE CASCADE; project_id and workspace_id (populated); kind CHECK (S4's final shape, connector, frame, pen, text, sticky, media and embed list); x, y, w, h, rotation finite and bounded; z BIGINT; style JSONB ≤ 8 KB; text ≤ 4000 (shape and text-box text only; a sticky's body is its markdown file); pen_points JSONB bounded; file_id UUID NULL REFERENCES files ON DELETE SET NULL (NULL draws the stop-sign tile with no filename); from_element_id and to_element_id with FOREIGN KEY (x, board_id) REFERENCES board_elements (id, board_id) ON DELETE SET NULL (same-board connectors) plus anchors; version BIGINT; audit. A validate trigger, running as the caller, refuses a file_id that is invisible or Moth-excluded ('Embeds must refuse money'). Hard delete; layout deletion never cascades to a files row. Not history-captured.
- **public.sticky_locks (Session 4, shape deferred)** (restates from: new): Provisional only: element_id PK REFERENCES board_elements ON DELETE CASCADE, board_id, workspace_id, holder_user_id, holder_session, a generation BIGINT fencing token, and expires_at set by the database clock. No client write grants; claim, renew and release run in DEFINER RPCs that SELECT … FOR UPDATE. The final columns, the TTL and where fencing is checked depend on S4's live protocol and on the file-body publication path (file_body_locations), so S4 designs it.
- **Fit of moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations (other areas, Session 1)** (restates from: new): moth_local_scopes: moth_history rows with source 'local' carry local_scope_id (no FK) and class 'local' with owner = the scope owner, read only by that owner. That area's observe RPC writes diff rows, each projection checked by moth_history_projection_ok, plus one mark_gap over (last_observed_at, now()); its first observation writes mark_recording. Workspace CASCADE. file_body_locations: never projected into moth_history (locator, provider and thumbnail keys are outside the allowlist, and the CHECK refuses them). Its existence must keep file_row_is_money true for moved bodies, or the capture's locked classification and the reader's live gate must move to the area's canonical helper in the same migration.
- **Dependencies from the eligibility and personal-space areas** (restates from: new): Before the history migration, these must exist. (1) projects.personal_owner_id UUID NULL (name owned by the personal-space area), unique per (workspace_id) where it is not null. (2) moth_passes_scope(p_project UUID) RETURNS BOOLEAN, sql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER, search_path = public: EXISTS (projects p WHERE id = p_project AND deleted_at IS NULL AND workspace_id = current_workspace_id() AND has_active_membership(p.workspace_id) AND (NOT is_private OR created_by = auth.uid() OR current_app_role() = 'admin') AND (personal_owner_id IS NULL OR personal_owner_id = auth.uid())). REVOKE from PUBLIC, anon and authenticated (definer callers only, like passes_project_privacy 0082). (3) moth_can_write_scope(p_project UUID), DEFINER, granted to authenticated: CASE WHEN personal_owner_id IS NOT NULL THEN personal_owner_id = auth.uid() AND has_active_membership ELSE can_write_project(p) AND passes_project_privacy(p) END; FALSE for an unknown id. (4) The canonical Moth file exclusion, file_row_is_money(is_financial, storage_path) OR a locked folder_id. If those areas name them differently, substitute; the history migration's pre-flight names whatever they are called.

That takes the count from 61 to 62 (MEASURED 61, counted in the array), plus whatever moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations add. Each later session adds its own tables the same way.
- **.github/workflows/rls.yml: the failure-replay 'for f in …' list**: Append NNN_moth_history.sql, keeping number order.
- **WILSON/src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/storage/rlsSuiteLists.test.js: the replay-tail pin (listed.slice(-5), the 'runs in number order' test) and the picked-suites pin (picked.slice(-6), the 'over the real directory' test)**: Move both tails to end with the new suite(s), and update the dated comment lines above each pin.
- **WILSON/supabase/migrations/NNNN_moth_history.sql (new, Session 1; number taken at the tip after the personal-space and eligibility migrations)**: Everything in schema_changes for moth_history: table, checker and projector, capture and the 11 triggers, marks backfill, reader and window RPCs, purge and cron, pre-flight and post-conditions. It holds no ALTER TYPE, so it can run as one transaction.
- **WILSON/supabase/tests/rls/NNN_moth_history.sql (new)**: The suite in tests[0].
- **WILSON/docs/SYSTEMS_HANDBOOK.md: the §4.1 FORCE-exception list, the §4.8 retention and cron list, the §13.3 'Edit history' paragraph, and the §14 'Arrows that deliberately do not exist'**: Add moth_history to the not-FORCE list with its reason. Add 'wilson-purge-moth-history' at 04:45 with 90 days. Describe moth_history beside edit_history: the different audience, the no-privacy-arm contrast, and RPC-only reads. Record that moth_history is on no realtime topic and in no takeout. All in the same commit as the migration.
- **WILSON/src/components/AdminTerminal/WorkspaceTakeout.jsx header comment (TAKEOUT_TABLES itself unchanged)**: Optional, one comment line: moth_history is excluded (derived, RPC-only, would carry personal-space metadata), as otter_* and notes already are. No code change, because admin RLS cannot read the table anyway.
- **Later sessions' restatements of guard bodies (named so their authors restate from the latest)**: S3 restates fn_trash_authz from 0092 and purge_soft_deleted from 0067, or from whatever later migration holds them by then. S3 and S4 restate moth_history's entity CHECK (DROP + ADD with the full list), moth_history_project and fn_moth_history_capture from Session 1's history migration, with the trigger-list-equals-CHECK post-condition run again. S2 and S4 restate fn_realtime_broadcast from its latest body (0101 or later; the md5 guard pattern of 0098) if views or boards broadcast.

### Client changes

- **Session 1: none for this area**: Session 1 ships moth_history's database side only: capture, purge, reader RPCs and pgTAP. No client export is added, because none would have a caller before Session 2's history scrub (plan §5 Session 1 step 9: 'No feature-specific client exports before consuming UI'). *(production caller: n/a (no export))*
- **Session 2: src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js (fetchMothHistory, fetchMothHistoryWindow), the dev fixtures adapter (src/dev/fixtures, an in-memory twin), and the Local Server adapter (returns the local-scope rows through the cloud RPC only when signed in, otherwise empty with a 'not recorded' window)**: Thin wrappers over moth_history_read and moth_history_window. They use the absent-function probe pattern (42883/PGRST202 → the history-unavailable state, never a failed load; rules digest §17 bins precedent). The client rebuild rule: start from the live authorised graph; walk the rows after T in reverse; 'added' means absent before; 'removed' means present before with the prior projection; an entity is visible at T only if its ancestors are. *(production caller: Session 2's HUD history scrub (Moth view widget, layout/history control))*
- **Session 2: moth_views and moth_layout_positions adapter functions (listMothViews, saveMothView, saveMothPositions) and realtimeMerge TABLE_TO_COLLECTION entries if they broadcast**: Created with their tables in Session 2. Each broadcast table gets its realtimeMerge entry and its trigger arm together, and goes into LEAF_COLLECTIONS only after it is measured to have no FK children. *(production caller: Session 2's view widget (save, pick, reset) and graph drag-settle)*
- **Session 3 and Session 4 adapters for tags, assignments, nominations, relationships, workspace notes, boards, elements and leases**: Created with their tables, each with its consuming UI in the same session. *(production caller: S3 tag, notes and link UI; S4 board window)*

### Migration order

- **S1-a (other areas, first)**: Personal-space classification column on projects, the Moth eligibility helpers (moth_passes_scope, moth_can_write_scope, the canonical Moth file exclusion), folder soft delete if that area adds it, moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations, each with its own suite and RLS_TABLES line.
  - *Constraints:* Every guard body is restated from its latest migration (fn_trash_authz from 0092, purge_soft_deleted from 0067, projects_select from 0072). moth_history's pre-flight depends on these, so they commit first.
- **S1-b NNNN_moth_history.sql + NNN_moth_history.sql + rls.yml + rlsSuiteLists.test.js (one commit)**: Pre-flight; table, CHECKs and indexes; checker and projector; RLS enabled, not forced, with revokes; capture function and the 11 triggers; mark_recording backfill; reader and window RPCs; purge and guarded cron; post-conditions.
  - *Constraints:* No enum ADD VALUE, so one transaction is fine. Rehearse rolled back on wilson-dev with tap-hosted.py (migration plus suite, collected = planned). Apply file by file; write the schema_migrations row with the md5; run the whole pgTAP set before pushing (35_platform_audit, 33_file_lifecycle, 78_file_events_money, 17 edit_history and 52_folders must stay green because triggers were added to their tables). CREATE TRIGGER takes a brief ShareRowExclusiveLock on projects, files, tasks and the rest: apply off-hours on staging and production (the controller's call, on her go).
- **S2 (Session 2's first migration)**: moth_views and moth_layout_positions, with their suites and RLS_TABLES lines, plus user_settings Moth keys (another area).
  - *Constraints:* Remeasure numbers at the tip. If views broadcast, restate fn_realtime_broadcast from its latest body with an md5 guard.
- **S3**: moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships, workspace_notes. Restated: fn_trash_authz, purge_soft_deleted, a trash-index RPC, and the moth_history entity CHECK, projector and capture (adding tags, assignments, relationships and note metadata, with mark_recording rows for every workspace).
  - *Constraints:* One suite per table, each in the same commit as its RLS_TABLES line. Post-conditions: trigger list equals CHECK list; the allowlist still refuses body keys.
- **S4**: boards (with folders UNIQUE (id, project_id)), board_elements, sticky_locks, the board channel's realtime.messages policies, and board existence added to moth_history capture.
  - *Constraints:* The channel policy suite is a no-table suite, so add it to the replay list by hand. Realtime is absent in CI (rls.yml excludes it), so channel probes run on the hosted dev environment.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/NNN_moth_history.sql (Session 1)**: STRUCTURE: RLS on and FORCE off (deliberate); zero policies; has_table_privilege false for anon and authenticated on SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE; no USAGE on the sequence; every new DEFINER function is not anon-executable and has search_path set. CAPTURE, positive: as a member, inserting a file in a shared project writes exactly one 'create' row whose state keys are a subset of the allowlist and contain no storage_path; a rename writes 'update'; a folder_id change writes 'move'; soft_delete_row writes 'trash' and restore_soft_deleted writes 'restore'; a hard delete of a scene writes 'delete' with its prior side; a description-only or notes-only update writes nothing (the WHEN filter). CAPTURE, money: inserting an INVOICES-path file writes no row; an is_financial file writes no row; a move of an ordinary file into INVOICES writes one row with state_locked true and state NULL. CHECKs: inserting as postgres with state '{"storage_path":"x"}' throws moth_history_d_projection_chk exactly, and each CHECK has one fixture that breaks only it. READER (moth_history_read): tenant, a user of workspace B naming A's project, gets the single refusal message; an inactive member gets the same refusal; a member reads a shared project; a reviewer (project role) reads it too; on a private project a member who is not the creator is refused, while the creator and an admin are allowed; a manager is refused a private project's history (the contrast with edit_history S4d-02); a personal space is readable by its owner and refused to an admin; an admin's aggregate read (p_scope NULL) contains no row of another person's personal space. ORIGINAL AUDIENCE: a file moved from creator X's private project into a shared project comes back to member M as one 'added' row with prior and prior_project_id NULL. PRESENT AUDIENCE: a file moved from a shared project into a private one returns no row at all to M, the earlier create included. LIVE GATE: a file created ordinary and later flagged is_financial returns none of its earlier rows, even to an admin. WINDOW: moth_history_window gives recording_since = GREATEST(mark, now() - 90 days); a workspace with no mark gives its created_at; a gap mark in the scope is listed; a gap in another person's personal space is not listed. PURGE: rows at now() - 91 days are deleted and rows at now() - 89 days are kept; a retention under 1 day raises; authenticated EXECUTE on purge throws 42501. ROBUSTNESS: deleting a workspace that has projects and files succeeds and leaves no moth_history rows (CASCADE, capture skipped); with the entity CHECK narrowed inside the transaction (folders dropped), a folder insert still succeeds and a mark_gap row exists. PLANTED CONTROLS that must turn their named probe red: (1) remove the personal arm from moth_passes_scope → 'admin refused another person's personal space'; (2) drop the prior-side audience test → the 'added with prior NULL' probe; (3) drop the entity present check → the moved-into-private probe; (4) drop the live money gate → the later-flagged probe; (5) delete a key from the allowlist check, so that storage_path is accepted → the CHECK probe; (6) remove the WHEN clause on files → the description-only probe; (7) remove the workspace-exists skip → the teardown probe; (8) remove the 'manager' refusal by adding a manager arm to the private class → the manager probe; (9) change the purge comparison to <= 89 days → the 89-day keep probe.
- **supabase/tests/rls/35_platform_audit.sql (existing, unchanged)**: Must stay green: its count of anon-executable SECURITY DEFINER functions in public is the tripwire for any REVOKE this design forgets. Planted: grant anon EXECUTE on moth_history_read → this suite goes red.
- **Existing suites on the captured tables: 01_projects, 02_phases, 03_assets, 04_tasks, 05_files, 17 edit_history, 33_file_lifecycle, 48 to 51 (scenes, shots, levels, experiences), 52_folders, 78_file_events_money, 82_private_project_definers, 99_task_items**: They run unchanged and stay green; the new AFTER triggers must not change any write outcome. A full tap-all run with collected = planned for every suite.
- **Session 2: NNN_moth_views.sql and NNN_moth_layout_positions.sql**: Tenant (a workspace B user sees and writes nothing); inactive member refused; project view: a member with a seat saves, a reviewer reads but cannot insert, update or delete, an admin saves in a shared project; a private project's view is visible to creator and admin only; personal-space views and positions are owner-only, and an admin is refused both read and write; aggregate views are owner-only with no admin bypass; the config refs CHECK refuses a uuid outside refs; validate refuses a personal tag in a project view's refs; the cap fires 54000 at 201; an upsert is refused (column grants); a position's target hop hides a money file from an admin; a position owner overlay is invisible to others; a purged target cascades away; a trashed target's position is hidden. Planted: drop the personal arm → the admin-refused probe goes red; replace moth_can_write_scope with can_write_project → the admin write into a personal space succeeds and its probe goes red; drop the money predicate in the hop → the admin money probe goes red.
- **Session 3: suites for moth_tag_types, moth_tags, moth_tag_assignments, moth_tag_nominations, moth_relationships, workspace_notes**: A personal tier is invisible to another member and to an admin, through every table and through moth_history_read. A company tier is readable by every active member and written only by a live admin: a manager is refused, and an admin demoted in the same transaction is refused (is_live_workspace_admin). A tier or owner change RAISEs. Typed foreign keys: a file id sent as 'asset' fails 23503. A company assignment needs target write (a reviewer is refused); a personal assignment needs only visibility. Nominations: a manager cannot decide; an admin can; self-approval writes app_events; the proposer cannot forge review fields; one live nomination per tag. Relationships: both endpoints must be visible; a destination change is checked again; a backlink to a hidden source is invisible; an inline row is unique per occurrence. workspace_notes: personal is owner-only with no admin bypass; company_standard transitions are live-admin only; the version guard holds; soft delete and restore go through the RPCs only. Planted: drop the overlay arm, add a manager arm to the decide gate, drop the destination recheck, drop the tier trigger: each turns its named probe red.
- **Session 4: suites for boards, board_elements and sticky_locks, plus a board-channel suite on the replay list**: A board in a personal space is owner-only; a board in a trashed folder is hidden; a cross-board connector fails its foreign key; an element referencing a money or invisible file is refused; deleting an element leaves its files row intact; the lease claim, renewal, expiry and stale-generation refusal hold. Planted: drop the same-board composite foreign key, drop the file validation, remove the fencing check.

### Risks

- A missed structural write (a new writer that bypasses the triggers, a WHEN list missing a column, a COPY or a capture failure) silently corrupts the backwards rebuild for the period before it.
  - *Mitigation:* Triggers fire for every role including service_role. The WHEN lists are pinned by a post-condition and by the description-only and rename probes. The capture handler writes an instant mark_gap. The trigger-list-equals-CHECK post-condition. S2's rebuild degrades safely: an entity with no rows simply shows its current state. Any later migration that suspends capture must write a mark_gap.
- Extra write cost on hot tables (tasks, files) and bursts on cascades: a purged project with thousands of files writes thousands of 'delete' rows.
  - *Mitigation:* WHEN-filtered UPDATE triggers, so only structural changes enter. One row per change. Three indexes beyond the PK. The volume matches what edit_history already absorbs on the same cascades. S1 records the row counts on wilson-dev, and S2 measures the reader's latency on the debug sample and the saved local project.
- Workspace teardown: capture inserting during the CASCADE would hit the workspace FK and abort, or flood warnings.
  - *Mitigation:* An explicit 'workspace row exists' skip before the insert, plus the exception handler. The teardown probe, and 35_platform_audit's workspace-delete probe, stay green.
- Replay traps: re-running the S1 history migration after S3 or S4 have restated the capture, projector or CHECK silently takes their tables out of capture.
  - *Mitigation:* The 0098-style pre-flight refuses a re-run once a later migration is recorded, plus an md5 constant for the capture body. Each restating migration carries the same guard and re-asserts the both-ways post-condition.
- Money reclassification paths change: file_body_locations may change what storage_path means, so file_row_is_money stops reflecting the body's folder.
  - *Mitigation:* Capture and reader call one canonical Moth exclusion helper, not split_part directly. The body-identity area must keep that helper true for moved bodies; the live-gate probe and the move-into-INVOICES probe catch a regression.
- The personal-space column and the eligibility helper names are owned by other areas and may land under different names.
  - *Mitigation:* The history migration's pre-flight names its exact dependencies and refuses to run without them. This design states the required semantics so a rename is a substitution, not a redesign.
- Admin JWT lag on reads: the private-class arm uses current_app_role() = 'admin', so a demoted admin keeps reading private-project history for up to an hour.
  - *Mitigation:* MEASURED to be the same rule projects_select applies (0072); rule 20 allows claims for reads. Personal and local arms use auth.uid() only. Every decision that acts uses is_live_workspace_admin.
- An aggregate read (p_scope NULL) on a large workspace is slow, because of per-row lateral live lookups.
  - *Mitigation:* p_limit is capped at 5000 with p_after_id paging; workspace and observed_at indexes; moth_passes_scope is cached per project inside the function (a temporary map of project ids already checked). S2 measures it.
- Deferring eleven tables pushes their suites and RLS_TABLES lines onto S2 to S4, and a session could start writing UI before its table's suite lands.
  - *Mitigation:* Each later session's brief keeps 'suite and RLS_TABLES line before anything depends on it' as step one of its database work. The shapes in this design are the starting point for each of those sessions' own design rounds.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- *Plan:* Session 1 In scope: 'All section 4 schemas/security/suites'; Ordered build 3: 'Schemas/backfills/identity constraints/live membership/RLS/grants/suites/allowlist together.' §4 New tables lists 14 tables with suites 110–123.
  - *Design:* Session 1 creates only moth_history, plus moth_local_scopes and file_body_locations through their areas. moth_views and moth_layout_positions move to Session 2; the four tag tables, moth_relationships and workspace_notes to Session 3; boards, board_elements and sticky_locks to Session 4. Reason: their shapes depend on those sessions' protocols, and none has a writer in Session 1 (0041 'arrives with its feature'). Sessions, order and dependencies are unchanged, and S3's and S4's Database lines already name these suites. S2's line 'S1 view/layout/preferences/history' becomes 'S2 creates view/layout; S1 provides history'.
- *Plan:* §4: provisional suite numbers 110_moth_views … 123_file_body_locations; 'these 14 additions would take RLS_TABLES from 61 to 75'.
  - *Design:* Numbers are taken at creation in each session's order, so moth_history is not 122. RLS_TABLES is MEASURED at 61 today; it reaches 62 to 64 after Session 1 and 75 only if every table survives its session's design.
- *Plan:* §4 moth_history: 'First baseline/subsequent structural metadata; target/scope; observation time; prior/current state; audience/retention.'
  - *Design:* There is no copy of the first baseline. The baseline is a per-workspace, per-type recording mark, and the past is rebuilt backwards from the live graph through rows that hold full prior and current projections. The 90-day purge is then a plain delete with no compaction. Gaps are mark rows in the same table.
- *Plan:* House rule and §4 Shared rules: every new table ENABLE + FORCE RLS, with policies.
  - *Design:* moth_history is ENABLE without FORCE, has zero policies and zero client grants, and is read only through DEFINER RPCs. This follows the edit_history and file_events precedent (0012/0027), and the rules digest names moth_history as the candidate for it. It is recorded in the handbook's exception list.
- *Plan:* §4 boards: 'Real folder; project/local scope; title/fixed semantic colour; revision/channel generation; audit/trash.'
  - *Design:* boards carries no deleted_at and no title. A board is trashed, restored and named through its folder (one trash entry: 0014 'the trash entry is the parent'). This depends on folder soft delete, which belongs to Session 1's folders area.
- *Plan:* §4 moth_views: 'revision/trash'.
  - *Design:* Views are hard-deleted with the client's re-insert undo, the 0098 table_views precedent, with no trash. Revision is kept.
- *Plan:* E14: 'project.entity.write matches Moth editing'.
  - *Design:* Moth writes use moth_can_write_scope. can_write_project alone admits every admin and manager to a personal space and knows nothing of privacy (MEASURED 0013 and 0082; moth1_core E14).
- *Plan:* §4 Existing changes, Trash/history: 'Extend explicit allowlists/shared undo/captures'.
  - *Design:* No Moth table joins edit_history (0012/0061). Structural Moth tables join moth_history in their own sessions; views, positions, board elements and leases join neither. edit_history_select's missing privacy arm (MEASURED, OUTSTANDING S4d-02) is not copied: moth_history's reader has the privacy, personal and money arms.
- *Plan:* §4 moth_layout_positions: 'View/target identity; coordinates; fixed position; revision; owner overlay where private.'
  - *Design:* There is no revision column: updated_at by fn_audit_touch serves realtimeMerge's stale guard. A position's owner overlay is forced for aggregate views and personal-tier targets.

### Review — Security: what the Moth tables and moth_history could leak (another company, private projects, personal spaces, money/Legal, personal tags and notes, including ids, counts and existence), escalation, forged authority, and existing gates weakened

**Verdict:** Buildable once the fixes are in, not as written. Fix before Session 1 builds moth_history: SEC-03 (money history comes back after a purge), SEC-05 (gaps can't be scoped) and SEC-06 (forged local rows). SEC-04 and SEC-07 are dependencies the history migration's pre-flight must name. The core structure holds: no client grants, a definer-only reader, a check on each side, and a live check on money and privacy. The Session 2 to 4 table shapes have two leaks that must be fixed before those sessions start. SEC-01: missing personal-space checks on layout positions and relationships. SEC-02: view refs and board file references show ids from other audiences. They also need SEC-08 (boards inside money folders) and SEC-09 (the history audience model cannot carry two-endpoint or workspace-level entities). SEC-10 to SEC-13 are minor hardening.

- **SEC-01 [blocker]** The specified SELECT hops for moth_layout_positions and moth_relationships check targets only 'under caller RLS with the Moth exclusion'. They have no personal-space arm. Caller RLS lets every workspace admin into every private project, and the ruling keeps admin reach into personal spaces from Files. So these two tables would show an admin another person's personal-space items in Moth. moth_views, boards and moth_tag_assignments do carry the personal arm, which makes the gap specific to these two tables.
  - *Scenario:* Owner U drags a dot for a file in U's personal space onto a project view of shared project P. This happens either when owner_id stays NULL, because the 'personal-tier' pin covers tags and notes but not personal-space targets, or later when U moves the file into the personal space. U also links that file to a file in P with a manual relationship. Admin A opens P in Moth. files_select passes for A (its EXISTS-projects hop runs under projects_select, which admits current_app_role()='admin'). The file is not money. A therefore reads the position row and the relationship row, and Moth draws an edge and a dot into U's personal space. The ruling says that space is never reachable in Moth.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0072_private_projects.sql projects_select (arm `OR public.current_app_role() = 'admin'`). 0092 files_select (`EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.projects p WHERE p.id = files.project_id)` plus the money arm only). Design schema_changes: moth_layout_positions SELECT ('the typed target hop under caller RLS with the Moth exclusion (for a file: … NOT <canonical exclusion>; for a folder: …)'), moth_relationships SELECT ('both endpoint hops under caller RLS with the Moth exclusion'). The 'canonical Moth file exclusion' is defined as file_row_is_money OR a locked folder, i.e. money only. moth_passes_scope is REVOKEd from authenticated (dependency 2), so a policy cannot call it.
  - *Fix:* Give every Moth policy hop on a typed target the personal arm inline, evaluated under caller RLS so it can never act as an oracle. Example for a file: EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM files f JOIN projects p ON p.id = f.project_id WHERE f.id = file_id AND (p.personal_owner_id IS NULL OR p.personal_owner_id = auth.uid()) AND NOT <exclusion>). Use the same shape for folder, phase, asset, task, scene, shot, level and experience targets, for both endpoints of a relationship, and in every WITH CHECK. Pin it in the S2 and S3 suites: an admin reading a position or relationship whose target or endpoint is in another person's personal space gets 0 rows. Planted control: drop the arm from the positions hop and from the relationships destination hop; the named probes go red.
- **SEC-02 [blocker]** Two references are checked only against what the writer could see at the moment of writing, and then every reader receives them: moth_views.config.refs and board_elements.file_id. Nothing ties them to the view's or board's audience, and nothing re-checks them when the target later moves or becomes money. Readers of a shared project view or board therefore receive ids, kinds and placeholders of private-project, personal-space, other-project or later-money items.
  - *Scenario:* (a) Admin A, or the creator of private project X, saves a project view of shared P whose refs include X's folder and file ids. The INVOKER validate passes because A can see them, and it rejects only personal tags and notes. Every member of P who reads the view receives X's ids and types. (b) Owner U saves refs to U's own personal-space files into P's view, with the same result. (c) A sticky-board element in P has file_id set to a file A can see in X. P's members receive the id, and the client draws a tile for a file they cannot read, which reveals that it exists. (d) A file embedded or referenced while ordinary is later flagged is_financial or moved into INVOICES. The element and the refs keep it, and an admin, who can read money in R.A.B.B.I.T., resolves and draws it in Moth, contrary to 'excluded for every role'.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: moth_views validate ('each refs entry visible to the caller under RLS and not Moth-excluded; in a project view, refs must not be personal-tier: no personal tag, no personal note'); board_elements ('A validate trigger, running as the caller, refuses a file_id that is invisible or Moth-excluded'; file_id REFERENCES files ON DELETE SET NULL); the moth_views SELECT hop checks only the view's own project. MEASURED: 0072 projects_select admin and creator arms; 0092 files_select money arm admits can_access_project_money. PLAN_MOTH §4 moth_views: 'Shared config contains no private target IDs'.
  - *Fix:* Do not keep target ids in readable JSON. Move view refs into a child table with 0096-style typed foreign keys and the SEC-01 RLS hop, so each reader sees only refs it can see, re-evaluated live. If refs stay in config, require every ref in a project view to belong to that view's project and to be not Moth-excluded. For board_elements: require file.project_id = board.project_id (a composite foreign key or trigger) and not excluded. Add a SELECT predicate that hides an element whose file_id the reader cannot see under the Moth hop, so no tile appears. Probes: a member of P gets no ref or element pointing into X or into a personal space; an admin gets none after the file is flagged is_financial. Planted control: drop the same-project check; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-03 [blocker]** The reader's gate for an entity whose live row is gone relies on 'the entity's newest row's last non-NULL side was not locked'. Capture writes nothing when every side is locked. So a money or Legal file's trash and purge leave no row behind, and the newest surviving row is the one that flagged it. Under the natural reading ('last side with a non-NULL projection'), that row's prior side is unlocked, so after the 30-day purge every pre-money row of the file shows again to every member, an admin included. Other rows can also become 'newest' and reopen the history: a local observe row reusing a cloud id (runMigration keeps ids), or a file locked only through its folder.
  - *Scenario:* File F is created in shared P (row R1) and renamed 'Vendor X settlement' (R2). A money-cleared person then flags it is_financial (R3: prior unlocked, state_locked, state NULL). The trash UPDATE has prior and state both locked, so it is skipped. Thirty days later purge_soft_deleted hard-deletes F; prior is locked, so that is skipped too. F's live row is gone and the newest row is R3. Its last non-NULL projection is the prior side, which is unlocked, and P passes moth_passes_scope. Every member of P now reads R1 and R2, the names of a now-money file. The design's own LIVE GATE probe ('later flagged returns none … even to an admin') covers only the live-row case.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: D5 ('A row is skipped when every side is locked'); D6 entity check ('if not, the last captured side was not locked AND its project passes now'); schema_changes capture step 6 ('Skip … when every present side is locked'). MEASURED: 0014_soft_delete.sql purge_soft_deleted hard-deletes trashed rows after 30 days (latest 0067, cron 'wilson-purge-soft-deleted'). runMigration.js insertOrSkip('files') keeps the desktop id (moth1_core storage fact 5).
  - *Fix:* (1) When a row's live classification is locked on trash, restore or delete, write a locked tombstone (prior_locked and state_locked true, no projection) instead of skipping. CHECK b and CHECK c already allow this; only the skip rule changes. (2) For a vanished entity, hide it when ANY of its cloud rows has prior_locked OR state_locked. Otherwise use the newest cloud row's state_project_id, or prior_project_id for a delete, never 'the last non-NULL projection'. (3) Count only source='cloud' rows for the entity gate. Probe: create, rename, flag is_financial, soft_delete_row, then purge_soft_deleted as postgres; an admin and a member each get 0 rows for F. Planted control: restore the skip; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-04 [major]** moth_can_write_scope is a SECURITY DEFINER function granted to authenticated, so anyone signed in can call it as an RPC. Its shared arm is can_write_project(p) AND passes_project_privacy(p), and neither function checks the workspace. As specified, it answers TRUE for any existing project in any company that is neither private nor a personal space, and FALSE for an unknown id. That makes it a cross-tenant oracle for existence and privacy.
  - *Scenario:* An admin or manager in workspace B, or anyone at all when the target project is unstaffed, calls rpc('moth_can_write_scope', {p_project: <A's project id>}). can_write_project returns true (current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager'), or NOT project_is_staffed). passes_project_privacy returns true because A's project is not private. The answer is TRUE. A random uuid returns FALSE (passes_project_privacy's EXISTS fails), and so does A's private project. The caller learns which ids exist in company A and which of them are private.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0013_project_members.sql can_write_project body (`current_app_role() IN ('admin','manager') OR NOT project_is_staffed(p) OR project_role_for(p) IN ('manager','member')`, no workspace or existence check); 0082 passes_project_privacy (EXISTS projects WHERE id = p AND (NOT is_private OR created_by = auth.uid() OR current_app_role()='admin'), no workspace check, REVOKEd from authenticated). Design dependency (3): 'moth_can_write_scope … DEFINER, granted to authenticated … FALSE for an unknown id'.
  - *Fix:* Start every arm with EXISTS (projects p WHERE p.id = p_project AND p.workspace_id = current_workspace_id() AND p.deleted_at IS NULL AND has_active_membership(p.workspace_id)), and return FALSE uniformly otherwise. The function then only ever answers about the caller's own workspace. State REVOKE ... FROM PUBLIC, anon explicitly. Probe: a user of workspace B gets FALSE for A's shared project, the same as for a random id. Planted control: drop the workspace term; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-05 [major]** Gap and recording marks cannot be scoped, yet the window promises scoped gaps. CHECK b_shape forces prior_project_id and state_project_id to NULL on every mark_* row. Since the window lists a gap when 'prior_project_id is NULL (workspace-wide) or passes', every gap is shown workspace-wide. The capture handler's gaps come from writes in private projects and personal spaces, and the local observe path writes gaps over one person's desktop observation intervals. All of these reach every member's window, showing which table was touched, when, and over what intervals another person's desktop was observed. The suite's own probe ('a gap in another person's personal space is not listed') cannot pass with this shape. Local mark_recording rows also have no rule keeping them out of the per-type recording_since.
  - *Scenario:* A capture failure on a write inside admin X's private project, or inside U's personal space, inserts mark_gap(entity_type='files', entity_id = the workspace id, no project). Every member's moth_history_window then lists a 'files' gap at that instant, showing that something happened at that moment in a scope they cannot see. U's desktop observations write gaps over U's own intervals, and every member sees U's working hours. U's first local observation writes mark_recording for 'files'. If the window takes the newest mark per type, everyone's recording_since jumps to U's first observation.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: schema_changes moth_history_b_shape_chk ('mark_* ⇒ prior, state and both project ids NULL'); capture step 8 (mark_gap with entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME, entity_id = workspace id); moth_history_window ('the gap rows whose prior_project_id is NULL (workspace-wide) or passes moth_passes_scope. A local gap is returned only to the scope's owner'), with no column named for that owner test; local-scope fit ('its first observation writes mark_recording').
  - *Fix:* Let mark_gap rows carry prior_project_id, prior_class, prior_owner_id and local_scope_id, with only the projections NULL. In capture, compute the side's scope before anything that can fail. In the window, apply the same side-visibility test as the reader, owner-only for 'personal' and 'local'. List a gap with no scope only when the failure happened before the scope was known, and then without entity_type. Compute recording_since from source='cloud' marks only. Local marks are returned only to prior_owner_id = auth.uid(). Probes: a personal-space gap and a local gap are each invisible to another member and to an admin. Planted control: drop the owner test; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-06 [major]** No constraint on moth_history ties source='local' rows to class 'local' and to the scope owner. The only thing between a desktop user and a forged entry in a shared project's history is the other area's observe RPC: entity_type and entity_id are caller-reported and have no foreign key. A forged row is also the 'newest row' that SEC-03's vanished-entity gate trusts.
  - *Scenario:* A member's desktop, or a crafted call to the observe RPC if that RPC copies kind, id, project or class from its arguments, writes source='local' rows with state_class='shared', state_project_id = shared P and entity_id = an existing cloud file. Every member of P then reads fabricated 'moved' or 'removed' events attributed to the real actor_user_id. Alternatively, a row for a purged money file's id reopens that file's earlier history (SEC-03). The house rule says caller-supplied kind/id pairs are never authority.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: CHECK moth_history_e_source_chk only ties source='local' to local_scope_id; CHECK a only requires an owner when the class is private, personal or local; entity_id has no FK ('rows outlive their subject'); local-scope fit ('That area's observe RPC writes diff rows … class local with owner = the scope owner'), which describes the behaviour without enforcing it.
  - *Fix:* Add CHECK moth_history_f_local_chk: source='local' ⇒ prior_class and state_class each NULL or 'local', and prior_project_id and state_project_id each NULL or equal to the scope's backing project. Have the observe RPC take the owner and the backing project from moth_local_scopes (owner = auth.uid(), FOR SHARE), never from its arguments, and refuse an entity_id that names a cloud row of a different scope. Exclude local rows from the cloud entity gate (SEC-03). Probe: a local row with state_class 'shared' throws exactly moth_history_f_local_chk.
- **SEC-07 [major]** The locked-folder test (rabbit_money_segment(split_part(folders.path,'/',1))) depends on a column clients write. folders.path is computed and stored by the client, authenticated holds UPDATE on every folders column, and the only guard is can_write_project. Any project writer can therefore change whether Moth treats a folder as money: in the capture's classification, in the reader's live gate, in the positions folder hop, and in board placement.
  - *Scenario:* A member of an unstaffed project, or any app manager, updates the INVOICES category folder and its children (for example INVOICES/<lineId>) to path 'FILES/…'. At the next capture and in the live gate these folders count as unlocked. Their rows (slug, label, parent and the subtree's shape) then enter moth_history and the Moth graph for every reader, contrary to 'money-gated folders … excluded … for everyone'. The reverse change, renaming an ordinary folder to 'LEGAL/…', hides it and its files from Moth.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0041_folders.sql header ('path IS COMPUTED CLIENT-SIDE AND STORED, NOT GENERATED IN POSTGRES'); folders_update (USING and WITH CHECK = workspace + can_write_project only); `GRANT SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE ON public.folders TO authenticated`; no trigger on folders besides populate_workspace and touch (grep over the migrations). Contrast with 0088's trg_files_legal_fixed, which pins Legal-ness for files rows only. Design D5 and the capture step 4 folder classification.
  - *Fix:* Before the history migration, the folders/eligibility area adds a guard trigger on folders, BEFORE UPDATE OF path, slug, parent_id, that refuses moving a folder into or out of a money segment (the shape of trg_files_legal_fixed). Name it in the history migration's pre-flight. Alternatively, classify through a server-pinned fact, such as the category row's kind plus a server-set locked flag, inherited down parent_id. Probe: a writer renaming INVOICES to FILES gets an error, and a capture after an attempted rename still writes no folder row. Planted control: drop the trigger; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-08 [major]** The specified boards (S4) gates have no locked-folder exclusion. A board is a real folder, folder rows under INVOICES, FINANCE and LEGAL are visible to every project reader by design, and the boards write gate is moth_can_write_scope. Any writer can therefore put a board inside a money folder, and Moth would show it and its elements.
  - *Scenario:* A member with write on P creates a board whose folder_id is a folder under P's LEGAL/ tree. The boards SELECT requires only EXISTS(folders under caller RLS) plus the projects personal arm, and folders_select passes for every project reader. The board, its colour, its shape and text elements, and its sticky files appear in Moth for every member, inside the money world the ruling excludes from boards 'entirely'.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0041_folders.sql §4 comment ('the folder ROW is visible to any project reader, because a folder is a name'); folders_select (workspace + EXISTS projects). Design text: boards ('SELECT: workspace AND membership AND EXISTS (folders under caller RLS) AND the projects hop with the personal arm. Writes: moth_can_write_scope(project_id) plus the hops'). MOTH_PLANNING_BRIEF §8: 'excluded from the graph and boards entirely, for everyone'.
  - *Fix:* Add to the boards and board_elements SELECT and WITH CHECK: NOT rabbit_money_segment(split_part(f.path,'/',1)) on the board's folder and every ancestor, made trustworthy by SEC-07's guard. Add a validate trigger refusing a board folder in a locked tree. Probe: creating a board under LEGAL/ is refused, and an existing board whose folder is moved under INVOICES is invisible to an admin. Planted control: drop the predicate; the probe goes red.
- **SEC-09 [major]** moth_history's audience model cannot express the tables S3 is told to capture. Each side has one project and one class. overlay_owner_id applies to the whole row, not to a side. A side is visible only when side_project IS NOT NULL. relationships have two endpoints that can sit in different audiences, workspace_notes and company tags have no project, and a note going personal→shared needs a tier for each side. S3 must therefore rewrite the reader, and the shapes on offer either hide those rows entirely or show the other endpoint or the personal-era title.
  - *Scenario:* S3 captures moth_relationships with the source's project as the side scope. The destination is a file in private project X, and its id sits in the projection (dest_id or similar). Member M, who sees the source's project, reads the history row and learns X's file id. moth_relationships SELECT would have hidden that row from M. A workspace note captured with project NULL is never visible ('side_project IS NOT NULL'), so S3 relaxes that test. Then a personal→shared transition row, whose overlay is either unset or set for the whole row, shows the note's personal-era title or its earlier existence to every member.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: moth_history columns (one prior_project_id and one state_project_id, one *_class and one *_owner_id per side, one overlay_owner_id per row); reader side test ('NOT side_locked AND side_project IS NOT NULL AND moth_passes_scope(side_project) AND CASE side_class …'); S3 entries ('moth_relationships … History: S3 captures it'; workspace_notes 'class shared for shared and company_standard, and overlay_owner for personal'). MEASURED: 0096 task_items FKs are on (id, project_id), so S1's own edges stay within one project and are safe today.
  - *Fix:* Fix the shape now, before S3 restates it. Either add per-side second-endpoint scope columns (prior_peer_project_id and state_peer_project_id, plus class and owner), required to pass for the side to be visible, or require an edge row to carry no peer id in its projection unless the peer passes too. Admit a 'workspace' class with NULL project for workspace-scoped entities. Use a per-side 'personal' class with an owner for notes and personal tags instead of the per-row overlay. Probes in S3: a relationship into a private project returns no row to M, and a personal→shared note shows to others as 'added' with prior NULL.
- **SEC-10 [minor]** moth_history_read returns the table's global BIGINT identity id for every row. One identity sequence serves every workspace, and the reader omits hidden rows, so the jump between two consecutive returned ids is a count of hidden events. Those hidden events come from other companies, private projects and personal spaces. That contradicts 'no count of omitted rows is ever returned'.
  - *Scenario:* A member pages a quiet shared project and sees rows with ids 50,112 and 50,161 a minute apart. 48 structural events happened elsewhere on the platform in that minute. Watched over time, that measures another tenant's activity, or a private project's activity during known quiet periods.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: moth_history id BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY; moth_history_read RETURNS TABLE(id BIGINT, …) and paginates by p_after_id. (MEASURED precedent: 0012 edit_history uses the same id type, but only admins and managers read it.)
  - *Fix:* Return no raw id. Page by an opaque cursor of (observed_at, row_key), where row_key is a random uuid column or an HMAC of the id, and keep the BIGINT internal for ordering. Probe: the returned columns contain no BIGINT.
- **SEC-11 [minor]** Several specified write paths can confirm that a hidden row exists. The INSERT grants include a client-chosen id (moth_views, moth_layout_positions, the re-insert undo), so a 23505 on the primary key reveals another person's aggregate view or overlay position. Foreign keys are checked as the owner: on moth_tags.canonical_id (its grant is not stated) and type_id (a 23503 differs from the validate trigger's refusal of another person's personal type), they reveal whether another person's personal tag or type id exists.
  - *Scenario:* A member who has learned a uuid inserts a moth_views row with that id, or sets their own personal tag's canonical_id to it. The answer is 23505 or success, against 23503, which shows whether another person's personal view or tag exists.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: moth_views INSERT grant '(id, scope_kind, project_id, name, position, config)'; positions INSERT '(id, view_id, …)'; moth_tags canonical_id REFERENCES moth_tags(id) with column grants listed only as 'no workspace_id, owner_id or tier on UPDATE'. MEASURED house precedent: OUTSTANDING S4b-07 (ON CONFLICT and 23505 oracles for hidden Legal ids, informational).
  - *Fix:* Leave canonical_id out of every client grant and write it only in the merge RPC. Make the validate trigger and foreign-key refusals give the same code and message. Record the uuid-only 23505 oracle as S4b-07's accepted class in each suite header.
- **SEC-12 [minor]** The design defers to the eligibility area for helpers whose security shape it does not pin. moth_can_write_target, used by the assignment and relationship write policies, is never defined. sticky_locks has no SELECT audience. The 'canonical Moth file exclusion' is file_row_is_money OR a locked folder, which misses receipts that are not flagged: the s3 receipts that cannot be flagged and the cloud receipts still open. Their names would enter moth_history even though the ruling excludes money for every role.
  - *Scenario:* S3 implements moth_can_write_target as plain can_write_project, which admits admins and managers into personal spaces (the gap E14 measured), and an admin attaches a company tag to another person's personal-space file. sticky_locks rows readable workspace-wide reveal who is editing a sticky on a personal-space board. A receipt held on an s3 bucket (it cannot be flagged financial) appears in Moth history under its vendor-bearing name.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: moth_tag_assignments and moth_relationships policies use moth_can_write_target; 'Dependencies' lists only moth_passes_scope, moth_can_write_scope and the file exclusion; sticky_locks 'shape deferred' with no SELECT. MEASURED: 0050 files_money_provider_chk prevents flagging a non-supabase row as financial (handbook §12.9 residual 2). INFERRED: the receipts_open rows (handbook §3.1, M2-L4) read as ordinary to file_row_is_money.
  - *Fix:* Define moth_can_write_target now as moth_can_write_scope of the target's project, with the SEC-04 workspace check, behind the SEC-01 personal arm. Give sticky_locks no client SELECT, or only the board hop. Have the eligibility area extend the canonical exclusion to receipts referenced by expenses, so that capture and the reader call one helper.
- **SEC-13 [minor]** The reader caches moth_passes_scope results in 'a temporary map of project ids already checked' while running as SECURITY DEFINER with search_path = public. pg_temp is searched before public for relations unless it is placed last explicitly, and a STABLE function cannot create a temp table anyway.
  - *Scenario:* A session able to create a temp table named like the cache, or like an unqualified table the body reads, has it resolved inside the definer body, which can decide which projects 'pass'. Only reachable through direct SQL, not PostgREST, so the risk is low.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED design text: risks ('moth_passes_scope is cached per project inside the function (a temporary map of project ids already checked)'); moth_history_read 'STABLE SECURITY DEFINER SET search_path = public'.
  - *Fix:* Keep the cache in a plpgsql variable (uuid[] or jsonb), set search_path = public, pg_temp, and schema-qualify every relation in the reader and the capture.

### Review — Correctness and house rules: the Moth tables (moth_history in Session 1; shapes of the later eleven tables)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes. The architecture holds. That covers a definer-only history table that is not forced, RPC-only reads, the backwards rebuild from the live graph, the two-audience reader, the guarded 04:45 purge, and the house-rule housekeeping (RLS_TABLES 61→62, the replay list, the rlsSuiteLists tails, the 35_platform_audit revokes). Each of those was checked against 0012, rls.yml, rlsSuiteLists.test.js and 35_platform_audit.sql. As specified, the capture does not yet do what its own tests require. Phases are never captured, because they have no workspace_id (T-01). The rule that skips a row when prior and state are equal drops trash, restore, project-only moves and privacy flips (T-02). Both must be fixed before build. T-03 to T-07 must be fixed too, or the gap labelling, the gap scoping and the planted-control proof do not hold, and folders would carry no names. T-08 is a scope ruling for the controller, not a code change. T-09 constrains how S3 and S4 widen the CHECK. The rest are minor.

- **T-01 [blocker]** Phases are never captured. Step (2) of fn_moth_history_capture takes the workspace from the row's own workspace_id and returns NULL when no workspaces row exists. public.phases has no workspace_id column (it is a parent-join table), so every phases INSERT, UPDATE and DELETE is skipped without a sound. If the skip were bypassed, moth_history.workspace_id NOT NULL would refuse the insert and the handler's mark_gap would fail on the same NULL. The both-ways trigger/CHECK post-condition still passes, and no probe touches phases.
  - *Scenario:* A member renames phase 'Previs' to 'Layout', or a project purge cascades its phases away. moth_history gets no row. Session 2's scrub then shows the phase under its current name across the whole window and never shows the deleted phases.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0061_phase_dependencies.sql comment 'phases has no workspace_id (it is a parent-join table)'. 0004_rls_rabbit.sql §2 adds workspace_id only to assets, tasks, files and comments. 0000 'create table phases' has no workspace_id. 0012 fn_edit_history_capture step (b) resolves phases by joining projects, falling back to current_workspace_id() for cascades. The design's capture step (2) has no such arm.
  - *Fix:* Resolve the workspace per table: the row's column, else a join from project_id to projects.workspace_id, else current_workspace_id() for cascades (0012's order), and only then run the workspaces-exists skip. Add a capture probe for phases: an insert, a rename and a cascade delete each write their row. Add a planted control that removes the phases arm and turns that probe red.
- **T-02 [blocker]** As written, the rule 'skip when prior = state (projection unchanged)' also drops trash, restore, project-only moves and privacy flips. The projector's allowlist has no deleted_at, project_id, is_private or personal_owner_id. Those columns are in the WHEN lists, but changing them leaves both projections identical, so the row is skipped. The design's own probe 'soft_delete_row writes trash and restore_soft_deleted writes restore' cannot pass. A file moved between projects with folder_id NULL on both sides is never recorded. A project turned private, or turned into a personal space, records nothing.
  - *Scenario:* An admin trashes file F with soft_delete_row('files', F). OLD and NEW differ only in deleted_at and deleted_by. moth_history_project gives the same JSON for both, so the row is skipped. The scrub never shows the trash, and the backwards rebuild keeps F in the graph after its trash date. The same happens when the body-identity move changes files.project_id from A to B with folder_id NULL → NULL: F appears in B for the whole window.
  - *Evidence:* Design schema_changes for fn_moth_history_capture, step (6) 'Skip when prior = state', and the allowlist in moth_history_projection_ok, which has no deleted_at or project_id. The WHEN lists include deleted_at (files, projects) and is_private and personal_owner_id (projects). 0014_soft_delete.sql soft_delete_row is 'UPDATE … SET deleted_at'.
  - *Fix:* Skip only when the action is 'update' AND the projections, both side project ids, classes, owners and locked flags are all unchanged. trash, restore and move are never skipped. Add probes: a project_id-only move writes 'move'; an is_private flip writes a row; trash and restore write rows. Add a planted control that skips on projection equality alone and turns the trash probe red.
- **T-03 [major]** The robustness probe cannot pass as designed. When capture fails, the handler writes a mark_gap with entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME. That gap row is subject to the same moth_history_entity_type_chk. When the probe narrows the entity CHECK to drop 'folders', the folder capture fails, the handler's gap insert fails on the same CHECK, and the nested BEGIN swallows the error. 'A mark_gap row exists' is therefore false. The one systematic capture failure the design names, a table missing from the CHECK, is exactly the case in which the gap cannot be recorded.
  - *Scenario:* Suite NNN_moth_history: ALTER TABLE moth_history DROP CONSTRAINT moth_history_entity_type_chk, then ADD it without 'folders'. INSERT a folder. The folder row commits (correct), but the probe's count of mark_gap rows WHERE entity_type = 'folders' is 0. The probe is red against a correct build.
  - *Evidence:* Design: fn_moth_history_capture step (8) 'inserts a mark_gap (… entity_type = TG_TABLE_NAME …) and swallows its own failure'; CHECK moth_history_entity_type_chk covers every row; the ROBUSTNESS probe in tests[0].
  - *Fix:* Exempt gap rows from the entity list: give mark rows their own entity_type value admitted by the CHECK (for example 'workspace', with the failed table recorded in a bounded column), or write the entity CHECK as (action LIKE 'mark_%' OR entity_type IN (…)). Alternatively, provoke the failure another way, such as a projection over the size bound. Plant: remove the gap insert, and the probe goes red.
- **T-04 [major]** An instant gap (period_from = period_to = now()) does not label the period that a missed change corrupts. The backwards rebuild applies every row after T in reverse. One missing row makes every state before the miss wrong for that entity, not just the instant. moth_history_window keeps reporting recording_since as the original mark, so the scrub shows months of reconstructed graph as recorded when it is not. This fails her ruling that 'periods with no record [are] labelled'.
  - *Scenario:* On day 40 a capture of a files move fails, because the projection is over its bound or a CHECK refuses it. The handler writes a zero-length gap. On day 60 a member scrubs back to day 10. The rebuild leaves the file where it is today, because the move row is missing. The window says history has been recorded since day 0, with one instant gap on day 40 that the UI cannot draw as a period.
  - *Evidence:* Design D7 and fn_moth_history_capture step (8): 'period_from = period_to = now()'. moth_history_window returns recording_since = GREATEST(mark or workspaces.created_at, now() - 90 days) and does not consider gaps. D2 rebuilds 'backwards from today's live graph through the changes after T'.
  - *Fix:* Treat a failed capture as a recording restart for that (workspace, entity_type). Either the handler writes a mark_recording at now(), or the window computes recording_since = GREATEST(latest mark_recording, latest mark_gap.period_to of that type, created_at, now() - 90 days). The scrub then labels everything before the failure as 'not recorded' for that type. Probe: after an induced failure, the window's recording_since for that type equals the failure time.
- **T-05 [major]** moth_history_b_shape_chk forbids project ids on mark rows ('mark_* ⇒ prior, state and both project ids NULL'). moth_history_window scopes gaps by prior_project_id ('gap rows whose prior_project_id is NULL … or passes moth_passes_scope'), and promises that 'a local gap is returned only to the scope's owner'. Under the CHECK every gap is workspace-wide, so the window's scoping arm never fires. A local-scope observe gap, or the start mark of a local scope, either cannot be written with its scope or reaches every member. Its start mark also cannot be told apart from the workspace's per-type mark.
  - *Scenario:* The local-scope area's observe RPC writes mark_gap over (last_observed_at, now()) for member X's desktop-only project. Either the CHECK refuses the project id, or the row is written workspace-wide. Member M's moth_history_window then returns a gap that reveals X's local scope exists and when it went unobserved, which breaks 'no placeholder that reveals existence'. The test 'a gap in another person's personal space is not listed' cannot even be set up, because its fixture violates b_shape.
  - *Evidence:* Design CHECK moth_history_b_shape_chk; moth_history_window spec ('gap rows whose prior_project_id …', 'A local gap is returned only to the scope's owner'); the Fit section on moth_local_scopes ('mark_gap over (last_observed_at, now()); its first observation writes mark_recording'); the WINDOW probes in tests[0].
  - *Fix:* Let mark rows carry a scope: allow prior_project_id or local_scope_id together with state_class and state_owner_id on mark_* rows, and keep prior, state and state_project_id NULL. The window then filters gaps and recording marks by moth_passes_scope(project) and, for class 'local', state_owner_id = auth.uid(). It computes recording_since per local scope from that scope's own first mark. Probes: X's local gap is invisible to M and visible to X; a local scope's recording starts at its first observation.
- **T-06 [major]** Four of the nine planted controls cannot bite, so they prove nothing. (2) The original-audience arm: in the probe, the private origin project is still private, so the present-audience moth_passes_scope(prior project) already hides the prior side. (6) The files WHEN clause: without it, a description-only update reaches the function, gets equal projections and is skipped, so the probe stays green. (7) The workspace-exists skip: without it, the insert raises a foreign-key violation, the handler catches it, the gap insert fails the same way, and the moth_history CASCADE removes any rows, so 'no rows and the delete succeeds' stays green. (8) A manager arm on the private class: moth_history_read's opening moth_passes_scope check refuses the manager before the class CASE runs.
  - *Scenario:* A builder deletes the class/owner CASE from the side check. The 'added with prior NULL' probe still passes, because project P is still private and moth_passes_scope(P) is false for M. The original-audience rule, which is half of her 'two audiences' requirement, ships with no test that would notice it gone.
  - *Evidence:* Design tests[0], PLANTED CONTROLS (2), (6), (7), (8) against the reader spec (opening check, then per-side moth_passes_scope AND the class CASE) and the capture's 'Skip when prior = state'. 0012 fn_edit_history_capture's EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS shows the catch behaviour.
  - *Fix:* (2) After the move, flip P to shared (is_private=false), so only the snapshot class keeps the prior side hidden, and probe again. (6) Pin the WHEN clause by catalog: pg_get_triggerdef equals the expected text, or count calls with a debug GUC. (7) Probe that no WARNING-producing insert is attempted, for example by counting through a debug GUC, or drop this control and keep the skip as defence. (8) Probe through an aggregate read (p_scope NULL) where the private row is reached only by the class arm, or plant the manager arm in moth_passes_scope instead.
- **T-07 [major]** A folder's name is never recorded. The folders WHEN list fires on slug, label and path, but the allowlist has none of label, slug or path. The design forbids 'folder path', and renames only files.tags → categories. Every folder rename therefore gives equal projections and is skipped, and a deleted folder's 'delete' row carries no name. A folder is the main object of the 'everything by folders' quick start and the folder widgets.
  - *Scenario:* Member renames folder 'SCENES/Wlsn-Sc001' (label 'Opening') to 'Cold open'. No row is written. After someone deletes a folder, the scrub's 'removed' row has a projection with only kind, parent_id and sort_order, so the rebuilt past graph shows a nameless folder.
  - *Evidence:* 0041_folders.sql CREATE TABLE folders (slug, label, path columns); the design's allowlist in moth_history_projection_ok; moth_history_project ('never emits … folder path'); the folders WHEN list 'project_id, parent_id, slug, label, kind, path'.
  - *Fix:* Project folders.label (falling back to slug) under the allowlisted key 'name', keep path out, and drop path from the WHEN list unless it is needed for the lock reclassification (keep it there for INVOICES/LEGAL changes only). Probe: a folder label change writes 'update' with prior.name ≠ state.name.
- **T-08 [major]** The design marks as 'material: false' a change that moves eleven of the fourteen tables out of Session 1. The approved plan puts 'All section 4 schemas/security/suites' in Session 1's scope and ordered build step 3. Session 2's Database line reads 'S1 view/layout/preferences/history'. The plan's own counts (RLS_TABLES 61 → 75, suites 110–123) and Session 1's test line ('every new table tenant/inactive/owner/admin/manager/reviewer probes') assume them. Re-allocating work between sessions changes what later briefs must do. The house rule says nothing the controller does not name becomes session scope.
  - *Scenario:* The controller writes Session 2's brief from PLAN_MOTH as approved. It tells S2 to consume moth_views and moth_layout_positions from S1 ('S1 view/layout/preferences/history'), but they do not exist. S2 starts UI work against a missing table, or improvises the schema without a design round.
  - *Evidence:* PLAN_MOTH.md §5 Session 1 'In scope: … All section 4 schemas/security/suites' and Ordered build 3; Session 2 'Database — proposed: S1 view/layout/preferences/history'; §4 table (110–123, 61 to 75); moth1_core 'Scope' rule (PLAN_TO_1_0 §5 rules 1, 8). The design's plan_differences[0] sets material:false.
  - *Fix:* Mark the reallocation as material and send it to the controller with the plan edits stated: S1 scope, S2/S3/S4 Database lines, suite numbering and the RLS_TABLES count. Do it before build, rather than recording it only as a plan difference. The technical reasoning (0041 'arrives with its feature') can stand.
- **T-09 [major]** The later restatements tell S3 and S4 to widen moth_history's entity CHECK by DROP CONSTRAINT + ADD. On this table, DROP CONSTRAINT takes an ACCESS EXCLUSIVE lock held to commit, and ADD CONSTRAINT then scans up to 90 days of rows. Every capture INSERT waits on that lock. The capture fires AFTER every structural write on the eleven hottest tables, so every R.A.B.B.I.T. project, task, file and folder write in every workspace blocks for the length of the S3 or S4 migration.
  - *Scenario:* S3 applies its tags migration on production: DROP + ADD the entity CHECK, create six tables, restate fn_trash_authz and purge_soft_deleted. For the whole transaction, every user saving a task in any workspace hangs, because the AFTER trigger's INSERT into moth_history is queued behind ACCESS EXCLUSIVE. It may hit statement timeouts. The handler cannot help, because a lock wait is not an exception.
  - *Evidence:* Design consumers_to_change 'S3 and S4 restate moth_history's entity CHECK (DROP + ADD with the full list)' and migration_order S3; fn_moth_history_capture is AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE on projects, tasks, files and the rest; house rule 'widen a CHECK by DROP CONSTRAINT + ADD restating the full list'.
  - *Fix:* Keep the house rule's full restatement, but on moth_history do it as DROP + ADD … NOT VALID in a short first transaction (applied first and alone, off-hours), then VALIDATE CONSTRAINT in its own transaction (SHARE UPDATE EXCLUSIVE does not block inserts). Alternatively, move the entity list into an IMMUTABLE function the CHECK calls, restated with CREATE OR REPLACE. Write this into the S3 and S4 restatement notes.
- **T-10 [minor]** The trigger post-condition compares only the set of tables. Each table needs two triggers: AFTER INSERT OR DELETE with no WHEN (a WHEN cannot name OLD on INSERT or NEW on DELETE), and a separate AFTER UPDATE … WHEN. The design names only 'trg_<t>_moth_history'. A table that keeps its INSERT/DELETE trigger but loses its UPDATE trigger, for example through a typo in one CREATE in the loop, passes the set check and silently loses every move and rename.
  - *Scenario:* The files UPDATE trigger is created on 'file' instead of 'files', or is dropped by a later restatement. Creates and deletes are still recorded, moves are not, and the post-condition is green.
  - *Evidence:* Design fn_moth_history_capture 'Triggers: AFTER INSERT OR DELETE FOR EACH ROW; AFTER UPDATE FOR EACH ROW WHEN (…)'; post-condition 'the set of tables with a trigger calling fn_moth_history_capture equals, both ways, the set the entity CHECK admits'.
  - *Fix:* Name both triggers (trg_<t>_moth_history and trg_<t>_moth_history_upd). Assert per table, through tgtype bits, that INSERT, DELETE and UPDATE are all covered, and that the UPDATE trigger's WHEN text equals the pinned list (pg_get_triggerdef).
- **T-11 [minor]** The size bounds can be exceeded by real rows. The CHECK forces at most 4096 octets and arrays of at most 200, but tasks.title, assets.name and files.name have no length CHECK, and assets.level_ids and experience_ids are uncapped uuid[] arrays. A hundred level ids is about 3.9 KB, so one large asset or a long pasted title fails moth_history_d_projection_chk. Every structural change to that row then turns into a (useless, see T-04) gap.
  - *Scenario:* An asset is linked to 110 levels, or a task title is a pasted 5 KB brief. From then on every capture of that row raises 23514 inside the handler, and its history is gone for good.
  - *Evidence:* 0099_asset_level_links.sql assets.level_ids / experience_ids uuid[] NOT NULL DEFAULT '{}' with no cardinality CHECK; 0000 tasks.title, assets.name and files.name with no length CHECK (grep of char_length(title|name) found none); the design's moth_history_projection_ok bounds.
  - *Fix:* Have moth_history_project bound its own output: truncate text values to a fixed length (for example 300 characters), cap arrays at 200 and set a 'truncated' flag key in the allowlist. The CHECK then only refuses what the projector can never emit. Add a probe that a 5 KB title still writes a row.
- **T-12 [minor]** The task_items projection is under-specified. The table's columns are entity_type, entity_id and position, but the allowlist has item_type and item_id, not entity_type and entity_id. The design names only the files.tags → categories rename. Built literally, the projector drops the edge's target and records a task_item as {task_id, position}, which cannot rebuild the edge.
  - *Scenario:* A member links shot S to task T. The 'create' row's state is {task_id: T, position: 0}, with no target. The scrub cannot draw the task→shot edge it is meant to show.
  - *Evidence:* 0096_task_items.sql CREATE TABLE task_items (entity_type, entity_id, position, generated asset_id … experience_id); the design's allowlist ('item_type', 'item_id'); moth_history_project names only the categories rename.
  - *Fix:* State the per-table key maps in moth_history_project (task_items: entity_type → item_type, entity_id → item_id; folders: label → name, see T-07). Add a probe that a task_items create carries item_type and item_id.
- **T-13 [minor]** When a cascade fires, the capture resolves each child's class by joining projects, but on a project hard delete the projects row is already gone. The spec then falls through to 'otherwise shared'. The reader hides these rows today, because moth_passes_scope on a deleted project is false. Still, the snapshot is wrong, and it fails open: a private or personal project's children are recorded as class 'shared', with full projections.
  - *Scenario:* An admin hard-deletes personal-space project PS (or the 30-day purge removes it). Its files' 'delete' rows are stored with prior_class 'shared'. Any later change that let a deleted id pass the scope gate again (an id reuse, or a restore-from-backup path) would expose them.
  - *Evidence:* Design capture step (3): 'class = personal when personal_owner_id is set, private when is_private, otherwise shared'; 0012 fn_edit_history_capture's own note that the parent row is gone when the child's AFTER DELETE fires on a cascade.
  - *Fix:* When the project row cannot be found, store the class as NULL (the reader's CASE then gives ELSE false), or snapshot the class from the projects row in a BEFORE DELETE on projects. Probe: after a private project's purge, its children's rows carry no 'shared' class.
- **T-14 [minor]** moth_history_read is declared STABLE, but its risk mitigation caches moth_passes_scope results in 'a temporary map of project ids already checked'. A non-volatile PL/pgSQL function is refused CREATE TEMP TABLE and INSERT. PostgREST also runs STABLE RPCs in a read-only transaction. Separately, p_after_id paging over an identity column misses rows from a long transaction that commits after the reader has paged past its lower ids.
  - *Scenario:* A builder follows the mitigation and creates a temp table inside moth_history_read. Every call fails ('CREATE TABLE is not allowed in a non-volatile function' / read-only transaction), and the adapter shows 'history unavailable'.
  - *Evidence:* Design schema_changes moth_history_read 'LANGUAGE plpgsql STABLE SECURITY DEFINER'; risks[7] mitigation ('a temporary map'); moth_history id BIGINT GENERATED ALWAYS AS IDENTITY with observed_at DEFAULT now().
  - *Fix:* Cache in a PL/pgSQL variable (a uuid[] of passed and refused ids) or in a CTE (one moth_passes_scope call per DISTINCT project), and keep STABLE. Document that a scrub reads one window in a single call, or page by (observed_at, id) with a small overlap.
- **T-15 [minor]** The 'restate from latest' citations for the later sessions are partly wrong. fn_realtime_broadcast's latest body is 0098, not '0101 or later': 0101 states it did not restate it, and pins it with an md5. projects.created_by is nullable, and moth_history_a_class_chk requires an owner for class private. A private project with created_by NULL (a service-role insert, since fn_audit_touch stamps auth.uid()) makes every capture in it fail the CHECK.
  - *Scenario:* S2 opens 0101 to restate fn_realtime_broadcast and finds only fn_budget_versions_broadcast and fn_task_items_ws_broadcast, so it copies a wrong base. Or a private project created through a service-role path loses all its history to repeated handler gaps.
  - *Evidence:* 0101_versions_and_items_realtime.sql header 'Neither generic body is restated: fn_realtime_broadcast stays 0098's (md5 …)'; 0098_table_views.sql §0 md5 pre-flight and §10; 0000 projects.created_by nullable; 0007 drops projects_created_by_fkey; design CHECK moth_history_a_class_chk.
  - *Fix:* Cite 0098 as fn_realtime_broadcast's latest body (and 0018 for fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast until A-R1-04 restates it), with its md5 guard.
