### Migration order

- **A — 0107_private_project_write_hops.sql (proposed number; remeasure) + suite 110_private_project_write_hops.sql**: Pre-flight; the owner guard function and trigger; projects_update; DROP + CREATE for tasks, assets, comments, folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, project_members (4), files_update / files_delete, milestones_update / milestones_delete and task_templates (4), each restated from the migration named in schema_changes; post-conditions including the catalogue scan. In the same commit: suite 110, the suite 90 and 95 files-text pins, rls.yml replay line, rlsSuiteLists tail pins.
  - *Constraints:* One transaction, no enum. Drift read first, then a rolled-back rehearsal on wilson-dev (tap-hosted.py with the migration and suite 110, collected = planned), then the LF apply and history row with md5. Run the WHOLE pgTAP set before the push: hops refuse writes into TRASHED projects' children and the owner guard can change persona-run fixtures. Suites 03, 04, 06, 18, 19, 48–52, 54, 71, 80, 82, 83, 90 and 95 must stay green. The post-conditions are load-bearing: comment-stripped body checks, policy checks by name.
- **B — 0108_private_project_money_and_history.sql + suite 111_private_project_money_and_history.sql**: Pre-flight (0092's can_access_project_legal and edit_history.is_legal present, 0107 present); can_access_project_money restated; edit_history_project_of; fn_edit_history_capture restated from 0061; the backfill; edit_history_select restated from 0092; comments; post-conditions. In the same commit: suite 111, suite 95 probes 17 and 167, the replay line and the tail pins.
  - *Constraints:* Must follow A, because suite 111's fixtures place a seat on a private project as the runner, which A's hops no longer allow for a client. The backfill is monotone (NULLs only). The whole set must run, including 17_edit_history, 43–47 (budget tables), 53, 63, 77, 78, 90, 95 and 104. Record that 0092 is no longer re-runnable after B, because its post-condition 12 pins 0037's body.
- **C — 0109_private_workspace_channel.sql + suite 112_private_workspace_channel.sql, with the client changes and their vitest in the same commit**: fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast restated from 0018; COMMENTs on it and on can_read_workspace_topic; post-conditions. The suite goes in the replay list only (it covers no table). The adapter, provider and realtimeMerge changes ship with it.
  - *Constraints:* It comes after the personal-space schema migration only if D9 is rejected (then it names that classifier). The CI stack has no realtime, so the behaviour probes are vacuous there ('no-schema'), and the rehearsal on wilson-dev, which has realtime.messages, is the bite; the body-structure probes bite on CI. Record the bundle before and after; the growth is expected to be well under 20 KB.
- **Then the Moth table migrations (schema area)**: Every Moth table's policies follow D2's shape. Suite 110's catalogue scan fails any write clause that lacks the hop, and any broadcast arm must keep private and personal rows off the workspace topic.
  - *Constraints:* PLAN_MOTH §4's provisional suites 110–123 shift by three. Remeasure free numbers at the tip.

### Tests

- **supabase/tests/rls/110_private_project_write_hops.sql (new, replay list)**: Fixtures (runner): workspace ws_a; creator C (app role manager) of private project P (auto-staffed); second manager M2 (app role manager, not creator); admin A; member U (app role user); unstaffed private project Pu created by C; public project Q with a row of each table. For EACH of tasks, assets, comments (entity 'project' and 'task'), folders, scenes, shots, levels, experiences, project_members, milestones, files and task_templates: (1) M2 runs a plain INSERT into P (no RETURNING, the return=minimal shape) and it throws 42501. (2) The same INSERT into Q lives (control). (3) C and (4) A insert into P and live. (5) M2 runs a filter-less, column-free `UPDATE t SET project_id = P` and it throws 42501; presence control: the Q row is still in Q. (6) M2 runs a filter-less `UPDATE t SET project_id = Q` and it lives; the runner sees the P row still in P (the failing control for the USING hop). (7) M2 runs a filter-less `DELETE FROM t` and it lives; the runner sees the P row still there while the Q row is gone (the control that the DELETE ran). For Pu: U's INSERT throws 42501, the unstaffed opening refused. project_members_select: M2 counts 0 seats of P, U sees Q's seats, C sees P's. projects: M2's filter-less `UPDATE projects SET title='x'` and `SET is_private=false` live, and P stays as it was. Owner guard: M2's `UPDATE projects SET created_by = M2 WHERE id = Q` leaves created_by = C; M2 INSERTs a project naming created_by = C and the stored value is M2; M2's `SET is_private = true WHERE id = Q` throws 42501; C on Q and A on P live. Catalogue scan probe (D3). Planted controls, one at a time, each restored by sha1: drop the hop from tasks_update USING (probe 6 red), from files_delete (7 red) or from comments_insert (1 red); remove the owner trigger (owner probes red); delete one hop anywhere (scan probe red).
- **supabase/tests/rls/111_private_project_money_and_history.sql (new, replay list)**: Money: the runner seats M2 as project manager on P and on Q. As M2: can_access_project_money(P) is false; budget_lines, expenses and budget_versions of P return 0 rows; storage.objects under projects/P/INVOICES/ and projects/P/LEGAL/ return 0 rows (rabbit-files and rabbit-thumbnails); can_access_project_legal(P) is false. On Q: true and the rows are visible (control). C (seated creator) and A see P's money. History: C inserts and updates a task in P and a task_link; the runner reads edit_history with project_id = P on each row (capture probe, one per entity type over the 14). As M2: 0 history rows for P and rows for Q (control); rate_cards history visible (workspace arm control). A sees P's rows. A cascaded task_links delete whose parent is gone has project_id NULL, so M2 sees 0 rows and A sees it (fail-closed probe). Resolver: closed to authenticated, and it answers the expected project for every type. Planted controls: remove passes_project_privacy from the money gate (money probes red); remove the can_read_project_topic arm (history probes red); drop the resolver call from capture (capture probe red).
- **supabase/tests/rls/112_private_workspace_channel.sql (new, replay list; suite 23's environment-tolerant shape)**: Count-delta helpers on realtime.messages (topic LIKE 'rabbit:workspace:%', extension 'broadcast') that answer 'skipped-ok' / 'landed' / 'no-schema' / 'no-partition'. Private P: an INSERT, a title UPDATE, an assigned-task INSERT, an asset trash and a project_members INSERT each add nothing. Public Q: the same writes land (controls). Flip Q→private: exactly one message, event 'AUDIENCE', payload record IS NULL, and strpos(payload::text, Q.title) = 0 and strpos(payload::text, Q.id::text) = 0. Flip back: one AUDIENCE message and no row. A task in P (title 'zx-secret') moved to Q with a new title: an INSERT-shaped message with old_record NULL and no 'zx-secret' in the payload. A task moved from Q to P: a DELETE-shaped message carrying only Q-side values. workspace_members UPDATE lands (unchanged arm). Body-structure probes that bite on CI: is_private ≥ 4, AUDIENCE payload built from literals, 0018's filters present, five triggers, no client EXECUTE. Planted controls: drop the projects is_private test (CI body probe red; private-insert probe 'leaked' on dev); replace the AUDIENCE send with broadcast_changes(NEW, OLD) (payload probes red on dev and the literals probe red on CI); drop the child EXISTS (assigned-task probe red).
- **WILSON/src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/realtimeMerge.test.js (extend)**: A projects UPDATE with record.is_private true and old false yields an 'audience' effect; a rename of a public project does not; a trashed project still yields project-trashed. Red first on the pre-change source; planted: remove the arm and the test goes red.
- **Provider and adapter vitest: extend WILSON/src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/state/mountRabbit.test.jsx if its fake adapter accepts subscribeWorkspaceChanges, otherwise a new state/workspaceAudience.test.jsx with a sentence in the hand-off on why; plus the adapter binding tested with a fake client.channel**: (1) An AUDIENCE event triggers one listProjects. The open project is missing from the result, so it closes (bundle empty, history cleared) and the notice shows. The open project is still listed, so nothing closes (the creator and admin case). (2) A workspace-channel projects row with is_private true is not merged into projectsIndex. (3) The adapter's channel has an 'AUDIENCE' broadcast handler and forwards op 'AUDIENCE'. Each new test is red on the pre-change source; plants: remove the AUDIENCE binding, remove the is_private drop, remove the close.

### Risks

- The hops evaluate under projects_select, which hides TRASHED projects, so plain client writes to a trashed project's children are now refused (previously allowed). Restore paths are DEFINER and unaffected.
  - *Mitigation:* Run the whole pgTAP set (19_soft_delete, 71, 82 keep the restore-under-trashed-parent behaviour through fn_trash_authz). An import or undo racing a trash gets an ordinary 42501 that the optimistic rollback already handles.
- Restating fn_edit_history_capture, which 14 tables share: a mistake silently drops history (EXCEPTION → WARNING).
  - *Mitigation:* Suite 111 has a capture probe per entity type, and post-conditions pin every 0061 arm in the comment-stripped body. 17_edit_history must stay green.
- Re-running an older migration (0013, 0020, 0040, 0041, 0044, 0067, 0082, 0092) drops the hops. 0092's post-condition 12 refuses to re-run after 0108.
  - *Mitigation:* OUTSTANDING and the migration headers record the re-run order. Suite 110's catalogue scan and suite 95 probe 167 fail CI if a replay drops an arm.
- Realtime authorises a channel at join (INFERRED: cached until rejoin or token refresh). A non-entitled client that was already joined to rabbit:project:<id> when the project went private keeps hearing that project's row events until it leaves; well-behaved clients close on AUDIENCE or the 'audience' effect.
  - *Mitigation:* Record it in OUTSTANDING as INFERRED with the settling measurement: on wilson-dev, join as a member, flip, write, observe. It cannot be filtered per recipient in the database. Exposure is bounded by the token lifetime.
- Old desktop and web clients do not bind AUDIENCE, so after a flip they keep the name until their next refetch (today's behaviour, no new leak); new clients on a lagging server still receive private rows.
  - *Mitigation:* The new client never merges a workspace-channel projects row with is_private true and refetches through RLS instead.
- realtime.broadcast_changes might refuse a bare NULL for the record parameter in the INSERT- and DELETE-shaped downgrades.
  - *Mitigation:* 0018 already passes a NULL OLD and NEW on insert and delete. If the rehearsal refuses it, use 0101's thin-event fallback through realtime.send with the visible side's jsonb.
- The owner guard changes fixtures in suites that insert or update projects as a logged-in persona with an explicit created_by.
  - *Mitigation:* The guard binds only when current_user = 'authenticated'; runner inserts are untouched. Run the whole pgTAP set in the rehearsal.
- D9 (personal space keeps is_private = true) is a cross-area decision; if the schema area chooses otherwise, the channel and passes_project_privacy miss personal spaces.
  - *Mitigation:* Make it a design-review item. If it is rejected, migration C names the classifier and lands after it, fn_task_items_ws_broadcast is restated, suite 104's is_private body probe moves, and passes_project_privacy gains the arm.
- An admin can join a personal space's rabbit:project:<id> topic (projects_select's admin arm), so any Moth table broadcast there would put owner-only Moth content in front of admins, against 'admins via Files only, never Moth'.
  - *Mitigation:* Cross-area rule for Moth channels: personal-space Moth rows never ride the project topic. Use an owner-only topic or no broadcast, and pin it in that area's channel suite.
- The channel behaviour probes are vacuous on CI (no realtime).
  - *Mitigation:* Body-structure probes bite on CI, and the rolled-back rehearsal on wilson-dev, which has realtime.messages, is mandatory and is recorded in the hand-off with planted-control results.
- Moth's own caches keyed by project (graph, glow, counts, trash view) would keep a project made private after the fact.
  - *Mitigation:* Session 2 subscribes Moth's catalogue to the same provider revalidation (projectsIndex change, AUDIENCE) and drops nodes whose project leaves the index. Write this into Session 2's brief.

### Differences from PLAN_MOTH

- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* Tasks/assets, E12 and A-R2-03 — Add missing parent-privacy checks as explicit Moth write prerequisite (PLAN_MOTH §4); OUTSTANDING A-R2-03: the one-line hop in each of the four policies' WITH CHECK
  - *Design:* The measured class is about 15 tables and every write clause, not four WITH CHECKs. A filter-less UPDATE ... SET project_id moves rows OUT through USING, and a filter-less DELETE removes them. projects_update itself has no privacy arm. project_members_select and task_templates_select leak on read. Migration A fixes all of them.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* OUTSTANDING / 0082 header: milestones closed by 0082 §3b; moth1_core digest: 'Hop present on … files, milestones'
  - *Design:* Only the WITH CHECK clauses carry the hop. milestones_update USING, milestones_delete, files_update USING and files_delete (latest 0092, not 0038) do not. All are restated in migration A.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* 0072 header: 'created_by is stamped by fn_audit_touch on every INSERT … no client can name somebody else as the owner of a private project'
  - *Design:* MEASURED false. fn_audit_touch (0001) stamps created_by only when it is NULL and never protects it on UPDATE, and no projects trigger guards it, so a writer can claim or privatise a public project, or take over every private project with a filter-less UPDATE (INFERRED; suite 110 pins it). The new owner guard closes this.
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* OUTSTANDING S4d-01: 'Owner: a session on private projects, with Audrey's nod (it narrows the money gate for a non-creator manager)'
  - *Design:* I treat her 2026-11-01 ruling ('private projects are visible only to creator and admins') as the nod. The fix goes inside can_access_project_money rather than into 28 policy hops, so suite 95 probe 167 moves and 0092 cannot be re-run after 0108.
- *Plan:* OUTSTANDING S4d-02: 'a hop would need the column for every entity type first'
  - *Design:* The column is filled by restating fn_edit_history_capture (0061) with one resolver and a backfill, not by a second edit_history trigger, because suite 95 probe 171 pins exactly one. A row whose project cannot be resolved fails closed to admins.
- *Plan:* OUTSTANDING A-R1-04 candidate fix: skip private rows; the name stays in others' index until their next refetch
  - *Design:* A payload-free AUDIENCE signal plus client revalidation through RLS and closing the open project. Downgrading to INSERT or DELETE shape keeps a hidden side's values off the topic. I chose this over the id-only option the task offered.
- *Plan:* Plan §4 Existing changes: 'Extend 03_assets/04_tasks'; 'Extend 20_realtime, 23_workspace_realtime, 104…'
  - *Design:* Three dedicated suites, 110, 111 and 112 (the 82/83 precedent), registered in the replay list. Existing suites change only where their whole-text pins move (90 probes 7 and 9; 95 probes 11, 13, 17 and 167).
- *Plan:* Plan §4 New tables provisional suites 110_moth_views … 123_file_body_locations
  - *Design:* The privacy repairs take 0107–0109 and suites 110–112 because Moth depends on them, so the Moth table numbers shift by three (remeasure at build).
- **MATERIAL** *Plan:* moth1_core digest: 'A personal space cannot reuse projects.is_private as-is'
  - *Design:* I recommend the personal space's backing project KEEPS is_private = true, so every privacy and suppression mechanism covers it, and that upload routing tests the personal classifier before projectIsPrivate. This is a cross-area decision for the design review.

### Review — security (privacy repairs: write hops, owner column, money gate, edit history, workspace channel)

**Verdict:** Buildable with the fixes, not as written. The core mechanics check out against the code. fn_audit_touch (0001) leaves created_by unguarded. The policies the design names (0013, 0020, 0040, 0041, 0044, 0067, 0082, 0092) are unhopped exactly as it says. Adding passes_project_privacy to can_access_project_money (0037) narrows it safely. 0018's whole-row broadcast is the A-R1-04 leak. Five gaps remain. (1) History gets the project wrong for rows that changed project, so private-era diffs reach public-project managers (SEC-1). (2) Under D9, the owner guard's admin exemption and the D2 shape required of Moth tables break the personal space's owner-only rule (SEC-2). (3) Cross-project dependency edges, both readable and writable, escape every hop, the resolver and the scan (SEC-3). (4) The project topic keeps the old-side leak that D7 fixes on the workspace topic (SEC-4). (5) The catalogue scan cannot catch a second SELECT policy on projects or a hop on one endpoint only (SEC-5). The producer_id seat-minting escalation (SEC-6) and the topic-epoch fix for the join cache (SEC-7) belong in the same migrations, since they touch the same projects policy, guard and topic. Fix SEC-1 through SEC-5 before migrations A, B and C are written; fold SEC-6 into A's guard and SEC-7 into C, or record each as an explicit, ruled residual.

- **SEC-1 [blocker]** D6 attaches history rows to the project the row is in now, not the project it was in when the change happened. Two consequences. (a) An UPDATE that moves a row out of a private project is stamped with the new, public project, and its diff holds the private project's id and the old values. (b) The backfill passes NULL for every 'update' row, so the resolver reads the live row, and every edit made while the row sat in a private project is re-labelled with whatever project holds it today. So S4d-02 is not closed for any entity that ever changed project.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED: C's task T sits in private project P and is edited for weeks (titles, descriptions, phase_id). C then moves T to public project Q. Capture: v_row = NEW, so the resolver gives Q, and the move row's diff carries {project_id:{old:P,new:Q}} plus any column changed in the same statement. Backfill: the old update rows have no project_id key, so the resolver reads the live row and stamps them Q. Manager M2 is not entitled to P, but can_read_project_topic(Q) is true for M2, so M2 reads P's id and every P-era diff. Files take the same route through fn_edit_history_money_snapshot, which overwrites project_id from the live files row (edit_history_file_class prefers f.project_id).
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: supabase/migrations/0061_phase_dependencies.sql fn_edit_history_capture (v_row := to_jsonb(NEW) on UPDATE; the update diff is {key:{old,new}} for changed keys only). 0088_legal_files.sql edit_history_file_class (project_id := COALESCE(v_proj from the live files row, …)). 0092 fn_edit_history_money_snapshot (BEFORE INSERT, sets NEW.project_id). The design's backfill expression (CASE action WHEN 'update' THEN NULL …) and its resolver's live-row fallback.
  - *Fix:* (1) At capture, resolve both sides. When the OLD project differs from the NEW one, stamp NULL, which fails closed to admins, or stamp the private side. The same rule goes into fn_edit_history_money_snapshot for files. (2) Backfill update rows from the history itself. For each entity, walk its retained rows in time order and take the project from the create row's diff->'new'->>'project_id' and from each later diff->'project_id'->>'new'. Use the live row only when no project change is recorded in the window; leave NULL when the create row has been purged and any project change is visible. (3) Add a suite 111 probe: a task moved P→Q leaves zero P-era rows, and no row whose diff names P, readable by M2, with a planted control that reverts to v_row-only.
- **SEC-2 [blocker]** D9 combined with D4 lets a workspace admin un-privatise someone's personal space, and the design's rule for Moth tables admits admins to personal-space Moth rows. The owner guard explicitly lets an admin change is_private on any project. projects_update's new privacy arm admits admins, and so do projects_select and can_write_project. A personal space modelled as an is_private project therefore gives no owner-only boundary. On top of that, migration_order step 4 says every Moth table follows D2's shape (hop through projects_select plus can_write_project), and that admits admins.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED from the policy text. An admin runs UPDATE projects SET is_private=false WHERE id=<Sofia's personal space>. The guard passes (admin arm) and the USING clause passes (admin arm). The space, its files, folders and tasks, and every Moth row that hops through projects become visible to every member and appear in their graphs. Even without the flip, an admin reads Sofia's personal-space Moth rows (personal tags, board content) directly through the API, because the D2 hop evaluates projects_select's admin arm. That breaks 'owner-only in Moth, admins via Files only' and 'personal tags never leak'.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0072_private_projects.sql projects_select (OR current_app_role()='admin'). 0013 can_write_project (admin|manager first arm). The design's fn_projects_owner_guard body (the COALESCE(current_app_role(),'')<>'admin' exemption), D9, and migration_order 'Every Moth table's policies follow D2's shape'.
  - *Fix:* If D9 stands: (1) the owner guard refuses ANY change of is_private (and of created_by) on a personal-space row, for admins too; only the provisioning definer may set them. (2) projects_update USING excludes personal spaces for everyone except the owner (admins reach the files only through the Files paths that are explicitly allowed). (3) Moth-table policies for personal-space rows carry an owner arm with NO admin bypass (the 0017 notes precedent), not the bare D2 hop. Write this rule into D3's scan so that a Moth policy on a personal-space-capable table that lacks the owner arm fails CI. (4) Probe it: an admin's flip throws 42501, and an admin's SELECT of the owner's Moth rows returns 0.
- **SEC-3 [major]** Dependency edges can span projects, and every path that classifies them looks at the predecessor only. The design exempts task_deps_* (and its D3 scan passes phase_deps_*) although: (a) task_deps_update and phase_deps_update have no WITH CHECK and their USING tests only the predecessor, so a writer of public Q can repoint successor_id at a private task or phase; (b) the design's resolver stamps a dependency's history row through predecessor_id only; (c) fn_realtime_broadcast routes a dependency to the predecessor's project topic only.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED schema, INFERRED exploit. (1) C links a Q task (predecessor) to a P task (successor), or moves a P task that already has edges into Q. Every rabbit:project:Q subscriber receives the edge row naming the P task's id, and Q's managers read the history row (stamped Q) carrying successor_id = the P task. (2) M2, a writer of Q who cannot see P, runs UPDATE task_dependencies SET successor_id='<P task id>' WHERE id=<a Q edge>. The USING-as-check tests only the Q predecessor, so the row lands as an edge into private P and changes C's schedule. That is the A-R2-03 write-into-private class, on a table the scan never examines.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0000_rabbit_base_schema.sql task_dependencies (no same-project constraint; only fn_audit_touch, realtime and history triggers). 0013 task_deps_update USING only (predecessor). 0061 phase_deps_update USING only (predecessor). 0004 task_deps_select (both endpoints under RLS). 0098 fn_realtime_broadcast 'task_dependencies' and 'phase_dependencies' arms (predecessor_id). The design's D3 exception list and its edit_history_project_of mapping 'task_dependencies→tasks via predecessor_id'.
  - *Fix:* Choose one of two fixes. (a) Recommended: a same-project rule, a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger (or composite FKs on (predecessor_id, project) and (successor_id, project)) refusing edges whose endpoints sit in different projects. Moving a task that has edges then needs the edges dropped first, which is the task_items NO ACTION precedent. (b) Otherwise: task_deps_update and phase_deps_update gain a WITH CHECK that tests BOTH endpoints under the caller's RLS; the resolver and fn_realtime_broadcast stamp or skip when the two endpoints' projects differ (NULL in history, no broadcast). Either way, remove task_deps_* from D3's exception list and make the scan require a WITH CHECK on every UPDATE policy that hops through a FK.
- **SEC-4 [major]** D7 keeps a hidden side's values off the WORKSPACE topic, but the per-project topic has the same flaw and the design leaves it alone. fn_realtime_broadcast routes an UPDATE by COALESCE(NEW, OLD), meaning NEW's project, and sends the full NEW and OLD rows. A row moved out of a private project is therefore delivered to the public project's topic carrying OLD.project_id = P and every P-side value changed in the same statement.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED routing, INFERRED payload use. C moves an asset, task, milestone, phase or comment from private P to public Q (allowed by D2: C passes both hops). Every rabbit:project:Q joiner receives old_record with P's uuid, its phase_id or asset_id in P, and any old field rewritten in the same statement. A rename-and-move sends the old title too. D7 downgrades exactly this event to INSERT-shaped on the workspace channel, so the channels end up inconsistent.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0098_table_views.sql fn_realtime_broadcast (v_row := COALESCE(to_jsonb(NEW), to_jsonb(OLD)); project from v_row; broadcast_changes(…, NEW, OLD)). The design's D7 scope covers fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast only.
  - *Fix:* Restate fn_realtime_broadcast (latest 0098, keep every arm, the files money arm and the comments resolver word for word). When the OLD and NEW project ids differ: send an INSERT-shaped (NEW, NULL) event to NEW's topic and a DELETE-shaped (NULL, OLD) event to OLD's topic. A delete-shaped event to P's topic is safe, because only P's audience can join it. Add suite 112 probes, vacuous on CI and biting on wilson-dev: for a P→Q move, the Q-topic message holds no P id. Add a CI body probe that the restated function compares the two sides' project ids.
- **SEC-5 [major]** D3's catalogue scan cannot fail on the shapes that matter. (a) For reads it requires that a table HAVE a SELECT policy carrying 'FROM projects'. Because permissive policies OR together, a second, broad SELECT policy beside it passes the scan. (b) It never pins that public.projects has exactly ONE permissive SELECT policy carrying the privacy arm, yet every hop in A, the hops in C (0101 shape), can_read_project_topic and the topic join all inherit privacy from that single policy. (c) Matching the 'FROM projects' string does not check which column is correlated, or that both endpoints of a two-FK table are hopped (SEC-3). (d) Tables without a project_id column, which includes most of Moth's planned node and edge tables, escape the read half of the scan.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED. A Moth schema migration adds projects_select_personal (owner reads their personal space) or an admin trash-view policy as a second SELECT policy on projects. Every hop in 0107 and in Moth's tables silently widens to whatever that policy admits, and suite 110's scan stays green. Likewise, a Moth edge table with hops only on source_id passes the scan while target_id points into a private project.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0072 projects_select is the only SELECT policy on projects today (0004 dropped 0002's projects_select_by_workspace). 0098 post-condition 11a is the precedent ('exactly four policies, none FOR ALL, none restrictive'). The design's D3 text ('must also have a SELECT policy carrying FROM projects').
  - *Fix:* Extend the scan. (1) Count the permissive SELECT policies on public.projects: exactly one, named projects_select, whose qual names is_private, created_by and the admin arm. (2) Check EVERY permissive policy on a table that carries project_id, or a FK to a project-scoped table, not just one per table. (3) Match the correlated column with a regex, as 0098 does (FROM projects p\s+WHERE \(p\.id = <t>\.project_id\)). (4) Require one hop per uuid FK column that references a project-scoped table (from pg_constraint), and a WITH CHECK on every UPDATE policy. (5) Plant a control for each: add a broad second SELECT policy on projects, and the scan probe must go red.
- **SEC-6 [major]** The project.producer_id auto-staff path mints manager seats past can_manage_project_roster, and the design restates projects_update and adds a projects guard trigger without closing it. Any caller who passes projects_update USING can set producer_id. That means any 'member' seat on a staffed project, or any member at all on an unstaffed one, because can_write_project opens to members. fn_projects_auto_staff (SECURITY DEFINER) then inserts that user as project 'manager'. A manager seat opens can_access_project_money (invoices, Legal objects, budgets) and roster management.
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED. Priya holds a 'member' seat on public project Q. She runs UPDATE projects SET producer_id='<Jonah>' WHERE id=Q. The trigger inserts Jonah as manager (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING applies only if he already has a seat). Jonah now reads Q's INVOICES and LEGAL objects and its budget tables, and can add or remove seats. On an unstaffed (legacy or imported) project, a user sets producer_id to themselves and becomes its only manager.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0020_admin_grants_and_alignment.sql fn_projects_auto_staff and trg_projects_auto_staff (AFTER INSERT OR UPDATE OF producer_id, SECURITY DEFINER, seats 'manager'). 0013 projects_update USING can_write_project(id). 0013 can_write_project (NOT project_is_staffed OR seat manager|member). 0037 can_access_project_money (project_role_for = 'manager'). supabase/tests/rls/24_admin_grants.sql probes 24-27 pin the seating but not who may cause it.
  - *Fix:* In fn_projects_owner_guard (or a sibling guard): on UPDATE, when producer_id IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.producer_id, refuse with 42501 unless can_manage_project_roster(OLD.id) is true (admin, app manager or project manager). Alternatively, make the trigger's UPDATE arm seat the new producer only when the caller passes can_manage_project_roster. Probe: a member-seat UPDATE of producer_id throws, and the roster count is unchanged.
- **SEC-7 [major]** The 'leak-free invalidation for a project made private after the fact' is not leak-free. The design files the Realtime join-cache case under OUTSTANDING as something the database cannot fix, but a server-side fix exists: rotate the topic. A non-entitled client that is already joined to rabbit:project:<id> keeps receiving full rows (tasks, files, comments, roster) for as long as Realtime's join authorisation stays cached. The provider keeps the channel through token refreshes, so that can be up to the token's lifetime or longer.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED (Realtime authorises at join). An admin makes Q private while M2 has Q open in a client patched to ignore 'audience' effects. Every later write in the now-private Q is broadcast to rabbit:project:Q and delivered to M2's cached subscription.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0016 rabbit_project_topic_read (authorises by can_read_project_topic at join). 0098 fn_realtime_broadcast topic 'rabbit:project:'||v_project. RabbitProvider.jsx wsAuthKey comment ('token refreshes … don't tear the channel down'). The design's risk 4 and D1's OUTSTANDING residual.
  - *Fix:* Add an epoch to the topic name: a projects.topic_epoch integer that the owner guard (or a definer path) bumps whenever visibility narrows (is_private false→true, or a later created_by or personal change). fn_realtime_broadcast sends to 'rabbit:project:<id>:<epoch>'. rabbit_project_topic_read checks the epoch segment against the live row through can_read_project_topic. Clients learn the epoch through RLS on their index refetch, so a stale subscription hears nothing after the flip. Probe on wilson-dev: after the flip, nothing lands on the old-epoch topic. If this is deferred, D1 must call it a known leak with Audrey's nod, not a database impossibility.
- **SEC-8 [minor]** D7 keeps 0018's relevance filters word for word and runs them on BOTH sides, including the side that is hidden. Whether a downgraded event is emitted at all then depends on hidden-side values, which leaks one bit.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED. C moves task T from public Q (no assignee) to private P, where it gets an assignee in the same UPDATE. The tasks filter passes because NEW.assignee_id is set, and a DELETE-shaped Q event goes out. If P's side had no assignee, no event would go out. Watchers learn that the task now has an assignee in a project they cannot see.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: 0018_workspace_channel.sql fn_workspace_realtime_broadcast (the tasks filter reads NEW and OLD assignee_id/reviewer_id; the assets filter compares NEW and OLD deleted_at/name/phase_id). The design's D7 sketch keeps both 'word for word' before the side judgement.
  - *Fix:* Compute the visible sides first, then apply the 0018 filters to the visible side(s) only (for a downgraded event, test only the side that is sent). Add a body probe that the filters run after the side flags.
- **SEC-9 [minor]** The hops check only <t>.project_id, while secondary uuid columns can still point into a private project because they lack same-project FKs: tasks.asset_id and phase_id, files.folder_id, folders.parent_id and milestones.phase_id. Their ids then reach non-viewers through ordinary RLS reads and through D7's INSERT-shaped NEW payload. A writer of Q who knows a P id can also attach Q rows under P's structure.
  - *Scenario:* INFERRED. C moves a task from P to Q without clearing asset_id or phase_id. Every Q reader, and the downgraded workspace event, now carries P's asset and phase ids. Or M2 sets a Q file's folder_id to a P folder id they learned before the fix, from the old workspace-channel leak.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: only task_items carries composite (entity_id, project_id) FKs (0096). tasks has tasks_id_project_key but no (asset_id, project_id) FK (grep of 0084/0096/0097). The digest's fact 12 (files.folder_id has no same-project check). 0041 folders_* policies (project_id only).
  - *Fix:* Add same-project composite FKs (MATCH SIMPLE, so NULLs pass), or BEFORE triggers, for tasks(asset_id|phase_id), files(folder_id), folders(parent_id) and milestones(phase_id). Alternatively, extend the hop to each non-null secondary FK under the caller's RLS. Cover them with the scan requirement in SEC-5(4).
- **SEC-10 [minor]** D1 says only two items go to OUTSTANDING, yet a count oracle the rulings forbid stays open and unlisted. workspace_storage_usage() is a SECURITY DEFINER function executable by every active member, and it returns workspace totals that include private-project money and Legal bodies, and, under D9, personal-space bodies stored in company storage. Polling it reveals when hidden files arrive and how big they are (S4b-06).
  - *Scenario:* MEASURED function, INFERRED use. A member polls used_bytes and sees a 40 MB rise while no visible project changed. They learn that a private project or a personal space received a file of that size.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED: workspace_storage_usage() (SECURITY DEFINER, GRANT EXECUTE TO authenticated; the body is workspace_petal_bytes(w.id) over every key). docs/OUTSTANDING.md S4b-06 (digest outstanding 1).
  - *Fix:* Restrict the function to admins (the Admin Terminal is its reader), or have it return only buckets the caller can see. At minimum, add it to D1's OUTSTANDING list beside the join-cache residual, with Audrey's ruling ('no count that reveals existence') cited.

### Review — Correctness and house rules (privacy repairs: write hops, money and history, the workspace channel, the owner column)

**Verdict:** The design can be built once the fixes above are made. As written it is not buildable. Migration A's own catalogue post-conditions would abort it on the current schema: the six JOIN-shaped hops on phase_deps and ingestion_chunks, gateway_ticket_mints with no policy, and edit_history before B. A would also turn the shipped suite 83 red (probe 20 and its presence control). D9 together with D4 lets the owner or an admin make a personal space public, and D9's claim that the admin arm 'covers' personal spaces contradicts her Files-only ruling. The rest checks out against the code: the policy texts named (0013, 0020, 0040, 0041, 0044, 0067, 0082, 0092) are the latest, the money-gate body (0037) and the capture body (0061) are the latest, the broadcast body (0018) has never been restated, and the replay list, tail pins and 35_platform_audit handling are correct.

- **PR-01 [blocker]** Migration A cannot apply as written. D3's catalogue post-condition says every policy that calls a gate must carry the literal 'FROM projects' on every clause. Six policies already hop correctly but fail that text test, and they are neither on the exception list nor in schema_changes. Their hop is a JOIN, and pg_policies prints a JOIN as 'FROM (phases pre JOIN projects pp ...' or 'FROM (ingestion_runs r JOIN projects p ...'.
  - *Scenario:* 0107 runs its D3 scan. phase_deps_insert, phase_deps_update and phase_deps_delete (0061) and ingestion_chunks_insert, ingestion_chunks_update and ingestion_chunks_delete (0013) all call can_write_project. None of them contains the text 'FROM projects', so the post-condition raises and the migration rolls back on wilson-dev and on CI. Suite 110's matching scan probe is red on a correct schema.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0061_phase_dependencies.sql phase_deps_* (the EXISTS goes through 'public.phases pre JOIN public.projects pp'). 0013_project_members.sql ingestion_chunks_* ('public.ingestion_runs r JOIN public.projects p'). 0092 c_hop shows how a plain hop prints ('FROM projects p'). The exception list in the design names only asset_versions_*, task_deps_*, task_links_* and projects_update.
  - *Fix:* Match the relation as a token after FROM or JOIN (for example the regex '(FROM|JOIN) \(?projects ' run on the deparsed text). Alternatively, add phase_deps_* and ingestion_chunks_* to the pinned exceptions with their reason (they hop to projects through a JOIN, under the caller's RLS). Run the scan in the rehearsal before writing the exception list and record the list it actually produced.
- **PR-02 [blocker]** Migration A also cannot pass D3's second scan, which requires every public table with a project_id column to have a SELECT policy with a hop or an allowed gate. Two tables fail it. gateway_ticket_mints (0093) has a project_id column and no policy at all; it is closed to clients by REVOKE ALL. edit_history_select gets can_read_project_topic only in migration B, so at A's post-condition it has neither 'FROM projects' nor that gate.
  - *Scenario:* 0107's post-condition finds gateway_ticket_mints with no SELECT policy and edit_history_select without the allowed gate, and raises. Migration A cannot land, and B, which requires 0107 to be present, cannot land either.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0093_file_gateway.sql: gateway_ticket_mints has project_id, ENABLE and FORCE RLS, 'REVOKE ALL ... FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated' and no CREATE POLICY. 0092_legal_gate_managers.sql edit_history_select (latest) has workspace, role, membership and money arms only. The project_id tables were found by enumerating CREATE TABLE columns across the migrations.
  - *Fix:* Allow a table that has no SELECT grant for authenticated (check has_table_privilege) and no policy. Check edit_history in B's post-condition and in suite 111, not in A's. Make the list of allowed tables explicit: budget_versions, budget_lines, budget_actuals, expenses, project_rate_overrides, upload_reservations, file_events, edit_history (B onward) and gateway_ticket_mints.
- **PR-03 [major]** Migration A turns suite 83 red, and the design does not list suite 83 among the files to change (it even says 83 must stay green). Probe 20 is the failing control for C-R2-01. It expects the second manager's column-free `UPDATE public.files SET description = 'x'` to throw 42501, because the WITH CHECK hop meets the private file a1. Once files_update's USING hops, a1 is never admitted, so the statement succeeds and rewrites the public file b1.
  - *Scenario:* Whole pgTAP set after 0107: suite 83 throws_ok(UPDATE public.files SET description = 'x', '42501') fails because nothing is thrown. The presence control that follows it (a1 and b1 both with description IS NULL, count 2) now counts 1, because b1 was rewritten. CI pgTAP goes red.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. supabase/tests/rls/83_private_project_file_gates.sql: probe 20 at `$$UPDATE public.files SET description = 'x'$$`, and the presence control 'the refused column-free UPDATE rewrote neither the private file nor the public one' (count = 2). The design's consumers_to_change lists only suites 90 and 95.
  - *Fix:* In 0107's commit, retarget suite 83 probe 20 to lives_ok. Its control becomes: a1 is untouched (still NULL) and b1 changed (proof the statement ran). Update 83's header and the OUTSTANDING C-R2-01 text. Before planning, grep every suite for column-free writes on the 15 restated tables.
- **PR-04 [major]** D4 together with D9 breaks her personal-space ruling. D9 makes a personal space private only because its backing project has is_private = true. D4's owner guard still lets OLD.created_by (the owner) or any workspace admin change is_private, and projects_update USING admits both of them. So one plain UPDATE makes a personal space visible to the whole workspace.
  - *Scenario:* An admin runs `UPDATE projects SET is_private = false WHERE id = <Sofia's personal space>`. projects_update admits the admin and the guard allows the change. Every member now sees the personal space's tasks, files and roster through projects_select, and the workspace channel starts carrying its rows. The ruling ('a single folder that counts as just them, owner-only') is broken. The owner can do the same by mistake from any client that resends the whole row.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. Design D4 guard body (the `OLD.created_by IS DISTINCT FROM auth.uid() AND ... <> 'admin'` refusal only) and D9. supabaseAdapter.js PROJECT_COLUMNS includes 'is_private'. 0072_private_projects.sql projects_select's admin arm.
  - *Fix:* The guard must refuse any change of is_private (and of created_by) on a personal-space backing project for every authenticated caller, owner and admin included, using the schema area's classifier. Pin it in suite 110 with a planted control. If the schema area does not keep is_private on personal spaces, D9's fallback must carry the same refusal.
- **PR-05 [major]** D9 says every privacy mechanism here covers personal spaces 'with no extra arm'. That is false against her ruling, because projects_select, passes_project_privacy and every D2 hop (which runs under projects_select) contain `current_app_role() = 'admin'`. With is_private = true, admins see every personal space wherever projects are read, not only in Files. A Moth table that follows D2's shape admits admins too.
  - *Scenario:* An admin opens R.A.B.B.I.T.'s project list, the Dashboard or Moth's catalogue. projectsIndex comes from listProjects through projects_select and contains every member's personal space, because the admin arm passes. A Moth policy written as D2's hop (EXISTS projects) also returns the personal space's Moth rows to the admin. Her ruling is 'reachable by workspace admins from the Files view only (never Moth)'.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0072 projects_select arm; 0082 passes_project_privacy (`OR public.current_app_role() = 'admin'`); 0016 can_read_project_topic (an EXISTS under the caller's RLS). The digest's client fact 3 (ProjectFilesExplorer and projectsIndex show admins the private projects).
  - *Fix:* State in D9 and in the Moth shape rule that a personal-space Moth row needs its own owner-only arm beside the D2 hop, along the lines of NOT personal(project) OR created_by = auth.uid(), with no admin arm. Make suite 110's scan (or the schema area's suite) require that arm on every Moth table. Hand the client-index exclusion to the schema area as a dependency rather than calling it covered.
- **PR-06 [minor]** Suite 110's per-table probe matrix (5) to (7) cannot run as written on several tables.
  - *Scenario:* (a) comments has no project_id column (0000: entity_type and entity_id), so `UPDATE comments SET project_id = P` raises 42703. (b) A filter-less `UPDATE project_members SET project_id = Q` collides on the primary key (project_id, user_id) whenever two seats share a user. The same move on folders collides on folders_project_path_uniq (project_id, path), because every project has a root folder. Either way probe (6)'s lives_ok gets 23505. (c) tasks, assets, scenes, shots, levels and experiences are referenced by same-project composite foreign keys (task_items 0096; bin_files and shot_takes 0091; shot_list_items and edits 0084), which are ON UPDATE NO ACTION. So probe (6) throws 23503 as soon as any visible fixture row has a dependent, for example a task with an asset, which fn_tasks_seed_items gives an item. This also means D2's 'moves rows out and reads them' is already refused by those foreign keys for many rows. OUTSTANDING C-R2-01 notes the column-free statement is refused outright where pg-safeupdate is preloaded for the API role.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0000 comments columns; 0013 project_members PRIMARY KEY (project_id, user_id); 0041 folders_project_path_uniq; 0096 task_items FOREIGN KEY (task_id, project_id) REFERENCES tasks (id, project_id); 0091 and 0084 composite keys; OUTSTANDING.md C-R2-01 (pg-safeupdate, unmeasured).
  - *Fix:* Give each table its own probe shape. For comments, move by entity_id. For project_members, use a seat whose user has no seat on Q. For folders, use a non-root fixture or restate the move test as a column-free update of another column. For the tables with composite foreign keys, use fixture rows with no dependents. Reword D2's rationale with the foreign-key and pg-safeupdate limits (still defence in depth).
- **PR-07 [minor]** D6's rationale ('a delete resolves its project even when a cascade has removed the parent') is wrong for asset_versions, task_dependencies, phase_dependencies, task_links and ingestion_chunks. Their row holds only the parent's foreign key, and the resolver looks the parent up live. In an AFTER DELETE fired by a cascade, the parent is already gone, so the result is NULL and the row is readable by admins only. B also makes can_read_project_topic decide all project history, and that function is false for a TRASHED project (projects_select's deleted_at arm). A non-admin manager therefore loses the history of a trashed project. That change of behaviour is not listed.
  - *Scenario:* A task is hard-deleted and its task_links cascade. capture → edit_history_project_of('task_links', ..., OLD row) → the tasks lookup by task_id finds no row → project_id NULL. A manager who could read that history today no longer can. A manager opening the history of a project an admin trashed also gets 0 rows.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0061 fn_edit_history_capture is an AFTER trigger and resolves parents in step (b) the same way. 0016 can_read_project_topic (an EXISTS under projects_select, which requires deleted_at IS NULL).
  - *Fix:* Correct the rationale. List both narrowings in plan_differences and in the hand-off (both are fail-closed and consistent with the rulings). Keep the fail-closed probe in suite 111 and add a trashed-project probe.
- **PR-08 [minor]** The re-run notes are incomplete. After B, 0092 fails post-condition 10, which pins edit_history_select's whole text (B changes it), as well as post-condition 12. passes_project_privacy's COMMENT (latest 0092) lists its callers and would be false once can_access_project_money calls it. The three OUTSTANDING entries whose fix shapes name '0107 / suite 110' (the avatar policy, the UNC CHECK and the bin_files audio columns) become stale, and the design does not list them under docs to change.
  - *Scenario:* Someone replays 0092 after 0108 and it fails on post-condition 10 before it reaches 12. A reader of passes_project_privacy's COMMENT misses the money gate when restating the privacy arm. A later session follows OUTSTANDING's '0107' for the avatars fix and collides with migration A.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0092_legal_gate_managers.sql post-condition 10 (edit_history_select qual IS DISTINCT FROM ...) and section 10 (COMMENT ON FUNCTION passes_project_privacy 'Callers: ...'). The digest's outstanding-1 notes on L350, L410 and L773.
  - *Fix:* Name post-conditions 10 and 12 in the re-run note. Restate the COMMENT on passes_project_privacy in 0108 to add can_access_project_money. Update the three OUTSTANDING fix-shape lines to the next free numbers after the Moth migrations.
- **PR-09 [minor]** One of D4's MEASURED premises is false. The design says no client writes projects.created_by, but PROJECT_COLUMNS includes 'created_by'. updateProject keeps whatever the patch carries, and RabbitProvider resends whole rows, so the column does reach PostgREST on every project save.
  - *Scenario:* A manager renames a project and the PATCH body includes created_by. Today that would let a forged value through. With the guard, the column is silently reset to OLD.created_by, so nothing breaks, but the premise as recorded in the hand-off and the handbook would be wrong.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. src/tools/rabbit_v0.1.0/adapters/supabaseAdapter.js PROJECT_COLUMNS (ends with 'created_at', 'created_by', 'updated_at', ...) and updateProject (toColumns('projects', ...)). migrationRows.js PROJECT_LEFT_AT_HOME excludes created_by on the migration path only.
  - *Fix:* Relabel the premise: the client sends created_by on every save; the guard makes it inert. Optionally drop created_by from PROJECT_COLUMNS in the same commit, with a production-caller check.
- **PR-10 [minor]** Channel suppression costs the people who are entitled. The creator's and admins' other windows stop receiving their own private projects' index events: rename, trash and restore, assigned tasks and seats. D8's defence ('a private row on the workspace channel schedules a refetch') never fires, because the server never sends such rows. The design does not list this loss of 0018's index liveness for private projects.
  - *Scenario:* An admin trashes the creator's private project. Before 0109, the creator's other window removed it through the workspace event carrying deleted_at. After 0109, nothing is sent, and the project stays in that window's index until the next join refetch.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. 0018 header ('projects — index liveness (create/rename/trash/restore)'). RabbitProvider.handleWorkspaceEvent (removes the project on deleted_at, merges it otherwise). Design D7, which sends nothing when neither side is visible.
  - *Fix:* Either accept it explicitly in plan_differences and the handbook §4.5, or, when a private project row changes, send a payload-free signal of the same kind as AUDIENCE (for example operation 'INDEX', no id). Clients would then refetch through RLS, which leaks nothing beyond AUDIENCE.
- **PR-11 [minor]** Gaps in the client plan for D8. refreshProjectsIndex returns nothing today and has no sequence guard. scheduleProjectsIndexRefetch shares wsIndexRefetchTimerRef with the refetch on every join and clears it on each call, so a 'revalidate the open project' flag set by AUDIENCE can be lost when the two coalesce. The project-trashed teardown leaves activeProjectId saved in the R.A.B.B.I.T. settings. Separately, suite 111's ordering rationale (a runner seat on a private project, which 'A's hops no longer allow for a client') is not a reason to order B after A, because the runner bypasses RLS.
  - *Scenario:* A join refetch is already scheduled when AUDIENCE arrives; one debounced call runs without the flag, and the hidden open project stays open. Or the teardown runs, the app restarts, and it tries to reopen the project it just closed.
  - *Evidence:* MEASURED. RabbitProvider.jsx refreshProjectsIndex (setProjectsIndex(indexById(list)), no return value), scheduleProjectsIndexRefetch (a single timer ref, clearTimeout on each call), the effects reducer's project-trashed branch (setActiveProjectIdState(null) with no saveRabbitSettings).
  - *Fix:* Keep the revalidate flag in a ref that the debounced run reads and clears. Have refreshProjectsIndex return the list behind a sequence number. On close, also clear the saved activeProjectId (or confirm a load failure tears down cleanly). Order B after A because B's pre-flight requires 0107, not because of the runner's fixtures.
